## `probstat.inverse.approximation_error`

### EQ-INV-AE-001 — Paired approximation error and its sample moments

`e_k = G_f(x_k) - G_c(x_k)`, `mu_e = (1/K) sum e_k`, `S = (1/(K-1)) sum (e_k - mu_e)(e_k - mu_e)'`, with `x_k` drawn from the prior and `K` the number of training pairs.

- Domain: `x_k` independent prior draws, supplied by the caller; both models finite vectors of equal length `m`; the training part excludes the held-out validation pairs.
- Meaning: Gaussian summary of the discrepancy between the fine and the coarse forward model. `S` has rank at most `K - 1`; a full covariance needs `K > m`.
- Code: `inverse.approximation_error.estimate_approximation_error`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-AE-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ae-001--paired-approximation-error-and-its-sample-moments)

### EQ-INV-AE-002 — Shrinkage of the error covariance

`Sigma_e = (1 - lambda) S + lambda D` with `D = diag(S)` for a fixed `lambda in (0, 1]`; `Sigma_e = (1 - lambda_LW) S + lambda_LW (tr(S)/m) I` for the Ledoit-Wolf intensity `lambda_LW`.

- Domain: `K_train >= 2`; the diagonal of `S` must be positive for the fixed-intensity target to give a positive definite result.
- Meaning: makes `Sigma_e` positive definite when `K_train <= m`. The Ledoit-Wolf intensity is computed by `statistics.ledoit_wolf_covariance` (divisor `K`) and applied to `S` with divisor `K - 1`. No jitter is added; a non positive definite result raises.
- Code: `inverse.approximation_error.estimate_approximation_error`.
- Source: [REF-LEDOIT-WOLF-2004], [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-LEDOIT-WOLF-2004](../references.md#ref-ledoit-wolf-2004): Olivier Ledoit and Michael Wolf. "A Well-Conditioned Estimator for Large-Dimensional Covariance Matrices." *Journal of Multivariate Analysis* 88(2), 365-411, 2004. DOI: [10.1016/S0047-259X(03)00096-4](https://doi.org/10.1016/S0047-259X(03)00096-4).
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-AE-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ae-002--shrinkage-of-the-error-covariance)

### EQ-INV-AE-003 — Approximation-error likelihood

`y = G_c(x) + e + eps`, `e ~ N(mu_e, Sigma_e)`, `eps ~ N(0, Gamma)`, so `y - mu_e | x ~ N(G_c(x), Gamma + Sigma_e)`.

- Domain: `e` independent of `eps` and, in this basic model, of `x`.
- Meaning: the coarse model is used with shifted data `y - mu_e` and the inflated noise covariance `Gamma + Sigma_e`. A prior-trained error model can be wrong in the posterior region; if the conditional mean or covariance of `e` depends on `x` an explicit likelihood is needed. Wider intervals and better coverage are not guaranteed.
- Code: `inverse.approximation_error.ApproximationErrorModel.combined_noise`, `inverse.approximation_error.ApproximationErrorModel.corrected_data`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-AE-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ae-003--approximation-error-likelihood)

### EQ-INV-AE-004 — Held-out standardized error

`d_k = (e_k - mu_e)' Sigma_e^-1 (e_k - mu_e)` for held-out pairs `k`; report `mean(d_k)` and the fraction of `d_k` above the 95% quantile of `chi2(m)`.

- Domain: held-out pairs disjoint from the training pairs.
- Meaning: under a correct Gaussian model `d_k` is approximately `chi2(m)` (mean `m`, exceedance fraction 0.05); training estimation error inflates it slightly for small `K_train`. Tuning shrinkage on these pairs and then scoring on them again is not allowed.
- Code: `inverse.approximation_error.ApproximationErrorModel`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-AE-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ae-004--held-out-standardized-error)

### EQ-INV-AE-005 — Error-parameter cross-covariance

`C_ex = (1/(K-1)) sum (e_k - mu_e)(x_k - mu_x)'`.

- Domain: training pairs only.
- Meaning: diagnostic of the independence of `e` and `x`; a large `C_ex` (relative to `Sigma_e` and the prior covariance) indicates that the additive independent error model is inadequate. It does not by itself detect nonlinear dependence. For a linear pair `e = (A_f - A_c) x`, `C_ex = (A_f - A_c) C0`.
- Code: `inverse.approximation_error.estimate_approximation_error`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-AE-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ae-005--error-parameter-cross-covariance)

## `probstat.inverse.benchmark_catalog`

### EQ-INV-BENCH3-001 — Poisson count model, scalar MLE and Fisher information

`y_i ~ Poisson(mu_i)`, `mu = A x`, `log p(y | x) = sum_i [y_i log mu_i - mu_i - log y_i!]`;
for one pixel with exposure `t`, `mu = t x`, score `y / x - t`, MLE `x_hat = y / t`,
Fisher information `I(x) = t / x`, Wald standard error `sqrt(x_hat / t)`.

- Domain: `x >= 0`, `t > 0`, `y` a non-negative integer, `A >= 0` entrywise for the image case.
- Meaning: the exact scalar case is the closed-form reference of benchmark S01; for `A` with several
  rows the information is `A' diag(1 / (A x)) A`. The term `y log mu` is dropped for `y = 0`.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_catalog.poisson_scalar_reference`,
  `inverse.benchmark_catalog.poisson_scalar_benchmark`,
  `inverse.benchmark_catalog.PoissonScalarReference`.
- Source: [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989].
  - [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989](../references.md#ref-mccullagh-nelder-1989): P. McCullagh and J. A. Nelder. *Generalized Linear Models*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH3-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench3-001--poisson-count-model-scalar-mle-and-fisher-information)

### EQ-INV-BENCH3-002 — Zero count: boundary MLE and exact limits

For `y = 0` the likelihood `exp(-t x)` is strictly decreasing, so the maximizer is the boundary `x = 0`
and the information is undefined. The exact one-sided upper limit at confidence `c` is
`x_up = -log(1 - c) / t` (from `exp(-t x_up) = 1 - c`). The exact central interval for `y = k` is
`[chi2_{alpha/2}(2k) / (2t), chi2_{1-alpha/2}(2k + 2) / (2t)]`, `alpha = 1 - level` (lower limit `0` for `k = 0`).

- Domain: `t > 0`, `0 < c < 1`, `0 < level < 1`.
- Meaning: zero counts are valid data, not errors; they put the constrained MLE on the constraint and make
  Wald intervals meaningless, while the exact limits stay finite.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_catalog.zero_count_upper_limit`,
  `inverse.benchmark_catalog.PoissonScalarReference.garwood_interval`.
- Source: [REF-GARWOOD-1936].
  - [REF-GARWOOD-1936](../references.md#ref-garwood-1936): F. Garwood. "Fiducial Limits for the Poisson Distribution." *Biometrika* 28(3-4), 437-442, 1936. DOI: [10.1093/biomet/28.3-4.437](https://doi.org/10.1093/biomet/28.3-4.437).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH3-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench3-002--zero-count-boundary-mle-and-exact-limits)

### EQ-INV-BENCH3-003 — Positivity-constrained Poisson MLE: KKT conditions and ML-EM

`min_{x >= 0} -log p(y | A x)` has the gradient `g = A'(1 - y / (A x))`; a maximizer satisfies `x >= 0`,
`g >= 0`, `x_j g_j = 0`. For `A >= 0` with column sums `s = A' 1` the multiplicative update
`x_j <- (x_j / s_j) [A'(y / (A x))]_j` keeps `x > 0`, never decreases the likelihood and converges to a
KKT point. The reported residual is `max_j |min(x_j, g_j)|`.

- Domain: `A >= 0`, `s > 0`, positive start, `y > 0` only where `(A x)_i > 0`.
- Meaning: positivity is a CONSTRAINT here (no log parametrisation). Fisher scoring with the identity link
  keeps `A x > 0` by line search but does not constrain `x`, so with zero counts it can return negative
  intensities; the benchmark reports `positive` and the KKT residual instead of hiding this.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_catalog.poisson_em`, `inverse.benchmark_catalog.PoissonReference`,
  `inverse.benchmark_catalog.PoissonEMResult`.
- Source: [REF-SHEPP-VARDI-1982], [REF-LUCY-1974], [REF-RICHARDSON-1972], [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-SHEPP-VARDI-1982](../references.md#ref-shepp-vardi-1982): L. A. Shepp and Y. Vardi. "Maximum Likelihood Reconstruction for Emission Tomography." *IEEE Transactions on Medical Imaging* 1(2), 113-122, 1982. Cited for the expectation-maximization (multiplicative) iteration for the Poisson likelihood with non-negativity. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1109/TMI.1982.4307558](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMI.1982.4307558).
  - [REF-LUCY-1974](../references.md#ref-lucy-1974): L. B. Lucy. "An Iterative Technique for the Rectification of Observed Distributions." *The Astronomical Journal* 79(6), 745-754, 1974. Cited for the Richardson-Lucy deconvolution iteration. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1086/111605](https://doi.org/10.1086/111605).
  - [REF-RICHARDSON-1972](../references.md#ref-richardson-1972): W. H. Richardson. "Bayesian-Based Iterative Method of Image Restoration." *Journal of the Optical Society of America* 62(1), 55-59, 1972. Cited for the Richardson-Lucy deconvolution iteration. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1364/JOSA.62.000055](https://doi.org/10.1364/JOSA.62.000055).
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH3-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench3-003--positivity-constrained-poisson-mle-kkt-conditions-and-ml-em)

### EQ-INV-BENCH3-004 — S01 image benchmark: fine-representation mean counts

`x_true(s) = b + p * shape(s)` with a strictly positive background `b`; the mean counts are
`bar y = t * Avg_r( B_fine x_fine )` where `B_fine` is the Gaussian blur with the SAME physical kernel on the
`r`-times finer grid, `Avg_r` the cell average, and the solver sees `A = t B` on the coarse grid with the
truth `Avg_r(x_fine)`; counts are `y_i ~ Poisson(bar y_i)` from the `noise` stream.

- Domain: `r >= 1`, `t > 0`, `b > 0`, blur width `> 0` (or none).
- Meaning: `r = 1` is the inverse crime (`bar y = A x_true`); `r > 1` leaves a genuine discretization error.
  Poisson noise is signal dependent, so no relative noise level is defined.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_catalog.poisson_image_benchmark`, `inverse.benchmark_catalog.PoissonBenchmark`.
- Source: [REF-GAZZOLA-HANSEN-NAGY-2019], [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989].
  - [REF-GAZZOLA-HANSEN-NAGY-2019](../references.md#ref-gazzola-hansen-nagy-2019): Silvia Gazzola, Per Christian Hansen, and James G. Nagy. "IR Tools: A MATLAB Package of Iterative Regularization Methods and Large-Scale Test Problems." *Numerical Algorithms* 81(3), 773-811, 2019. Cited for matrix-free operators, iterative regularization by early stopping, hybrid projection methods and their test problems. No code was copied. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/s11075-018-0570-7](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11075-018-0570-7).
  - [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989](../references.md#ref-mccullagh-nelder-1989): P. McCullagh and J. A. Nelder. *Generalized Linear Models*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH3-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench3-004--s01-image-benchmark-fine-representation-mean-counts)

### EQ-INV-BENCH3-005 — M01 model: linear simulator, GP discrepancy, AR(1) noise

`y = H theta + delta + e`, `H = [1, x_i]`, `delta ~ N(0, K + nu I)`, `K_ij = k_psi(x_i, x_j)`,
`e ~ N(0, Sigma_e)`, `(Sigma_e)_ij = sigma_e^2 rho^|i-j|`; the noise is generated by the recursion
`e_1 = sigma_e z_1`, `e_i = rho e_{i-1} + sigma_e sqrt(1 - rho^2) z_i`.

- Domain: `sigma_delta > 0`, `ell > 0`, `nu >= 0`, `sigma_e > 0`, `0 <= rho < 1`.
- Meaning: Kennedy-O'Hagan discrepancy with a known covariance structure; the nugget `nu` is part of the
  declared model so that `K + nu I` is positive definite without hidden jitter. The recursion and the
  Cholesky-based discrepancy draw are independent of the covariance matrix used by the reference.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_catalog.discrepancy_benchmark`, `inverse.benchmark_catalog.DiscrepancyBenchmark`.
- Source: [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001], [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006].
  - [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001](../references.md#ref-kennedy-ohagan-2001): Marc C. Kennedy and Anthony O'Hagan. "Bayesian Calibration of Computer Models." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B* 63(3), 425-464, 2001. DOI: [10.1111/1467-9868.00294](https://doi.org/10.1111/1467-9868.00294).
  - [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006](../references.md#ref-rasmussen-williams-2006): Carl Edward Rasmussen and Christopher K. I. Williams. *Gaussian Processes for Machine Learning*. MIT Press, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH3-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench3-005--m01-model-linear-simulator-gp-discrepancy-ar1-noise)

### EQ-INV-BENCH3-006 — Marginalised Gaussian conditional of the parameters

With `C = K + nu I + Sigma_e`, `theta ~ N(m0, P0)`:
`Sigma_post = (H' C^-1 H + P0^-1)^-1`, `mu_post = Sigma_post (H' C^-1 y + P0^-1 m0)`,
`log p(y) = log N(y; H m0, C + H P0 H')`; the central marginal interval is `mu_i +- z sd_i`.

- Domain: `C`, `P0` symmetric positive definite.
- Meaning: the discrepancy is integrated out exactly, so this is the exact conditional of `theta` under the
  true hyperparameters (not an estimate of them). Dropping `K` (or also the noise correlation) gives the
  misspecified `no_discrepancy` and `white_noise` models.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_catalog.gaussian_conditional_reference`,
  `inverse.benchmark_catalog.GaussianConditionalReference`,
  `inverse.benchmark_catalog.DiscrepancyBenchmark.conditional_reference`.
- Source: [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006, Section 2.2], [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001].
  - [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006](../references.md#ref-rasmussen-williams-2006): Carl Edward Rasmussen and Christopher K. I. Williams. *Gaussian Processes for Machine Learning*. MIT Press, 2006.
  - [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001](../references.md#ref-kennedy-ohagan-2001): Marc C. Kennedy and Anthony O'Hagan. "Bayesian Calibration of Computer Models." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B* 63(3), 425-464, 2001. DOI: [10.1111/1467-9868.00294](https://doi.org/10.1111/1467-9868.00294).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH3-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench3-006--marginalised-gaussian-conditional-of-the-parameters)

### EQ-INV-BENCH3-007 — Measured interval coverage over predeclared seeds

`cov_j = (1/N) sum_s 1[ theta_j in I_j(s) ]` with one benchmark per seed (fixed `theta`, fresh `delta` and
`e`); the binomial Monte Carlo standard error at the nominal level is `sqrt(level (1 - level) / N)`.

- Domain: distinct integer seeds, `0 < level < 1`.
- Meaning: with a wide prior the full model is calibrated up to prior shrinkage; models that drop the
  discrepancy use a covariance that is too small and give overconfident intervals. Values are measured and
  reported, not assumed.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_catalog.discrepancy_coverage_study`, `inverse.benchmark_catalog.CoverageStudy`.
- Source: [REF-BRYNJARSDOTTIR-OHAGAN-2014], [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001].
  - [REF-BRYNJARSDOTTIR-OHAGAN-2014](../references.md#ref-brynjarsdottir-ohagan-2014): Jenný Brynjarsdóttir and Anthony O'Hagan. "Learning about Physical Parameters: The Importance of Model Discrepancy." *Inverse Problems* 30(11), 114007, 2014. DOI: [10.1088/0266-5611/30/11/114007](https://doi.org/10.1088/0266-5611/30/11/114007).
  - [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001](../references.md#ref-kennedy-ohagan-2001): Marc C. Kennedy and Anthony O'Hagan. "Bayesian Calibration of Computer Models." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B* 63(3), 425-464, 2001. DOI: [10.1111/1467-9868.00294](https://doi.org/10.1111/1467-9868.00294).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH3-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench3-007--measured-interval-coverage-over-predeclared-seeds)

### EQ-INV-BENCH3-008 — F01 Fredholm first-kind problem with independent fine data

`g(s_i) = int_0^1 k(s_i, t) f(t) dt`; solver operator `A_ij = int_{cell j} k(s_i, t) dt` on `n` piecewise-constant
cells (panels contain the cell edges and `s_i`); data from composite Gauss quadrature of the analytic `f` on
many panels. Brownian kernel `k = min(s, t)` has the exact continuum singular values
`sigma_k = 1 / ((k - 1/2)^2 pi^2)` with eigenfunctions `sqrt(2) sin((k - 1/2) pi s)`; Gaussian kernel
`k = exp(-(s - t)^2 / (2 w^2))` is severely ill-posed.

- Domain: `n >= 8`, `m >= 2`, `w > 0`.
- Meaning: the quadrature is independent of the solver's basis, so `same_discretization = False` (genuine
  discretization error); `fine_data = False` gives the inverse-crime variant `b = A x_true`.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_catalog.functional_benchmark`, `inverse.benchmark_catalog.fredholm_kernel`,
  `inverse.benchmark_catalog.functional_truth_profile`, `inverse.benchmark_catalog.FunctionalBenchmark`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008], [REF-GOLUB-WELSCH-1969].
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
  - [REF-GOLUB-WELSCH-1969](../references.md#ref-golub-welsch-1969): Gene H. Golub and John H. Welsch. "Calculation of Gauss Quadrature Rules." *Mathematics of Computation* 23(106), 221-230, 1969.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH3-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench3-008--f01-fredholm-first-kind-problem-with-independent-fine-data)

### EQ-INV-BENCH3-009 — Functional estimate, data-informed modes and averaging kernel

`Q(x) = q' x` (window average, `q_j = |cell_j and W| / |W|`). With `A = U S V'`: TSVD estimate
`Q_k = sum_{i<=k} (q'v_i / sigma_i) u_i' y = a' y`, standard error `s ||a||`, noise-free value `kappa' x`
with the averaging kernel `kappa = V_k V_k' q`, resolved fraction `||kappa||^2 / ||q||^2`, bias
`kappa' x - q' x`. Data-informed modes: leading `i` with `sigma_i / s >= threshold`.

- Domain: `1 <= k <= rank(A)`, iid noise of standard deviation `s`.
- Meaning: the functional is learnable from the data exactly to the extent that `q` lies in the span of the
  retained right singular vectors; the trade-off table shows resolution against noise amplification. The
  truth enters only through the optional benchmark-only bias.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_catalog.functional_analysis`, `inverse.benchmark_catalog.FunctionalAnalysis`,
  `inverse.benchmark_catalog.FunctionalTradeoff`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008], [REF-BACKUS-GILBERT-1968], [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013].
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
  - [REF-BACKUS-GILBERT-1968](../references.md#ref-backus-gilbert-1968): G. Backus and F. Gilbert. "The Resolving Power of Gross Earth Data." *Geophysical Journal of the Royal Astronomical Society* 16(2), 169-205, 1968. Cited for averaging kernels and the resolution versus noise trade-off of linear functionals of an inverse problem. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1111/j.1365-246X.1968.tb00216.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1365-246X.1968.tb00216.x).
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH3-009](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench3-009--functional-estimate-data-informed-modes-and-averaging-kernel)

### EQ-INV-BENCH3-010 — Benchmark contract of the S, M and F cases

Truth and noise come from distinct child streams of one `RandomStream`; `same_discretization` is `True`
exactly when the data were generated by the discrete model the solver inverts; withheld fields (truth,
noise-free data, discrepancy and noise draws, functional truth) are never inputs of a reference solution
unless a function documents it.

- Domain: seeds as in `probstat.foundations.random_streams.resolve_stream`.
- Meaning: mirrors `InverseBenchmark` and `NonlinearBenchmark` for Poisson counts, discrepancy and functional
  problems; the realized noise norm is reported, never rescaled.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_catalog.CATALOG_BENCHMARK_CASES`,
  `inverse.benchmark_catalog.PoissonBenchmark`, `inverse.benchmark_catalog.DiscrepancyBenchmark`,
  `inverse.benchmark_catalog.FunctionalBenchmark`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007], [REF-GAZZOLA-HANSEN-NAGY-2019].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
  - [REF-GAZZOLA-HANSEN-NAGY-2019](../references.md#ref-gazzola-hansen-nagy-2019): Silvia Gazzola, Per Christian Hansen, and James G. Nagy. "IR Tools: A MATLAB Package of Iterative Regularization Methods and Large-Scale Test Problems." *Numerical Algorithms* 81(3), 773-811, 2019. Cited for matrix-free operators, iterative regularization by early stopping, hybrid projection methods and their test problems. No code was copied. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/s11075-018-0570-7](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11075-018-0570-7).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH3-010](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench3-010--benchmark-contract-of-the-s-m-and-f-cases)

## `probstat.inverse.benchmark_cosmology`

### EQ-INV-COSMO-001 — Flat FLRW expansion rate with dark energy

`E^2(z) = Om (1+z)^3 + (1-Om) X(z)`, `X(z) = exp(3 int_0^z (1 + w(t)) / (1 + t) dt)`, `H(z) = H0 E(z)`.

- Domain: spatially flat, pressureless matter plus one dark-energy fluid, `0 < Om < 1`, `z >= 0`; radiation neglected (low-redshift regime only).
- Meaning: background expansion of a synthetic test universe; no CMB or high-redshift physics, no statement about the real universe.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.ExpansionHistoryModel`.
- Source: [REF-HOGG-1999], [REF-CHEVALLIER-POLARSKI-2001], [REF-LINDER-2003].
  - [REF-HOGG-1999](../references.md#ref-hogg-1999): David W. Hogg. "Distance Measures in Cosmology." arXiv:astro-ph/9905116, 1999. Cited for the comoving and luminosity distances, the distance modulus and the Hubble distance `c / H0` of a flat Friedmann-Lemaitre-Robertson-Walker model. Metadata checked against DataCite (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.48550/arXiv.astro-ph/9905116](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.astro-ph/9905116).
  - [REF-CHEVALLIER-POLARSKI-2001](../references.md#ref-chevallier-polarski-2001): Michel Chevallier and David Polarski. "Accelerating Universes with Scaling Dark Matter." *International Journal of Modern Physics D* 10(2), 213-223, 2001. Cited, with [REF-LINDER-2003], for the dark-energy equation of state `w(z) = w0 + wa z / (1 + z)` used as a synthetic truth. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1142/S0218271801000822](https://doi.org/10.1142/S0218271801000822).
  - [REF-LINDER-2003](../references.md#ref-linder-2003): Eric V. Linder. "Exploring the Expansion History of the Universe." *Physical Review Letters* 90(9), 091301, 2003. Cited for the `w0`-`wa` parameterization of the dark-energy equation of state. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1103/PhysRevLett.90.091301](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.90.091301).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-COSMO-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-cosmo-001--flat-flrw-expansion-rate-with-dark-energy)

### EQ-INV-COSMO-002 — Piecewise-constant equation of state, exact exponent integral

`w(z) = -1 + sum_j x_j phi_j(z)`, `phi_j = 1[e_j <= z < e_{j+1}]`; `int_0^z (1+w)/(1+t) dt = sum_j x_j ln((1 + min(z, e_{j+1})) / (1 + e_j))` over bins with `e_j < z`.

- Domain: `0 = e_0 < e_1 < ... < e_K`; `w = -1` beyond `e_K`.
- Meaning: the integral is exact per bin, so `X(z)` has no quadrature error; `x = 0` is LCDM, `x_j = 1 + w_j`.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.ExpansionHistoryModel.dark_energy_factor`.
- Source: [REF-CHEVALLIER-POLARSKI-2001], [REF-LINDER-2003].
  - [REF-CHEVALLIER-POLARSKI-2001](../references.md#ref-chevallier-polarski-2001): Michel Chevallier and David Polarski. "Accelerating Universes with Scaling Dark Matter." *International Journal of Modern Physics D* 10(2), 213-223, 2001. Cited, with [REF-LINDER-2003], for the dark-energy equation of state `w(z) = w0 + wa z / (1 + z)` used as a synthetic truth. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1142/S0218271801000822](https://doi.org/10.1142/S0218271801000822).
  - [REF-LINDER-2003](../references.md#ref-linder-2003): Eric V. Linder. "Exploring the Expansion History of the Universe." *Physical Review Letters* 90(9), 091301, 2003. Cited for the `w0`-`wa` parameterization of the dark-energy equation of state. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1103/PhysRevLett.90.091301](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.90.091301).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-COSMO-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-cosmo-002--piecewise-constant-equation-of-state-exact-exponent-integral)

### EQ-INV-COSMO-003 — Distances, distance modulus and the discrete quadrature

`D_C = (c/H0) int_0^z dt / E(t)`, `D_L = (1+z) D_C`, `mu = 5 log10(D_L / Mpc) + 25`, `c = 299792.458 km/s`; discrete model `D_C ~ (c/H0) sum_q w_q / E(t_q)` with 5-point Gauss-Legendre panels cut at the bin edges.

- Domain: `z > 0`, `H0` in km/s/Mpc, flat geometry.
- Meaning: the quadrature is the model that `jvp`/`vjp` differentiate exactly; its error against an independent Simpson oracle is at roundoff level for smooth segments.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.ExpansionHistoryModel.value`.
- Source: [REF-HOGG-1999].
  - [REF-HOGG-1999](../references.md#ref-hogg-1999): David W. Hogg. "Distance Measures in Cosmology." arXiv:astro-ph/9905116, 1999. Cited for the comoving and luminosity distances, the distance modulus and the Hubble distance `c / H0` of a flat Friedmann-Lemaitre-Robertson-Walker model. Metadata checked against DataCite (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.48550/arXiv.astro-ph/9905116](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.astro-ph/9905116).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-COSMO-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-cosmo-003--distances-distance-modulus-and-the-discrete-quadrature)

### EQ-INV-COSMO-004 — Sensitivity of the expansion rate

`dE^2/dx_j = 3 (1-Om) X(z) int_0^z phi_j(t)/(1+t) dt`, `d(1/E)/dx_j = -(dE^2/dx_j) / (2 E^3)`, `dE^2/dOm = (1+z)^3 - X(z)`.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-COSMO-001.
- Meaning: a change of `w` in one bin moves `E` through `X` only after that bin; high-redshift bins act only through integrals, hence weak identifiability.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.ExpansionHistoryModel.jvp`.
- Source: [REF-CHEVALLIER-POLARSKI-2001], [REF-STUART-2010].
  - [REF-CHEVALLIER-POLARSKI-2001](../references.md#ref-chevallier-polarski-2001): Michel Chevallier and David Polarski. "Accelerating Universes with Scaling Dark Matter." *International Journal of Modern Physics D* 10(2), 213-223, 2001. Cited, with [REF-LINDER-2003], for the dark-energy equation of state `w(z) = w0 + wa z / (1 + z)` used as a synthetic truth. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1142/S0218271801000822](https://doi.org/10.1142/S0218271801000822).
  - [REF-STUART-2010](../references.md#ref-stuart-2010): Andrew M. Stuart. "Inverse Problems: A Bayesian Perspective." *Acta Numerica* 19, 451-559, 2010. Cited for the Gaussian linear inverse problem, its posterior mean and covariance, function-space priors, and weighted inner products. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/S0962492910000061](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0962492910000061).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-COSMO-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-cosmo-004--sensitivity-of-the-expansion-rate)

### EQ-INV-COSMO-005 — Exact derivative of the discrete distance modulus

`dmu = (5 / ln 10) dD_L / D_L` with `dD_L / D_L = sum_q w_q d(1/E)(t_q) / sum_q w_q / E(t_q)` taken at the same nodes; `dmu/d log10 H0 = -5`, `dmu/dM = 1`.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-COSMO-003.
- Meaning: derivatives are exact for the discrete model (verified by Taylor sweep, central differences and the dot test), not an approximation of the continuous derivative.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.ExpansionHistoryModel.vjp`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006], [REF-STUART-2010].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
  - [REF-STUART-2010](../references.md#ref-stuart-2010): Andrew M. Stuart. "Inverse Problems: A Bayesian Perspective." *Acta Numerica* 19, 451-559, 2010. Cited for the Gaussian linear inverse problem, its posterior mean and covariance, function-space priors, and weighted inner products. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/S0962492910000061](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0962492910000061).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-COSMO-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-cosmo-005--exact-derivative-of-the-discrete-distance-modulus)

### EQ-INV-COSMO-006 — Constant-w factor and low-redshift limit

`w = const => X(z) = (1+z)^(3 (1+w))`; `D_L = (c z / H0) (1 + (1 - q0) z / 2 + O(z^2))`, `q0 = Om/2 - (1 - Om)` for `w = -1`.

- Domain: flat model, `z -> 0`.
- Meaning: exact reference identities used as closed-form tests of the implementation.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.ExpansionHistoryModel.dark_energy_factor`.
- Source: [REF-HOGG-1999].
  - [REF-HOGG-1999](../references.md#ref-hogg-1999): David W. Hogg. "Distance Measures in Cosmology." arXiv:astro-ph/9905116, 1999. Cited for the comoving and luminosity distances, the distance modulus and the Hubble distance `c / H0` of a flat Friedmann-Lemaitre-Robertson-Walker model. Metadata checked against DataCite (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.48550/arXiv.astro-ph/9905116](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.astro-ph/9905116).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-COSMO-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-cosmo-006--constant-w-factor-and-low-redshift-limit)

### EQ-INV-COSMO-007 — Offset and H0 degeneracy

`mu(z; H0, M) = mu(z; H0_ref, 0) - 5 log10(H0 / H0_ref) + M`; hence `dmu/d log10 H0 = -5 (dmu/dM)` for every `z`.

- Domain: uncalibrated distance moduli with a free additive offset `M`.
- Meaning: the Jacobian columns of `log10 H0` and `M` are exactly collinear; only the combination `M - 5 log10 H0` is identifiable.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.ExpansionHistoryModel`.
- Source: [REF-HOGG-1999], [REF-STUART-2010].
  - [REF-HOGG-1999](../references.md#ref-hogg-1999): David W. Hogg. "Distance Measures in Cosmology." arXiv:astro-ph/9905116, 1999. Cited for the comoving and luminosity distances, the distance modulus and the Hubble distance `c / H0` of a flat Friedmann-Lemaitre-Robertson-Walker model. Cited for the Gaussian linear inverse problem, its posterior mean and covariance, function-space priors, and weighted inner products. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). - Registry: [EQ-INV-COSMO-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-cosmo-007--offset-and-h0-degeneracy)

### EQ-INV-COSMO-008 — Synthetic A02 benchmark with finer-representation truth

`b_i = mu_fine(z_i; w_true) + M + e_i`, `e_i ~ N(0, sigma_mu^2)`; coarse truth `x_j = int_{bin j} (1+w)/(1+t) dt / ln((1+e_{j+1})/(1+e_j))`.

- Domain: truths LCDM, CPL (`w = w0 + wa z/(1+z)`) and an oscillation; data from a 300-panel Gauss-Legendre grid built from `w` itself, inversion on coarse bins.
- Meaning: the projection preserves each bin's exponent integral; the mismatch makes `same_discretization=False` except for LCDM. Synthetic only; the Gaussian prior is a declared assumption.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.synthetic_expansion_history_benchmark`.
- Source: [REF-STUART-2010], [REF-HANSEN-2008], [REF-CHEVALLIER-POLARSKI-2001], [REF-LINDER-2003].

### EQ-INV-COSMO-009 — Hubble-rate observable

`G_i(x) = H0 E(z_i)`, `dG/dx_j = H0 (3 (1-Om) X / (2E)) L_j(z_i)`, `dG/dOm = H0 ((1+z)^3 - X) / (2E)`, `dG/d log10 H0 = ln(10) G`.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-COSMO-001; no additive offset.
- Meaning: direct measurement of the expansion rate without a distance integral.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.ExpansionHistoryModel`.

### EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-001 — Correlated calibration noise covariance

`Gamma = s_w^2 I + s_o^2 1 1' + s_s^2 K`, `K_ij = exp(-(z_i - z_j)^2 / (2 l^2))`.

- Domain: `s_w > 0`, `s_o, s_s >= 0`, `l > 0`; positive definite; synthetic, not a survey covariance.
- Meaning: white noise plus a common magnitude zero-point error plus a smooth drift across redshift, handled through the covariance factor. A common offset is exactly confounded with a free `"offset"` nuisance.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.CalibrationNoise`.
- Source: [REF-HOGG-1999].
  - [REF-HOGG-1999](../references.md#ref-hogg-1999): David W. Hogg. "Distance Measures in Cosmology." arXiv:astro-ph/9905116, 1999. Cited for the comoving and luminosity distances, the distance modulus and the Hubble distance `c / H0` of a flat Friedmann-Lemaitre-Robertson-Walker model. Metadata checked against DataCite (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.48550/arXiv.astro-ph/9905116](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.astro-ph/9905116).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-cosmo-001--correlated-calibration-noise-covariance)

### EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-002 — Benchmark A02c

`y = b + F z`, `F F' = Gamma` of EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-001, `z ~ N(0, I)`, with `b` the finer-representation noise-free data of A02 (EQ-INV-COSMO-008).

- Domain: as A02; `same_discretization` follows A02; the diagonal surrogate `diag(Gamma)` is provided as `naive_noise`.
- Meaning: lets a solver be run with the true correlated factor or with the diagonal one that ignores the correlation.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.correlated_calibration_benchmark`.
- Source: [REF-HOGG-1999], [REF-STUART-2010].
  - [REF-HOGG-1999](../references.md#ref-hogg-1999): David W. Hogg. "Distance Measures in Cosmology." arXiv:astro-ph/9905116, 1999. Cited for the comoving and luminosity distances, the distance modulus and the Hubble distance `c / H0` of a flat Friedmann-Lemaitre-Robertson-Walker model. Cited for the Gaussian linear inverse problem, its posterior mean and covariance, function-space priors, and weighted inner products. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/S0962492910000061](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0962492910000061).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-cosmo-002--benchmark-a02c)

### EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-003 — Simplified BAO observable

`D_V(z) = [z D_M(z)^2 c / H(z)]^(1/3)`, `y = D_V / r_s`, `D_M = D_C` (flat), `r_s = 10^(log10_rs)` a free nuisance (nominal 147 Mpc).

- Domain: flat model of EQ-INV-COSMO-001; `r_s` is NOT computed from physics (no baryon density, radiation or drag epoch); no reconstruction, no anisotropic split, no survey covariance; synthetic.
- Meaning: the angle-averaged BAO distance relative to a standard ruler of unknown length; implemented by composing the distance-modulus and Hubble-rate models, so derivatives are exact for the same discrete quadrature.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.BaoDistanceModel`.
- Source: [REF-EISENSTEIN-2005], [REF-HOGG-1999].
  - [REF-EISENSTEIN-2005](../references.md#ref-eisenstein-2005): Daniel J. Eisenstein et al. "Detection of the Baryon Acoustic Peak in the Large-Scale Correlation Function of SDSS Luminous Red Galaxies." *The Astrophysical Journal* 633(2), 560-574, 2005. Cited for the baryon acoustic oscillation feature as a standard ruler and for the volume-averaged distance `D_V(z) = [D_M^2 c z / H]^(1/3)`. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1086/466512](https://doi.org/10.1086/466512).
  - [REF-HOGG-1999](../references.md#ref-hogg-1999): David W. Hogg. "Distance Measures in Cosmology." arXiv:astro-ph/9905116, 1999. Cited for the comoving and luminosity distances, the distance modulus and the Hubble distance `c / H0` of a flat Friedmann-Lemaitre-Robertson-Walker model. Metadata checked against DataCite (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.48550/arXiv.astro-ph/9905116](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.astro-ph/9905116).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-cosmo-003--simplified-bao-observable)

### EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-004 — BAO derivatives and the H0-r_s degeneracy

`d y = y [ (2 ln10 / 15) d mu - (1/3) dH / H - ln10 d log10_rs ]`; `y` depends on `H0` and `r_s` only through `H0 r_s`, so the `log10_h0` and `log10_rs` Jacobian columns coincide.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-003; derivatives of `mu` and `H` from EQ-INV-COSMO-005.
- Meaning: BAO alone cannot separate `H0` from `r_s`; supernovae alone cannot separate `H0` from the magnitude offset (EQ-INV-COSMO-007); both together still leave one combination (`d log10 H0 : d offset : d log10 r_s = 1 : 5 : -1`); only a datum that fixes `H0` (the Hubble rate) breaks it, as measured by `bao_degeneracy_report`.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.BaoDistanceModel`, `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.bao_degeneracy_report`.
- Source: [REF-EISENSTEIN-2005].
  - [REF-EISENSTEIN-2005](../references.md#ref-eisenstein-2005): Daniel J. Eisenstein et al. "Detection of the Baryon Acoustic Peak in the Large-Scale Correlation Function of SDSS Luminous Red Galaxies." *The Astrophysical Journal* 633(2), 560-574, 2005. Cited for the baryon acoustic oscillation feature as a standard ruler and for the volume-averaged distance `D_V(z) = [D_M^2 c z / H]^(1/3)`. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1086/466512](https://doi.org/10.1086/466512).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-cosmo-004--bao-derivatives-and-the-h0-r_s-degeneracy)

### EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-005 — Joint model with shared parameters

`G(x) = (G_1(P_1 x), ..., G_K(P_K x))`, `J = [J_1 P_1; ...; J_K P_K]`, `P_k` the coordinate selection of the member's parameters (union of the nuisance names in the order `omega_m, log10_h0, offset, log10_rs`).

- Domain: members share the bin parameters; a member lacking a nuisance uses its own nominal value and has a zero column there.
- Meaning: stacks supernova, BAO and Hubble-rate data to show which degeneracy another observable breaks.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.JointExpansionModel`.
- Source: [REF-HOGG-1999].
  - [REF-HOGG-1999](../references.md#ref-hogg-1999): David W. Hogg. "Distance Measures in Cosmology." arXiv:astro-ph/9905116, 1999. Cited for the comoving and luminosity distances, the distance modulus and the Hubble distance `c / H0` of a flat Friedmann-Lemaitre-Robertson-Walker model. Metadata checked against DataCite (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.48550/arXiv.astro-ph/9905116](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.astro-ph/9905116).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-cosmo-005--joint-model-with-shared-parameters)

### EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-006 — Whitened singular spectrum of a Jacobian block

`W J_S = U S V'`, `W' W = Gamma^-1`, `S` a subset of the parameters (others fixed at the base point), numerical rank `#{s_i > tol s_1}`, weakest direction the last column of `V`.

- Domain: local (linearized) at the base point; `Gamma` any covariance factor, correlated or not; zero rows appended when `m < n`.
- Meaning: the data-resolved directions of a parameter block in noise units. For a common-offset noise the whitened norm of the offset column is `sqrt(m / (s_w^2 + m s_o^2))`, an exact check.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.whitened_spectrum`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008], [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003].
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-cosmo-006--whitened-singular-spectrum-of-a-jacobian-block)

### EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-007 — Linear-Gaussian expected information gain

`EIG = 1/2 log det(I + P^(1/2) J' Gamma^-1 J P^(1/2)) = 1/2 [log det(P^-1 + J' Gamma^-1 J) - log det P^-1]`.

- Domain: linear-Gaussian model (or the local linearization of a nonlinear one), `P` diagonal prior covariance, `Gamma` any SPD covariance factor.
- Meaning: the expected Kullback-Leibler divergence from prior to posterior in nats; a local value for nonlinear models, not a survey forecast.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.linear_gaussian_eig`.
- Source: [REF-CHALONER-VERDINELLI-1995].
  - [REF-CHALONER-VERDINELLI-1995](../references.md#ref-chaloner-verdinelli-1995): Kathryn Chaloner and Isabella Verdinelli. "Bayesian Experimental Design: A Review." *Statistical Science* 10(3), 273-304, 1995.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-cosmo-007--linear-gaussian-expected-information-gain)

### EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-008 — Greedy redshift design

`z_(k+1) = argmax_c EIG(base + {z_1..z_k, c})`; for independent noise the rank-one recursion `Delta = 1/2 log(1 + j' S j / s^2)`, `S <- S - S j j' S / (s^2 + j' S j)` gives the same total.

- Domain: candidate redshifts `> 0`; covariance of each trial design from the calibration model at exactly those redshifts; the rank-one check applies to independent noise only.
- Meaning: the dense EIG is the telescoping sum of the marginal gains (verified to roundoff); for independent noise the EIG is monotone submodular, so greedy attains at least `1 - 1/e` of the optimum.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.greedy_redshift_design`.
- Source: [REF-CHALONER-VERDINELLI-1995].
  - [REF-CHALONER-VERDINELLI-1995](../references.md#ref-chaloner-verdinelli-1995): Kathryn Chaloner and Isabella Verdinelli. "Bayesian Experimental Design: A Review." *Statistical Science* 10(3), 273-304, 1995.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-cosmo-008--greedy-redshift-design)

### EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-009 — Nominal versus actual posterior spread

`K = (P^-1 + J' G_a^-1 J)^-1 J' G_a^-1`, `MSE = (I - K J) P (I - K J)' + K G_t K'`, ratio `sqrt(diag MSE) / sqrt(diag (P^-1 + J' G_a^-1 J)^-1)`.

- Domain: linearized MAP with the assumed noise `G_a` while the data errors have covariance `G_t`; truth drawn from the prior.
- Meaning: equals one when `G_a = G_t`; above one means the nominal intervals are too narrow. Local and synthetic.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_cosmology.interval_calibration`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-009](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-cosmo-009--nominal-versus-actual-posterior-spread)

## `probstat.inverse.benchmark_extended`

### EQ-INV-BENCH2-001 — Separable 2D Gaussian blur and its exact transpose

`A = B_r (x) B_c`, `(B x)_i = sum_d k_d x_(i+d)` with zero values outside the image,
`k_d ∝ exp(-(d h)^2 / (2 w^2))`, `sum_d k_d = 1`; `A' = B_r' (x) B_c'` with the
reversed kernels. Images are row-major (`pixel (i, j) = i cols + j`) on the unit
square (`h_r = 1/rows`, `h_c = 1/cols`).

- Domain: `rows, cols >= 2`, `w > 0`; the zero boundary darkens pixels within the
  kernel half-width of an edge, interior pixels of a constant image are unchanged.
- Meaning: matrix-free L07a forward model. With `refinement r > 1` the data are the
  block average of the same blur on an `r`-times finer grid applied to the analytic
  phantom, and the solver's truth is the block average of the fine phantom, so the
  data carry a genuine discretization error; `refinement = 1` is an inverse crime.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_extended.GaussianBlur2DOperator`,
  `inverse.benchmark_extended.image_deblur_benchmark`.
- Source: [REF-GAZZOLA-HANSEN-NAGY-2019], [REF-HANSEN-2008].
  - [REF-GAZZOLA-HANSEN-NAGY-2019](../references.md#ref-gazzola-hansen-nagy-2019): Silvia Gazzola, Per Christian Hansen, and James G. Nagy. "IR Tools: A MATLAB Package of Iterative Regularization Methods and Large-Scale Test Problems." *Numerical Algorithms* 81(3), 773-811, 2019. Cited for matrix-free operators, iterative regularization by early stopping, hybrid projection methods and their test problems. No code was copied. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/s11075-018-0570-7](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11075-018-0570-7).
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH2-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench2-001--separable-2d-gaussian-blur-and-its-exact-transpose)

### EQ-INV-BENCH2-002 — Exact ray-pixel intersection lengths and the matched transpose

`A_(k, p) = |ray_k ∩ pixel_p|`, `(A x)_k = sum_p A_(k,p) x_p`, `(A' y)_p = sum_k A_(k,p) y_k`.
The ray `c + t (-sin θ, cos θ) + s (cos θ, sin θ)` is cut at its entry and exit of the
square `[0, n]^2` and at every grid line `x = j`, `y = j`; consecutive sorted
parameters bound segments in one pixel and their difference is the intersection
length (unit direction).

- Domain: `n >= 2`; a ray lying exactly on a grid line is assigned wholly to the
  pixel row or column of larger index (clamped at the grid edge); a ray missing the
  square is an empty row.
- Meaning: the line integrals of a piecewise-constant pixel image are exact, and the
  scatter-add transpose is the exact adjoint. This is a small discretized prototype,
  not a CT-grade projector (no detector model, no fan or cone geometry, no
  attenuation physics). Parallel-beam offsets span the projected width
  `W = n (|cos θ| + |sin θ|)` at `((k + 1/2)/R - 1/2) W`.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_extended.ray_pixel_intersections`,
  `inverse.benchmark_extended.parallel_beam_rays`,
  `inverse.benchmark_extended.RayTransformOperator`,
  `inverse.benchmark_extended.masked_tomography_benchmark`.
- Source: [REF-GAZZOLA-HANSEN-NAGY-2019], [REF-HANSEN-2008].
  - [REF-GAZZOLA-HANSEN-NAGY-2019](../references.md#ref-gazzola-hansen-nagy-2019): Silvia Gazzola, Per Christian Hansen, and James G. Nagy. "IR Tools: A MATLAB Package of Iterative Regularization Methods and Large-Scale Test Problems." *Numerical Algorithms* 81(3), 773-811, 2019. Cited for matrix-free operators, iterative regularization by early stopping, hybrid projection methods and their test problems. No code was copied. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/s11075-018-0570-7](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11075-018-0570-7).
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH2-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench2-002--exact-ray-pixel-intersection-lengths-and-the-matched-transpose)

### EQ-INV-BENCH2-003 — Analytic line integrals of the disc phantom

`∫ f ds = sum_j v_j 2 sqrt(R_j^2 - q_j^2)` for `q_j < R_j`, where `q_j` is the distance
of disc centre `j` from the line, `R_j` its radius and `v_j` its additive value
(all discs lie inside the square).

- Domain: discs contained in the grid; the solver's truth is the pixel average of the
  disc phantom (`4 x 4` sub-samples per pixel).
- Meaning: with `analytic=True` the data are independent of the pixel grid
  (`same_discretization=False`); the gap to `A x_true` measures the discretization
  error of the piecewise-constant pixel model, not noise.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_extended.masked_tomography_benchmark`.
- Source: [REF-GAZZOLA-HANSEN-NAGY-2019].
  - [REF-GAZZOLA-HANSEN-NAGY-2019](../references.md#ref-gazzola-hansen-nagy-2019): Silvia Gazzola, Per Christian Hansen, and James G. Nagy. "IR Tools: A MATLAB Package of Iterative Regularization Methods and Large-Scale Test Problems." *Numerical Algorithms* 81(3), 773-811, 2019. Cited for matrix-free operators, iterative regularization by early stopping, hybrid projection methods and their test problems. No code was copied. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/s11075-018-0570-7](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11075-018-0570-7).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH2-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench2-003--analytic-line-integrals-of-the-disc-phantom)

### EQ-INV-BENCH2-004 — Exponential decay model, Jacobian and designs

`G_i(a, r) = a exp(-r t_i)`, `J_i = (exp(-r t_i), -a t_i exp(-r t_i))`,
`J v = (e_i v_a - a t_i e_i v_r)_i`, `J' w = (sum_i w_i e_i, -a sum_i w_i t_i e_i)`,
`e_i = exp(-r t_i)`.

- Domain: `a != 0`, `r > 0`, `t_i >= 0` with at least two distinct times.
- Meaning: the identifiable design spans `t in [0, 3/r]`; the weak design uses only
  `t in [5/r, 6/r]`, where the two Jacobian columns are nearly parallel and
  amplitude and rate trade off (large Jacobian condition number, measured in the tests).
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_extended.exponential_decay_benchmark`.
- Source: [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003], [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH2-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench2-004--exponential-decay-model-jacobian-and-designs)

### EQ-INV-BENCH2-005 — Squared and product maps with structural ambiguity

`G(x) = x o x` (elementwise) or `G_j(x) = x_(2j) x_(2j+1)`; `J v = 2 x o v`,
`J' w = 2 x o w` for the square, `(J v)_j = x_(2j+1) v_(2j) + x_(2j) v_(2j+1)`.
`G(x) = G(-x)`; the square is invariant under every sign pattern, the product under
`(a, b) -> (s a, b / s)`.

- Domain: `n >= 1` (even `>= 2` for the product); truth entries `±(0.5 + U)`.
- Meaning: the noise-free problem has `2^n` exact optima (square) or a continuum of
  them (product); the Jacobian vanishes at the origin. Solvers started near it show
  the multimodal pathology; no uniqueness is claimed.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_extended.squared_map_benchmark`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH2-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench2-005--squared-and-product-maps-with-structural-ambiguity)

### EQ-INV-BENCH2-006 — Elliptic log-conductivity benchmark with fine-mesh data

`-(k u')' = 1 + s` on `(0, 1)`, `x = log k` per cell, truth `x(s) = 0.5 sin(2πs) + 0.3 s`;
data `b = G_fine(x_fine)` on an `r n`-cell mesh with the same sensor positions,
solver truth `x_coarse = P_coarse x_fine` (cell average); prior
`x ~ N(0, C)`, `C_ij = exp(-|s_i - s_j| / 0.3)`.

- Domain: `n >= 4`, `r >= 1`, sensors in `(0, 1)`.
- Meaning: `r = 1` is an inverse crime; `r > 1` leaves the discretization error of
  the coarse finite-volume model in the data. The zero-mean prior is a smoothness
  prior, not centred on the truth.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_extended.elliptic_benchmark`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007], [REF-STUART-2010].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
  - [REF-STUART-2010](../references.md#ref-stuart-2010): Andrew M. Stuart. "Inverse Problems: A Bayesian Perspective." *Acta Numerica* 19, 451-559, 2010. Cited for the Gaussian linear inverse problem, its posterior mean and covariance, function-space priors, and weighted inner products. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/S0962492910000061](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0962492910000061).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH2-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench2-006--elliptic-log-conductivity-benchmark-with-fine-mesh-data)

### EQ-INV-BENCH2-007 — Heat initial-condition benchmark with refined time stepping

`b = A_(q N) x`, `A_N = H S^N` the final-time discrete heat map; the solver inverts
`A_N`. The singular values of `A_N` are the closed forms
`sigma_k = |g(mu_k T / N)|^N`, `mu_k = (4 κ / h^2) sin^2(k π h / (2 L))`.

- Domain: `n >= 4`, `T > 0`, `N >= 1`, `q >= 1`, same spatial grid and scheme for
  both operators.
- Meaning: `q = 1` is an inverse crime; `q > 1` puts the time-discretization error
  of the coarse model into the data. The spectrum decays like `exp(-k^2 π^2 T)`
  (severely ill-posed).
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_extended.heat_initial_condition_benchmark`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008], [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH2-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench2-007--heat-initial-condition-benchmark-with-refined-time-stepping)

### EQ-INV-BENCH2-008 — Nonlinear benchmark contract and noise provenance

`y = b + e`, `e_i ~ N(0, s^2)` iid, `s = ρ ||b|| / sqrt(m)`, `b = G(x_true)` for the
solver's model when `same_discretization`, otherwise from an independent finer model;
truth and noise come from distinct children of one random stream.

- Domain: `ρ > 0`, `b != 0`.
- Meaning: `NonlinearBenchmark` mirrors `InverseBenchmark` with a differentiable
  forward model in place of the operator; the realized `||e||` is reported, never
  rescaled to a fixed norm. Same-discretization results verify algebra only.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_extended.NonlinearBenchmark`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007], [REF-GAZZOLA-HANSEN-NAGY-2019].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
  - [REF-GAZZOLA-HANSEN-NAGY-2019](../references.md#ref-gazzola-hansen-nagy-2019): Silvia Gazzola, Per Christian Hansen, and James G. Nagy. "IR Tools: A MATLAB Package of Iterative Regularization Methods and Large-Scale Test Problems." *Numerical Algorithms* 81(3), 773-811, 2019. Cited for matrix-free operators, iterative regularization by early stopping, hybrid projection methods and their test problems. No code was copied. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/s11075-018-0570-7](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11075-018-0570-7).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH2-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench2-008--nonlinear-benchmark-contract-and-noise-provenance)

## `probstat.inverse.benchmark_gravity`

### EQ-INV-APP-GRAV-001 — Exact 2D prism attraction

`g_z = 2 G rho [F(a_2, b_2) - F(a_1, b_2) - F(a_2, b_1) + F(a_1, b_1)]`, `F(a, b) = b atan(a / b) + (a / 2) ln(a^2 + b^2)`, `a_k = x_k - x_o`, `b_k = z_k + h`.

- Domain: infinite horizontal prism `[x_1, x_2] x [z_1, z_2]`, depth `z >= 0` downward, observation at height `h >= 0` above the surface, `b -> 0` limit `a ln|a|`; units with `G = 1` canonical.
- Meaning: `F` is a primitive of the line-mass kernel `b / (a^2 + b^2)` (`d^2 F / da db`), so the cell integral is exact; an infinitely wide slab gives `2 pi G rho t`.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_gravity.prism_attraction`, `inverse.benchmark_gravity.gravity_kernel_matrix`.
- Source: [REF-BLAKELY-1995].
  - [REF-BLAKELY-1995](../references.md#ref-blakely-1995): Richard J. Blakely. *Potential Theory in Gravity and Magnetic Applications*. Cambridge University Press, Cambridge, 1995. Cited for the vertical attraction of buried sources, the line-mass kernel and the non-uniqueness of potential fields (equivalent sources at different depths). Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/CBO9780511549816](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511549816).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-GRAV-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-grav-001--exact-2d-prism-attraction)

### EQ-INV-APP-GRAV-002 — Benchmark G01 with finer-cell truth

`y = A_fine rho_fine + e`, `e ~ N(0, s^2 I)`, `s = level ||b|| / sqrt(m)`, truth on the inversion mesh = block means of `rho_fine`, `A` the kernel matrix of the inversion mesh.

- Domain: `refinement >= 1` (1 is the inverse crime, algebraic verification only); named analytic densities that do not align with the coarse cells; noise not rescaled; truth withheld from the solver inputs.
- Meaning: synthetic gravity-anomaly laboratory; no real field, body or interior is represented.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_gravity.gravity_inversion_benchmark`.
- Source: [REF-BLAKELY-1995], [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-BLAKELY-1995](../references.md#ref-blakely-1995): Richard J. Blakely. *Potential Theory in Gravity and Magnetic Applications*. Cambridge University Press, Cambridge, 1995. Cited for the vertical attraction of buried sources, the line-mass kernel and the non-uniqueness of potential fields (equivalent sources at different depths). Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/CBO9780511549816](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511549816).
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-GRAV-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-grav-002--benchmark-g01-with-finer-cell-truth)

### EQ-INV-APP-GRAV-003 — Depth-weighted Tikhonov regularization

`min ||A x - y||^2 + lambda^2 ||W x||^2`, `W = diag((z_j + z_0)^(-beta / 2))`; the sensitivity slope `d log ||A e_j|| / d log z_j` suggests `beta = -2 slope`.

- Domain: `lambda > 0`, `beta >= 0`, `z_0 > 0`; solved by stacked QR (no normal equations); `beta = 0` is ordinary Tikhonov.
- Meaning: plain Tikhonov concentrates density near the surface because deep cells have small sensitivity; the weights counteract this (a heuristic after Li and Oldenburg, with tuning choices `beta`, `z_0`).
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_gravity.depth_weighted_tikhonov`, `inverse.benchmark_gravity.depth_weights`, `inverse.benchmark_gravity.sensitivity_depth_exponent`.
- Source: [REF-LI-OLDENBURG-1998], [REF-TIKHONOV-1963].
  - [REF-LI-OLDENBURG-1998](../references.md#ref-li-oldenburg-1998): Yaoguo Li and Douglas W. Oldenburg. "3-D Inversion of Gravity Data." *Geophysics* 63(1), 109-119, 1998. Cited for depth weighting of the model penalty in gravity inversion (the weights here are a heuristic in that style, with the tuning choices stated in the code). Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1190/1.1444302](https://doi.org/10.1190/1.1444302).
  - [REF-TIKHONOV-1963](../references.md#ref-tikhonov-1963): Andrei N. Tikhonov. "On the Solution of Ill-Posed Problems and the Method of Regularization." *Doklady Akademii Nauk SSSR* 151(3), 501-504, 1963 (in Russian); English translation "Solution of Incorrectly Formulated Problems and the Regularization Method," *Soviet Mathematics Doklady* 4, 1035-1038, 1963. The penalized least-squares regularization named after him.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-GRAV-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-grav-003--depth-weighted-tikhonov-regularization)

### EQ-INV-APP-GRAV-004 — Singular spectrum, null space and distinguishability

`A = U S V'`, rank `r = #{s_i > tol s_1}`, null dimension `n - r`, null element `N v = v - V_r V_r' v`, distinguishability `||A (x_1 - x_2)|| / sigma`, resolved modes `#{s_i p / sigma > 1}`.

- Domain: dense `A` (`m x n`), iid noise `sigma`, unit prior `p`.
- Meaning: with more cells than data there is an exact null space (density patterns with no surface signature); a distinguishability well below one means no method can tell two densities apart.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_gravity.gravity_spectrum`, `inverse.benchmark_gravity.null_space_element`, `inverse.benchmark_gravity.distinguishability`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008], [REF-BLAKELY-1995].

Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Cited for the vertical attraction of buried sources, the line-mass kernel and the non-uniqueness of potential fields (equivalent sources at different depths). Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/CBO9780511549816](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511549816).

### EQ-INV-APP-GRAV-005 — Equivalent single layer (depth ambiguity)

`min ||A_L x - y||^2 + d^2 ||x||^2` over the cells of one depth layer `L`; residual compared with the realized noise norm.

- Domain: explicit damping `d > 0` (never added silently); layers of the depth-major cell order.
- Meaning: a single sheet at several depths can reproduce the same surface data to the noise level, the classical depth/density non-uniqueness of potential-field data.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_gravity.single_layer_fit`.

## `probstat.inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere`

### EQ-INV-APP-GRAV-006 — Fully normalized associated Legendre functions by forward-column recursion

`Pbar_lm = sqrt((2 - delta_m0)(2 l + 1)(l - m)! / (l + m)!) P_lm` (no Condon-Shortley phase); `Pbar_00 = 1`, `Pbar_11 = sqrt(3) sin(theta)`, `Pbar_mm = sqrt((2m + 1) / (2m)) sin(theta) Pbar_{m-1,m-1}`, `Pbar_{m+1,m} = sqrt(2m + 3) cos(theta) Pbar_mm`, `Pbar_lm = a_lm cos(theta) Pbar_{l-1,m} - b_lm Pbar_{l-2,m}`, `a_lm = sqrt((4 l^2 - 1) / (l^2 - m^2))`, `b_lm = sqrt((2 l + 1)(l + m - 1)(l - m - 1) / ((2 l - 3)(l^2 - m^2)))`.

- Domain: `0 <= m <= l <= 100`, `theta in [0, pi]`. Orthonormality `(1/2) int_-1^1 Pbar_lm Pbar_l'm dt = (2 - delta_m0) delta_ll'` (equivalently `(1 / 4 pi) int (Pbar_lm cos m phi)^2 d Omega = 1`); addition theorem `sum_m Pbar_lm(t)^2 = 2 l + 1`.
- Meaning: no factorials appear, so there is no overflow at the degrees used; the recursion in `l` at fixed `m` is the stable direction. Degrees above 100 are rejected because the `sin^m` seed underflows near the poles (the scaling of Holmes and Featherstone is not implemented).
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.normalized_legendre_table`, `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.legendre_orthonormality_error`.
- Source: [REF-HOLMES-FEATHERSTONE-2002], [REF-HEISKANEN-MORITZ-1967].
  - [REF-HOLMES-FEATHERSTONE-2002](../references.md#ref-holmes-featherstone-2002): Simon A. Holmes and Will E. Featherstone. "A unified approach to the Clenshaw summation and the recursive computation of very high degree and order normalised associated Legendre functions." *Journal of Geodesy* 76(5), 279-299, 2002. Cited for the stable recursive computation of fully normalized associated Legendre functions and the scaling needed at very high degree. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/s00190-002-0216-2](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00190-002-0216-2).
  - [REF-HEISKANEN-MORITZ-1967](../references.md#ref-heiskanen-moritz-1967): Weikko A. Heiskanen and Helmut Moritz. *Physical Geodesy*. W. H. Freeman and Company, San Francisco, 1967. Cited for the spherical-harmonic expansion of the exterior gravitational potential and the fully normalized surface harmonics. The book has no DOI; its title, authors and year were confirmed through the Crossref record 10.1007/bf02525647 on 2026-11-26; the publisher was not checked independently.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-GRAV-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-grav-006--fully-normalized-associated-legendre-functions-by-forward-column-recursion)

### EQ-INV-APP-GRAV-007 — Spherical-harmonic potential and radial-gravity design with altitude attenuation

`V = (GM / r) sum_l (R / r)^l sum_m (C_lm cos m phi + S_lm sin m phi) Pbar_lm(cos theta)`, observation `g = -dV/dr = (GM / R^2) sum_l (l + 1)(R / r)^(l + 2) sum_m (C_lm cos m phi + S_lm sin m phi) Pbar_lm(cos theta)`, `y = A c + e`; interior point mass `q` at `r_s < R`: `C_lm = q r_s^l Pbar_lm(cos theta_s) cos(m phi_s) / (2 l + 1)` (and `sin` for `S_lm`).

- Domain: `r >= R`; units `R = GM = 1`; unknowns are degrees `l_min = 2` to `L` (degrees 0 and 1, the central term and the centre-of-mass offset, are not estimated); `(L + 1)^2 - l_min^2` coefficients; the point-mass coefficients follow from the addition theorem and give the closed-form attraction `q (r - r_s cos gamma) / rho^3` when summed.
- Meaning: the factor `(R / r)^(l + 2)` is the degree-dependent attenuation: altitude `h` reduces degree `l` by `(1 + h / R)^-(l + 2)`, so high degrees leave the data first. Synthetic geometry; not real tracking data, and an exterior field does not determine an interior density.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.gravity_design`, `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.potential`, `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.point_mass_coefficients`, `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.point_mass_radial_gravity`, `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.attenuation`, `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.sphere_design`.
- Source: [REF-HEISKANEN-MORITZ-1967], [REF-KAULA-1966].
  - [REF-HEISKANEN-MORITZ-1967](../references.md#ref-heiskanen-moritz-1967): Weikko A. Heiskanen and Helmut Moritz. *Physical Geodesy*. W. H. Freeman and Company, San Francisco, 1967. Cited for the spherical-harmonic expansion of the exterior gravitational potential and the fully normalized surface harmonics. The book has no DOI; its title, authors and year were confirmed through the Crossref record 10.1007/bf02525647 on 2026-11-26; the publisher was not checked independently.
  - [REF-KAULA-1966](../references.md#ref-kaula-1966): William M. Kaula. *Theory of Satellite Geodesy: Applications of Satellites to Geodesy*. Blaisdell, 1966. Cited for the power-law decay of the spherical-harmonic coefficients with degree (the Kaula rule of thumb) used as a degree-variance prior and for the exterior-potential series in satellite geodesy. The book has no DOI; its title and author were confirmed through a 1967 review indexed by Crossref (DOI 10.1063/1.3033941) on 2026-11-26; the publisher was not checked independently.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-GRAV-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-grav-007--spherical-harmonic-potential-and-radial-gravity-design-with-altitude-attenuation)

### EQ-INV-APP-GRAV-008 — Kaula-type degree-variance prior and degree signal variance

`C_lm, S_lm ~ N(0, s_l^2)` independent, `s_l = kappa / l^p` (`p = 2` is the Kaula rule of thumb); degree signal variance of the observation `(2 l + 1)(l + 1)^2 (R / r)^(2 l + 4) s_l^2`.

- Domain: `l >= 1`, `kappa > 0` a declared scale (synthetic), `p >= 0`; the mean square of a fully normalized harmonic over the sphere is one, and the degree has `2 l + 1` coefficients.
- Meaning: a smoothness prior that gives power-law decay of the field with degree; the numerical value of `kappa` carries no claim about any real body.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.kaula_prior_sd`, `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.degree_signal_variance`.
- Source: [REF-KAULA-1966].
  - [REF-KAULA-1966](../references.md#ref-kaula-1966): William M. Kaula. *Theory of Satellite Geodesy: Applications of Satellites to Geodesy*. Blaisdell, 1966. Cited for the power-law decay of the spherical-harmonic coefficients with degree (the Kaula rule of thumb) used as a degree-variance prior and for the exterior-potential series in satellite geodesy. The book has no DOI; its title and author were confirmed through a 1967 review indexed by Crossref (DOI 10.1063/1.3033941) on 2026-11-26; the publisher was not checked independently.

### EQ-INV-APP-GRAV-009 — Exact Gaussian posterior, resolution matrix and resolved degree

`Q = A'A / s^2 + Lambda^-1`, `Sigma = Q^-1`, `mean = Sigma A'y / s^2`, resolution diagonal `1 - Sigma_jj / Lambda_jj`, `log p(y) = -1/2 (y'y / s^2 - b' Sigma b) - 1/2 (m log s^2 + log det Lambda + log det Q) - (m / 2) log 2 pi`, `b = A'y / s^2`; degree resolved when the mean resolution diagonal of its coefficients is `>= 1/2`; for an orthogonal design the fraction is `tau / (1 + tau)`, `tau = m_obs ((l + 1)(R / r)^(l + 2) s_l / s)^2`.

- Domain: iid Gaussian noise `s`, diagonal prior `Lambda`, `Q` positive definite (Cholesky, no jitter); the resolved degree is the largest `l` with every degree up to `l` resolved.
- Meaning: which degrees the data resolve at a given noise level and altitude under the declared prior; a posterior-variance criterion, not a statement about a real field. The orthogonal prediction is a check on the exact diagonal, not a replacement.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.gravity_posterior`, `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.degree_resolution`, `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.resolved_degree`, `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.resolution_sweep`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007], [REF-HANSEN-2008].

"Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. ### EQ-INV-APP-GRAV-010 — Omission error and the higher-degree truth benchmark

`y = A_t c_t + e`, `A_t` of degree `L_t > L_s`, solver unknowns of degree `L_s`; expected omission variance `sum_{l = L_s + 1}^{L_t} (2 l + 1)(l + 1)^2 (R / r)^(2 l + 4) s_l^2`; `L_t = L_s` is the inverse crime and sets `same_discretization = True`.

- Domain: Kaula truth drawn from the prior (or point masses in closed form with the degree 0 and 1 parts removed); truth withheld; omission rms reported.
- Meaning: the truncated solver cannot represent the omitted signal, which aliases into the low degrees; with small noise the posterior is then overconfident (standardized error much greater than one), a property of truncation and not of the solver arithmetic.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.gravity_sphere_benchmark`, `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.omission_variance`.

### EQ-INV-APP-GRAV-011 — Singular-value clusters by degree

`s_k(A) ~ sqrt(m_obs) (l + 1)(R / r)^(l + 2)` for the `2 l + 1` singular values of degree `l`; whitened spectrum `svd(A diag(s_l) / s)`, modes with whitened singular value `> 1` are data-dominated.

- Domain: quasi-uniform observation set (Fibonacci), so the columns are nearly orthogonal with unit mean square; exact only for exactly orthogonal columns.
- Meaning: the attenuation is visible in the spectrum: the lowest cluster is the highest degree and it falls by the factor `(r_1 / r_2)^(l + 2)` when the altitude grows; the derivative factor `l + 1` offsets it at low altitude (`(l + 1) 1.1^-(l + 2)` peaks at `l = 9`), so the plain spectrum is flat there and the high-degree loss shows in the prior-whitened spectrum; the measured spread around the prediction is reported by `gravity_spectrum`.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_gravity_sphere.gravity_spectrum`.
