## `probstat.inverse.benchmark_inference`

### EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-010 — Closed-form dark-energy factor, exact for three parameterizations

`ln X(z) = 3 int_0^z (1 + w(t)) / (1 + t) dt = c(z) + g(z) . p`, with `E^2 = Om (1 + z)^3 + (1 - Om) X`, `D_L = (1 + z)(c / H0) int_0^z dt / E`, `mu = 5 log10 (D_L / Mpc) + 25`; constant `w`: `c = 3 L`, `g = 3 L`, `L = ln(1 + z)`; CPL `w = w0 + wa z / (1 + z)`: `c = 3 L`, `g = (3 L, 3 (L - z / (1 + z)))` for `p = (w0, wa)`; two bins `w1` for `z < z_b`, `w2` beyond: `c = 3 (l_b + l_c)`, `g = (3 l_b, 3 l_c)`, `l_b = ln(1 + min(z, z_b))`, `l_c = max(0, ln((1 + z) / (1 + z_b)))`. Jacobian: `d(1/E)/d theta = -(d E^2 / d theta) / (2 E^3)`, `d E^2 / d Om = (1 + z)^3 - X`, `d E^2 / d p_k = (1 - Om) X g_k`, `d mu / d theta = (5 / ln 10)(d I / d theta) / I`, `I = int_0^z dt / E`.

- Domain: flat FLRW, radiation neglected (`z <~ 2`), `E^2 > 0` (otherwise the forward value is NaN and the likelihood is zero); `H0` fixed (degenerate with the magnitude offset); the integral is a 6-point Gauss-Legendre rule on segments cut at every data redshift and at `z_b`, so the integrand is smooth on each segment.
- Meaning: the three `w(z)` families nest (`lcdm` in `wcdm` in `cpl`; `pw2` is a different two-parameter family); `ln X` is linear in the `w` parameters, which makes the analytic Jacobian a few lines. Constant `w` gives `X = (1 + z)^(3 (1 + w))` and `D_L ~ c z / H0` at low `z`. SYNTHETIC test bed: nothing here constrains the real universe.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_inference.build_problem`, `inverse.benchmark_inference.ModelSpec`, `inverse.benchmark_inference.synthetic_expansion_data`.
- Source: [REF-HOGG-1999], [REF-CHEVALLIER-POLARSKI-2001], [REF-LINDER-2003].
  - [REF-HOGG-1999](../references.md#ref-hogg-1999): David W. Hogg. "Distance Measures in Cosmology." arXiv:astro-ph/9905116, 1999. Cited for the comoving and luminosity distances, the distance modulus and the Hubble distance `c / H0` of a flat Friedmann-Lemaitre-Robertson-Walker model. Metadata checked against DataCite (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.48550/arXiv.astro-ph/9905116](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.astro-ph/9905116).
  - [REF-CHEVALLIER-POLARSKI-2001](../references.md#ref-chevallier-polarski-2001): Michel Chevallier and David Polarski. "Accelerating Universes with Scaling Dark Matter." *International Journal of Modern Physics D* 10(2), 213-223, 2001. Cited, with [REF-LINDER-2003], for the dark-energy equation of state `w(z) = w0 + wa z / (1 + z)` used as a synthetic truth. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1142/S0218271801000822](https://doi.org/10.1142/S0218271801000822).

Cited for the `w0`-`wa` parameterization of the dark-energy equation of state. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1103/PhysRevLett.90.091301](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.90.091301).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-010](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-cosmo-010--closed-form-dark-energy-factor-exact-for-three-parameterizations)

### EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-011 — Magnitude offset integrated out analytically

`y = mu(theta) + M 1 + e`, `e ~ N(0, s^2 I)`, `M ~ N(0, s_M^2)` gives `y | theta ~ N(mu(theta), C)`, `C = s^2 I + s_M^2 1 1'`, `C^-1 = (I - s_M^2 / (s^2 + m s_M^2) 1 1') / s^2`, `log det C = (m - 1) log s^2 + log(s^2 + m s_M^2)`.

- Domain: `s, s_M > 0`; the offset enters every datum with coefficient one and has a Gaussian prior, so the marginalization is exact (Sherman-Morrison and the matrix determinant lemma).
- Meaning: removes one dimension from every posterior and reproduces the exact `H0`-offset degeneracy of uncalibrated supernovae without sampling it. A model without other free parameters has the exact evidence `log N(y; mu, C)`.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_inference.offset_marginal_log_likelihood`, `inverse.benchmark_inference.offset_marginal_covariance`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007], [REF-HOGG-1999].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
  - [REF-HOGG-1999](../references.md#ref-hogg-1999): David W. Hogg. "Distance Measures in Cosmology." arXiv:astro-ph/9905116, 1999. Cited for the comoving and luminosity distances, the distance modulus and the Hubble distance `c / H0` of a flat Friedmann-Lemaitre-Robertson-Walker model. Metadata checked against DataCite (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.48550/arXiv.astro-ph/9905116](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.astro-ph/9905116).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-011](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-cosmo-011--magnitude-offset-integrated-out-analytically)

### EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-012 — Laplace approximation with the Gauss-Newton Hessian

`z* = argmin 1/2 ||W (G(mu + S z) - y)||^2 + 1/2 ||z||^2`, `H = J_z' J_z + I`, `J_z = W J S`, `log Z_L = log L(z*) + log N(z*; 0, I) + (d / 2) log 2 pi - 1/2 log det H`, posterior `N(mu + S z*, S H^-1 S)`.

- Domain: prior `N(mu, S^2)` diagonal, noise factor `W = L^-1`, `C = L L'`; `H` omits the residual-curvature term `sum_i r_i grad^2 G_i` of the exact Hessian.
- Meaning: exact for linear-Gaussian problems; for a nonlinear forward map it is a local, symmetric Gaussian that misses skewness, and its evidence misses the same. Reported errors against quadrature are measured, not assumed small.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_inference.laplace_fit`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006], [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-012](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-cosmo-012--laplace-approximation-with-the-gauss-newton-hessian)

### EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-013 — Posterior errors against a quadrature reference

`e_mean,i = |m_i - m_i^ref| / s_i^ref`, `r_sd,i = s_i / s_i^ref`, `e_q,i = max_k |q_i(p_k) - q_i^ref(p_k)| / s_i^ref`, `p in (0.025, 0.5, 0.975)`; the reference is the tensor Gauss-Legendre quadrature of `L(z) N(z; 0, I)` on a Laplace-centred box.

- Domain: one or two free parameters (the quadrature reference exists only there; three or more parameters have none and the comparison says so); box truncation and doubled-panel refinement error are reported with the reference.
- Meaning: errors are in reference standard deviations, so they are comparable across parameters and methods; Monte Carlo errors scale as `1 / sqrt(N_eff)` and need a band, not an equality.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_inference.compare_inference_methods`, `inverse.benchmark_inference.MethodSummary`.
- Source: [REF-COTTER-ROBERTS-STUART-WHITE-2013], [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984], [REF-LEE-WHITELEY-2018].
  - [REF-COTTER-ROBERTS-STUART-WHITE-2013](../references.md#ref-cotter-roberts-stuart-white-2013): Simon L. Cotter, Gareth O. Roberts, Andrew M. Stuart, and David White. "MCMC Methods for Functions: Modifying Old Algorithms to Make Them Faster." *Statistical Science* 28(3), 424-446, 2013. The preconditioned Crank-Nicolson proposal. DOI: [10.1214/13-STS421](https://doi.org/10.1214/13-STS421).
  - [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984](../references.md#ref-davis-rabinowitz-1984): Philip J. Davis and Philip Rabinowitz. *Methods of Numerical Integration*. 2nd edition. Academic Press, 1984. ISBN 978-0-12-206360-2.
  - [REF-LEE-WHITELEY-2018](../references.md#ref-lee-whiteley-2018): Anthony Lee and Nick Whiteley. "Variance Estimation in the Particle Filter." *Biometrika* 105(3), 609-625, 2018. Unbiased and consistent variance estimators from the genealogy (Eve indices) of one particle run. DOI: [10.1093/biomet/asy028](https://doi.org/10.1093/biomet/asy028).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-013](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-cosmo-013--posterior-errors-against-a-quadrature-reference)

### EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-014 — Bayes factor with propagated uncertainty and prior sensitivity

`ln B_i0 = ln Z_i - ln Z_0`, `se(ln B_i0) = sqrt(se_i^2 + se_0^2)` for independent SMC runs (`se` = across-run standard deviation / `sqrt(runs)`; zero for an exactly known evidence), quadrature value `ln Z_i^q - ln Z_0^q` with error `e_i + e_0` (doubled-panel change); Kass-Raftery category of `2 ln B`: `< 2` bare mention, `2-6` positive, `6-10` strong, `> 10` very strong.

- Domain: normalized proper priors, the same data for every model; `Z = int L(theta) p(theta) d theta`.
- Meaning: the Occam factor grows with prior volume, so `ln B` depends on the declared prior widths (`prior_width_sensitivity` sweeps them with deterministic quadrature); a toy Bayes factor from synthetic data is a property of that data set and prior only, not a statement about dark energy; the verbal scale is a convention, and a mean of `log Z_hat` over runs carries a small downward Jensen bias of order half the run variance.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_inference.compare_models`, `inverse.benchmark_inference.evidence_category`, `inverse.benchmark_inference.prior_width_sensitivity`.
- Source: [REF-KASS-RAFTERY-1995], [REF-DELMORAL-DOUCET-JASRA-2006], [REF-LEE-WHITELEY-2018].
  - [REF-KASS-RAFTERY-1995](../references.md#ref-kass-raftery-1995): Robert E. Kass and Adrian E. Raftery. "Bayes Factors." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 90(430), 773-795, 1995.
  - [REF-DELMORAL-DOUCET-JASRA-2006](../references.md#ref-delmoral-doucet-jasra-2006): Pierre Del Moral, Arnaud Doucet, and Ajay Jasra. "Sequential Monte Carlo Samplers." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B (Statistical Methodology)* 68(3), 411-436, 2006. Tempered sequences of targets, backward kernels, and the SMC estimate of normalizing constants. DOI: [10.1111/j.1467-9868.2006.00553.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1467-9868.2006.00553.x).
  - [REF-LEE-WHITELEY-2018](../references.md#ref-lee-whiteley-2018): Anthony Lee and Nick Whiteley. "Variance Estimation in the Particle Filter." *Biometrika* 105(3), 609-625, 2018. Unbiased and consistent variance estimators from the genealogy (Eve indices) of one particle run. DOI: [10.1093/biomet/asy028](https://doi.org/10.1093/biomet/asy028).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-COSMO-014](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-cosmo-014--bayes-factor-with-propagated-uncertainty-and-prior-sensitivity)

## `probstat.inverse.benchmark_orbit`

### EQ-INV-ORBIT-001 — Two-body dynamics and specific energy

`r' = v`, `v' = -mu r / ||r||^3`, `E = ||v||^2 / 2 - mu / ||r||`, in an inertial frame with canonical units (`mu = 1`).

- Domain: `r` in R^d (d = 2 or 3), `r != 0`, `mu > 0`; point-mass central body, no perturbations, no Earth rotation or time systems.
- Meaning: the synthetic orbit model; `E` is conserved by the continuous flow and, up to the O(h^4) integrator error, by classical RK4.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_orbit.TwoBodyRangeModel`.
- Source: [REF-TAPLEY-SCHUTZ-BORN-2004], [REF-HAIRER-NORSETT-WANNER-1993].
  - [REF-TAPLEY-SCHUTZ-BORN-2004](../references.md#ref-tapley-schutz-born-2004): Byron D. Tapley, Bob E. Schutz, and George H. Born. *Statistical Orbit Determination*. Elsevier Academic Press, Burlington, 2004. Cited for batch least-squares orbit determination, the state transition matrix `Phi' = F Phi` and range observation partials. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26; chapter "Fundamentals of Orbit Determination"). DOI: [10.1016/B978-012683630-1/50023-0](https://doi.org/10.1016/B978-012683630-1/50023-0).
  - [REF-HAIRER-NORSETT-WANNER-1993](../references.md#ref-hairer-norsett-wanner-1993): Ernst Hairer, Syvert P. Nørsett, and Gerhard Wanner. *Solving Ordinary Differential Equations I: Nonstiff Problems*. Second revised edition. Springer Series in Computational Mathematics 8. Springer, Berlin, Heidelberg, 1993. ISBN 978-3-540-56670-0. DOI: [10.1007/978-3-540-78862-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-78862-1).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ORBIT-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-orbit-001--two-body-dynamics-and-specific-energy)

### EQ-INV-ORBIT-002 — State transition matrix and mu-sensitivity by augmented RK4

`Z' = F(s) Z + [0 | F_mu(s)]`, `Z(0) = [I | 0]`, `F = [[0, I], [A, 0]]`, `A = -mu (I/r^3 - 3 r r' / r^5)`, `F_mu = [0; -r/r^3]`, with `Z = d(r, v)(t) / d(x)` (`Phi` in the state columns, `S` in the optional last column).

- Domain: same as EQ-INV-ORBIT-001; one classical RK4 applied to the augmented system `(s, Z)` with fixed steps.
- Meaning: because Runge-Kutta schemes commute with differentiation in the initial data, `Z` is the EXACT derivative of the RK4-discretized map for the same step sizes (to rounding), not only an O(h^4) approximation of the continuous flow's derivative. Volume preservation `det Phi = 1` holds only to integrator accuracy.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_orbit.TwoBodyRangeModel.sensitivities`.
- Source: [REF-TAPLEY-SCHUTZ-BORN-2004], [REF-HAIRER-NORSETT-WANNER-1993].
  - [REF-TAPLEY-SCHUTZ-BORN-2004](../references.md#ref-tapley-schutz-born-2004): Byron D. Tapley, Bob E. Schutz, and George H. Born. *Statistical Orbit Determination*. Elsevier Academic Press, Burlington, 2004. Cited for batch least-squares orbit determination, the state transition matrix `Phi' = F Phi` and range observation partials. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26; chapter "Fundamentals of Orbit Determination"). DOI: [10.1016/B978-012683630-1/50023-0](https://doi.org/10.1016/B978-012683630-1/50023-0).
  - [REF-HAIRER-NORSETT-WANNER-1993](../references.md#ref-hairer-norsett-wanner-1993): Ernst Hairer, Syvert P. Nørsett, and Gerhard Wanner. *Solving Ordinary Differential Equations I: Nonstiff Problems*. Second revised edition. Springer Series in Computational Mathematics 8. Springer, Berlin, Heidelberg, 1993. ISBN 978-3-540-56670-0. DOI: [10.1007/978-3-540-78862-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-78862-1).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ORBIT-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-orbit-002--state-transition-matrix-and-mu-sensitivity-by-augmented-rk4)

### EQ-INV-ORBIT-003 — Range observation and its Jacobian

`rho_ij = ||r(t_i) - s_j||`, `J_(ij),: = u_ij' [Z(t_i)]_(position rows)`, `u_ij = (r(t_i) - s_j) / rho_ij`.

- Domain: `rho_ij > 0` (a zero range raises); stations `s_j` fixed in the inertial frame; observations time-major, station-minor.
- Meaning: `jvp` applies `J`, `vjp` applies `J'` through the transposed position rows of `Z` (no dense finite-difference Jacobian).
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_orbit.TwoBodyRangeModel.jvp`, `inverse.benchmark_orbit.TwoBodyRangeModel.vjp`.
- Source: [REF-TAPLEY-SCHUTZ-BORN-2004].
  - [REF-TAPLEY-SCHUTZ-BORN-2004](../references.md#ref-tapley-schutz-born-2004): Byron D. Tapley, Bob E. Schutz, and George H. Born. *Statistical Orbit Determination*. Elsevier Academic Press, Burlington, 2004. Cited for batch least-squares orbit determination, the state transition matrix `Phi' = F Phi` and range observation partials. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26; chapter "Fundamentals of Orbit Determination"). DOI: [10.1016/B978-012683630-1/50023-0](https://doi.org/10.1016/B978-012683630-1/50023-0).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ORBIT-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-orbit-003--range-observation-and-its-jacobian)

### EQ-INV-ORBIT-004 — Orbit-determination forward map

`G(x) = (rho_ij)` with `x = (r0, v0)` or `x = (r0, v0, mu)`; `G: R^n -> R^m`, `n = 2d (+1)`, `m = #times * #stations`.

- Domain: increasing positive times; with `estimate_mu`, `mu <= 0` in `x` gives a `nan` value (a rejected trial for the solvers), never a repaired one.
- Meaning: the `DifferentiableForward` model inverted by Gauss-Newton; weak observability (single station, short arc; in 3D an exact null direction, the rotation about the line of sight) appears as a large condition number or rank deficiency of `J` at the truth.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_orbit.TwoBodyRangeModel`.
- Source: [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003], [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ORBIT-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-orbit-004--orbit-determination-forward-map)

### EQ-INV-ORBIT-005 — Benchmark A01

`y = G_fine(x_true) + e`, `e ~ N(0, sd^2 I)`, with `G_fine` the same model integrated with `data_refinement` times more RK4 steps.

- Domain: `data_refinement >= 1` (1 is the inverse crime, algebraic verification only); noise is absolute (canonical length units), not rescaled; truth withheld from the solver inputs.
- Meaning: synthetic geometry laboratory (`well_observed` versus `single_station_short_arc`); no claim of realistic tracking.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_orbit.orbit_determination_benchmark`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007], [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ORBIT-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-orbit-005--benchmark-a01)

### EQ-INV-APP-ORBIT-001 — Per-station range bias nuisance

`rho_ij^obs = ||r(t_i) - s_j|| + b_j`, parameters `x = (r0, v0, [mu], b_1..b_S)`; `d rho_ij / d b_k = delta_jk`, and the biases have no dynamics (no columns in the state sensitivity `Z`).

- Domain: stations fixed in the inertial frame, one constant additive range bias per station, `b_j` in canonical length units; synthetic.
- Meaning: a nuisance that can mimic an orbit change when a single station observes a short arc; whether it is determined is measured by EQ-INV-APP-ORBIT-002, not assumed.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_orbit.TwoBodyRangeModel`, `inverse.benchmark_orbit.orbit_determination_benchmark`.
- Source: [REF-TAPLEY-SCHUTZ-BORN-2004].
  - [REF-TAPLEY-SCHUTZ-BORN-2004](../references.md#ref-tapley-schutz-born-2004): Byron D. Tapley, Bob E. Schutz, and George H. Born. *Statistical Orbit Determination*. Elsevier Academic Press, Burlington, 2004. Cited for batch least-squares orbit determination, the state transition matrix `Phi' = F Phi` and range observation partials. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26; chapter "Fundamentals of Orbit Determination"). DOI: [10.1016/B978-012683630-1/50023-0](https://doi.org/10.1016/B978-012683630-1/50023-0).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-ORBIT-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-orbit-001--per-station-range-bias-nuisance)

### EQ-INV-APP-ORBIT-002 — Whitened identifiability of orbit and bias parameters

`W J = U S V'`, `W' W = Gamma^-1`, `rank = #{s_i > tol s_1}`, `loading_i = ||N' e_i||` with `N` the right singular vectors with `s_i <= tol s_1`, `sd_i = sqrt([(J' Gamma^-1 J)^-1]_ii)`.

- Domain: `J` is the Jacobian of the range map at the synthetic truth (local, linearized statement); `Gamma` a covariance factor; zero rows are appended when `m < n`; `sd` is reported only at full numerical rank.
- Meaning: parameter `i` is determined exactly when its unit vector is orthogonal to the null space (`loading_i = 0`); a full numerical rank with a huge `sd_i` (relative to the parameter scale) means practical non-identifiability. No claim about real tracking.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_orbit.station_bias_identifiability`.
- Source: [REF-TAPLEY-SCHUTZ-BORN-2004], [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003].
  - [REF-TAPLEY-SCHUTZ-BORN-2004](../references.md#ref-tapley-schutz-born-2004): Byron D. Tapley, Bob E. Schutz, and George H. Born. *Statistical Orbit Determination*. Elsevier Academic Press, Burlington, 2004. Cited for batch least-squares orbit determination, the state transition matrix `Phi' = F Phi` and range observation partials. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26; chapter "Fundamentals of Orbit Determination"). DOI: [10.1016/B978-012683630-1/50023-0](https://doi.org/10.1016/B978-012683630-1/50023-0).
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-ORBIT-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-orbit-002--whitened-identifiability-of-orbit-and-bias-parameters)

### EQ-INV-APP-ORBIT-003 — Separable correlated range-noise covariance

`Gamma = w^2 I + c^2 (T kron S)`, `T_ik = exp(-|t_i - t_k| / tau)`, `S_jl = 1 (j = l), rho (j != l)`, observations time-major and station-minor.

- Domain: `w > 0`, `c >= 0`, `tau > 0`, `rho` in `[0, 1]`; the white part guarantees positive definiteness.
- Meaning: an exponential time correlation and a common cross-station correlation, a synthetic stand-in for slowly varying measurement errors; handled through its Cholesky factor.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_orbit.range_noise_covariance`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-ORBIT-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-orbit-003--separable-correlated-range-noise-covariance)

### EQ-INV-APP-ORBIT-004 — Interval calibration when the correlation is ignored

`dx = A e`, `A = (J' D^-1 J)^-1 J' D^-1` (naive, `D = diag Gamma`) with nominal covariance `(J' D^-1 J)^-1` and actual covariance `A Gamma A'`; GLS: `(J' Gamma^-1 J)^-1`; empirical coverage of `|dx_k| <= z_((1+level)/2) sd_k` over predeclared seeds, Monte Carlo standard error `sqrt(p (1 - p) / N)`.

- Domain: linearized estimator at the synthetic truth, `e ~ N(0, Gamma)`, full numerical rank (otherwise the study refuses).
- Meaning: ignoring correlation can make intervals too wide or too narrow depending on the parameter (a smooth error is partly absorbed by the orbit); GLS is calibrated and no worse than the naive estimator (Gauss-Markov). Not a nonlinear coverage study.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_orbit.correlation_calibration_study`.
- Source: [REF-TAPLEY-SCHUTZ-BORN-2004], [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003].
  - [REF-TAPLEY-SCHUTZ-BORN-2004](../references.md#ref-tapley-schutz-born-2004): Byron D. Tapley, Bob E. Schutz, and George H. Born. *Statistical Orbit Determination*. Elsevier Academic Press, Burlington, 2004. Cited for batch least-squares orbit determination, the state transition matrix `Phi' = F Phi` and range observation partials. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26; chapter "Fundamentals of Orbit Determination"). DOI: [10.1016/B978-012683630-1/50023-0](https://doi.org/10.1016/B978-012683630-1/50023-0).
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-APP-ORBIT-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-app-orbit-004--interval-calibration-when-the-correlation-is-ignored)

## `probstat.inverse.benchmark_problems`

### EQ-INV-BENCH-001 — Benchmark data generation and the inverse-crime flag

`y = b + e`, `e_i ~ N(0, s^2)` iid with `s = rho ||b|| / sqrt(m)` (not
rescaled to a fixed norm; the realized `||e||` is reported); `b = A x_true`
when `same_discretization`, otherwise `b = P_coarse(A_fine x_fine)` from an
`r`-times finer grid with cell averaging `P_coarse`, and
`x_true = P_coarse x_fine`.

- Domain: `rho > 0`, `b != 0`; separate random-stream children for truth,
  design and noise.
- Meaning: benchmark inputs with withheld truth. Same-discretization data
  verify algebra only (inverse crime); refined data carry a genuine model
  error of the coarse operator.
- Code: `inverse.benchmark_problems.diagonal_spectrum_benchmark`,
  `inverse.benchmark_problems.deconvolution_benchmark`,
  `inverse.benchmark_problems.sparse_sensing_benchmark`,
  `inverse.benchmark_problems.correlated_gaussian_benchmark`,
  `inverse.benchmark_problems.InverseBenchmark`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007], [REF-HANSEN-2008, Chapter 4],
  [REF-GAZZOLA-HANSEN-NAGY-2019].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
  - [REF-GAZZOLA-HANSEN-NAGY-2019](../references.md#ref-gazzola-hansen-nagy-2019): Silvia Gazzola, Per Christian Hansen, and James G. Nagy. "IR Tools: A MATLAB Package of Iterative Regularization Methods and Large-Scale Test Problems." *Numerical Algorithms* 81(3), 773-811, 2019. Cited for matrix-free operators, iterative regularization by early stopping, hybrid projection methods and their test problems. No code was copied. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/s11075-018-0570-7](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11075-018-0570-7).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-BENCH-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-bench-001--benchmark-data-generation-and-the-inverse-crime-flag)

## `probstat.inverse.calibration`

### EQ-INV-IDENT-001 — Linear-design identifiability and conditioning

`rank(H)=p` and `kappa_2(H^T H)=lambda_max/lambda_min`.

- Domain: finite design matrix; the implemented diagnostic specializes to the
  two-column intercept/slope design.
- Meaning: full column rank is structural identifiability; a large condition
  number warns of weak practical separation.
- Code: `inverse.diagnose_linear_identifiability`.
- Source: [REF-TARANTOLA-2005, Chapter 3] and
  [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013, Section 8.1].
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-IDENT-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ident-001--linear-design-identifiability-and-conditioning)

## `probstat.inverse.consensus_sampling`

### EQ-INV-CBS-001 — Consensus weights and weighted moments

`w_j = exp(-beta Phi(u_j)) / sum_k exp(-beta Phi(u_k))`,
`m_b = sum_j w_j u_j`, `C_b = sum_j w_j (u_j - m_b)(u_j - m_b)'`,
`J_eff = 1 / sum_j w_j^2`, with `Phi` of [EQ-INV-ALDI-001].

- Domain: `beta > 0`; the library uses the largest
  `beta <= inverse_temperature` with `J_eff >= f J` (bisection), a heuristic.
- Meaning: importance weights of the tempered posterior relative to the
  current ensemble; only values of `Phi` are needed.
- Code: `inverse.consensus_based_sampling`.
- Source: [REF-CARRILLO-ETAL-2022].
  - [REF-CARRILLO-ETAL-2022](../references.md#ref-carrillo-etal-2022): José A. Carrillo, Franca Hoffmann, Andrew M. Stuart, and Urbain Vaes. "Consensus-Based Sampling." *Studies in Applied Mathematics* 148(3), 1069-1140, 2022. Weighted-ensemble dynamics with a sampling and an optimization mode and Gaussian steady states. DOI: [10.1111/sapm.12470](https://doi.org/10.1111/sapm.12470).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-CBS-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-cbs-001--consensus-weights-and-weighted-moments)

### EQ-INV-CBS-002 — Consensus-based sampling update

`u_j <- m_b + alpha (u_j - m_b) + sqrt((1 - alpha^2) / lambda) C_b^1/2 xi_j`,
`xi_j ~ N(0, I)`, with `lambda = 1 / (1 + beta)` (sampling) or `lambda = 1`
(optimization).

- Domain: `0 <= alpha < 1`; `C_b` positive definite.
- Meaning: relaxation towards the weighted mean plus noise shaped by the
  weighted covariance; `alpha = 0` redraws the ensemble from
  `N(m_b, C_b / lambda)`.
- Code: `inverse.consensus_based_sampling`.
- Source: [REF-CARRILLO-ETAL-2022].
  - [REF-CARRILLO-ETAL-2022](../references.md#ref-carrillo-etal-2022): José A. Carrillo, Franca Hoffmann, Andrew M. Stuart, and Urbain Vaes. "Consensus-Based Sampling." *Studies in Applied Mathematics* 148(3), 1069-1140, 2022. Weighted-ensemble dynamics with a sampling and an optimization mode and Gaussian steady states. DOI: [10.1111/sapm.12470](https://doi.org/10.1111/sapm.12470).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-CBS-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-cbs-002--consensus-based-sampling-update)

### EQ-INV-CBS-003 — Gaussian fixed point of consensus-based sampling

If the ensemble law is `N(m, C)` and
`Phi(u) = (u - mu)' Sigma^-1 (u - mu) / 2 + const`, then
`C_b^-1 = C^-1 + beta Sigma^-1`, `m_b = C_b (C^-1 m + beta Sigma^-1 mu)`, and
the update of [EQ-INV-CBS-002] gives the law
`N(m_b + alpha (m - m_b), alpha^2 C + (1 - alpha^2) C_b / lambda)`.

- Domain: mean-field (infinite-ensemble) iteration; quadratic `Phi`.
- Meaning: a fixed point needs `m = mu` and
  `(1 - lambda) C^-1 = lambda beta Sigma^-1`. With `lambda = 1 / (1 + beta)`
  this is `C = Sigma` for every `beta`: the posterior `N(mu, Sigma)` is
  stationary (the reweighted covariance of the target is
  `Sigma / (1 + beta)`). With `lambda = 1` only `C = 0` is fixed and the
  mean tends to the minimizer `mu`. For `J` members `C_b` is low by about
  `1 / J_eff`, which shifts the fixed point to about
  `(1 - (1 + beta) / (beta J_eff)) Sigma`. For a non-Gaussian posterior the
  method is an approximation.
- Code: `inverse.consensus_based_sampling`.
- Source: derived in ADR-0130; Gaussian steady states are the subject of
  [REF-CARRILLO-ETAL-2022].
  - [REF-CARRILLO-ETAL-2022](../references.md#ref-carrillo-etal-2022): José A. Carrillo, Franca Hoffmann, Andrew M. Stuart, and Urbain Vaes. "Consensus-Based Sampling." *Studies in Applied Mathematics* 148(3), 1069-1140, 2022. Weighted-ensemble dynamics with a sampling and an optimization mode and Gaussian steady states. DOI: [10.1111/sapm.12470](https://doi.org/10.1111/sapm.12470).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-CBS-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-cbs-003--gaussian-fixed-point-of-consensus-based-sampling)

## `probstat.inverse.discrepancy`

### EQ-KOH-MODEL-001 — Kennedy-O'Hagan observation model

`y_i = eta(c_i, theta) + delta(x_i) + eps_i`, `delta ~ GP(0, k_psi)`,
`eps_i ~ N(0, sigma_i^2)` independent of each other and of `delta`; `x_i` is
the subset of the conditions `c_i` the discrepancy depends on.

- Domain: one scalar observed quantity; simulator evaluated directly (no
  emulator); zero-mean discrepancy; no simulator scale factor `rho`.
- Meaning: the simulator at the best-fitting `theta` differs from reality by
  a smooth unknown function plus measurement error.
- Code: `inverse.DiscrepancyCalibration`,
  `inverse.DiscrepancyCalibration.predictions`.
- Source: [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001] and [REF-HIGDON-ETAL-2004].
  - [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001](../references.md#ref-kennedy-ohagan-2001): Marc C. Kennedy and Anthony O'Hagan. "Bayesian Calibration of Computer Models." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B* 63(3), 425-464, 2001. DOI: [10.1111/1467-9868.00294](https://doi.org/10.1111/1467-9868.00294).
  - [REF-HIGDON-ETAL-2004](../references.md#ref-higdon-etal-2004): Dave Higdon, Marc Kennedy, James C. Cavendish, John A. Cafeo, and Robert D. Ryne. "Combining Field Data and Computer Simulations for Calibration and Prediction." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 26(2), 448-466, 2004. DOI: [10.1137/S1064827503426693](https://doi.org/10.1137/S1064827503426693).
- Registry: [EQ-KOH-MODEL-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-koh-model-001--kennedy-ohagan-observation-model)

### EQ-KOH-KERNEL-001 — Discrepancy covariance kernels

`k_psi(x, x') = sigma_delta^2 exp(-r^2/2)` (squared exponential) or
`sigma_delta^2 (1 + sqrt(5) r + 5 r^2/3) exp(-sqrt(5) r)` (Matern 5/2), with
`r^2 = sum_i ((x_i - x'_i)/ell_i)^2` and `psi = (sigma_delta, ell_1..ell_p)`.

- Domain: `sigma_delta >= 0`, `ell_i > 0`.
- Meaning: stationary prior on the discrepancy; `sigma_delta` sets its size,
  `ell_i` its smoothness scale along input `i`.
- Code: `inverse.discrepancy_kernel`,
  `inverse.DiscrepancyCalibration.discrepancy_covariance`.
- Source: [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006, Section 4.2].
  - [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006](../references.md#ref-rasmussen-williams-2006): Carl Edward Rasmussen and Christopher K. I. Williams. *Gaussian Processes for Machine Learning*. MIT Press, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-KOH-KERNEL-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-koh-kernel-001--discrepancy-covariance-kernels)

### EQ-KOH-MARG-001 — Marginal likelihood with the discrepancy integrated out

`C_psi = K_psi + diag(sigma_i^2) = L L'`, `r = y - eta(C, theta)`,
`log p(y | theta, psi, sigma) = -(1/2)|L^-1 r|^2 - sum_i log L_ii
- (n/2) log 2pi`. Without an active discrepancy `C_psi` is diagonal and the
value is `sum_i -(1/2)(log 2pi + 2 log sigma_i + r_i^2/sigma_i^2)`.

- Domain: positive-definite `C_psi` (guaranteed in exact arithmetic when every
  `sigma_i > 0`); a failed factorization returns `-inf`. Cost `O(n^3)`.
- Meaning: `delta` is Gaussian and enters linearly, so it can be integrated
  out exactly; the remaining parameters are `(theta, psi, sigma)`.
- Code: `inverse.DiscrepancyCalibration.log_likelihood`,
  `inverse.DiscrepancyCalibration.marginal_covariance`.
- Source: [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001] and
  [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006, Section 2.2].
  - [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001](../references.md#ref-kennedy-ohagan-2001): Marc C. Kennedy and Anthony O'Hagan. "Bayesian Calibration of Computer Models." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B* 63(3), 425-464, 2001. DOI: [10.1111/1467-9868.00294](https://doi.org/10.1111/1467-9868.00294).
  - [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006](../references.md#ref-rasmussen-williams-2006): Carl Edward Rasmussen and Christopher K. I. Williams. *Gaussian Processes for Machine Learning*. MIT Press, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-KOH-MARG-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-koh-marg-001--marginal-likelihood-with-the-discrepancy-integrated-out)

### EQ-KOH-POST-001 — Posterior of parameters and log hyperparameters

`log p(theta, eta, beta | y) = log L + log pi(theta)
+ sum_k [log p_k(e^eta_k) + eta_k] + sum_j log p_j(beta_j) + const`, with
`eta = (log sigma_delta, log ell)` for free hyperparameters and `beta =
(a, b)` the noise coefficients. In sampling coordinates `theta = T^-1(u)` and
`log pi(theta)` is replaced by `-|u|^2/2`.

- Domain: proper priors; hyperparameter priors on `(0, inf)`.
- Meaning: `+ eta_k` is the Jacobian of the log transform; maximizing gives
  MAP (or, without priors, the MLE) and MCMC samples the joint posterior.
- Code: `inverse.DiscrepancyCalibration.log_prior`,
  `inverse.DiscrepancyCalibration.log_posterior`,
  `inverse.DiscrepancyCalibration.standard_log_posterior`,
  `inverse.DiscrepancyCalibration.fit`,
  `inverse.DiscrepancyCalibration.sample_posterior`.
- Source: [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001], [REF-HIGDON-ETAL-2004], and
  [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013, Section 1.3].
  - [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001](../references.md#ref-kennedy-ohagan-2001): Marc C. Kennedy and Anthony O'Hagan. "Bayesian Calibration of Computer Models." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B* 63(3), 425-464, 2001. DOI: [10.1111/1467-9868.00294](https://doi.org/10.1111/1467-9868.00294).
  - [REF-HIGDON-ETAL-2004](../references.md#ref-higdon-etal-2004): Dave Higdon, Marc Kennedy, James C. Cavendish, John A. Cafeo, and Robert D. Ryne. "Combining Field Data and Computer Simulations for Calibration and Prediction." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 26(2), 448-466, 2004. DOI: [10.1137/S1064827503426693](https://doi.org/10.1137/S1064827503426693).
  - [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-gelman-etal-2013): Andrew Gelman, John B. Carlin, Hal S. Stern, David B. Dunson, Aki Vehtari, and Donald B. Rubin. *Bayesian Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. CRC Press, 2013. ISBN 978-1-4398-4095-5.
- Registry: [EQ-KOH-POST-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-koh-post-001--posterior-of-parameters-and-log-hyperparameters)

### EQ-KOH-PRED-001 — Bias-corrected and simulator-only prediction

Per draw `s`: `m_s = k*' C_psi^-1 r_s`, `v_s = k(x*, x*) - |L^-1 k*|^2`; the
bias-corrected process is `S^-1 sum_s N(eta(c*, theta_s) + m_s, v_s)` with
mean `mean_s(eta_s + m_s)` and variance `Var_s(eta_s + m_s) + mean_s(v_s)`;
the simulator alone is the sample `eta(c*, theta_s)`; a new observation adds
`sigma(c*)^2`. Interval limits invert the mixture CDF by bisection.

- Domain: positive-definite `C_psi` at every draw; marginal (pointwise)
  predictions only.
- Meaning: Gaussian conditioning of `delta(x*)` on the residuals, averaged
  over parameter uncertainty; the gap between the two predictions is the
  estimated discrepancy contribution.
- Code: `inverse.DiscrepancyCalibration.predict`,
  `inverse.DiscrepancyPrediction.interval`.
- Source: [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001] and
  [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006, Section 2.2].
  - [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001](../references.md#ref-kennedy-ohagan-2001): Marc C. Kennedy and Anthony O'Hagan. "Bayesian Calibration of Computer Models." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B* 63(3), 425-464, 2001. DOI: [10.1111/1467-9868.00294](https://doi.org/10.1111/1467-9868.00294).
  - [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006](../references.md#ref-rasmussen-williams-2006): Carl Edward Rasmussen and Christopher K. I. Williams. *Gaussian Processes for Machine Learning*. MIT Press, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-KOH-PRED-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-koh-pred-001--bias-corrected-and-simulator-only-prediction)

### EQ-KOH-HET-001 — Log-linear heteroscedastic noise

`sigma(c) = exp(a + b' g(c))`, with user covariates `g(c)` and unconstrained
coefficients `(a, b)` estimated jointly with `theta`.

- Domain: finite covariates; `b = ()` gives an unknown constant noise level.
- Meaning: multiplicative heteroscedasticity; the log link keeps every
  standard deviation positive.
- Code: `inverse.LogLinearNoise`,
  `inverse.DiscrepancyCalibration.noise_standard_deviations`.
- Source: [REF-HARVEY-1976].
  - [REF-HARVEY-1976](../references.md#ref-harvey-1976): A. C. Harvey. "Estimating Regression Models with Multiplicative Heteroscedasticity." *Econometrica* 44(3), 461-465, 1976. DOI: [10.2307/1913974](https://doi.org/10.2307/1913974).
- Registry: [EQ-KOH-HET-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-koh-het-001--log-linear-heteroscedastic-noise)

### EQ-KOH-IDENT-001 — Theta-discrepancy confounding diagnostics

`rho_jk = Cov(theta_j, eta_k) / (sd(theta_j) sd(eta_k))` from posterior draws
or from the Hessian covariance, and `ratio_j = sd_post(theta_j) /
sd_prior(theta_j)`.

- Domain: at least two posterior draws, or a positive-definite Hessian.
- Meaning: large `|rho|` or a ratio near one signals that the data cannot
  separate `theta` from `delta`; small values do not prove identifiability.
- Code: `inverse.DiscrepancyCalibration.identifiability`,
  `inverse.DiscrepancyFit.correlation`.
- Source: [REF-BRYNJARSDOTTIR-OHAGAN-2014] and [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001].
  - [REF-BRYNJARSDOTTIR-OHAGAN-2014](../references.md#ref-brynjarsdottir-ohagan-2014): Jenný Brynjarsdóttir and Anthony O'Hagan. "Learning about Physical Parameters: The Importance of Model Discrepancy." *Inverse Problems* 30(11), 114007, 2014. DOI: [10.1088/0266-5611/30/11/114007](https://doi.org/10.1088/0266-5611/30/11/114007).
  - [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001](../references.md#ref-kennedy-ohagan-2001): Marc C. Kennedy and Anthony O'Hagan. "Bayesian Calibration of Computer Models." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B* 63(3), 425-464, 2001. DOI: [10.1111/1467-9868.00294](https://doi.org/10.1111/1467-9868.00294).
- Registry: [EQ-KOH-IDENT-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-koh-ident-001--theta-discrepancy-confounding-diagnostics)

## `probstat.inverse.ensemble_extended`

### EQ-INV-ENX-001 — Gaspari-Cohn taper and tapered gain

`rho(d) = -z^5/4 + z^4/2 + 5z^3/8 - 5z^2/3 + 1` for `z = |d|/c <= 1`,
`z^5/12 - z^4/2 + 5z^3/8 + 5z^2/3 - 5z + 4 - 2/(3z)` for `1 < z <= 2`, `0`
beyond; the tapered gain is
`K = (C_xg o rho(D_xg)) (C_gg o rho(D_gg) + alpha Gamma)^-1`, with `o` the
Schur (entrywise) product.

- Domain: half-width `c > 0` finite; caller-supplied non-negative distances
  `D_xg` (`n x m`) and symmetric `D_gg` (`m x m`); `rho(D_gg)` positive
  semi-definite (true for points on a line), otherwise the tapered system may
  fail its Cholesky factorization, which is reported.
- Meaning: damps sample covariances between distant quantities, mostly
  sampling error for small ensembles, at the cost of a bias for genuine
  long-range correlation. A regularization of the sample gain, not a derivation
  of the exact Bayesian update. Equals the plain sample gain when no taper is
  used.
- Code: `inverse.ensemble_extended.gaspari_cohn_taper`.
- Source: [REF-GASPARI-COHN-1999].
  - [REF-GASPARI-COHN-1999](../references.md#ref-gaspari-cohn-1999): Gregory Gaspari and Stephen E. Cohn. "Construction of Correlation Functions in Two and Three Dimensions." *Quarterly Journal of the Royal Meteorological Society* 125(554), 723-757, 1999. The fifth-order piecewise rational function, their Equation (4.10). DOI: [10.1002/qj.49712555417](https://doi.org/10.1002/qj.49712555417).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ENX-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-enx-001--gaspari-cohn-taper-and-tapered-gain)

### EQ-INV-ENX-002 — Localized stochastic ensemble update

`x_j^+ = x_j + K (y + sqrt(alpha) eta_j - g_j)`, `eta_j ~ N(0, Gamma)`, with `K`
of [EQ-INV-ENX-001]; `analysis_mean = x_bar + K (y - g_bar)`.

- Domain: `J >= 2`, `alpha > 0`, `Gamma` SPD, finite entries.
- Meaning: for linear `G`, a Gaussian prior and no taper, `K` converges to the
  exact Kalman gain as `J -> infinity` (sampling error of order `J^-1/2`).
  With a taper it converges to the tapered population gain. The perturbed
  ensemble covariance is that of the stochastic EnKF.
- Code: `inverse.ensemble_extended.localized_ensemble_kalman_update`.
- Source: [REF-EVENSEN-2003], [REF-GASPARI-COHN-1999].
  - [REF-EVENSEN-2003](../references.md#ref-evensen-2003): Geir Evensen. "The Ensemble Kalman Filter: Theoretical Formulation and Practical Implementation." *Ocean Dynamics* 53(4), 343-367, 2003. DOI: [10.1007/s10236-003-0036-9](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10236-003-0036-9).
  - [REF-GASPARI-COHN-1999](../references.md#ref-gaspari-cohn-1999): Gregory Gaspari and Stephen E. Cohn. "Construction of Correlation Functions in Two and Three Dimensions." *Quarterly Journal of the Royal Meteorological Society* 125(554), 723-757, 1999. The fifth-order piecewise rational function, their Equation (4.10). DOI: [10.1002/qj.49712555417](https://doi.org/10.1002/qj.49712555417).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ENX-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-enx-002--localized-stochastic-ensemble-update)

### EQ-INV-ENX-003 — Innovation-based inflation estimator

`lambda_hat = (|L^-1 (y - g_bar)|^2 - m) / tr(Zw Zw')`, clipped to
`[lower, upper]`, where `Gamma = L L'`, `Zw` has columns
`L^-1 (g_j - g_bar)/sqrt(J - 1)`.

- Domain: `J >= 2`, positive spread, `0 < lower <= upper`.
- Meaning: if the true prior prediction covariance is `lambda` times the sample
  one and the innovation is Gaussian with covariance `H P H' + Gamma`, then
  `E |L^-1 d|^2 = lambda tr(Zw Zw') + m` approximately, so `lambda_hat` is a
  moment (ratio) estimator: slightly biased at small `J`, noisy at small `m`.
  Clipping reports the unclipped value and a flag.
- Code: `inverse.ensemble_extended.estimate_inflation`.
- Source: [REF-WANG-BISHOP-2003], [REF-ANDERSON-2007].
  - [REF-WANG-BISHOP-2003](../references.md#ref-wang-bishop-2003): Xuguang Wang and Craig H. Bishop. "A Comparison of Breeding and Ensemble Transform Kalman Filter Ensemble Forecast Schemes." *Journal of the Atmospheric Sciences* 60(9), 1140-1158, 2003. Cited for the innovation-based moment estimate of the ensemble variance inflation factor. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1175/1520-0469(2003)060<1140:ACOBAE>2.0.CO;2](https://doi.org/10.1175/1520-0469(2003)060%3C1140:ACOBAE%3E2.0.CO;2).
  - [REF-ANDERSON-2007](../references.md#ref-anderson-2007): Jeffrey L. Anderson. "An Adaptive Covariance Inflation Error Correction Algorithm for Ensemble Filters." *Tellus A* 59(2), 210-224, 2007. Cited for estimating a multiplicative covariance inflation from the observation-space innovation statistics. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1111/j.1600-0870.2006.00216.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1600-0870.2006.00216.x).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ENX-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-enx-003--innovation-based-inflation-estimator)

### EQ-INV-ENX-004 — Multiplicative anomaly inflation

`x_j -> x_bar + sqrt(lambda) (x_j - x_bar)`, likewise `g_j`.

- Domain: `lambda > 0`.
- Meaning: leaves sample means unchanged and multiplies the sample covariances
  `C_xx`, `C_xg`, `C_gg` by `lambda` exactly. For a nonlinear `G` the scaled
  predictions are not `G` of the scaled members.
- Code: `inverse.ensemble_extended.adaptive_inflation`.
- Source: [REF-ANDERSON-2007].
  - [REF-ANDERSON-2007](../references.md#ref-anderson-2007): Jeffrey L. Anderson. "An Adaptive Covariance Inflation Error Correction Algorithm for Ensemble Filters." *Tellus A* 59(2), 210-224, 2007. Cited for estimating a multiplicative covariance inflation from the observation-space innovation statistics. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1111/j.1600-0870.2006.00216.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1600-0870.2006.00216.x).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ENX-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-enx-004--multiplicative-anomaly-inflation)

### EQ-INV-ENX-005 — ETPF likelihood weights

`w_i = exp(-|L^-1 (y - g_i)|^2 / 2) / sum_k exp(-|L^-1 (y - g_k)|^2 / 2)`.

- Domain: Gaussian observation noise `N(0, Gamma)`, equally weighted prior
  ensemble.
- Meaning: self-normalized importance weights; `1/sum w_i^2` is the effective
  sample size. Computed with the maximum subtracted, so no overflow.
- Code: `inverse.ensemble_extended.ensemble_transform_particle_filter`.
- Source: [REF-REICH-2013].
  - [REF-REICH-2013](../references.md#ref-reich-2013): Sebastian Reich. "A Nonparametric Ensemble Transform Method for Bayesian Inference." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 35(4), A2013-A2024, 2013. Cited for the ensemble transform particle filter: likelihood weights, optimal coupling to the uniform ensemble and the deterministic transform. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/130907367](https://doi.org/10.1137/130907367).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ENX-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-enx-005--etpf-likelihood-weights)

### EQ-INV-ENX-006 — ETPF optimal coupling and transform

`t = argmin sum_ij t_ij |x_i - x_j|^2` subject to `sum_j t_ij = w_i`,
`sum_i t_ij = 1/J`, `t >= 0`; `x_j^+ = J sum_i t_ij x_i`.

- Domain: `J >= 2` particles.
- Meaning: the coupling is solved exactly by the transportation simplex
  (`probstat.information.transport.exact_transport`); the transform maps the
  uniform ensemble to a deterministic one with the weighted mean.
- Code: `inverse.ensemble_extended.ensemble_transform_particle_filter`.
- Source: [REF-REICH-2013], [REF-PEYRE-CUTURI-2019].
  - [REF-REICH-2013](../references.md#ref-reich-2013): Sebastian Reich. "A Nonparametric Ensemble Transform Method for Bayesian Inference." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 35(4), A2013-A2024, 2013. Cited for the ensemble transform particle filter: likelihood weights, optimal coupling to the uniform ensemble and the deterministic transform. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/130907367](https://doi.org/10.1137/130907367).
  - [REF-PEYRE-CUTURI-2019](../references.md#ref-peyre-cuturi-2019): Gabriel Peyré and Marco Cuturi. "Computational Optimal Transport." *Foundations and Trends in Machine Learning* 11(5-6), 355-607, 2019. arXiv: [1803.00567](https://arxiv.org/abs/1803.00567).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ENX-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-enx-006--etpf-optimal-coupling-and-transform)

### EQ-INV-ENX-007 — ETPF mean identity and spread contraction

`(1/J) sum_j x_j^+ = sum_i w_i x_i` and
`(1/J) sum_j (x_j^+ - m)(x_j^+ - m)' <= sum_i w_i (x_i - m)(x_i - m)'`
in the Loewner order, `m = sum_i w_i x_i`.

- Domain: any feasible coupling.
- Meaning: the first identity follows from the marginals. `x_j^+` is the
  conditional mean `E[x_I | J = j]` under `t`, so the law of total variance
  gives the second. The transformed covariance is therefore not the weighted
  covariance and not the posterior covariance at finite `J`. The same record
  states the assumption of the stochastic ensemble Kalman update used for
  comparison: it is exact in the posterior mean and covariance only for linear
  `G` and Gaussian laws, and cannot reproduce a multimodal posterior.
- Code: `inverse.ensemble_extended.ensemble_transform_particle_filter`.
- Source: [REF-REICH-2013].
  - [REF-REICH-2013](../references.md#ref-reich-2013): Sebastian Reich. "A Nonparametric Ensemble Transform Method for Bayesian Inference." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 35(4), A2013-A2024, 2013. Cited for the ensemble transform particle filter: likelihood weights, optimal coupling to the uniform ensemble and the deterministic transform. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/130907367](https://doi.org/10.1137/130907367).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ENX-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-enx-007--etpf-mean-identity-and-spread-contraction)

### EQ-INV-ENX-008 — Quadrature reference for `y = x^2 + e`

`p(x | y) = N(x; mu, sigma^2) N(y; x^2, s^2) / Z`, integrated by the trapezoid
rule on `[mu - w sigma, mu + w sigma]`; mode occupation `P(x > 0 | y)` and valley mass `P(|x| < sqrt(y)/2 | y)`.

- Domain: `sigma, s > 0`, at least 3 nodes, `Z > 0` after scaling.
- Meaning: the grid reference for the sign-symmetric problem, whose posterior
  is bimodal at `+- sqrt(y)` for `y` well above the noise; used to measure
  mode occupation, mean and variance of approximate updates.
- Code: `inverse.ensemble_extended.square_map_reference`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ENX-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-enx-008--quadrature-reference-for-y--x2--e)

## `probstat.inverse.ensemble_inversion`

### EQ-INV-EKI-001 — Ensemble Kalman inversion update

For members `u_j`, `j = 1..N`, in the prior's standard-normal coordinates
with predictions `g_j = G(T^-1(u_j))`, ensemble means `u_bar`, `g_bar` and
`C^up = sum_j (u_j - u_bar)(g_j - g_bar)' / (N - 1)`,
`C^pp = sum_j (g_j - g_bar)(g_j - g_bar)' / (N - 1)`:
`u_j <- u_j + C^up (C^pp + Gamma)^-1 (y + eta_j - g_j)` with
`eta_j ~ N(0, Gamma)` drawn afresh at every iteration (or `eta_j = 0`
without perturbation). In whitened form, with `A` and `B` the deviations of
`u` and of `Gamma^-1/2 g` from their means,
`C^up (C^pp + alpha Gamma)^-1 = A'B (B'B + alpha (N - 1) I)^-1 Gamma^-1/2
= A' (B B' + alpha (N - 1) I)^-1 B Gamma^-1/2`.

- Domain: `N >= 2`; `Gamma` symmetric positive definite (here diagonal with
  entries `sigma_q^2 + sigma_delta_q^2` of known Gaussian noise); `alpha > 0`.
- Meaning: a derivative-free iteration that decreases the data misfit; each
  member stays in the affine span of the initial ensemble and the ensemble
  covariance collapses as the iterations proceed, so the method is an
  optimizer and iterative regularizer, not a posterior sampler. Iglesias,
  Law and Stuart normalize the covariances by `N`; this library uses
  `N - 1` in both, as in the ensemble Kalman filter.
- Code: `inverse.ensemble_kalman_inversion`, `inverse.ensemble_smoother_mda`.
- Source: [REF-IGLESIAS-LAW-STUART-2013, Algorithm 1];
  [REF-SCHILLINGS-STUART-2017] (gradient-flow limit and collapse);
  [REF-EVENSEN-2003] (ensemble gain).
  - [REF-IGLESIAS-LAW-STUART-2013](../references.md#ref-iglesias-law-stuart-2013): Marco A. Iglesias, Kody J. H. Law, and Andrew M. Stuart. "Ensemble Kalman Methods for Inverse Problems." *Inverse Problems* 29(4), 045001, 2013. The iterative ensemble Kalman method with perturbed data `y + eta_j`, `eta_j ~ N(0, Gamma)`, the invariant-subspace property of the ensemble, and its use as a derivative-free optimizer (Algorithm 1, Section 2). DOI: [10.1088/0266-5611/29/4/045001](https://doi.org/10.1088/0266-5611/29/4/045001).
  - [REF-SCHILLINGS-STUART-2017](../references.md#ref-schillings-stuart-2017): Claudia Schillings and Andrew M. Stuart. "Analysis of the Ensemble Kalman Filter for Inverse Problems." *SIAM Journal on Numerical Analysis* 55(3), 1264-1290, 2017. Continuous-time limit of ensemble Kalman inversion as preconditioned gradient flows of the data misfit; ensemble collapse and over-fitting of noisy data for linear forward models (Sections 3 and 4). DOI: [10.1137/16M105959X](https://doi.org/10.1137/16M105959X).
  - [REF-EVENSEN-2003](../references.md#ref-evensen-2003): Geir Evensen. "The Ensemble Kalman Filter: Theoretical Formulation and Practical Implementation." *Ocean Dynamics* 53(4), 343-367, 2003. DOI: [10.1007/s10236-003-0036-9](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10236-003-0036-9).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-EKI-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-eki-001--ensemble-kalman-inversion-update)

### EQ-INV-EKI-002 — Discrepancy-principle stopping of ensemble Kalman inversion

Stop before the first update at which
`|Gamma^-1/2 (y - g_bar_n)| <= tau eta`, where `g_bar_n` is the ensemble
mean of the predictions at iteration `n`, `eta = |Gamma^-1/2 (y - G(u_true))|`
is the noise level, and `tau > 1`.

- Domain: `tau >= 1` (enforced); `eta > 0`. When the noise realization is
  unknown the library uses `eta = sqrt(M)` for `M` observations, the root of
  `E |Gamma^-1/2 eps|^2 = M` for Gaussian errors; the default `tau = 1.5` is
  a library choice.
- Meaning: iterating past the noise level fits the noise of an ill-posed
  problem; stopping there makes the iteration a regularization method. The
  criterion tests the mean of the predictions, not the prediction at the
  mean, so it needs no extra forward run.
- Code: `inverse.ensemble_kalman_inversion(stopping="discrepancy")`,
  `inverse.EnsembleInversionResult.misfit_history`.
- Source: [REF-IGLESIAS-2016, Algorithm 1, Eq. (6)] (criterion on the mean
  prediction, `tau > 1 / rho`); [REF-IGLESIAS-LAW-STUART-2013, Section 2.3]
  (discrepancy principle with `tau > 1`).
  - [REF-IGLESIAS-2016](../references.md#ref-iglesias-2016): Marco A. Iglesias. "A Regularizing Iterative Ensemble Kalman Method for PDE-Constrained Inverse Problems." *Inverse Problems* 32(2), 025002, 2016. Algorithm 1: the discrepancy principle `|Gamma^-1/2 (y - mean_j G(u_j))| <= tau eta` applied to the ensemble mean of the predictions, with a Levenberg-Marquardt choice of the regularization parameter. DOI: [10.1088/0266-5611/32/2/025002](https://doi.org/10.1088/0266-5611/32/2/025002).
  - [REF-IGLESIAS-LAW-STUART-2013](../references.md#ref-iglesias-law-stuart-2013): Marco A. Iglesias, Kody J. H. Law, and Andrew M. Stuart. "Ensemble Kalman Methods for Inverse Problems." *Inverse Problems* 29(4), 045001, 2013. The iterative ensemble Kalman method with perturbed data `y + eta_j`, `eta_j ~ N(0, Gamma)`, the invariant-subspace property of the ensemble, and its use as a derivative-free optimizer (Algorithm 1, Section 2). DOI: [10.1088/0266-5611/29/4/045001](https://doi.org/10.1088/0266-5611/29/4/045001).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-EKI-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-eki-002--discrepancy-principle-stopping-of-ensemble-kalman-inversion)

### EQ-INV-ESMDA-001 — ES-MDA update and inflation condition

For `i = 1..N_a`: `u_j <- u_j + C^up (C^pp + alpha_i Gamma)^-1
(y + sqrt(alpha_i) Gamma^1/2 z_ij - g_j)`, `z_ij ~ N(0, I)`, with the
covariances of [EQ-INV-EKI-001] recomputed from the current ensemble, and
`sum_{i=1}^{N_a} 1 / alpha_i = 1`.

- Domain: `alpha_i > 0` finite; the sum condition is checked to a relative
  tolerance of `1e-9`; Gaussian errors with known `Gamma`.
- Meaning: the product of the tempered likelihoods `L^(1/alpha_i)` is the
  full likelihood, so for a forward model affine in `u` the composition of
  the `N_a` Gaussian conditionings is the exact Bayesian update and the
  ensemble samples the posterior as `N -> infinity`; `N_a = 1`, `alpha = 1`
  is the one-step ensemble smoother. For nonlinear models it is an
  approximation.
- Code: `inverse.ensemble_smoother_mda`.
- Source: [REF-EMERICK-REYNOLDS-2013].
  - [REF-EMERICK-REYNOLDS-2013](../references.md#ref-emerick-reynolds-2013): Alexandre A. Emerick and Albert C. Reynolds. "Ensemble Smoother with Multiple Data Assimilation." *Computers & Geosciences* 55, 3-15, 2013. Repeated assimilation of the same data with inflated error covariances `alpha_i C_D`, `sum 1/alpha_i = 1`, exact for linear-Gaussian problems in the large-ensemble limit. DOI: [10.1016/j.cageo.2012.03.011](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cageo.2012.03.011).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ESMDA-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-esmda-001--es-mda-update-and-inflation-condition)

## `probstat.inverse.error_correlation`

### EQ-CAL-CORR-001 — Stationary correlation of measurement errors

`M_ij = rho(|c_i - c_j| / ell)` over a numeric experimental condition `c`,
with `rho(h) = exp(-h)` (exponential, Matern 1/2),
`rho(h) = (1 + sqrt(3) h) exp(-sqrt(3) h)` (Matern 3/2), or
`rho(h) = (1 + sqrt(5) h + 5 h^2 / 3) exp(-sqrt(5) h)` (Matern 5/2).

- Domain: `ell > 0`; distinct condition values, or a positive discrepancy
  variance (nugget), for a positive-definite `M`.
- Meaning: errors of one quantity measured under nearby conditions (times,
  loads, positions) are correlated, with correlation decaying over the
  length `ell`; `rho(0) = 1`, so `sigma_q` stays the marginal standard
  deviation.
- Code: `inverse.error_correlation_kernel`,
  `inverse.ErrorCorrelation.correlation_matrix`.
- Source: [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006, Section 4.2] (Matern class with
  `nu = 1/2, 3/2, 5/2`).
  - [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006](../references.md#ref-rasmussen-williams-2006): Carl Edward Rasmussen and Christopher K. I. Williams. *Gaussian Processes for Machine Learning*. MIT Press, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-CAL-CORR-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-cal-corr-001--stationary-correlation-of-measurement-errors)

## `probstat.inverse.flexible_hybrid`

### EQ-INV-FLEX-001 — Flexible Golub-Kahan relation

`B Z_k = U_(k+1) T_k`, `Z_k = [M_1 v_1, ..., M_k v_k]`, `v_k` from `B' u_k`
orthogonalised against `v_1..v_(k-1)`, `u_(k+1) t_(.,k) = B z_k` orthogonalised
against `u_1..u_k`; `U_(k+1)` and `V_k` have orthonormal columns, `Z_k` does not,
and `T_k` (size `(k+1) x k`) is upper Hessenberg.

- Domain: `B = W A` (whitened), `M_j` positive diagonal (`M_j = diag(w^(-1/2))`,
  `w` the IRLS weights of the previous iterate; unit weights at `j = 1`).
- Meaning: with `M_j = I` the process is Golub-Kahan and `T_k` is bidiagonal up
  to rounding (tested to agree with `hybrid_lsqr` iterates to about 1e-10). A
  changing `M_j` makes the search space `range(Z_k)` depend on the iterates, so
  the method is not a Krylov method in the classical sense. Full
  reorthogonalisation (two passes) is used; memory is `O(k (m + n))` plus the
  stored columns `L z_j`.
- Code: `inverse.flexible_hybrid.flexible_hybrid_lsqr`.
- Source: [REF-CHUNG-GAZZOLA-2019], [REF-GOLUB-KAHAN-1965].
  - [REF-CHUNG-GAZZOLA-2019](../references.md#ref-chung-gazzola-2019): Julianne Chung and Silvia Gazzola. "Flexible Krylov Methods for lp Regularization." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 41(5), S149-S171, 2019. Cited for the flexible Golub-Kahan process with an iteration-dependent preconditioner built from IRLS weights and the projected Tikhonov problem with a QR-reduced penalty. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/18M1194456](https://doi.org/10.1137/18M1194456).
  - [REF-GOLUB-KAHAN-1965](../references.md#ref-golub-kahan-1965): Gene H. Golub and William Kahan. "Calculating the Singular Values and Pseudo-Inverse of a Matrix." *Journal of the Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, Series B: Numerical Analysis* 2(2), 205-224, 1965. Cited for the bidiagonalization `A V_k = U_(k+1) B_k`. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/0702016](https://doi.org/10.1137/0702016).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-FLEX-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-flex-001--flexible-golub-kahan-relation)

### EQ-INV-FLEX-002 — Projected reweighted Tikhonov problem

`q_k = argmin_q ||T_k q - beta e_1||^2 + lambda_k^2 ||S_k L Z_k q||^2`,
`S_k = diag(sqrt(w^(k)))`, `w_i^(k) = 1 / sqrt((L z^(k-1))_i^2 + eps^2)`,
`S_k L Z_k = Q_k R_k` (QR), so the penalty is `||R_k q||`; `x_k = x_ref + Z_k q_k`.

- Domain: `beta = ||W (y - A x_ref)||`; `L` with columns `L z_j` computed once
  per step and re-weighted by `S_k`; `lambda_k` by projected discrepancy,
  projected (weighted) GCV on this small problem, or fixed.
- Meaning: one reweighting step of IRLS restricted to a `k`-dimensional
  subspace. The projected residual `||T_k q_k - beta e_1||` equals the whitened
  data misfit `||W (A x_k - y)||` because `U_(k+1)` is orthonormal. The
  projected GCV is a criterion of the projected problem, not full-problem GCV.
- Code: `inverse.flexible_hybrid.flexible_hybrid_lsqr`.
- Source: [REF-CHUNG-GAZZOLA-2019], [REF-CHUNG-NAGY-OLEARY-2008].
  - [REF-CHUNG-GAZZOLA-2019](../references.md#ref-chung-gazzola-2019): Julianne Chung and Silvia Gazzola. "Flexible Krylov Methods for lp Regularization." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 41(5), S149-S171, 2019. Cited for the flexible Golub-Kahan process with an iteration-dependent preconditioner built from IRLS weights and the projected Tikhonov problem with a QR-reduced penalty. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/18M1194456](https://doi.org/10.1137/18M1194456).
  - [REF-CHUNG-NAGY-OLEARY-2008](../references.md#ref-chung-nagy-oleary-2008): Julianne Chung, James G. Nagy, and Dianne P. O'Leary. "A Weighted-GCV Method for Lanczos-Hybrid Regularization." *Electronic Transactions on Numerical Analysis* 28, 149-167, 2007-2008. ISSN 1068-9613. Cited for the weighted GCV function of the projected Tikhonov problem and the adaptive choice of the weight (Sections 5.1-5.4). The journal assigns no DOI; metadata read from the journal's article page and PDF on 2026-11-26: <https://etna.ricam.oeaw.ac.at/volumes/2001-2010/vol28/abstract.php?vol=28&pages=149-167>.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-FLEX-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-flex-002--projected-reweighted-tikhonov-problem)

### EQ-INV-FLEX-003 — True penalised objective of the flexible iterates

`J_k = 1/2 ||W (A x_k - y)||^2 + lambda_k^2 ||L (x_k - x_ref)||_1`; the smoothed
version replaces `|t|` by `sqrt(t^2 + eps^2)`; for `penalty = "none"` the
quadratic `1/2 ||.||^2 + lambda_k^2/2 ||L (x_k - x_ref)||^2` is used.

- Domain: `alpha = lambda_k^2` is the coefficient of
  `irls_regularized_least_squares`, whose objective is `J` with a constant
  `lambda`; with an adaptive rule `lambda_k` changes, so `J_k` values at
  different `k` belong to different functionals.
- Meaning: the iteration is not guaranteed to decrease `J_k` (each step
  minimises a majorizer over a subspace with weights frozen at the previous
  iterate and a new basis column); the history is reported, never assumed
  monotone, and the gap to the IRLS minimiser is measured, not claimed.
- Code: `inverse.flexible_hybrid.flexible_hybrid_lsqr`.
- Source: [REF-CHUNG-GAZZOLA-2019], [REF-RODRIGUEZ-WOHLBERG-2009].
  - [REF-CHUNG-GAZZOLA-2019](../references.md#ref-chung-gazzola-2019): Julianne Chung and Silvia Gazzola. "Flexible Krylov Methods for lp Regularization." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 41(5), S149-S171, 2019. Cited for the flexible Golub-Kahan process with an iteration-dependent preconditioner built from IRLS weights and the projected Tikhonov problem with a QR-reduced penalty. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/18M1194456](https://doi.org/10.1137/18M1194456).
  - [REF-RODRIGUEZ-WOHLBERG-2009](../references.md#ref-rodriguez-wohlberg-2009): Paul Rodriguez and Brendt Wohlberg. "Efficient Minimization Method for a Generalized Total Variation Functional." *IEEE Transactions on Image Processing* 18(2), 322-332, 2009. Cited for the iteratively reweighted norm method with a smoothed absolute value for L1 and total-variation penalties. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1109/TIP.2008.2008420](https://doi.org/10.1109/TIP.2008.2008420).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-FLEX-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-flex-003--true-penalised-objective-of-the-flexible-iterates)

## `probstat.inverse.function_space`

### EQ-INV-FS-001 — Basis expansion of a field

`f(s) = sum_{j=1}^n x_j phi_j(s)`, `s in [0, L]`; point evaluation `f(s_i) = (Phi x)_i` with `Phi_ij = phi_j(s_i)`.

- Domain: `L > 0`; `phi_j` piecewise constant on `n` equal cells, nodal hats at the `n` interior nodes `j L/(n+1)` (zero Dirichlet ends), or `sqrt(2/L) sin(j pi s / L)`; evaluation points in `[0, L]`.
- Meaning: coefficients `x` are the unknowns of a discretized inverse problem; they are not field values unless the basis is nodal (hats). Hat fields vanish at `0` and `L`.
- Code: `inverse.function_space.SineBasis`.
- Source: [REF-STUART-2010].
  - [REF-STUART-2010](../references.md#ref-stuart-2010): Andrew M. Stuart. "Inverse Problems: A Bayesian Perspective." *Acta Numerica* 19, 451-559, 2010. Cited for the Gaussian linear inverse problem, its posterior mean and covariance, function-space priors, and weighted inner products. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/S0962492910000061](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0962492910000061).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-FS-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-fs-001--basis-expansion-of-a-field)

### EQ-INV-FS-002 — Exact mass (Gram) matrices

`M_ij = int_0^L phi_i phi_j ds`: piecewise constant `h I` (`h = L/n`); hats `h/6 * tridiag(1, 4, 1)` (`h = L/(n+1)`); sine `I`. Then `||f||_{L2}^2 = x' M x`.

- Domain: bases as in EQ-INV-FS-001.
- Meaning: converts coefficient norms to `L2` norms; for non-orthonormal bases the discrete `l2` norm of `x` is not the `L2` norm of `f`.
- Code: `inverse.function_space.HatBasis`.
- Source: [REF-STUART-2010], [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-STUART-2010](../references.md#ref-stuart-2010): Andrew M. Stuart. "Inverse Problems: A Bayesian Perspective." *Acta Numerica* 19, 451-559, 2010. Cited for the Gaussian linear inverse problem, its posterior mean and covariance, function-space priors, and weighted inner products. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/S0962492910000061](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0962492910000061).
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-FS-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-fs-002--exact-mass-gram-matrices)

### EQ-INV-FS-003 — Observation operator by composite Gauss-Legendre quadrature

`A_ij = int_0^L K_i(s) phi_j(s) ds ~= sum_p sum_{q=1}^5 w_q (b_p - a_p)/2 * K_i(s_pq) phi_j(s_pq)`, with five-point Gauss-Legendre nodes `x_q` on each panel `[a_p, b_p]`, `s_pq = (a_p+b_p)/2 + (b_p-a_p) x_q / 2`.

- Domain: kernels continuous on panels; the panel grid is independent of the basis mesh.
- Meaning: exact for polynomial integrands of degree <= 9 per panel; with a kink or jump of `phi_j` inside a panel the error is only convergent in the panel count. The nodes `0, +-sqrt(5 -+ 2 sqrt(10/7))/3` and weights `128/225, (322 +- 13 sqrt 70)/900` are the closed-form five-point rule.
- Code: `inverse.function_space.SineBasis.integral_operator`.
- Source: [REF-GOLUB-WELSCH-1969], [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984].
  - [REF-GOLUB-WELSCH-1969](../references.md#ref-golub-welsch-1969): Gene H. Golub and John H. Welsch. "Calculation of Gauss Quadrature Rules." *Mathematics of Computation* 23(106), 221-230, 1969.
  - [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984](../references.md#ref-davis-rabinowitz-1984): Philip J. Davis and Philip Rabinowitz. *Methods of Numerical Integration*. 2nd edition. Academic Press, 1984. ISBN 978-0-12-206360-2.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-FS-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-fs-003--observation-operator-by-composite-gauss-legendre-quadrature)

### EQ-INV-FS-004 — Spectral Gaussian prior, eigenpairs and coefficient variances

`C = a^2 (kappa^2 - Delta)^(-s)` with Dirichlet conditions on `[0, L]`; eigenfunctions `phi_k`, eigenvalues `lambda_k = a^2 (kappa^2 + (k pi/L)^2)^(-s)`; coefficients `x_k ~ N(0, lambda_k)` independent.

- Domain: `a > 0`, `kappa >= 0`, `s > 1/2`.
- Meaning: the variance of mode `k` depends only on `k`, not on the number of modes retained, so discretizations of different size represent the same prior. Larger `s` gives smoother fields.
- Code: `inverse.function_space.SpectralGaussianPrior`.
- Source: [REF-STUART-2010], [REF-COTTER-ROBERTS-STUART-WHITE-2013].
  - [REF-STUART-2010](../references.md#ref-stuart-2010): Andrew M. Stuart. "Inverse Problems: A Bayesian Perspective." *Acta Numerica* 19, 451-559, 2010. Cited for the Gaussian linear inverse problem, its posterior mean and covariance, function-space priors, and weighted inner products. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/S0962492910000061](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0962492910000061).
  - [REF-COTTER-ROBERTS-STUART-WHITE-2013](../references.md#ref-cotter-roberts-stuart-white-2013): Simon L. Cotter, Gareth O. Roberts, Andrew M. Stuart, and David White. "MCMC Methods for Functions: Modifying Old Algorithms to Make Them Faster." *Statistical Science* 28(3), 424-446, 2013. The preconditioned Crank-Nicolson proposal. DOI: [10.1214/13-STS421](https://doi.org/10.1214/13-STS421).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-FS-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-fs-004--spectral-gaussian-prior-eigenpairs-and-coefficient-variances)

### EQ-INV-FS-005 — Trace and truncation tail bound

`tr C = sum_k lambda_k < infinity` iff `s > 1/2`; `sum_{k>N} lambda_k <= a^2 (L/pi)^(2s) N^(1-2s) / (2s-1)`.

- Domain: `s > 1/2`, `N >= 1`.
- Meaning: tail sum bounded by `int_N^inf a^2 (pi x/L)^(-2s) dx` because `lambda_k` decreases in `k` and `kappa^2 >= 0`. It bounds the expected squared `L2` truncation error and, multiplied by `2/L`, the pointwise variance error. It is a statement about the prior, not about the infinite-dimensional posterior.
- Code: `inverse.function_space.SpectralGaussianPrior.remaining_trace_bound`.
- Source: [REF-STUART-2010].
  - [REF-STUART-2010](../references.md#ref-stuart-2010): Andrew M. Stuart. "Inverse Problems: A Bayesian Perspective." *Acta Numerica* 19, 451-559, 2010. Cited for the Gaussian linear inverse problem, its posterior mean and covariance, function-space priors, and weighted inner products. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/S0962492910000061](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0962492910000061).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-FS-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-fs-005--trace-and-truncation-tail-bound)

### EQ-INV-FS-006 — Karhunen-Loeve draws and pointwise variance

`f(s) = sum_{k<=N} sqrt(lambda_k) xi_k phi_k(s)`, `xi_k ~ N(0, 1)` iid; `Var f(s) = sum_{k<=N} lambda_k phi_k(s)^2`.

- Domain: `s in [0, L]`.
- Meaning: samples of the truncated prior; the variance vanishes at the Dirichlet ends. Truncation error of the variance is at most `(2/L) sum_{k>N} lambda_k`.
- Code: `inverse.function_space.SpectralGaussianPrior.sample`.
- Source: [REF-STUART-2010], [REF-COTTER-ROBERTS-STUART-WHITE-2013].
  - [REF-STUART-2010](../references.md#ref-stuart-2010): Andrew M. Stuart. "Inverse Problems: A Bayesian Perspective." *Acta Numerica* 19, 451-559, 2010. Cited for the Gaussian linear inverse problem, its posterior mean and covariance, function-space priors, and weighted inner products. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/S0962492910000061](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0962492910000061).
  - [REF-COTTER-ROBERTS-STUART-WHITE-2013](../references.md#ref-cotter-roberts-stuart-white-2013): Simon L. Cotter, Gareth O. Roberts, Andrew M. Stuart, and David White. "MCMC Methods for Functions: Modifying Old Algorithms to Make Them Faster." *Statistical Science* 28(3), 424-446, 2013. The preconditioned Crank-Nicolson proposal. DOI: [10.1214/13-STS421](https://doi.org/10.1214/13-STS421).

### EQ-INV-FS-007 — Empirical pointwise and simultaneous credible bands

Pointwise: sample quantiles at `(1 -+ level)/2`. Simultaneous: `mean_i +- c sd_i` with `c` the empirical `level` quantile of `T_j = max_i |f_ji - mean_i| / sd_i` over samples `j`.

- Domain: `N >= 2` samples on a common grid with `sd_i > 0` at every grid point; `level in (0, 1)`.
- Meaning: finite-sample empirical bands; `c` carries Monte Carlo error, reported as an approximate order-statistic interval. The simultaneous band covers the sampled grid only, not the continuum.
- Code: `inverse.function_space.credible_bands`.
- Source: [REF-STUART-2010], [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-STUART-2010](../references.md#ref-stuart-2010): Andrew M. Stuart. "Inverse Problems: A Bayesian Perspective." *Acta Numerica* 19, 451-559, 2010. Cited for the Gaussian linear inverse problem, its posterior mean and covariance, function-space priors, and weighted inner products. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
