## `probstat.inverse.function_space_extended`

### EQ-INV-LIS-001 — Averaged prior-preconditioned Gauss-Newton Hessian

`H = sum_k w_k K_k' K_k`, `K_k = W J_k P`, `H w = lambda w`; equivalently `J' Gamma^-1 J v = lambda C0^-1 v` with `v = P w`, where `C0 = P P'`, `W' W = Gamma^-1`, `J_k` is the Jacobian at the point `x_k` and `w_k >= 0`, `sum w_k = 1`.

- Domain: finite dimensions, `P` square invertible for the generalized form; the whitened form needs no inverse of `C0`.
- Meaning: `lambda` is the ratio of data-induced to prior precision along the whitened direction `w`. For a nonlinear model the average is a Gauss-Newton Hessian over the supplied points (posterior samples or a fixed design); it neglects the second-order misfit term and depends on the points, so it is not a global statement.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_extended.likelihood_informed_subspace`.
- Source: [REF-CUI-MARTIN-MARZOUK-SOLONEN-SPANTINI-2014], [REF-SPANTINI-ETAL-2015].
  - [REF-CUI-MARTIN-MARZOUK-SOLONEN-SPANTINI-2014](../references.md#ref-cui-martin-marzouk-solonen-spantini-2014): Tiangang Cui, James Martin, Youssef M. Marzouk, Antti Solonen and Alessio Spantini. Likelihood-informed dimension reduction for nonlinear inverse problems. *Inverse Problems*, 30(11):114015, 2014. Cited for the likelihood-informed subspace defined by the averaged Gauss-Newton Hessian against the prior and for the projected-likelihood posterior approximation. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1088/0266-5611/30/11/114015](https://doi.org/10.1088/0266-5611/30/11/114015).
  - [REF-SPANTINI-ETAL-2015](../references.md#ref-spantini-etal-2015): Alessio Spantini, Antti Solonen, Tiangang Cui, James Martin, Luis Tenorio, and Youssef Marzouk. "Optimal Low-Rank Approximations of Bayesian Linear Inverse Problems." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 37(6), A2451-A2487, 2015. Cited for the update `C_post = P (I - V diag(lambda / (1 + lambda)) V') P'` in prior-whitened coordinates. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/140977308](https://doi.org/10.1137/140977308).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-001--averaged-prior-preconditioned-gauss-newton-hessian)

### EQ-INV-LIS-002 — Eigenvalue-threshold rank and informed rank

`r_LIS = #{i : lambda_i > tau}` (optionally capped by `r_max`), `r_info = #{i : lambda_i > 1e-12 lambda_1}`.

- Domain: `tau > 0` finite.
- Meaning: the threshold is explicit and user-chosen; `r_info` is the numerically data-informed rank (equals the rank of `K` for one linear Jacobian). The sample spread `sqrt(sum_k w_k ||H_k - H||_F^2) / ||H||_F` and its division by `sqrt(N)` quantify the point-to-point variability; the second is a standard error only for independent points.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_extended.likelihood_informed_subspace`, `inverse.function_space_extended.nonlinear_lis`.
- Source: [REF-CUI-MARTIN-MARZOUK-SOLONEN-SPANTINI-2014].
  - [REF-CUI-MARTIN-MARZOUK-SOLONEN-SPANTINI-2014](../references.md#ref-cui-martin-marzouk-solonen-spantini-2014): Tiangang Cui, James Martin, Youssef M. Marzouk, Antti Solonen and Alessio Spantini. Likelihood-informed dimension reduction for nonlinear inverse problems. *Inverse Problems*, 30(11):114015, 2014. Cited for the likelihood-informed subspace defined by the averaged Gauss-Newton Hessian against the prior and for the projected-likelihood posterior approximation. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1088/0266-5611/30/11/114015](https://doi.org/10.1088/0266-5611/30/11/114015).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-002--eigenvalue-threshold-rank-and-informed-rank)

### EQ-INV-LIS-003 — LIS-projected linear-Gaussian posterior

`Pi = W_r W_r'`, posterior of `z` with likelihood `L(Pi z)`: precision `I + Pi H Pi`, mean `W_r (I + W_r' H W_r)^-1 W_r' K' W (y - A mu)`, covariance `I - W_r (I - (I + W_r' H W_r)^-1) W_r'`, and `x = mu + P z`.

- Domain: `W_r` has orthonormal columns (checked); `H = K'K` for the linear model.
- Meaning: exact for the restricted likelihood; the prior is retained in the complement of the subspace. It equals the exact posterior when `range(W_r)` contains `range(K')`, in particular when the rank equals the data-informed rank, and the low-rank posterior of the leading eigenspace of `H`.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_extended.lis_linear_posterior`.
- Source: [REF-CUI-MARTIN-MARZOUK-SOLONEN-SPANTINI-2014], [REF-SPANTINI-ETAL-2015].
  - [REF-CUI-MARTIN-MARZOUK-SOLONEN-SPANTINI-2014](../references.md#ref-cui-martin-marzouk-solonen-spantini-2014): Tiangang Cui, James Martin, Youssef M. Marzouk, Antti Solonen and Alessio Spantini. "Optimal Low-Rank Approximations of Bayesian Linear Inverse Problems." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 37(6), A2451-A2487, 2015. ### EQ-INV-LIS-004 — Exact error of the LIS projection

Exact `Sigma = (I + H)^-1`, projected `Sigma_r`; `2 KL(N_exact || N_r) = tr(S_r^-1 S) - r + dm' S_r^-1 dm + log det S_r - log det S` with `S_r^-1 = I + Pi H Pi`. For `W_r` the leading `k` eigenvectors of `H`: `Sigma - Sigma_r = -sum_{i>k} lambda_i/(1 + lambda_i) v_i v_i'`, `||.||_F^2 = sum_{i>k} (lambda_i/(1 + lambda_i))^2`, `||.||_2 = lambda_{k+1}/(1 + lambda_{k+1})`, `tr(Sigma_r - Sigma) = sum_{i>k} lambda_i/(1 + lambda_i)`, `2 KL = sum_{i>k} [log(1 + lambda_i) - lambda_i/(1 + lambda_i)] + sum_{i>k} (v_i' b/(1 + lambda_i))^2` with `b = K' W (y - A mu)`.

- Domain: dense whitened dimension `r` (refused above `max_dimension`).
- Meaning: the error is computed densely against the exact posterior and, for the eigen-subspace, equals the omitted-spectrum formulas, which are reported as `omitted_spectrum_trace` and `omitted_spectrum_bound`; for another subspace they are reference values only.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_extended.lis_projection_error`.
- Source: [REF-SPANTINI-ETAL-2015], [REF-CUI-MARTIN-MARZOUK-SOLONEN-SPANTINI-2014].
  - [REF-SPANTINI-ETAL-2015](../references.md#ref-spantini-etal-2015): Alessio Spantini, Antti Solonen, Tiangang Cui, James Martin, Luis Tenorio, and Youssef Marzouk. "Optimal Low-Rank Approximations of Bayesian Linear Inverse Problems." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 37(6), A2451-A2487, 2015. Likelihood-informed dimension reduction for nonlinear inverse problems. *Inverse Problems*, 30(11):114015, 2014. ### EQ-INV-LIS-005 — Resolution operator by the data-space formula

`R = (G' Gamma^-1 G + C^-1)^-1 G' Gamma^-1 G = C G' S^-1 G`, `S = G C G' + Gamma`.

- Domain: `S` symmetric positive definite (Cholesky; no pseudo-inverse).
- Meaning: `x_hat - mu = R (x - mu) + noise` for the posterior-mean estimator; `tr R` is the effective number of resolved coefficients. The second form does not invert `C`, so singular priors are allowed.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_extended.resolution_operator`.
- Source: [REF-TARANTOLA-2005], [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-005--resolution-operator-by-the-data-space-formula)

### EQ-INV-LIS-006 — Averaging kernel in function space with the mass matrix

`f = sum_j x_j phi_j`, `M_ij = int phi_i phi_j`, functional `l(f) = l' x`: `a = M^-1 R' l`, `l(f_hat - f_0) = int a (f - f_0) ds` for all `f` in the span, `int a ds = m' a = l' R c` with `m_j = int phi_j`, `c = M^-1 m`, kernel surface `a(s, s') = phi(s)' R M^-1 phi(s')`.

- Domain: `M` symmetric positive definite; point functional `l_j = phi_j(s0)`, local average `l_j = int w phi_j`.
- Meaning: `a` is the Riesz representer of the estimated functional inside the span of the basis; the mass matrix converts coefficient-space `R` to an `L2` kernel and cannot be omitted for non-orthonormal bases. The kernel integrates to the functional of the constant `l' c` exactly when `R c = c` (the method resolves constants); `constant_defect = ||R c - c||_M / ||c||_M` measures the failure.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_extended.averaging_kernel`, `inverse.function_space_extended.resolution_kernel_surface`, `inverse.function_space_extended.point_functional`, `inverse.function_space_extended.local_average_functional`.
- Source: [REF-TARANTOLA-2005], [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984].
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
  - [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984](../references.md#ref-davis-rabinowitz-1984): Philip J. Davis and Philip Rabinowitz. *Methods of Numerical Integration*. 2nd edition. Academic Press, 1984. ISBN 978-0-12-206360-2.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-006--averaging-kernel-in-function-space-with-the-mass-matrix)

### EQ-INV-LIS-007 — Averaging-kernel width measures

`centroid = int s |a| / int |a|`, `width = sqrt(int (s - centroid)^2 |a| / int |a|)`, Backus-Gilbert spread `12 int (s - s0)^2 a^2 ds / (int a ds)^2`, negative mass `int a^- / int |a|`.

- Domain: `int a ds != 0` for the spread.
- Meaning: lengths in the units of `s`; a box kernel of width `w` centred at `s0` has spread `w`, an off-centre box adds `12 d^2 / w`. Integrals use composite Gauss-Legendre panels aligned with the basis cells; they are exact for aligned piecewise bases with sign-definite kernels and converge with the panel count otherwise.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_extended.averaging_kernel`.
- Source: [REF-TARANTOLA-2005].
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-007--averaging-kernel-width-measures)

### EQ-INV-LIS-008 — Finite-difference precision of the Whittle-Matern prior

`Q = (h / a^2) L_h^alpha`, `L_h = kappa^2 I - D2`, `D2 = (1/h^2) tridiag(1, -2, 1)`; Dirichlet: `n` nodes `s_i = i h`, `h = L/(n+1)`; Neumann: `n` cells, `s_i = (i - 1/2) h`, `h = L/n`, first and last diagonal of `D2` equal to `-1/h^2` (mirror ghost cell); `alpha in {1, 2}`.

- Domain: `kappa > 0`, `a > 0`, `n >= 4`. `Q` is symmetric positive definite with half bandwidth `alpha`.
- Meaning: discretizes the precision of `N(0, a^2 (kappa^2 - Laplacian)^-alpha)` with the stated boundary condition; `Q^-1 = a^2 L_h^-alpha / h` approximates the covariance function (the factor `1/h` converts the discrete Green function to a density), so the same continuum prior is represented at every `n` with error `O(h^2)`.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_extended.WhittleMaternPrior1D`.
- Source: [REF-LINDGREN-RUE-LINDSTROM-2011].
  - [REF-LINDGREN-RUE-LINDSTROM-2011](../references.md#ref-lindgren-rue-lindstrom-2011): Finn Lindgren, Havard Rue and Johan Lindstrom. An explicit link between Gaussian fields and Gaussian Markov random fields: the stochastic partial differential equation approach. *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B*, 73(4):423-498, 2011. Cited for the Whittle-Matern field as the solution of a fractional SPDE `(kappa^2 - Laplacian)^(alpha/2) u = white noise` and its sparse precision discretization. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1111/j.1467-9868.2011.00777.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1467-9868.2011.00777.x).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-008--finite-difference-precision-of-the-whittle-matern-prior)

### EQ-INV-LIS-009 — Banded Cholesky sampling and factor operator

`Q = L L'` (banded, `O(n alpha^2)` work), `u = L^-T xi`, `xi ~ N(0, I)` has covariance `Q^-1`; `P = L^-T` satisfies `P P' = Q^-1`; `log det Q = 2 sum log L_ii`.

- Domain: `Q` symmetric positive definite; a non-positive pivot raises, no jitter is added.
- Meaning: samples and linear-Gaussian updates use only banded triangular solves and never a dense `n x n` covariance; `prior_factor()` returns the operator `P` for the exact solvers.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_extended.WhittleMaternPrior1D`.
- Source: [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013], [REF-LINDGREN-RUE-LINDSTROM-2011].
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
  - [REF-LINDGREN-RUE-LINDSTROM-2011](../references.md#ref-lindgren-rue-lindstrom-2011): Finn Lindgren, Havard Rue and Johan Lindstrom. An explicit link between Gaussian fields and Gaussian Markov random fields: the stochastic partial differential equation approach. *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B*, 73(4):423-498, 2011. Cited for the Whittle-Matern field as the solution of a fractional SPDE `(kappa^2 - Laplacian)^(alpha/2) u = white noise` and its sparse precision discretization. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1111/j.1467-9868.2011.00777.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1467-9868.2011.00777.x).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-009](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-009--banded-cholesky-sampling-and-factor-operator)

### EQ-INV-LIS-010 — Matern covariance and its method of images

`C_1(r) = a^2 e^{-kappa r}/(2 kappa)`, `C_2(r) = a^2 (1 + kappa r) e^{-kappa r}/(4 kappa^3)`; with a boundary condition on `[0, L]`: `C_BC(s, t) = sum_{k in Z} [C(s - t + 2 k L) +- C(s + t + 2 k L)]` (`+` Neumann, `-` Dirichlet).

- Domain: `alpha in {1, 2}`; the image sum is truncated when the terms fall below `1e-18 C(0)`.
- Meaning: `C_alpha` is the Matern covariance of smoothness `alpha - 1/2` (exponential and `3/2`); the image sum is the covariance of `(kappa^2 - Laplacian_BC)^-alpha`, valid for any power because the operator commutes with reflection. The cosine spectral covariance `sum_k a^2 (kappa^2 + (k pi/L)^2)^-alpha phi_k(s) phi_k(t)` converges to the Neumann case, with the omitted modes contributing at most `2 a^2 L / (pi^2 (n-1))` for `alpha = 1`.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_extended.matern_covariance`, `inverse.function_space_extended.WhittleMaternPrior1D`, `inverse.function_space_extended.cosine_spectral_prior`.
- Source: [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006], [REF-LINDGREN-RUE-LINDSTROM-2011].
  - [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006](../references.md#ref-rasmussen-williams-2006): Carl Edward Rasmussen and Christopher K. I. Williams. *Gaussian Processes for Machine Learning*. MIT Press, 2006.
  - [REF-LINDGREN-RUE-LINDSTROM-2011](../references.md#ref-lindgren-rue-lindstrom-2011): Finn Lindgren, Havard Rue and Johan Lindstrom. An explicit link between Gaussian fields and Gaussian Markov random fields: the stochastic partial differential equation approach. *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B*, 73(4):423-498, 2011. Cited for the Whittle-Matern field as the solution of a fractional SPDE `(kappa^2 - Laplacian)^(alpha/2) u = white noise` and its sparse precision discretization. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1111/j.1467-9868.2011.00777.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1467-9868.2011.00777.x).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-010](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-010--matern-covariance-and-its-method-of-images)

### EQ-INV-LIS-011 — Measured boundary effect

Variance ratio `Var_h(s_i)/C(0)`, decay distance `max{ min(s_i, L - s_i) : |Var_h(s_i)/C(0) - 1| > tol }`, column errors `max_j |C_h(s_c, s_j) - C(s_c - s_j)|/C(0)` and discretization error `max_j |C_h(s_c, s_j) - C_BC(s_c, s_j)|/C(0)`.

- Domain: `0 < tol < 1`.
- Meaning: separates the boundary effect (difference between `C_BC` and the whole-line `C`: variance ratio `1 +- e^{-2 kappa d}` at distance `d` from the boundary for `alpha = 1`, so a decay distance `ln(1/tol)/(2 kappa)`) from the `O(h^2)` discretization error. `interior_reached = False` reports a domain too short for a stationary interior.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_extended.WhittleMaternPrior1D`, `inverse.function_space_extended.BoundaryEffect`.
- Source: [REF-LINDGREN-RUE-LINDSTROM-2011].
  - [REF-LINDGREN-RUE-LINDSTROM-2011](../references.md#ref-lindgren-rue-lindstrom-2011): Finn Lindgren, Havard Rue and Johan Lindstrom. An explicit link between Gaussian fields and Gaussian Markov random fields: the stochastic partial differential equation approach. *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B*, 73(4):423-498, 2011. Cited for the Whittle-Matern field as the solution of a fractional SPDE `(kappa^2 - Laplacian)^(alpha/2) u = white noise` and its sparse precision discretization. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1111/j.1467-9868.2011.00777.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1467-9868.2011.00777.x).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-011](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-011--measured-boundary-effect)

### EQ-INV-LIS-012 — Cosine basis and its Gram matrix

`phi_0 = 1/sqrt(L)`, `phi_k = sqrt(2/L) cos(k pi s/L)`, `k = 1..n-1`; `M = I`.

- Domain: `s in [0, L]`.
- Meaning: Neumann eigenfunctions of the Laplacian; the constants lie in the span, so averaging kernels in this basis integrate to the functional of the constant when the method resolves constants. With the eigenvalues `kappa^2 + (k pi/L)^2` they give the Neumann spectral prior.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_extended.CosineBasis`, `inverse.function_space_extended.cosine_spectral_prior`.
- Source: [REF-STUART-2010].
  - [REF-STUART-2010](../references.md#ref-stuart-2010): Andrew M. Stuart. "Inverse Problems: A Bayesian Perspective." *Acta Numerica* 19, 451-559, 2010. Cited for the Gaussian linear inverse problem, its posterior mean and covariance, function-space priors, and weighted inner products. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/S0962492910000061](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0962492910000061).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-012](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-012--cosine-basis-and-its-gram-matrix)

### EQ-INV-LIS-013 — Shifted Legendre basis and its Gram matrix

`phi_k(s) = P_k(2 s/L - 1)` (optionally times `sqrt((2k+1)/L)`), `k = 0..n-1`, `(k+1) P_{k+1}(t) = (2k+1) t P_k(t) - k P_{k-1}(t)`; `M = diag(L/(2k+1))`, or `I` when normalized.

- Domain: `s in [0, L]`.
- Meaning: global polynomial basis with an exact diagonal mass matrix; the constant is `phi_0`, and the normalized point values grow like `sqrt(2k+1)` at the ends.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_extended.ShiftedLegendreBasis`.
- Source: [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984].
  - [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984](../references.md#ref-davis-rabinowitz-1984): Philip J. Davis and Philip Rabinowitz. *Methods of Numerical Integration*. 2nd edition. Academic Press, 1984. ISBN 978-0-12-206360-2.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-013](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-013--shifted-legendre-basis-and-its-gram-matrix)

## `probstat.inverse.function_space_matrix_free`

### EQ-INV-LIS-014 — Matrix-free prior-preconditioned Hessian action

`H z = P' ( sum_k w_k J_k' Gamma^-1 J_k ) P z`, with `C0 = P P'`, Jacobians `J_k` (`m x n`), noise covariance `Gamma` and weights `w_k >= 0`, `sum_k w_k = 1`; one application costs one `P`, one `P'`, one `Gamma^-1` and one forward/transpose pair of each `J_k` with `w_k > 0`.

- Domain: `J_k` given as operators or as Jacobian-vector / vector-Jacobian callback pairs (the adjoint identity is the caller's responsibility, `adjoint_test`); `Gamma` a covariance factor; `H` is symmetric positive semi-definite and equals `sum_k w_k K_k' K_k` with `K_k = W J_k P`, `W' W = Gamma^-1`.
- Meaning: the same operator the dense LIS diagonalizes, applied without forming any `r x r` matrix; operator counts of the Jacobians and of `P` are reported by `matrix_free_lis`.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_matrix_free.matrix_free_lis`.
- Source: [REF-CUI-MARTIN-MARZOUK-SOLONEN-SPANTINI-2014], [REF-SPANTINI-ETAL-2015].
  - [REF-CUI-MARTIN-MARZOUK-SOLONEN-SPANTINI-2014](../references.md#ref-cui-martin-marzouk-solonen-spantini-2014): Tiangang Cui, James Martin, Youssef M. Marzouk, Antti Solonen and Alessio Spantini. Likelihood-informed dimension reduction for nonlinear inverse problems. *Inverse Problems*, 30(11):114015, 2014. Cited for the likelihood-informed subspace defined by the averaged Gauss-Newton Hessian against the prior and for the projected-likelihood posterior approximation. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1088/0266-5611/30/11/114015](https://doi.org/10.1088/0266-5611/30/11/114015).
  - [REF-SPANTINI-ETAL-2015](../references.md#ref-spantini-etal-2015): Alessio Spantini, Antti Solonen, Tiangang Cui, James Martin, Luis Tenorio, and Youssef Marzouk. "Optimal Low-Rank Approximations of Bayesian Linear Inverse Problems." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 37(6), A2451-A2487, 2015. Cited for the update `C_post = P (I - V diag(lambda / (1 + lambda)) V') P'` in prior-whitened coordinates. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/140977308](https://doi.org/10.1137/140977308).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-014](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-014--matrix-free-prior-preconditioned-hessian-action)

### EQ-INV-LIS-015 — Eigenpair residual bound and the bracketed rank rule

`theta_i = y_i' H y_i`, `rho_i = ||H y_i - theta_i y_i||`, `min_j |lambda_j - theta_i| <= rho_i`; rank `= min(#{theta_i > tau}, max_rank)`; decided iff (threshold bracketed or invariant subspace or `s = r` or cap met) and `max_{i <= rank} rho_i <= eps theta_1` and no `|theta_i - tau| <= rho_i`.

- Domain: `y_i` unit vectors from Lanczos (`s` steps) or a randomized Rayleigh-Ritz block (`s + p` columns, `q` power passes), `H` symmetric; the subspace size `s` doubles until the rule is decided or `max_size` is reached (`converged = False`, `stop_reason = "max_steps"`).
- Meaning: the residual bound is a theorem for symmetric `H` (the interval contains an eigenvalue) but does not certify that the eigenvalue is a leading one, and it does not count multiplicity: an eigenvalue absent from the start vectors is not found, and a single-start Lanczos run is blind to multiplicity in exact arithmetic. The rank from an unconverged run must not be used as is.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_matrix_free.matrix_free_lis`.
- Source: [REF-LANCZOS-1950], [REF-HALKO-MARTINSSON-TROPP-2011], [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013].
  - [REF-LANCZOS-1950](../references.md#ref-lanczos-1950): Cornelius Lanczos. "An Iteration Method for the Solution of the Eigenvalue Problem of Linear Differential and Integral Operators." *Journal of Research of the National Bureau of Standards* 45(4), 255-282, 1950. Cited for the symmetric Lanczos tridiagonalization. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26; Crossref lists the first page only). DOI: [10.6028/jres.045.026](https://doi.org/10.6028/jres.045.026).
  - [REF-HALKO-MARTINSSON-TROPP-2011](../references.md#ref-halko-martinsson-tropp-2011): Nathan Halko, Per-Gunnar Martinsson, and Joel A. Tropp. "Finding Structure with Randomness: Probabilistic Algorithms for Constructing Approximate Matrix Decompositions." *SIAM Review* 53(2), 217-288, 2011. Cited for the randomized range finder with oversampling and power iterations and the resulting truncated SVD. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/090771806](https://doi.org/10.1137/090771806).
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-015](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-015--eigenpair-residual-bound-and-the-bracketed-rank-rule)

### EQ-INV-LIS-016 — Principal angles from sines and cosines

`E = B - (B A') A`, `sin theta_i = sigma_i(E)` (ascending), `cos theta_i = sigma_i(B A')` (descending, zero padded to the number of rows of `B`), `theta_i = atan2(sin theta_i, cos theta_i)`.

- Domain: rows of `A` (`ka x r`) and `B` (`kb x r`) orthonormal; angles of the span of `B` against the span of `A`; with `ka = kb` they are the principal angles, directions of `B` outside `span(A)` count as `pi/2`.
- Meaning: the sine route keeps full relative accuracy for tiny angles where `acos` of a cosine keeps only half the digits, and `atan2` keeps absolute accuracy near `pi/2`; used to compare the matrix-free subspace with the dense one.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_matrix_free.subspace_angles`.
- Source: [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013].
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-016](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-016--principal-angles-from-sines-and-cosines)

### EQ-INV-LIS-017 — Resolution columns and rows by one posterior-covariance solve

`R = C G' S^-1 G = P (I + H)^-1 P' G' Gamma^-1 G`, `R x = P z`, `(I + H) z = P' G' Gamma^-1 G x`; `R' l = G' Gamma^-1 G P z`, `(I + H) z = P' l`; here `H = P' G' Gamma^-1 G P` and `Sigma = P (I + H)^-1 P'` is the posterior covariance.

- Domain: `C = P P'` (`P` is `n x r`), `Gamma` a covariance factor, `I + H` symmetric positive definite with spectrum in `[1, 1 + ||H||]`; solved by conjugate gradients to a relative residual tolerance; the identity `C G' S^-1 = Sigma G' Gamma^-1` holds for every factor `P`.
- Meaning: one column or row of the resolution operator costs one conjugate-gradient solve; each solve is recorded (`iterations`, `stop_reason`, true relative residual) and a solve that misses its tolerance is counted in `nonconverged_count`, never hidden.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_matrix_free.MatrixFreeResolution`.
- Source: [REF-TARANTOLA-2005], [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007], [REF-HESTENES-STIEFEL-1952].
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
  - [REF-HESTENES-STIEFEL-1952](../references.md#ref-hestenes-stiefel-1952): Magnus R. Hestenes and Eduard Stiefel. "Methods of Conjugate Gradients for Solving Linear Systems." *Journal of Research of the National Bureau of Standards* 49(6), 409-436, 1952. Cited for the conjugate gradient method and its least-squares variant CGLS. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26; Crossref lists the first page only). DOI: [10.6028/jres.049.044](https://doi.org/10.6028/jres.049.044).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-017](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-017--resolution-columns-and-rows-by-one-posterior-covariance-solve)

### EQ-INV-LIS-018 — Mass-weighted averaging kernel from operator actions

`a = M^-1 R' l`, `int a = m' a`, target `l' c` with `c = M^-1 m`; centroid `sum_j x_j |a_j| / sum_j |a_j|` and width `sqrt(sum_j (x_j - centroid)^2 |a_j| / sum_j |a_j|)` over supplied coefficient positions.

- Domain: `M` the symmetric positive definite mass matrix of the basis given as an operator (identity if omitted), `m_j = int phi_j`, `l` the coefficient vector of a point or local-average functional; `M^-1` applied by conjugate gradients with recorded outcome.
- Meaning: the kernel of the dense `averaging_kernel` without the dense `R`; the centroid and width use the coefficient positions and are meaningful only for nodal bases (they are not the quadrature measures of the dense routine).
- Code: `inverse.function_space_matrix_free.probe_resolution`.
- Source: [REF-TARANTOLA-2005].
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-018](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-018--mass-weighted-averaging-kernel-from-operator-actions)

### EQ-INV-LIS-019 — Degrees of freedom of the resolution operator

`tr R = tr((I + H)^-1 H) = sum_i lambda_i / (1 + lambda_i)`, estimated by `(1/s) sum_k z_k' (I + H)^-1 H z_k` with Rademacher `z_k`, standard error `sd / sqrt(s)`.

- Domain: `lambda_i` the eigenvalues of `H`; the first equality is the cyclic property of the trace (`R = P (I+H)^-1 P' G' Gamma^-1 G`); the whitened operator is symmetric, so the Hutchinson variance is `2 (||T||_F^2 - sum T_ii^2) / s`, zero for a multiple of the identity; probing `R` itself (`trace_method = "coefficient"`) is unbiased but not symmetric.
- Meaning: the effective number of resolved degrees of freedom with a Monte-Carlo error that is reported; each probe costs one conjugate-gradient solve.
- Code: `inverse.function_space_matrix_free.probe_resolution`.
- Source: [REF-HUTCHINSON-1989], [REF-TARANTOLA-2005].
  - [REF-HUTCHINSON-1989](../references.md#ref-hutchinson-1989): M. F. Hutchinson. "A Stochastic Estimator of the Trace of the Influence Matrix for Laplacian Smoothing Splines." *Communications in Statistics - Simulation and Computation* 18(3), 1059-1076, 1989. Cited for the Rademacher-probe trace estimator `tr(H) ~= (1/s) sum_k z_k'H z_k`. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1080/03610918908812806](https://doi.org/10.1080/03610918908812806).
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIS-019](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lis-019--degrees-of-freedom-of-the-resolution-operator)

## `probstat.inverse.gauss_newton`

### EQ-INV-GN-001 — Differentiable forward contract and derivative checks

`G: R^n -> R^m`, `jvp(x, v) = J(x) v`, `vjp(x, w) = J(x)' w`; Taylor remainder `r(h) = ||G(x + h v) - G(x) - h J(x) v|| = O(h^2)` and adjoint dot test `w'(J v) = (J'w)'v`.

- Domain: `G` continuously differentiable near `x`; real finite `x`, `v`, `w`.
- Meaning: `r(h)` decays with observed order 2 until roundoff (about `eps ||G||`) for an exact JVP and order 1 for a wrong one; the dot test verifies that `vjp` is the Euclidean adjoint of `jvp`. The `passed` flag of `check_derivatives` is a heuristic, not a proof, and degrades near non-smooth points.
- Code: `inverse.gauss_newton.check_derivatives`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-GN-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-gn-001--differentiable-forward-contract-and-derivative-checks)

### EQ-INV-GN-002 — Central-difference JVP and dense finite-difference VJP

`J(x) v ~ (G(x + e v) - G(x - e v)) / (2 e)` with `e = s (1 + ||x||_2) / ||v||_2`, `s = eps^(1/3)` by default; `J'w` by assembling the `n` columns `J(x) e_j` and forming `(J'w)_j = w' (J e_j)`.

- Domain: `v != 0`; the dense VJP costs `2 n` forward evaluations and requires `allow_dense_fd_vjp=True`.
- Meaning: error `O(e^2)` truncation plus `O(eps ||G|| / e)` roundoff; the mode is chosen by the user, never a silent fallback from analytic derivatives.
- Code: `inverse.gauss_newton.CallbackForward`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-GN-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-gn-002--central-difference-jvp-and-dense-finite-difference-vjp)

### EQ-INV-GN-003 — Penalized nonlinear least-squares objective and gradient

`Phi(x) = 1/2 ||W (G(x) - y)||^2 + lambda^2/2 ||L (x - x_ref)||^2`, `grad Phi = J' W'W r + lambda^2 L'L (x - x_ref)` with `r = G(x) - y`, `W'W = Gamma^-1`.

- Domain: `lambda >= 0`; `Gamma` symmetric positive definite (whitening from a `probstat.linalg` covariance factor); `L` a linear operator with `n` columns.
- Meaning: the MAP/Tikhonov objective in physical coordinates; `x_ref` is explicit (default zero) and is not the starting point; no transformed-coordinate Jacobian terms are involved.
- Code: `inverse.gauss_newton.gauss_newton_krylov`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006]; [REF-TIKHONOV-1963].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
  - [REF-TIKHONOV-1963](../references.md#ref-tikhonov-1963): Andrei N. Tikhonov. "On the Solution of Ill-Posed Problems and the Method of Regularization." *Doklady Akademii Nauk SSSR* 151(3), 501-504, 1963 (in Russian); English translation "Solution of Incorrectly Formulated Problems and the Regularization Method," *Soviet Mathematics Doklady* 4, 1035-1038, 1963. The penalized least-squares regularization named after him.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-GN-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-gn-003--penalized-nonlinear-least-squares-objective-and-gradient)

### EQ-INV-GN-004 — Gauss-Newton step as a stacked linearized least-squares problem

`p = argmin_p || [W J; lambda L] p + [W r; lambda L (x - x_ref)] ||_2`, equivalently `(J'W'W J + lambda^2 L'L) p = - grad Phi`.

- Domain: the stacked matrix has full column rank when `lambda > 0` and `L` has trivial null space; otherwise the Krylov iterate from zero is the minimum-norm least-squares solution within the iteration count.
- Meaning: solved by LSQR or CGLS using only `jvp` and `vjp`; neither `J` nor the Hessian is formed. The Gauss-Newton Hessian omits the term `sum_i (W r)_i W grad^2 G_i`.
- Code: `inverse.gauss_newton.gauss_newton_krylov`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006]; [REF-PAIGE-SAUNDERS-1982].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
  - [REF-PAIGE-SAUNDERS-1982](../references.md#ref-paige-saunders-1982): Christopher C. Paige and Michael A. Saunders. "LSQR: An Algorithm for Sparse Linear Equations and Sparse Least Squares." *ACM Transactions on Mathematical Software* 8(1), 43-71, 1982. Cited for the Golub-Kahan bidiagonalization recurrence, the plane-rotation update of LSQR and its residual and normal-residual estimates. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1145/355984.355989](https://doi.org/10.1145/355984.355989).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-GN-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-gn-004--gauss-newton-step-as-a-stacked-linearized-least-squares-problem)

### EQ-INV-GN-005 — Armijo backtracking on the full objective

Accept the first `t in {1, f, f^2, ...}` with `Phi(x + t p) <= Phi(x) + c t grad Phi' p`, `0 < c < 1`, `0 < f < 1`, plus a roundoff slack `4 eps |Phi|`.

- Domain: `grad Phi' p < 0` (descent); otherwise the iteration stops with `line_search_failure`.
- Meaning: globalization on the penalized objective including the penalty; trials with non-finite forward values are rejected (recorded as `nan`), never clipped.
- Code: `inverse.gauss_newton.gauss_newton_krylov`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-GN-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-gn-005--armijo-backtracking-on-the-full-objective)

### EQ-INV-GN-006 — Discrepancy stopping rule

Stop when `||W (G(x_k) - y)|| <= tau delta`.

- Domain: `delta` is the noise level in the whitened norm, `tau >= 1` chosen by the caller (`tau > 1` for the classical theory).
- Meaning: Morozov's principle applied to the nonlinear residual; for an ill-posed `G` the level, not the objective decrease, is the regularization.
- Code: `inverse.gauss_newton.iteratively_regularized_gauss_newton`.
- Source: [REF-MOROZOV-1966]; [REF-ENGL-HANKE-NEUBAUER-1996].
  - [REF-MOROZOV-1966](../references.md#ref-morozov-1966): Vladimir A. Morozov. "On the Solution of Functional Equations by the Method of Regularization." *Doklady Akademii Nauk SSSR* 167(3), 510-512, 1966 (in Russian). The choice of the regularization parameter from the noise level, known as the discrepancy principle.
  - [REF-ENGL-HANKE-NEUBAUER-1996](../references.md#ref-engl-hanke-neubauer-1996): Heinz W. Engl, Martin Hanke, and Andreas Neubauer. *Regularization of Inverse Problems*. Kluwer Academic Publishers (now Springer Netherlands), Dordrecht, 1996. Cited for iterative regularization, the discrepancy principle as a stopping rule, and Landweber's semi-convergence. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/978-94-009-1740-8](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-94-009-1740-8).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-GN-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-gn-006--discrepancy-stopping-rule)

### EQ-INV-GN-007 — Iteratively regularized Gauss-Newton (IRGNM)

`x_(k+1) = x_k + p_k`, `p_k = argmin_p ||W (G(x_k) + J_k p - y)||^2 + lambda_k^2 ||L (x_k + p - x_ref)||^2`, `lambda_(k+1) = q lambda_k`, `0 < q < 1`.

- Domain: `lambda_0 > 0`; convergence theory needs a tangential-cone-type condition on `G` and moderate `q`, which the code does not verify.
- Meaning: full steps without line search, penalty centred at `x_ref` (not `x_k`), regularization decreasing with the iteration and stopped by [EQ-INV-GN-006]. Different from fixed-`lambda` Gauss-Newton ([EQ-INV-GN-004]/[EQ-INV-GN-005]) and from Levenberg-Marquardt damping.
- Code: `inverse.gauss_newton.iteratively_regularized_gauss_newton`.
- Source: [REF-ENGL-HANKE-NEUBAUER-1996].
  - [REF-ENGL-HANKE-NEUBAUER-1996](../references.md#ref-engl-hanke-neubauer-1996): Heinz W. Engl, Martin Hanke, and Andreas Neubauer. *Regularization of Inverse Problems*. Kluwer Academic Publishers (now Springer Netherlands), Dordrecht, 1996. Cited for iterative regularization, the discrepancy principle as a stopping rule, and Landweber's semi-convergence. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/978-94-009-1740-8](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-94-009-1740-8).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-GN-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-gn-007--iteratively-regularized-gauss-newton-irgnm)

### EQ-INV-GN-008 — Inexact (forcing-term) inner tolerance

`eta_k = eta` (constant) or `eta_k = min(1/2, max(eta, sqrt(||g_k|| / ||g_0||)))` (adaptive), used as the LSQR `atol = btol` or the CGLS relative normal-residual tolerance.

- Domain: `eta > 0`.
- Meaning: loosens the linearized solves far from the solution and tightens them as the gradient falls; the two Krylov solvers measure `eta` differently (see their stopping tests).
- Code: `inverse.gauss_newton.gauss_newton_krylov`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-GN-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-gn-008--inexact-forcing-term-inner-tolerance)

## `probstat.inverse.heat_models`

### EQ-INV-HEAT-001 — Discrete heat equation and its one-step maps

`u_t = kappa u_ss`, `u(0) = u(L) = 0`; `h = L/(n+1)`,
`D = (kappa/h^2) tridiag(-1, 2, -1)`, `dt = T/N`; implicit Euler
`(I + dt D) u^(k+1) = u^k`, Crank-Nicolson
`(I + dt D/2) u^(k+1) = (I - dt D/2) u^k`; in both cases
`u^(k+1) = S u^k` with `S = M^-1 B`, `M = I + theta dt D`, `theta in {1, 1/2}`.

- Domain: `n >= 2` interior nodes, `kappa, T > 0`, `N >= 1`.
- Meaning: the forward map from the initial state to the state at time `T` is
  `S^N`; implicit Euler is first order and Crank-Nicolson second order in `dt`
  (observed orders in `tests/test_heat_models.py`). `M` is tridiagonal and
  factorized once; each step costs one `O(n)` solve.
- Code: `inverse.heat_models.HeatEquation1D`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007], [REF-HANSEN-2008].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-HEAT-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-heat-001--discrete-heat-equation-and-its-one-step-maps)

### EQ-INV-HEAT-002 — Exact discrete adjoint of the time stepping

`A = sum_k H_k S^k` (initial-state unknown); `A' y = a_0` with
`a_K = H_K' y`, `a_k = H_k' y + S' a_(k+1)`, `S' = B M^-1` (`M`, `B`
symmetric).

- Domain: as EQ-INV-HEAT-001.
- Meaning: `rmatvec` is the Euclidean transpose of the discrete forward map to
  rounding (dot-product test relative error below `1e-12`), not a discretized
  continuous adjoint. Cost: `K` tridiagonal solves, `O(N n)`; no dense matrix.
- Code: `inverse.heat_models.HeatEquation1D`.
- Source: [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013], [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-HEAT-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-heat-002--exact-discrete-adjoint-of-the-time-stepping)

### EQ-INV-HEAT-003 — Closed-form singular values of the final-time map

`mu_k = (4 kappa/h^2) sin^2(k pi h / (2 L))`, `sigma_k = |g(mu_k dt)|^N`,
`g(x) = 1/(1 + x)` (implicit Euler) or `(1 - x/2)/(1 + x/2)` (Crank-Nicolson).

- Domain: final-time observation of all `n` nodes.
- Meaning: the orthonormal sine vectors diagonalize `D`, hence `S^N`; the
  singular values decay like `exp(-kappa pi^2 k^2 T / L^2)` (severe
  ill-posedness of the backward heat problem) up to the scheme error. For
  Crank-Nicolson `|g| -> 1` for stiff modes, so the values are sorted rather
  than assumed monotone in `k`.
- Code: `inverse.heat_models.heat_semigroup_singular_values`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008], [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013].
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-HEAT-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-heat-003--closed-form-singular-values-of-the-final-time-map)

### EQ-INV-HEAT-004 — Space-time linear observation operator

`y_(t,s) = sum_j w_j(s) [(1 - theta) u^k_j + theta u^(k+1)_j]`, with
`t = (k + theta) dt` and `w(s)` the two hat-function weights of `s`
(boundary nodes carry the value zero).

- Domain: sensors in `(0, L)`, times in `[0, T]`.
- Meaning: the interpolation is part of the discrete model; its transpose
  scatters the weights back to the nodes of steps `k` and `k+1`, so the adjoint
  stays exact. Rows are time-major (all sensors at the first time, ...).
  Evaluation stops at the last step needed.
- Code: `inverse.heat_models.HeatEquation1D`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-HEAT-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-heat-004--space-time-linear-observation-operator)

### EQ-INV-HEAT-005 — Time-constant source operator

`M u^k = B u^(k-1) + dt q`, `u^0 = 0`, i.e. `u^k = S u^(k-1) + dt M^-1 q`;
`A q = sum_k H_k u^k`, `A' y = dt M^-1 sum_(k=1..K) a_k`.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-HEAT-001; the source `q` is constant in time.
- Meaning: linear counterpart of coefficient inversion (which is nonlinear).
  For a sine source the final state is `(1 - g^N)/mu` times the sine mode for
  both schemes (verified in the tests); the adjoint sums the adjoint states
  and spends one extra solve, so `rmatvec` costs `K` solves as `matvec` does.
- Code: `inverse.heat_models.HeatSource1D`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007], [REF-HANSEN-2008].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-HEAT-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-heat-005--time-constant-source-operator)

### EQ-INV-HEAT-006 — Work counts

One `matvec` and one `rmatvec` of a final-time operator each cost exactly `N`
tridiagonal solves (`HeatSolveCounts`).

- Domain: any configuration; sensor observations cost the index of the last
  needed step.
- Meaning: reported work for iterative solvers; solves are counted, assembly
  and factorization (once, `O(n)`) are not.
- Code: `inverse.heat_models.HeatSolveCounts`.
- Source: [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013].
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-HEAT-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-heat-006--work-counts)

### EQ-INV-HEAT-007 — Unconditional stability and the Crank-Nicolson caveat

`|g(x)| <= 1` for all `x >= 0`, with `g_IE(x) = 1/(1+x)` and
`g_CN(x) = (1 - x/2)/(1 + x/2)`; `g_CN(x) -> -1` as `x -> infinity`.

- Domain: `x = mu dt`, `mu` an eigenvalue of `D`.
- Meaning: both schemes are contractive for any `dt` (no CFL restriction; the
  explicit Euler method would need `dt <= h^2/(2 kappa)` and is not offered).
  Implicit Euler also preserves non-negativity (`M^-1 >= 0` entrywise);
  Crank-Nicolson does not, and produces sign-alternating oscillations for
  nonsmooth data when `mu dt >> 1`.
- Code: `inverse.heat_models.heat_amplification`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008]. Elementary algebra; checked in
  `tests/test_heat_models.py`.
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-HEAT-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-heat-007--unconditional-stability-and-the-crank-nicolson-caveat)

## `probstat.inverse.interacting_langevin`

### EQ-INV-ALDI-001 — Affine-invariant interacting Langevin dynamics

For members `u_j`, `j = 1..J`, in `R^d` with mean `m`, covariance
`C = sum_k (u_k - m)(u_k - m)' / J` and potential
`Phi(u) = |Gamma^-1/2 (y - G(u))|^2 / 2 + |u|^2 / 2`:
`du_j = -C grad Phi(u_j) dt + ((d + 1)/J)(u_j - m) dt + sqrt(2) C^1/2 dW_j`,
with `C^1/2 = (u_1 - m, ..., u_J - m) / sqrt(J)` a `d x J` matrix and `W_j`
independent `J`-dimensional Brownian motions. The correction is the
divergence `div_{u_j} C = ((d + 1)/J)(u_j - m)`.

- Domain: `J >= d + 2` (non-singular `C`); prior `N(0, I)` in `u`; `Gamma`
  symmetric positive definite (here diagonal, known Gaussian noise).
- Meaning: the noise of member `j` has covariance `2 C dt` and is
  independent across members, so the probability flux of member `j` in the
  Fokker-Planck equation of the ensemble is
  `rho (-C grad_j Phi + div_j C) - div_j (C rho) = -rho C grad_j Phi - C grad_j rho`,
  which vanishes at `rho = prod_j exp(-Phi(u_j))`: the product of posteriors
  is invariant at every finite ensemble size. The dynamics is invariant
  under affine changes of `u`, so its relaxation rate does not depend on the
  conditioning of the posterior.
- Code: `inverse.interacting_langevin_sampler`.
- Source: [REF-GARBUNO-INIGO-NUSKEN-REICH-2020] (corrected dynamics);
  [REF-GARBUNO-INIGO-ETAL-2020] (dynamics without the correction). The flux
  argument is rederived in ADR-0130.
  - [REF-GARBUNO-INIGO-NUSKEN-REICH-2020](../references.md#ref-garbuno-inigo-nusken-reich-2020): Alfredo Garbuno-Inigo, Nikolas Nüsken, and Sebastian Reich. "Affine Invariant Interacting Langevin Dynamics for Bayesian Inference." *SIAM Journal on Applied Dynamical Systems*, 2020. The finite-ensemble correction that makes the product of posteriors invariant, and the gradient-free formulation. [arXiv:1912.02859](https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.02859).
  - [REF-GARBUNO-INIGO-ETAL-2020](../references.md#ref-garbuno-inigo-etal-2020): Alfredo Garbuno-Inigo, Franca Hoffmann, Wuchen Li, and Andrew M. Stuart. "Interacting Langevin Diffusions: Gradient Structure and Ensemble Kalman Sampler." *SIAM Journal on Applied Dynamical Systems* 19(1), 412-441, 2020. The covariance-preconditioned interacting Langevin diffusion and its derivative-free ensemble approximation (the ensemble Kalman sampler). [arXiv:1903.08866](https://arxiv.org/abs/1903.08866).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ALDI-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-aldi-001--affine-invariant-interacting-langevin-dynamics)

### EQ-INV-ALDI-002 — Gradient-free drift of the interacting Langevin sampler

With `g_k = G(u_k)`, mean `g_bar` and
`C^ug = sum_k (u_k - m)(g_k - g_bar)' / J`:
`C grad Phi(u_j) ~ C^ug Gamma^-1 (g_j - y) + C u_j`, i.e. the data part is
`(1/J) sum_k <g_k - g_bar, g_j - y>_Gamma (u_k - m)` and the prior part
`C u_j` is exact.

- Domain: as [EQ-INV-ALDI-001].
- Meaning: for an affine map `G(u) = A u + b`, `C^ug = C A'` and the
  expression equals `C A' Gamma^-1 (g_j - y) + C u_j = C grad Phi(u_j)`
  without error, so the invariance of [EQ-INV-ALDI-001] carries over. For a
  nonlinear map it is a statistical linearization and the stationary law is
  an approximation of the posterior.
- Code: `inverse.interacting_langevin_sampler`.
- Source: [REF-GARBUNO-INIGO-ETAL-2020] and
  [REF-GARBUNO-INIGO-NUSKEN-REICH-2020].
  - [REF-GARBUNO-INIGO-ETAL-2020](../references.md#ref-garbuno-inigo-etal-2020): Alfredo Garbuno-Inigo, Franca Hoffmann, Wuchen Li, and Andrew M. Stuart. "Interacting Langevin Diffusions: Gradient Structure and Ensemble Kalman Sampler." *SIAM Journal on Applied Dynamical Systems* 19(1), 412-441, 2020. The covariance-preconditioned interacting Langevin diffusion and its derivative-free ensemble approximation (the ensemble Kalman sampler). [arXiv:1903.08866](https://arxiv.org/abs/1903.08866).
  - [REF-GARBUNO-INIGO-NUSKEN-REICH-2020](../references.md#ref-garbuno-inigo-nusken-reich-2020): Alfredo Garbuno-Inigo, Nikolas Nüsken, and Sebastian Reich. "Affine Invariant Interacting Langevin Dynamics for Bayesian Inference." *SIAM Journal on Applied Dynamical Systems*, 2020. The finite-ensemble correction that makes the product of posteriors invariant, and the gradient-free formulation. [arXiv:1912.02859](https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.02859).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ALDI-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-aldi-002--gradient-free-drift-of-the-interacting-langevin-sampler)

### EQ-INV-ALDI-003 — Euler-Maruyama step, step rule and time averages

`u_j <- u_j - h_n [C^ug Gamma^-1 (g_j - y) + C u_j - kappa (u_j - m)]
+ sqrt(2 h_n / J) sum_k (u_k - m) xi_jk`, `xi_jk ~ N(0, 1)`,
`kappa = (d + 1)/J` (or 0 without the correction). Adaptive step
`h_n = eta / (tr C + tr C^gg)` with
`C^gg = sum_k Gamma^-1/2 (g_k - g_bar)(g_k - g_bar)' Gamma^-1/2 / J`.
Estimates: `E[f] ~ sum_n h_n (1/J) sum_j f(u_j^n) / sum_n h_n` over the
ensembles after the burn-in.

- Domain: `0 < eta < 2`, or a fixed `h > 0`.
- Meaning: for an affine map the drift matrix is `C (I + A' Gamma^-1 A)`,
  whose eigenvalues are positive with sum `tr C + tr C^gg`, so
  `h_n lambda_max <= eta` and the deterministic part of the step is stable;
  in stationarity `h_n ~ eta / d`. A heuristic, not an error-controlled
  integrator: the discrete chain has an `O(h)` bias, and because `h_n`
  depends on the state the averages are weighted by `h_n`.
- Code: `inverse.interacting_langevin_sampler`,
  `inverse.InteractingLangevinResult`.
- Source: library discretization (ADR-0130) of the dynamics of
  [REF-GARBUNO-INIGO-NUSKEN-REICH-2020]; [REF-GARBUNO-INIGO-ETAL-2020] use
  an adaptive step inversely proportional to a norm of the drift.
  - [REF-GARBUNO-INIGO-NUSKEN-REICH-2020](../references.md#ref-garbuno-inigo-nusken-reich-2020): Alfredo Garbuno-Inigo, Nikolas Nüsken, and Sebastian Reich. "Affine Invariant Interacting Langevin Dynamics for Bayesian Inference." *SIAM Journal on Applied Dynamical Systems*, 2020. The finite-ensemble correction that makes the product of posteriors invariant, and the gradient-free formulation. [arXiv:1912.02859](https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.02859).
  - [REF-GARBUNO-INIGO-ETAL-2020](../references.md#ref-garbuno-inigo-etal-2020): Alfredo Garbuno-Inigo, Franca Hoffmann, Wuchen Li, and Andrew M. Stuart. "Interacting Langevin Diffusions: Gradient Structure and Ensemble Kalman Sampler." *SIAM Journal on Applied Dynamical Systems* 19(1), 412-441, 2020. The covariance-preconditioned interacting Langevin diffusion and its derivative-free ensemble approximation (the ensemble Kalman sampler). [arXiv:1903.08866](https://arxiv.org/abs/1903.08866).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ALDI-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-aldi-003--euler-maruyama-step-step-rule-and-time-averages)

### EQ-INV-ALDI-004 — Covariance deficit without the finite-ensemble correction

Without the term `((d + 1)/J)(u_j - m)` and for a Gaussian posterior
`N(mu, Sigma)` the invariant density of the ensemble is
`prod_j pi(u_j) det(C)^(-(d + 1)/2)`, so `J C ~ Wishart(Sigma, J - d - 2)`,
`m ~ N(mu, Sigma / J)` independently, `E[C] = ((J - d - 2)/J) Sigma`, and the
pooled covariance of the members is `((J - d - 1)/J) Sigma`.

- Domain: affine forward map; `J > 2 d + 1` for a normalizable density.
- Meaning: with `rho = prod_j pi(u_j) phi(U)` the flux of
  [EQ-INV-ALDI-001] without the correction is
  `-prod pi (C grad_j phi + phi div_j C)`, which vanishes for
  `grad_j log phi = -((d + 1)/J) C^-1 (u_j - m) = -((d + 1)/2) grad_j log det C`.
  The uncorrected ensemble Kalman sampler therefore underestimates the
  posterior covariance, by half for `J = 6`, `d = 2`; the deficit vanishes
  as `J -> infinity`.
- Code: `inverse.interacting_langevin_sampler`.
- Source: derived in ADR-0130 from the dynamics of
  [REF-GARBUNO-INIGO-ETAL-2020]; the need for the correction is the subject
  of [REF-GARBUNO-INIGO-NUSKEN-REICH-2020].
  - [REF-GARBUNO-INIGO-ETAL-2020](../references.md#ref-garbuno-inigo-etal-2020): Alfredo Garbuno-Inigo, Franca Hoffmann, Wuchen Li, and Andrew M. Stuart. "Interacting Langevin Diffusions: Gradient Structure and Ensemble Kalman Sampler." *SIAM Journal on Applied Dynamical Systems* 19(1), 412-441, 2020. The covariance-preconditioned interacting Langevin diffusion and its derivative-free ensemble approximation (the ensemble Kalman sampler). [arXiv:1903.08866](https://arxiv.org/abs/1903.08866).
  - [REF-GARBUNO-INIGO-NUSKEN-REICH-2020](../references.md#ref-garbuno-inigo-nusken-reich-2020): Alfredo Garbuno-Inigo, Nikolas Nüsken, and Sebastian Reich. "Affine Invariant Interacting Langevin Dynamics for Bayesian Inference." *SIAM Journal on Applied Dynamical Systems*, 2020. The finite-ensemble correction that makes the product of posteriors invariant, and the gradient-free formulation. [arXiv:1912.02859](https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.02859).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ALDI-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-aldi-004--covariance-deficit-without-the-finite-ensemble-correction)

## `probstat.inverse.iterative`

### EQ-INV-ITER-001 — Landweber iteration

`z_(k+1) = z_k + omega B'(b - B z_k)`, `B = W A`, `b = W (y - A x_ref)`,
`z_0 = 0`; on singular components `z_k = sum_i (1 - (1 - omega sigma_i^2)^k)
(u_i'b / sigma_i) v_i`.

- Domain: `0 < omega < 2 / ||B||^2` with a certified upper bound on `||B||`.
- Meaning: iteration count regularizes; small singular components are
  suppressed for small `k` (semi-convergence).
- Code: `inverse.iterative.landweber`.
- Source: [REF-LANDWEBER-1951], [REF-ENGL-HANKE-NEUBAUER-1996].
  - [REF-LANDWEBER-1951](../references.md#ref-landweber-1951): L. Landweber. "An Iteration Formula for Fredholm Integral Equations of the First Kind." *American Journal of Mathematics* 73(3), 615-624, 1951. Cited for the gradient iteration `x_(k+1) = x_k + omega A'(b - A x_k)` and its regularizing effect when stopped early. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26; Crossref lists the first page only). DOI: [10.2307/2372313](https://doi.org/10.2307/2372313).
  - [REF-ENGL-HANKE-NEUBAUER-1996](../references.md#ref-engl-hanke-neubauer-1996): Heinz W. Engl, Martin Hanke, and Andreas Neubauer. *Regularization of Inverse Problems*. Kluwer Academic Publishers (now Springer Netherlands), Dordrecht, 1996. Cited for iterative regularization, the discrepancy principle as a stopping rule, and Landweber's semi-convergence. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/978-94-009-1740-8](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-94-009-1740-8).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ITER-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-iter-001--landweber-iteration)

### EQ-INV-ITER-002 — Discrepancy principle as a stopping rule

Stop at the first `k` with `||W (A x_k - y)|| <= tau delta`, `tau >= 1`,
`delta ~ sqrt(m)` for whitened unit noise.

- Domain: known noise covariance; the test uses the DATA misfit, never an
  augmented residual.
- Meaning: the earliest iterate consistent with the noise level; `delta` is
  a norm, not a standard deviation, and a misestimated `delta` biases the
  stop.
- Code: `inverse.iterative.krylov_regularization`,
  `inverse.iterative.landweber`, `inverse.iterative.expected_noise_norm`.
- Source: [REF-MOROZOV-1966], [REF-ENGL-HANKE-NEUBAUER-1996].
  - [REF-MOROZOV-1966](../references.md#ref-morozov-1966): Vladimir A. Morozov. "On the Solution of Functional Equations by the Method of Regularization." *Doklady Akademii Nauk SSSR* 167(3), 510-512, 1966 (in Russian). The choice of the regularization parameter from the noise level, known as the discrepancy principle.
  - [REF-ENGL-HANKE-NEUBAUER-1996](../references.md#ref-engl-hanke-neubauer-1996): Heinz W. Engl, Martin Hanke, and Andreas Neubauer. *Regularization of Inverse Problems*. Kluwer Academic Publishers (now Springer Netherlands), Dordrecht, 1996. Cited for iterative regularization, the discrepancy principle as a stopping rule, and Landweber's semi-convergence. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/978-94-009-1740-8](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-94-009-1740-8).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ITER-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-iter-002--discrepancy-principle-as-a-stopping-rule)

### EQ-INV-ITER-003 — Hybrid LSQR: projected Tikhonov on the Golub-Kahan basis

`B V_k = U_(k+1) T_k`, `q_k = argmin ||T_k q - beta e_1||^2 + lambda_k^2
||q||^2`, `x_k = x_ref + V_k q_k`; projected GCV
`G_k(lambda) = ||(I - T_k T_lambda^#) beta e_1||^2 / (k + 1 - sum_i f_i)^2`.

- Domain: full reorthogonalization (stored bases); a general penalty enters
  as `||L V_k q||` through the triangular factor of `L V_k`.
- Meaning: regularization of the projected problem stabilizes the
  semi-convergence of LSQR. Projected GCV is a criterion on the projected
  problem and is not the full-problem GCV; on deriv2 it was observed in the
  tests to under-regularize, while the projected discrepancy rule tracked
  the noise level.
- Code: `inverse.iterative.hybrid_lsqr`.
- Source: [REF-GAZZOLA-HANSEN-NAGY-2019], [REF-GOLUB-KAHAN-1965].
  - [REF-GAZZOLA-HANSEN-NAGY-2019](../references.md#ref-gazzola-hansen-nagy-2019): Silvia Gazzola, Per Christian Hansen, and James G. Nagy. "IR Tools: A MATLAB Package of Iterative Regularization Methods and Large-Scale Test Problems." *Numerical Algorithms* 81(3), 773-811, 2019. Cited for matrix-free operators, iterative regularization by early stopping, hybrid projection methods and their test problems. No code was copied. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/s11075-018-0570-7](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11075-018-0570-7).
  - [REF-GOLUB-KAHAN-1965](../references.md#ref-golub-kahan-1965): Gene H. Golub and William Kahan. "Calculating the Singular Values and Pseudo-Inverse of a Matrix." *Journal of the Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, Series B: Numerical Analysis* 2(2), 205-224, 1965. Cited for the bidiagonalization `A V_k = U_(k+1) B_k`. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/0702016](https://doi.org/10.1137/0702016).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ITER-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-iter-003--hybrid-lsqr-projected-tikhonov-on-the-golub-kahan-basis)

### EQ-INV-ITER-004 — Weighted GCV for the projected Tikhonov problem

`G(omega, lambda) = k ||(I - T_k T_(k,lambda)^#) beta e_1||^2 /
(trace(I - omega T_k T_(k,lambda)^#))^2`, with
`trace(I - omega T_k T_(k,lambda)^#) = 1 + sum_i ((1 - omega) delta_i^2 +
lambda^2) / (delta_i^2 + lambda^2)` for the identity penalty. Adaptive weight:
`omega_hat_k` solves `dG/dlambda (omega, lambda) = 0` at
`lambda = delta_min(T_k)`, which gives
`omega_hat_k = (k + 1) rho' / (rho' F + 2 rho S)` (`rho` the squared projected
residual, `F = sum_i delta_i^2 / (delta_i^2 + lambda^2)`,
`S = sum_i 2 lambda delta_i^2 / (delta_i^2 + lambda^2)^2`), and
`omega_k = mean(omega_hat_1, ..., omega_hat_k)`.

- Domain: the projected problem of [EQ-INV-ITER-003]; fixed `omega` in
  `(0, 1]` for any penalty, the adaptive rule for the identity penalty only;
  `omega = 1` is standard projected GCV.
- Meaning: projected GCV tends to choose `lambda` too large (over-smoothing)
  in hybrid methods; `omega < 1` subtracts less of the trace and selects smaller
  `lambda`. The closed form of `omega_hat_k` follows by differentiating the
  quotient (linear in `omega`); averaging uses the early, well-conditioned
  iterations. The adaptive values are reported, not clipped.
- Code: `inverse.iterative.hybrid_lsqr`.
- Source: [REF-CHUNG-NAGY-OLEARY-2008, Sections 5.1, 5.3 and 5.4].
  - [REF-CHUNG-NAGY-OLEARY-2008](../references.md#ref-chung-nagy-oleary-2008): Julianne Chung, James G. Nagy, and Dianne P. O'Leary. "A Weighted-GCV Method for Lanczos-Hybrid Regularization." *Electronic Transactions on Numerical Analysis* 28, 149-167, 2007-2008. ISSN 1068-9613. Cited for the weighted GCV function of the projected Tikhonov problem and the adaptive choice of the weight (Sections 5.1-5.4). The journal assigns no DOI; metadata read from the journal's article page and PDF on 2026-11-26: <https://etna.ricam.oeaw.ac.at/volumes/2001-2010/vol28/abstract.php?vol=28&pages=149-167>.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ITER-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-iter-004--weighted-gcv-for-the-projected-tikhonov-problem)

## `probstat.inverse.linear_gaussian`

### EQ-INV-LG-001 — Linear-Gaussian posterior in data space

`mean = mu + C0 A' S^-1 (y - A mu)`, `C_post = C0 - C0 A' S^-1 A C0`, `S = A C0 A' + Gamma`, with `C0 = P P'`.

- Domain: `x ~ N(mu, C0)`, `y = A x + eps`, `eps ~ N(0, Gamma)` independent; `Gamma` SPD, `C0` PSD (may be singular), `S` SPD. Proper priors only.
- Meaning: exact Gaussian conjugate update; `C0` is never inverted. The covariance is a difference and loses digits in strongly informed directions; the parameter-space form avoids this.
- Code: `inverse.linear_gaussian.linear_gaussian_posterior`.
- Source: [REF-TARANTOLA-2005], [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LG-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lg-001--linear-gaussian-posterior-in-data-space)

### EQ-INV-LG-002 — Log evidence

`log p(y) = -1/2 [ (y - A mu)' S^-1 (y - A mu) + log det S + m log 2 pi ]`.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-LG-001.
- Meaning: normal log density of `y` under `N(A mu, S)`; a density in the units of `y` (log of unit^-m).
- Code: `inverse.linear_gaussian.linear_gaussian_posterior`.
- Source: [REF-TARANTOLA-2005], [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LG-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lg-002--log-evidence)

### EQ-INV-LG-003 — Prior-whitened (parameter-space) posterior

`x = mu + P z`, `K = W A P`, `W = Gamma^-1/2`; `z | y ~ N((I + K'K)^-1 K' W (y - A mu), (I + K'K)^-1)`; `C_post = P (I + K'K)^-1 P'`.

- Domain: `P` is `n x r`; the dense system is `r x r`.
- Meaning: the covariance form is nonnegative by construction (no subtractive cancellation); no inverse of `C0`.
- Code: `inverse.linear_gaussian.linear_gaussian_posterior`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007], [REF-BUI-THANH-ETAL-2013].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
  - [REF-BUI-THANH-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-bui-thanh-etal-2013): Tan Bui-Thanh, Omar Ghattas, James Martin, and Georg Stadler. "A Computational Framework for Infinite-Dimensional Bayesian Inverse Problems Part I: The Linearized Case, with Application to Global Seismic Inversion." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 35(6), A2494-A2523, 2013. Cited for the low-rank prior-preconditioned Hessian approximation of the posterior covariance. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/12089586X](https://doi.org/10.1137/12089586X).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LG-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lg-003--prior-whitened-parameter-space-posterior)

### EQ-INV-LG-004 — Determinant lemma for the evidence

`log det S = log det Gamma + log det(I + K'K)` and `r' S^-1 r = |W r|^2 - u'(I + K'K)^-1 u`, `u = K' W r`.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-LG-003.
- Meaning: evidence computed in parameter space agrees with the data-space value to rounding.
- Code: `inverse.linear_gaussian.linear_gaussian_posterior`.
- Source: [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013].
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LG-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lg-004--determinant-lemma-for-the-evidence)

### EQ-INV-LG-005 — Exact conditional simulation

`x = x0 + C0 A' S^-1 (y - A x0 - eps0)`, `x0 ~ N(mu, C0)`, `eps0 ~ N(0, Gamma)`.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-LG-001.
- Meaning: an exact draw from the posterior (not an approximation); in parameter space `z = z_mean + L^-T xi`, `L L' = I + K'K`.
- Code: `inverse.linear_gaussian.GaussianLinearPosterior.sample`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LG-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lg-005--exact-conditional-simulation)

### EQ-INV-LG-006 — Posterior predictive of a linear functional

`H x | y ~ N(H mean, H C_post H' [+ Gamma_new])`.

- Domain: `H` is `m' x n`; the optional noise term predicts new noisy observations.
- Meaning: propagates posterior uncertainty to new linear quantities; cost `m'` covariance actions.
- Code: `inverse.linear_gaussian.GaussianLinearPosterior.predictive`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LG-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lg-006--posterior-predictive-of-a-linear-functional)

### EQ-INV-LG-007 — Low-rank posterior covariance and mean

`K'K ~ V diag(lambda) V'`; `Cov(z | y) ~ I - V diag(lambda/(1+lambda)) V'`; `z_mean ~ V diag(1/(1+lambda)) V' K' W (y - A mu)`.

- Domain: eigenpairs of the prior-preconditioned data-misfit Hessian.
- Meaning: exact when every direction with `lambda > 0` is retained; otherwise the prior variance is kept in unresolved directions. The smallest retained `lambda` is a truncation indicator, not a certified bound.
- Code: `inverse.linear_gaussian.low_rank_posterior`.
- Source: [REF-BUI-THANH-ETAL-2013], [REF-SPANTINI-ETAL-2015].
  - [REF-BUI-THANH-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-bui-thanh-etal-2013): Tan Bui-Thanh, Omar Ghattas, James Martin, and Georg Stadler. "A Computational Framework for Infinite-Dimensional Bayesian Inverse Problems Part I: The Linearized Case, with Application to Global Seismic Inversion." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 35(6), A2494-A2523, 2013. Cited for the low-rank prior-preconditioned Hessian approximation of the posterior covariance. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/12089586X](https://doi.org/10.1137/12089586X).
  - [REF-SPANTINI-ETAL-2015](../references.md#ref-spantini-etal-2015): Alessio Spantini, Antti Solonen, Tiangang Cui, James Martin, Luis Tenorio, and Youssef Marzouk. "Optimal Low-Rank Approximations of Bayesian Linear Inverse Problems." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 37(6), A2451-A2487, 2015. Cited for the update `C_post = P (I - V diag(lambda / (1 + lambda)) V') P'` in prior-whitened coordinates. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/140977308](https://doi.org/10.1137/140977308).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LG-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lg-007--low-rank-posterior-covariance-and-mean)

### EQ-INV-LG-008 — Low-rank posterior sampling

`z = z_mean + xi + V diag(1/sqrt(1+lambda) - 1) V' xi`, `xi ~ N(0, I)`.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-LG-007.
- Meaning: covariance of the noise part is `I - V diag(lambda/(1+lambda)) V'`; the draw is `mu + P z`.
- Code: `inverse.linear_gaussian.LowRankGaussianPosterior.sample`.
- Source: [REF-BUI-THANH-ETAL-2013].
  - [REF-BUI-THANH-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-bui-thanh-etal-2013): Tan Bui-Thanh, Omar Ghattas, James Martin, and Georg Stadler. "A Computational Framework for Infinite-Dimensional Bayesian Inverse Problems Part I: The Linearized Case, with Application to Global Seismic Inversion." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 35(6), A2494-A2523, 2013. Cited for the low-rank prior-preconditioned Hessian approximation of the posterior covariance. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/12089586X](https://doi.org/10.1137/12089586X).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LG-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lg-008--low-rank-posterior-sampling)

### EQ-INV-LG-009 — Information gain and effective dimension

`I(x; y) = 1/2 sum_i log(1 + lambda_i)`, `d_eff = sum_i lambda_i/(1 + lambda_i)`, shrinkage `1/(1 + lambda_i)`.

- Domain: `lambda_i` eigenvalues of `K'K`.
- Meaning: mutual information in nats equals `1/2 (log det S - log det Gamma)`; effective dimension counts data-informed directions. Truncated sums are lower bounds.
- Code: `inverse.linear_gaussian.prior_whitened_information`.
- Source: [REF-SPANTINI-ETAL-2015], [REF-ALEXANDERIAN-2021].
  - [REF-SPANTINI-ETAL-2015](../references.md#ref-spantini-etal-2015): Alessio Spantini, Antti Solonen, Tiangang Cui, James Martin, Luis Tenorio, and Youssef Marzouk. "Optimal Low-Rank Approximations of Bayesian Linear Inverse Problems." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 37(6), A2451-A2487, 2015. Cited for the update `C_post = P (I - V diag(lambda / (1 + lambda)) V') P'` in prior-whitened coordinates. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/140977308](https://doi.org/10.1137/140977308).
  - [REF-ALEXANDERIAN-2021](../references.md#ref-alexanderian-2021): Alen Alexanderian. "Optimal Experimental Design for Infinite-Dimensional Bayesian Inverse Problems Governed by PDEs: A Review." *Inverse Problems* 37(4), 043001, 2021. Cited for A-, D- and goal-oriented optimal design criteria of linear Gaussian inverse problems. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1088/1361-6420/abe10c](https://doi.org/10.1088/1361-6420/abe10c).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LG-009](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lg-009--information-gain-and-effective-dimension)

## `probstat.inverse.linear_problem`

### EQ-INV-LIN-001 — Weighted affine Tikhonov functional and its stacked form

`Phi(x) = 1/2 ||W (A x - y)||^2 + lambda^2/2 ||L (x - x_ref)||^2` with
`W'W = Gamma^-1`; with `z = x - x_ref` the minimizer solves
`min ||[W A; lambda L] z - [W (y - A x_ref); 0]||`, and
`grad Phi = A'Gamma^-1 (A x - y) + lambda^2 L'L (x - x_ref)`.

- Domain: SPD noise covariance `Gamma`, `lambda >= 0`; the solution is
  unique iff the stacked operator has full column rank
  (`null(W A) ∩ null(L) = {0}` for `lambda > 0`).
- Meaning: generalized least squares with a penalty centred at an explicit
