  reference. `lambda = 0` is GLS; the minimum-norm SVD route minimizes
  `||x - x_ref||`, not `||x||`. With `W = I` and `x_ref = 0` it is
  [EQ-INV-REG-001].
- Code: `inverse.linear_problem.LinearInverseProblem`,
  `inverse.linear_problem.solve_regularized`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008, Section 2.7.1], [REF-TARANTOLA-2005].
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIN-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lin-001--weighted-affine-tikhonov-functional-and-its-stacked-form)

### EQ-INV-LIN-002 — Gain, resolution, influence and covariances of the linear estimator

`M = (A'Gamma^-1 A + lambda^2 L'L)^-1 A'Gamma^-1`, `R = M A`,
`H = W A M W^-1`, `df = tr(H) = tr(R)`, `Cov(x_hat) = M Gamma M'`,
`E[x_hat] - x = (R - I)(x - x_ref)`,
`MSE = tr(M Gamma M') + ||(R - I)(x - x_ref)||^2`; from the thin QR
`[W A; lambda L] = [Q_1; Q_2] R_qr`: `M = R_qr^-1 Q_1'W`, `H = Q_1 Q_1'` and
`(A'Gamma^-1 A + lambda^2 L'L)^-1 = R_qr^-1 R_qr^-T`.

- Domain: [EQ-INV-LIN-001] with a full-column-rank stack.
- Meaning: rows of `R` are averaging kernels, columns point-spread
  functions; the estimator's sampling covariance under repeated noise for a
  fixed truth differs from the Bayesian posterior covariance
  `(A'Gamma^-1 A + lambda^2 L'L)^-1`, which applies only when
  `lambda^2 L'L` is the precision of a proper prior.
- Code: `inverse.linear_problem.linear_estimator_analysis`,
  `inverse.linear_problem.LinearEstimatorAnalysis.bias`,
  `inverse.linear_problem.LinearEstimatorAnalysis.risk`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008], [REF-TARANTOLA-2005].
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIN-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lin-002--gain-resolution-influence-and-covariances-of-the-linear-estimator)

### EQ-INV-LIN-003 — Equality-constrained least squares by the nullspace method

`C = U_C S V_C'`, `x_p = C^+ d`, `Z` an orthonormal basis of `null(C)`,
`x = x_p + Z z` with `z = argmin ||W A Z z - W (y - A x_p)||`; multipliers
`mu` from `C'mu = A'Gamma^-1 (A x - y)`.

- Domain: consistent constraints (`||C x_p - d||` at rounding level), full
  column rank of `W A Z`; redundant consistent rows are allowed.
- Meaning: the exact constrained optimum, never a large-penalty
  approximation.
- Code: `inverse.linear_problem.equality_constrained_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-LAWSON-HANSON-1995], [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013, Section 6.2].
  - [REF-LAWSON-HANSON-1995](../references.md#ref-lawson-hanson-1995): Charles L. Lawson and Richard J. Hanson. *Solving Least Squares Problems*. SIAM, Philadelphia, 1995 (republication of the Prentice-Hall edition of 1974). Cited for the active-set non-negative least-squares algorithm (NNLS) and least squares with linear equality constraints. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/1.9781611971217](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9781611971217).
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIN-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lin-003--equality-constrained-least-squares-by-the-nullspace-method)

### EQ-INV-LIN-004 — Non-negative least squares (Lawson-Hanson) and its KKT conditions

`min ||W (A x - y)||` s.t. `x >= 0`; dual `w = (W A)'(W y - W A x)`;
optimality: `x >= 0`, `w_i <= 0` where `x_i = 0`, `w_i = 0` where `x_i > 0`.

- Domain: dense `A`; passive-set subproblems of full column rank.
- Meaning: the active-set algorithm moves the most positive dual index into
  the passive set and steps back to feasibility; clipping an unconstrained
  solution is not the constrained optimum.
- Code: `inverse.linear_problem.nonnegative_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-LAWSON-HANSON-1995, Chapter 23].
  - [REF-LAWSON-HANSON-1995](../references.md#ref-lawson-hanson-1995): Charles L. Lawson and Richard J. Hanson. *Solving Least Squares Problems*. SIAM, Philadelphia, 1995 (republication of the Prentice-Hall edition of 1974). Cited for the active-set non-negative least-squares algorithm (NNLS) and least squares with linear equality constraints. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/1.9781611971217](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9781611971217).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIN-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lin-004--non-negative-least-squares-lawson-hanson-and-its-kkt-conditions)

### EQ-INV-LIN-005 — Projected gradient for box-constrained regularized least squares

`x_(k+1) = P_[l,u](x_k - t_k grad Phi(x_k))` with backtracking until
`Phi(x+) <= Phi(x) + grad'(x+ - x) + ||x+ - x||^2 / (2 t)`; optimality
`||x - P(x - grad Phi(x))||`.

- Domain: `l <= u` componentwise (infinite bounds allowed), [EQ-INV-LIN-001].
- Meaning: matrix-free constrained solve; at the optimum `-grad Phi` is
  non-positive at active lower bounds, non-negative at active upper bounds
  and zero at free variables.
- Code: `inverse.linear_problem.bounded_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006, Section 16.7], [REF-BECK-TEBOULLE-2009].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
  - [REF-BECK-TEBOULLE-2009](../references.md#ref-beck-teboulle-2009): Amir Beck and Marc Teboulle. "A Fast Iterative Shrinkage-Thresholding Algorithm for Linear Inverse Problems." *SIAM Journal on Imaging Sciences* 2(1), 183-202, 2009. Cited for ISTA with backtracking and the accelerated FISTA momentum sequence `t_(k+1) = (1 + sqrt(1 + 4 t_k^2)) / 2`. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/080716542](https://doi.org/10.1137/080716542).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIN-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lin-005--projected-gradient-for-box-constrained-regularized-least-squares)

### EQ-INV-LIN-006 — Total least squares

`min ||[dA, dy]||_F` s.t. `(A + dA) x = y + dy`; with `v` the right
singular vector of `sigma_(n+1)([A, y])`, `x = -v_(1:n) / v_(n+1)`, unique
when `sigma_n(A) > sigma_(n+1)([A, y])`.

- Domain: `m >= n + 1`; independent errors of equal variance in `A` and `y`
  (rescale otherwise).
- Meaning: errors-in-variables fit; not uniformly better than least squares
  and noise-sensitive when the gap is small.
- Code: `inverse.linear_problem.total_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-1980], [REF-VAN-HUFFEL-VANDEWALLE-1991].
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-1980](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-1980): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. "An Analysis of the Total Least Squares Problem." *SIAM Journal on Numerical Analysis* 17(6), 883-893, 1980. Cited for the SVD solution of total least squares and its existence condition on the smallest singular value. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/0717073](https://doi.org/10.1137/0717073).
  - [REF-VAN-HUFFEL-VANDEWALLE-1991](../references.md#ref-van-huffel-vandewalle-1991): Sabine Van Huffel and Joos Vandewalle. *The Total Least Squares Problem: Computational Aspects and Analysis*. SIAM, Philadelphia, 1991. Cited for the assumptions of total least squares (independent, identically scaled errors in the matrix and the data) and its nongeneric cases. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/1.9781611971002](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9781611971002).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIN-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lin-006--total-least-squares)

### EQ-INV-LIN-007 — One factorization of the stacked operator for many data vectors

`B = [W A; lambda L] = Q R` once; for each `(y_k, x_ref,k)`:
`b_k = [W (y_k - A x_ref,k); 0]`, `z_k = R^-1 Q' b_k`, `x_k = x_ref,k + z_k`.

- Domain: full column rank of `B` (unique solutions); `A`, `Gamma`, `L` and
  `lambda` shared by every right-hand side.
- Meaning: replicate studies, Monte Carlo estimator covariances and
  multiple-experiment inversions pay for one QR and then one forward action and
  a triangular solve per data vector; each solution equals the one of
  [EQ-INV-LIN-001] solved separately.
- Code: `inverse.linear_problem.regularized_factorization`,
  `inverse.linear_problem.RegularizedFactorization.solve`,
  `inverse.linear_problem.RegularizedFactorization.solve_many`.
- Source: [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013, Section 5.3], [REF-HANSEN-2008].
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-LIN-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-lin-007--one-factorization-of-the-stacked-operator-for-many-data-vectors)

## `probstat.inverse.multifidelity`

### EQ-INV-MFX-001 — Fine-equivalent cost

`C = n_f + n_c * (c_c / c_f)`, with `n_f`, `n_c` the numbers of fine and coarse evaluations and `c_f`, `c_c` the cost of one evaluation of each.

- Domain: `0 < c_c <= c_f`; costs declared by the user or measured as the median single-call wall time (`measure_cost_model` states the timer, the statistic and the number of timings).
- Meaning: all work is expressed in units of one fine evaluation, so runs with different mixes of coarse and fine calls are comparable. Measured ratios depend on hardware and implementation; a declared ratio is an assumption, not a result.
- Code: `inverse.multifidelity.fine_equivalent_cost`, `inverse.multifidelity.measure_cost_model`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-MFX-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-mfx-001--fine-equivalent-cost)

### EQ-INV-MFX-002 — Cost-normalised effective sample size

`E = ESS / C`, with `ESS` the (smallest per-coordinate) effective sample size and `C` the fine-equivalent cost of EQ-INV-MFX-001; relative efficiency `E / E_ref` with `E_ref` the ESS per fine evaluation of a plain fine-model chain.

- Domain: `C > 0`, `ESS >= 0`.
- Meaning: work-normalised efficiency. Acceleration is claimed only if `E / E_ref > 1` on the same problem; a surrogate whose stage-1 acceptance is low can lose.
- Code: `inverse.multifidelity.cost_normalised_ess`, `inverse.multifidelity.delayed_acceptance_cost`.
- Source: [REF-CHRISTEN-FOX-2005].
  - [REF-CHRISTEN-FOX-2005](../references.md#ref-christen-fox-2005): J. Andrés Christen and Colin Fox. "Markov Chain Monte Carlo Using an Approximation." *Journal of Computational and Graphical Statistics* 14(4), 795-810, 2005. Cited for two-stage delayed-acceptance Metropolis-Hastings with a cheap approximate target. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1198/106186005X76983](https://doi.org/10.1198/106186005X76983).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-MFX-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-mfx-002--cost-normalised-effective-sample-size)

### EQ-INV-MFX-003 — Cost and break-even of an approximation-error setup

`C_setup = K (1 + c_c / c_f)`, `C_total = C_setup + N c_c / c_f`, `C_fine = N`, and `N* = C_setup / (1 - c_c / c_f)`, with `K` the number of paired training evaluations and `N` the number of likelihood evaluations of the posterior computation.

- Domain: `c_c < c_f` for a finite break-even `N*`.
- Meaning: the coarse model with an error model is cheaper than the fine model alone only if `N > N*`. This is a cost statement; whether the resulting posterior is adequate is a separate accuracy question.
- Code: `inverse.multifidelity.approximation_error_cost`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-MFX-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-mfx-003--cost-and-break-even-of-an-approximation-error-setup)

### EQ-INV-MFX-004 — Acquisition for active surrogate refinement

`a(x) = exp(mu(x) - mu_ref) * sigma^2(x)`, `mu_ref = max over the candidates of mu`, with `mu`, `sigma^2` the surrogate predictive mean and variance of the log-posterior.

- Domain: finite candidate set; `mu`, `sigma^2` finite.
- Meaning: posterior density (up to a constant) times predictive variance of the log-density; large where the posterior has mass and the surrogate is uncertain. A heuristic, not an optimal design; it does not account for the variance of the exponentiated surrogate.
- Code: `inverse.multifidelity.active_surrogate_posterior`.
- Source: [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006], [REF-LI-MARZOUK-2014].
  - [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006](../references.md#ref-rasmussen-williams-2006): Carl Edward Rasmussen and Christopher K. I. Williams. *Gaussian Processes for Machine Learning*. MIT Press, 2006.
  - [REF-LI-MARZOUK-2014](../references.md#ref-li-marzouk-2014): Jinglai Li and Youssef M. Marzouk. "Adaptive Construction of Surrogates for the Bayesian Solution of Inverse Problems." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 36(3), A1163-A1186, 2014. Cited for refining a surrogate of the forward map or log-posterior where the posterior mass is, rather than uniformly over the prior. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/130938189](https://doi.org/10.1137/130938189).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-MFX-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-mfx-004--acquisition-for-active-surrogate-refinement)

### EQ-INV-MFX-005 — Stopping rule of the refinement loop

Stop at the first of: `RMSE_val <= tau_val`; no candidate left; `max a <= tau_a`; `n_train + n_val >= budget`, with `RMSE_val = sqrt(mean (mu(x_j) - f(x_j))^2)` over held-out points `x_j`.

- Domain: `tau_val > 0`, `tau_a >= 0` (squared log-density units; at the mode `a = sigma^2`, the squared relative density error).
- Meaning: only the first and third conditions are accuracy-based; stopping on budget is reported as not converged. The held-out RMSE is unweighted, so the held-out points should lie where the posterior has mass. Because the held-out set steers the stop, its error is no longer an unbiased accuracy estimate; score on an independent set.
- Code: `inverse.multifidelity.active_surrogate_posterior`.
- Source: [REF-LI-MARZOUK-2014].
  - [REF-LI-MARZOUK-2014](../references.md#ref-li-marzouk-2014): Jinglai Li and Youssef M. Marzouk. "Adaptive Construction of Surrogates for the Bayesian Solution of Inverse Problems." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 36(3), A1163-A1186, 2014. Cited for refining a surrogate of the forward map or log-posterior where the posterior mass is, rather than uniformly over the prior. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/130938189](https://doi.org/10.1137/130938189).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-MFX-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-mfx-005--stopping-rule-of-the-refinement-loop)

### EQ-INV-MFX-006 — Extrapolation indicator

`x` is flagged if `x` is outside the bounding box of the design `X`, or `dist(x, X) > s * h`, with `h = max_i min_{j != i} |x_i - x_j|` and `s` a user factor (default 1).

- Domain: design with at least one point.
- Meaning: a geometric heuristic for "no training data nearby"; it says nothing about the actual surrogate error.
- Code: `inverse.multifidelity.extrapolation_report`.
- Source: [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006].
  - [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006](../references.md#ref-rasmussen-williams-2006): Carl Edward Rasmussen and Christopher K. I. Williams. *Gaussian Processes for Machine Learning*. MIT Press, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-MFX-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-mfx-006--extrapolation-indicator)

### EQ-INV-MFX-007 — Grid KL divergence and moment errors

`KL(p || q) = sum_i w_i p_i log(p_i / q_i)`, with `p_i`, `q_i` the two densities normalised by trapezoid weights `w_i` on a tensor grid; moment errors `|m_q - m_p|` and `|s_q - s_p|` per coordinate.

- Domain: grid covering the posterior mass; `q > 0` where `p > 0`.
- Meaning: measured accuracy of a surrogate posterior against an exact one in 1-3 dimensions; quadrature error is not estimated.
- Code: `inverse.multifidelity.grid_posterior_error`.
- Source: [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006].
  - [REF-RASMUSSEN-WILLIAMS-2006](../references.md#ref-rasmussen-williams-2006): Carl Edward Rasmussen and Christopher K. I. Williams. *Gaussian Processes for Machine Learning*. MIT Press, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-MFX-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-mfx-007--grid-kl-divergence-and-moment-errors)

### EQ-INV-MFX-008 — Multilevel MCMC telescoping estimator

`E_{pi_L}[Q_L] = E_{pi_0}[Q_0] + sum_{l=1}^{L} (E_{pi_l}[Q_l] - E_{pi_{l-1}}[Q_{l-1}])`; estimator `sum_l mean(Y_l)` with `Y_l = Q_l(x_n) - Q_{l-1}(z_n)`, standard error `sqrt(sum_l V_l / ESS_l)`.

- Domain: posteriors `pi_l` on one parameter space; level runs independent.
- Meaning: the discretisation bias of the finest level is not estimated. The variance sum is valid when each level's ESS-based MCSE is.
- Code: `inverse.multifidelity.multilevel_mcmc`.
- Source: [REF-DODWELL-ETAL-2015], [REF-GILES-2015].
  - [REF-DODWELL-ETAL-2015](../references.md#ref-dodwell-etal-2015): T. J. Dodwell, C. Ketelsen, R. Scheichl and A. L. Teckentrup. "A Hierarchical Multilevel Markov Chain Monte Carlo Algorithm with Applications to Uncertainty Quantification in Subsurface Flow." *SIAM/ASA Journal on Uncertainty Quantification* 3(1), 1075-1108, 2015. Cited for the multilevel MCMC telescoping estimator with coarse-chain proposals and the coupled acceptance probability. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/130915005](https://doi.org/10.1137/130915005).
  - [REF-GILES-2015](../references.md#ref-giles-2015): Michael B. Giles. "Multilevel Monte Carlo Methods." *Acta Numerica* 24, 259-328, 2015.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-MFX-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-mfx-008--multilevel-mcmc-telescoping-estimator)

### EQ-INV-MFX-009 — Coupled acceptance probability

`alpha = min(1, [pi_l(z) pi_{l-1}(x)] / [pi_l(x) pi_{l-1}(z)])`, with `x` the current level-`l` state and `z` the proposed state of the (sub-sampled) level-`l-1` chain.

- Domain: `pi_{l-1}(z) > 0`; the support of `pi_{l-1}` contains that of `pi_l`.
- Meaning: independence sampler with proposal law `pi_{l-1}`. Invariant for `pi_l` when proposals are independent; with correlated (finitely sub-sampled) proposals the fine marginal is approximate, while the coarse sequence `z_n` is a stationary `pi_{l-1}` chain.
- Code: `inverse.multifidelity.multilevel_mcmc`.
- Source: [REF-DODWELL-ETAL-2015].
  - [REF-DODWELL-ETAL-2015](../references.md#ref-dodwell-etal-2015): T. J. Dodwell, C. Ketelsen, R. Scheichl and A. L. Teckentrup. "A Hierarchical Multilevel Markov Chain Monte Carlo Algorithm with Applications to Uncertainty Quantification in Subsurface Flow." *SIAM/ASA Journal on Uncertainty Quantification* 3(1), 1075-1108, 2015. Cited for the multilevel MCMC telescoping estimator with coarse-chain proposals and the coupled acceptance probability. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/130915005](https://doi.org/10.1137/130915005).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-MFX-009](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-mfx-009--coupled-acceptance-probability)

### EQ-INV-MFX-010 — Level cost and sample allocation

`cost_l = sum_k e_{l,k} c_k / c_L` with `e_{l,k}` the evaluations of `pi_k` in level `l`'s run; allocation `N_l` by Giles' rule on `V_l tau_l` (`tau_l = N_l / ESS_l`) and `cost_l / (burn_in + N_l)`.

- Domain: `c_k > 0`, pilot run with `ESS_l` finite.
- Meaning: reports where the work goes and the cost-optimal chain lengths for a target estimator variance; levels with a constant difference series get zero samples.
- Code: `inverse.multifidelity.multilevel_sample_allocation`.
- Source: [REF-GILES-2015].
  - [REF-GILES-2015](../references.md#ref-giles-2015): Michael B. Giles. "Multilevel Monte Carlo Methods." *Acta Numerica* 24, 259-328, 2015.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-MFX-010](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-mfx-010--level-cost-and-sample-allocation)

### EQ-INV-MFX-011 — Coarse-to-fine warm-started regularised least squares

`Phi_l(x) = 1/2 ||W (G_l(x) - y)||^2 + lambda_l^2/2 ||L_l (x - x_ref,l)||^2`, `x_l^0 = P_(l-1)(x_(l-1)^*)` for `l = 1..L`, `x_0^0` given; work `e_l = n_value + n_derivative_value + c_d (n_jvp + n_vjp)` calls per level and cost `C = sum_l e_l c_l / c_L` in finest-level units, compared with the cold solve `e_L^cold` from `P_(L-1) ... P_0 x_0^0`.

- Domain: `G_0, ..., G_L` differentiable forward maps with one common output dimension; `P_l` user-supplied prolongations between the parameter spaces of consecutive levels; `0 < c_l <= c_L` declared per-call costs (default `c_l = n_l`, an assumption); `c_d > 0` the declared cost of one `jvp`/`vjp` relative to a value call.
- Meaning: the coarse solution is only a starting point; each level is solved to its own stopping criterion by Gauss-Newton-Krylov, so the final iterate does not depend on the coarse levels except through the basin reached. Whether the warm start is cheaper than a cold start is a measured outcome: the report gives per-level iterations, both costs, their ratio and the objective gap, and a ratio above 1 (a loss) is possible and was measured on the 8/16/32-cell elliptic test. A consistent prolongation is the caller's responsibility and is not checked.
- Code: `inverse.multifidelity.coarse_to_fine_warm_start`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-MFX-011](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-mfx-011--coarse-to-fine-warm-started-regularised-least-squares)

### EQ-INV-MFX-012 — Relative sensitivity error of an approximate model

`eps_J(x, v) = ||J_a(x) v - J_f(x) v|| / ||J_f(x) v||`, `eps_g(x) = ||g_a(x) - g_f(x)|| / ||g_f(x)||` with `g = J' w` (shared adjoint weight `w`) or `g = J(x)' (G(x) - y)` (misfit gradient, each model with its own value and Jacobian).

- Domain: `G_f`, `G_a` differentiable maps `R^n -> R^m` with `jvp` and `vjp`; held-out points disjoint from the points used to build `G_a`; unit noise in the misfit gradient.
- Meaning: the worst case over the probed points and directions is compared with a declared tolerance to decide whether `G_a` is fit for gradient-based use. This is an empirical statement about the sample (random directions are seeded), not a bound over the parameter space. A zero fine sensitivity with a nonzero approximate one gives `inf`.
- Code: `inverse.multifidelity.validate_sensitivities`, `inverse.multifidelity.surrogate_mean_forward`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-MFX-012](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-mfx-012--relative-sensitivity-error-of-an-approximate-model)

### EQ-INV-MFX-013 — Gradient angle and the angle implied by a relative error

`theta = atan2(||g_a - (g_a . u) u||, g_a . u)`, `u = g_f / ||g_f||`; if `eps_g <= eps < 1` then `theta <= arcsin(eps)` and `g_a . g_f >= (1 - eps) ||g_f||^2 > 0`.

- Domain: `g_f != 0`, `g_a != 0` (otherwise the angle is undefined and counted separately).
- Meaning: an approximate gradient with relative error below 1 is a descent direction of the fine objective to first order; the default angle tolerance `arcsin(min(tol, 1))` is the largest angle compatible with the declared relative tolerance. The `atan2` form stays accurate for small angles, where `acos` of a cosine near 1 does not.
- Code: `inverse.multifidelity.validate_sensitivities`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-MFX-013](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-mfx-013--gradient-angle-and-the-angle-implied-by-a-relative-error)

## `probstat.inverse.newton_trust_region`

### EQ-INV-NTR-001 — Penalized least-squares objective and gradient

`Phi(x) = 1/2 ||W (G(x) - y)||^2 + lambda^2/2 ||L (x - x_ref)||^2`, `grad Phi(x) = J(x)' W'W r(x) + lambda^2 L'L (x - x_ref)`, with `r = G - y`, `J = G'(x)`, `W'W = Gamma^-1`.

- Domain: `G` continuously differentiable; `lambda >= 0`; `jvp`/`vjp` are exact adjoints.
- Meaning: one `vjp` per gradient, no Jacobian formed. `lambda` is the statistical Tikhonov weight; the trust radius is numerical globalization and not a regularization parameter.
- Code: `inverse.newton_trust_region.newton_trust_region_inversion`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006]; [REF-TIKHONOV-1963].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
  - [REF-TIKHONOV-1963](../references.md#ref-tikhonov-1963): Andrei N. Tikhonov. "On the Solution of Ill-Posed Problems and the Method of Regularization." *Doklady Akademii Nauk SSSR* 151(3), 501-504, 1963 (in Russian); English translation "Solution of Incorrectly Formulated Problems and the Regularization Method," *Soviet Mathematics Doklady* 4, 1035-1038, 1963. The penalized least-squares regularization named after him.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-NTR-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ntr-001--penalized-least-squares-objective-and-gradient)

### EQ-INV-NTR-002 — Gauss-Newton Hessian action

`H_GN v = J' W'W J v + lambda^2 L'L v`.

- Domain: positive semidefinite; positive definite if `lambda > 0` and `L` has trivial null space.
- Meaning: one `jvp` and one `vjp` per action; drops the residual-weighted second derivative of [EQ-INV-NTR-003], so it equals the full Hessian exactly when `W'W r = 0` and approximates it for small residual or weak nonlinearity.
- Code: `inverse.newton_trust_region.newton_trust_region_inversion`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-NTR-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ntr-002--gauss-newton-hessian-action)

### EQ-INV-NTR-003 — Full Newton Hessian action with residual-weighted second derivative

`H v = J' W'W J v + sum_i (W'W r)_i Hess G_i(x) v + lambda^2 L'L v`.

- Domain: `G` twice continuously differentiable; the second-derivative sum is supplied by the user as `hvp_callback(x, v, W'W r)`.
- Meaning: the exact Hessian of [EQ-INV-NTR-001]; it can be indefinite away from the optimum, which the trust region handles by the negative-curvature exit of [EQ-OPT-TR-001]. No finite-difference fallback exists unless [EQ-INV-NTR-004] is requested explicitly. A covariance that depends on `x` would add terms that are not included.
- Code: `inverse.newton_trust_region.newton_trust_region_inversion`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-NTR-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ntr-003--full-newton-hessian-action-with-residual-weighted-second-derivative)

### EQ-INV-NTR-004 — Central difference of the gradient as Hessian action

`H v ~ (grad Phi(x + e v) - grad Phi(x - e v)) / (2 e)`, `e = h (1 + ||x||) / ||v||`, `h = eps^(1/3)` by default.

- Domain: explicit request `hvp="central_difference_of_gradient"`; smooth `G`.
- Meaning: costs two gradient evaluations (two forward values and two `vjp` calls) per action and carries a truncation error `O(e^2)` and a rounding error `O(eps ||grad|| / e)`; it replaces the whole Hessian (including the Gauss-Newton and penalty parts) and never the first-derivative callbacks. A non-finite perturbed forward value ends the run with `"nonfinite"`.
- Code: `inverse.newton_trust_region.newton_trust_region_inversion`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-NTR-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ntr-004--central-difference-of-the-gradient-as-hessian-action)

### EQ-INV-NTR-005 — Trust-region reduction of the inverse problem and evaluation accounting

Attempt `k`: `p_k = steihaug_cg(grad Phi(x_k), v -> H(x_k) v, Delta_k)` ([EQ-OPT-TR-001]); accept by `rho_k` ([EQ-OPT-TR-003]) with `Phi` evaluated through the cached forward value.

- Domain: initial forward value finite; stop reasons `"gradient"` (the only converged one), `"radius_collapse"`, `"max_iterations"` (attempts, including rejected steps), `"nonfinite"`.
- Meaning: `counts.jvp` equals the number of Hessian actions for Gauss-Newton, so the cost scales with CG iterations, not with the parameter count `n`; a trial with a non-finite forward value is a rejected step and appears as `nan` in the history.
- Code: `inverse.newton_trust_region.newton_trust_region_inversion`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006]; [REF-STEIHAUG-1983].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
  - [REF-STEIHAUG-1983](../references.md#ref-steihaug-1983): Trond Steihaug. "The Conjugate Gradient Method and Trust Regions in Large Scale Optimization." *SIAM Journal on Numerical Analysis* 20(3), 626-637, 1983. Cited for truncated conjugate gradients inside a trust region with termination on negative curvature or the boundary. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/0720042](https://doi.org/10.1137/0720042).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-NTR-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ntr-005--trust-region-reduction-of-the-inverse-problem-and-evaluation-accounting)

### EQ-INV-NLX-007 — Discrepancy stop of the Newton trust-region inversion

Stop at the first accepted iterate `x` with `||W (G(x) - y)|| <= tau delta`, `tau > 1`.

- Domain: `delta > 0` the noise level of the whitened data (`||W (G(x_true) - y)|| <= delta`), `tau > 1`.
- Meaning: Morozov's discrepancy principle used as an early stop of the trust-region iteration (`stop_reason="discrepancy"`, counted as converged); a true gradient stop has priority. With fixed `lambda` this stops the iteration, it does not choose `lambda`. Off by default.
- Code: `inverse.newton_trust_region.newton_trust_region_inversion`.
- Source: [REF-MOROZOV-1966], [REF-ENGL-HANKE-NEUBAUER-1996].
  - [REF-MOROZOV-1966](../references.md#ref-morozov-1966): Vladimir A. Morozov. "On the Solution of Functional Equations by the Method of Regularization." *Doklady Akademii Nauk SSSR* 167(3), 510-512, 1966 (in Russian). The choice of the regularization parameter from the noise level, known as the discrepancy principle.
  - [REF-ENGL-HANKE-NEUBAUER-1996](../references.md#ref-engl-hanke-neubauer-1996): Heinz W. Engl, Martin Hanke, and Andreas Neubauer. *Regularization of Inverse Problems*. Kluwer Academic Publishers (now Springer Netherlands), Dordrecht, 1996. Cited for iterative regularization, the discrepancy principle as a stopping rule, and Landweber's semi-convergence. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/978-94-009-1740-8](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-94-009-1740-8).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-NLX-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-nlx-007--discrepancy-stop-of-the-newton-trust-region-inversion)

## `probstat.inverse.nonlinear`

### EQ-CAL-NL-001 — Nonlinear observation model with experimental conditions

`y_jq = g_q(theta; c_j) + delta_jq + eps_jq`, where `c_j` are fixed
experimental conditions merged over the model's fixed inputs.

- Domain: deterministic forward model; independent errors across
  observations; conditions that do not overlap calibration parameters.
- Meaning: one calibration problem may combine many experiments and several
  observed quantities of interest.
- Code: `inverse.Experiment`, `inverse.CalibrationProblem.predictions`,
  `inverse.CalibrationProblem.evaluate`.
- Source: [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001] and [REF-SMITH-UQ-2024, Chapter 8].
  - [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001](../references.md#ref-kennedy-ohagan-2001): Marc C. Kennedy and Anthony O'Hagan. "Bayesian Calibration of Computer Models." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B* 63(3), 425-464, 2001. DOI: [10.1111/1467-9868.00294](https://doi.org/10.1111/1467-9868.00294).
  - [REF-SMITH-UQ-2024](../references.md#ref-smith-uq-2024): Ralph C. Smith. *Uncertainty Quantification: Theory, Implementation, and Applications*. 2nd edition. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2024. DOI: [10.1137/1.9781611977844](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9781611977844).
- Registry: [EQ-CAL-NL-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-cal-nl-001--nonlinear-observation-model-with-experimental-conditions)

### EQ-CAL-LIK-001 — Gaussian and Student-t calibration likelihood

With total scale `s^2 = sigma_q^2 + sigma_delta_q^2` and residual `r`:
Gaussian `log p = -0.5 (log(2 pi s^2) + r^2/s^2)`; Student-t
`log p = log G((nu+1)/2) - log G(nu/2) - 0.5 log(nu pi s^2)
- (nu+1)/2 log(1 + r^2/(nu s^2))`; the log-likelihood is the sum over
observations.

- Domain: `sigma_q > 0`, `sigma_delta_q^2 >= 0`, `nu > 0`.
- Meaning: Gaussian noise or a heavy-tailed robust alternative; the
  discrepancy variance is a declared additive model-inadequacy term.
- Code: `inverse.NoiseModel.log_density`,
  `inverse.CalibrationProblem.log_likelihood`.
- Source: [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013, Section 17.2],
  [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989], and [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001].
  - [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-gelman-etal-2013): Andrew Gelman, John B. Carlin, Hal S. Stern, David B. Dunson, Aki Vehtari, and Donald B. Rubin. *Bayesian Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. CRC Press, 2013. ISBN 978-1-4398-4095-5.
  - [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989](../references.md#ref-lange-little-taylor-1989): Kenneth L. Lange, Roderick J. A. Little, and Jeremy M. G. Taylor. "Robust Statistical Modeling Using the t Distribution." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 84(408), 881-896, 1989.
  - [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001](../references.md#ref-kennedy-ohagan-2001): Marc C. Kennedy and Anthony O'Hagan. "Bayesian Calibration of Computer Models." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B* 63(3), 425-464, 2001. DOI: [10.1111/1467-9868.00294](https://doi.org/10.1111/1467-9868.00294).
- Registry: [EQ-CAL-LIK-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-cal-lik-001--gaussian-and-student-t-calibration-likelihood)

### EQ-CAL-POST-001 — Calibration posterior with log-noise parameters

`log p(theta, eta | y) = log L(theta, e^eta) + log pi(theta)
+ sum_q [log p_sigma(e^eta_q) + eta_q] + const`, with `eta_q = log sigma_q`.

- Domain: proper prior `pi` (a `JointInput`), proper noise priors on
  `(0, inf)`.
- Meaning: the `+ eta_q` term is the Jacobian `|d sigma / d eta|`, so the
  log-scale density is the same prior expressed in unconstrained coordinates.
- Code: `inverse.CalibrationProblem.log_prior`,
  `inverse.CalibrationProblem.log_posterior`.
- Source: [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013, Section 1.3] and
  [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Section 4.3].
  - [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-gelman-etal-2013): Andrew Gelman, John B. Carlin, Hal S. Stern, David B. Dunson, Aki Vehtari, and Donald B. Rubin. *Bayesian Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. CRC Press, 2013. ISBN 978-1-4398-4095-5.
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-CAL-POST-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-cal-post-001--calibration-posterior-with-log-noise-parameters)

### EQ-CAL-STD-001 — Posterior in the prior's standard-normal space

For the Nataf map `u = T(theta)` of the prior,
`pi(T^-1(u)) |det dT^-1/du| = phi_d(u)`, so
`p(u, eta | y) proportional to L(T^-1(u), e^eta) phi_d(u)
prod_q p_sigma(e^eta_q) e^eta_q`.

- Domain: continuous prior marginals with a Gaussian copula; `u` in `R^d`.
- Meaning: sampling in `u` needs no explicit prior Jacobian; bounded or skewed
  priors become unbounded and roughly isotropic, which conditions the
  random-walk sampler.
- Code: `inverse.CalibrationProblem.standard_log_posterior`,
  `inverse.CalibrationProblem.to_physical`,
  `inverse.CalibrationProblem.sample_posterior`.
- Source: [REF-LIU-DERKIUREGHIAN-1986] and
  [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Section 4.3].
  - [REF-LIU-DERKIUREGHIAN-1986](../references.md#ref-liu-derkiureghian-1986): Pei-Ling Liu and Armen Der Kiureghian. "Multivariate Distribution Models with Prescribed Marginals and Covariances." *Probabilistic Engineering Mechanics* 1(2), 105-112, 1986.
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-CAL-STD-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-cal-std-001--posterior-in-the-priors-standard-normal-space)

### EQ-CAL-WLS-001 — Weighted calibration least squares

`theta_hat = argmin 0.5 sum_i (r_i(theta) / s_i)^2`, `s_i` the total noise
scale of observation `i`; unknown scales are re-estimated as
`s_q^2 = (RSS_q / n_q) n / (n - d)`.

- Domain: more observations than parameters when scales are estimated.
- Meaning: Gaussian maximum-likelihood estimate of `theta` (prior ignored)
  with Gauss-Newton covariance [EQ-OPT-LM-002] at unit weighted variance.
- Code: `inverse.CalibrationProblem.fit_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003, Chapter 2] and [REF-MORE-1978].
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
  - [REF-MORE-1978](../references.md#ref-more-1978): Jorge J. Moré. "The Levenberg-Marquardt Algorithm: Implementation and Theory." In *Numerical Analysis*, Lecture Notes in Mathematics 630, Springer, 105-116, 1978.
- Registry: [EQ-CAL-WLS-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-cal-wls-001--weighted-calibration-least-squares)

### EQ-CAL-FISHER-001 — Fisher-information identifiability spectrum

`F = J_w' J_w` with the Jacobian `J_w` of weighted predictions; analyse
`D F D = Q Lambda Q'` with `D = diag(scales)` (prior standard deviations by
default); `kappa = lambda_max / lambda_min`; eigenvectors with small
`lambda` are weakly identified parameter combinations.

- Domain: local linearization at a parameter point; Gaussian errors.
- Meaning: rank deficiency means non-identifiability; a large `kappa` means
  practical (weak) identifiability; prior-scaled eigenvalues compare data
  information with prior information.
- Code: `inverse.fisher_identifiability`,
  `inverse.CalibrationProblem.identifiability`,
  `inverse.CalibrationProblem.fit_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003, Section 3.4], [REF-TARANTOLA-2005, Chapter 3],
  and [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013, Section 8.5].
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-CAL-FISHER-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-cal-fisher-001--fisher-information-identifiability-spectrum)

### EQ-CAL-STD-002 — Fully standardized calibration coordinates

`v = (u, w)` with `theta = T^-1(u)` (Nataf map of the prior) and
`sigma_q = F_q^-1(Phi(w_q))` for every unknown noise scale; the prior of
`v` is `N(0, I)`, the posterior is
`L(T^-1(u), sigma(w)) N(v; 0, I) / p(y)`, and
`p(y) = integral L(T^-1(u), sigma(w)) N(v; 0, I) dv` is the marginal
likelihood of the original problem.

- Domain: continuous priors with a Gaussian copula for `theta`, continuous
  noise-scale priors; Gaussian or Student-t noise densities with their
  normalizing constants.
- Meaning: the change of variables makes pCN and the SMC evidence
  applicable to unknown-noise problems without changing the posterior; draws
  are mapped back to `(u, log sigma)` and `(theta, sigma)`.
- Code: `inverse.CalibrationProblem.standard_log_likelihood`,
  `inverse.CalibrationProblem.log_evidence`,
  `inverse.CalibrationProblem.sample_posterior`.
- Source: [REF-LIU-DERKIUREGHIAN-1986] (Nataf transformation);
  [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Section 4.3] (change of variables).
  - [REF-LIU-DERKIUREGHIAN-1986](../references.md#ref-liu-derkiureghian-1986): Pei-Ling Liu and Armen Der Kiureghian. "Multivariate Distribution Models with Prescribed Marginals and Covariances." *Probabilistic Engineering Mechanics* 1(2), 105-112, 1986.
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-CAL-STD-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-cal-std-002--fully-standardized-calibration-coordinates)

### EQ-CAL-CORR-002 — Correlated Gaussian calibration log-likelihood

For the `n_q` observations of a correlated quantity with residual vector
`r_q`, `Sigma_q = sigma_q^2 M_q + sigma_delta_q^2 I = L L'` and
`log p(y_q | theta) = -0.5 (n_q log 2 pi + log det Sigma_q
+ r_q' Sigma_q^-1 r_q) = -0.5 n_q log 2 pi - sum_i log L_ii
- 0.5 |L^-1 r_q|^2`; the log-likelihood adds this term for every
correlated quantity to the independent terms of [EQ-CAL-LIK-001]. With
`sigma_delta_q = 0` the factor is `sigma_q L_M` with `M_q = L_M L_M'`
computed once.

- Domain: `sigma_q > 0`, `M_q` symmetric positive definite, Gaussian
  errors (a Student-t law is not combined with correlation).
- Meaning: generalized least squares in likelihood form; `M_q = I`
  reproduces the independent Gaussian likelihood.
- Code: `inverse.CalibrationProblem.log_likelihood`,
  `inverse.CalibrationProblem.standard_log_posterior`,
  `inverse.predict`.
- Source: [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003, Chapter 6] (nonlinear regression with
  correlated errors) and [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013, Section 4.2] (Cholesky
  evaluation).
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-CAL-CORR-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-cal-corr-002--correlated-gaussian-calibration-log-likelihood)

### EQ-CAL-CORR-003 — Whitened residuals

`e = W r` with `W' W = Sigma^-1`: `e_i = r_i / s_i` for independent
observations and `e_q = L^-1 r_q` for a correlated quantity
(`Sigma_q = L L'`), so `|e|^2 = r' Sigma^-1 r`. Least squares minimizes
`0.5 |e(theta)|^2`, its Gauss-Newton covariance is `(J_e' J_e)^-1` with
`J_e = -W J`, and an unknown scale of a correlated quantity with
`Sigma_q = sigma_q^2 M_q` is re-estimated as
`s_q^2 = (|L_M^-1 r_q|^2 / n_q) n / (n - d)`.

- Domain: positive-definite `Sigma`; closed-form scale update only without
  a discrepancy variance on a correlated quantity.
- Meaning: generalized least squares by prewhitening; `J_e' J_e` is the
  Fisher information of `theta` under correlated Gaussian errors.
- Code: `inverse.CalibrationProblem.whitened_residuals`,
  `inverse.CalibrationProblem.fit_least_squares`,
  `inverse.CalibrationProblem.identifiability`.
- Source: [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003, Chapters 2 and 6].
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
- Registry: [EQ-CAL-CORR-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-cal-corr-003--whitened-residuals)

### EQ-CAL-AD-001 — First derivative of the prior's standard-normal map

For `x_i = F_i^-1(Phi(z_i))` with `z = L u`,
`d x_i / d u_k = phi(z_i) / f_i(x_i) L_ik`, and for the normal score
`z_i = Phi^-1(F_i(x_i))`, `d z_i / d x_i = f_i(x_i) / phi(z_i)`. A
reverse-mode variable takes its value from the float map and this
derivative through `x~ = x_0 + (g - g_0)` with `g = c sum_k L_ik u_k`
([EQ-DIST-AD-001]).

- Domain: continuous marginals with `f_i(x_i) > 0`; independence or a
  Gaussian copula; first derivatives only (second derivatives through the
  map are refused, not approximated).
- Meaning: the inverse-function rule differentiates the Nataf map without
  differentiating quantile functions, so the calibration log posterior is
  traceable in reverse mode for every continuous prior family.
- Code: `inverse.CalibrationProblem.standard_log_posterior_and_gradient`,
  `inverse.CalibrationProblem.log_posterior_and_gradient`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Section 2.1] (derivative of an inverse
  transformation), [REF-LIU-DERKIUREGHIAN-1986] (Nataf map) and
  [REF-GRIEWANK-WALTHER-2008, Chapter 3] (reverse mode).
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
  - [REF-LIU-DERKIUREGHIAN-1986](../references.md#ref-liu-derkiureghian-1986): Pei-Ling Liu and Armen Der Kiureghian. "Multivariate Distribution Models with Prescribed Marginals and Covariances." *Probabilistic Engineering Mechanics* 1(2), 105-112, 1986.
  - [REF-GRIEWANK-WALTHER-2008](../references.md#ref-griewank-walther-2008): Andreas Griewank and Andrea Walther. *Evaluating Derivatives: Principles and Techniques of Algorithmic Differentiation*. 2nd edition. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics (SIAM), Philadelphia, 2008. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717761](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717761).
- Registry: [EQ-CAL-AD-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-cal-ad-001--first-derivative-of-the-priors-standard-normal-map)

## `probstat.inverse.nonlinear_extended`

### EQ-INV-NLX-001 — Box-constrained least squares, projected gradient and KKT measure

`min_x Phi(x) = 1/2 ||W (G(x) - y)||^2  s.t. l <= x <= u`, `g = J' W' W (G(x) - y)`, `P(z) = min(max(z, l), u)`, projected gradient `pg(x) = x - P(x - g)`. At a KKT point `g_i = 0` for `l_i < x_i < u_i`, `g_i >= 0` at `x_i = l_i`, `g_i <= 0` at `x_i = u_i`; the multipliers are `g_i` (lower) and `-g_i` (upper).

- Domain: `G` continuously differentiable, `l < u` entrywise (infinite bounds allowed), feasible start.
- Meaning: `pg(x) = 0` if and only if `x` is a KKT point, so `||pg||` is the convergence measure (never the norm of the unconstrained gradient). The solver also reports the KKT violation `max_i` of `|g_i|` (free), `max(0, -g_i)` (at `l_i`), `max(0, g_i)` (at `u_i`). A local KKT point is a global minimizer only for a convex problem (for example linear `G`).
- Code: `inverse.nonlinear_extended.bounded_gauss_newton`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006], [REF-BERTSEKAS-1982].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
  - [REF-BERTSEKAS-1982](../references.md#ref-bertsekas-1982): Dimitri P. Bertsekas. "Projected Newton Methods for Optimization Problems with Simple Constraints." *SIAM Journal on Control and Optimization* 20(2), 221-246, 1982. Cited for the epsilon-active set and the projected-arc Armijo rule of the bound-constrained Gauss-Newton solver. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/0320018](https://doi.org/10.1137/0320018).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-NLX-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-nlx-001--box-constrained-least-squares-projected-gradient-and-kkt-measure)

### EQ-INV-NLX-002 — Epsilon-active set and reduced Gauss-Newton direction

`A_k = {i : x_i - l_i <= e_k and g_i > 0} union {i : u_i - x_i <= e_k and g_i < 0}`, `e_k = min(m, ||pg(x_k)||)`, `F_k` the complement; `p_F = argmin_q ||W (r + J_F q)||` (LSQR), `p_A = -s g_A` with `s = ||g_A||^2 / ||W J g_A||^2`.

- Domain: `m > 0` the `active_margin`; `J_F` the columns of `J` indexed by `F_k`, applied only through `jvp` and `vjp`.
- Meaning: variables pushed against a bound by the gradient are frozen to the reduced Gauss-Newton step of the free ones and move only by a scaled steepest descent step, so `g'p < 0` whenever `g != 0`. This is projected Gauss-Newton with an epsilon-active set, not a scaled reflective method. It is a local method (no global optimality for nonlinear `G`).
- Code: `inverse.nonlinear_extended.bounded_gauss_newton`.
- Source: [REF-BERTSEKAS-1982], [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-BERTSEKAS-1982](../references.md#ref-bertsekas-1982): Dimitri P. Bertsekas. "Projected Newton Methods for Optimization Problems with Simple Constraints." *SIAM Journal on Control and Optimization* 20(2), 221-246, 1982. Cited for the epsilon-active set and the projected-arc Armijo rule of the bound-constrained Gauss-Newton solver. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/0320018](https://doi.org/10.1137/0320018).
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-NLX-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-nlx-002--epsilon-active-set-and-reduced-gauss-newton-direction)

### EQ-INV-NLX-003 — Projected-arc Armijo line search

`x(t) = P(x + t p)`, accept the first `t in {1, f, f^2, ...}` with `Phi(x(t)) <= Phi(x) + c g'(x(t) - x)`.

- Domain: `c in (0, 1)`, `f in (0, 1)`; `g'(x(t) - x) < 0` is required for acceptance.
- Meaning: the sufficient-decrease test uses the actual displacement after projection, so a step truncated by the bounds is judged by the decrease it really achieves. A trial with a non-finite forward value is rejected and recorded as `nan`. Failure to find an admissible `t` is reported as `line_search_failure`.
- Code: `inverse.nonlinear_extended.bounded_gauss_newton`.
- Source: [REF-BERTSEKAS-1982], [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-BERTSEKAS-1982](../references.md#ref-bertsekas-1982): Dimitri P. Bertsekas. "Projected Newton Methods for Optimization Problems with Simple Constraints." *SIAM Journal on Control and Optimization* 20(2), 221-246, 1982. Cited for the epsilon-active set and the projected-arc Armijo rule of the bound-constrained Gauss-Newton solver. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/0320018](https://doi.org/10.1137/0320018).
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-NLX-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-nlx-003--projected-arc-armijo-line-search)

### EQ-INV-NLX-004 — Huber and Cauchy loss and IRLS weights

Huber: `rho(e) = e^2/2` for `|e| <= c`, `c|e| - c^2/2` otherwise, `w(e) = min(1, c/|e|)`. Cauchy: `rho(e) = (c^2/2) log(1 + (e/c)^2)`, `w(e) = 1/(1 + (e/c)^2)`. In both cases `w(e) = rho'(e)/e` and `rho(e) = e^2/2 + O(e^4)`.

- Domain: `c > 0`; `e` a whitened residual `(W (G(x) - y))_i`.
- Meaning: the Huber loss is convex with linear tails (bounded influence `c`); the Cauchy loss is non-convex with logarithmic tails (redescending influence), so its minimizer is local and depends on the start. `c` is in whitened units: defaults `1.345` (Huber) and `2.385` (Cauchy) assume unit-variance whitened noise. For `c` above every `|e_i|` the Huber objective is plain least squares.
- Code: `inverse.nonlinear_extended.robust_loss`, `inverse.nonlinear_extended.robust_weight`.
- Source: [REF-HUBER-1981].
  - [REF-HUBER-1981](../references.md#ref-huber-1981): Peter J. Huber. *Robust Statistics*. Wiley Series in Probability and Statistics. Wiley, 1981. M-estimators of location, scale, and regression, their asymptotic covariances, and the iterative algorithms that compute them. DOI: [10.1002/0471725250](https://doi.org/10.1002/0471725250).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-NLX-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-nlx-004--huber-and-cauchy-loss-and-irls-weights)

### EQ-INV-NLX-005 — Iteratively reweighted Gauss-Newton step and gradient

`Psi(x) = sum_i rho(e_i)`, `e = W r`, `r = G(x) - y`; `grad Psi = J' W' (w * e)`; step `p = argmin_q ||diag(sqrt w) W (r + J q)||`; accept by Armijo on `Psi`: `Psi(x + t p) <= Psi(x) + c t grad Psi' p`.

- Domain: `w_i = rho'(e_i)/e_i` frozen at the current iterate.
- Meaning: the step matrix `J' W' diag(w) W J` uses `rho'/e` instead of `rho''`, which stays positive semidefinite for the non-convex Cauchy loss; it equals the Gauss-Newton matrix for `rho = e^2/2`. The line search tests the true robust objective (never the frozen-weight quadratic). Weights near zero flag outliers; a majority of outliers breaks the estimator (no breakdown guarantee beyond the Huber/Cauchy influence bounds).
- Code: `inverse.nonlinear_extended.robust_gauss_newton`.
- Source: [REF-HUBER-1981], [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-HUBER-1981](../references.md#ref-huber-1981): Peter J. Huber. *Robust Statistics*. Wiley Series in Probability and Statistics. Wiley, 1981. M-estimators of location, scale, and regression, their asymptotic covariances, and the iterative algorithms that compute them. DOI: [10.1002/0471725250](https://doi.org/10.1002/0471725250).
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-NLX-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-nlx-005--iteratively-reweighted-gauss-newton-step-and-gradient)

### EQ-INV-NLX-006 — Variable projection reduced residual and Jacobian

`y ~ Phi(alpha) c`; `c = Phi^+ y`, `r(alpha) = (I - Phi Phi^+) y`, `d r / d alpha_j = -(P D_j c + (Phi^+)' D_j' r)` (full, Golub-Pereyra) with `D_j = dPhi/dalpha_j`, `P = I - Phi Phi^+`; Kaufman's approximation keeps only `-P D_j c`.

- Domain: `Phi(alpha)` of constant full column rank `k <= m` in a neighbourhood; the derivative formula is not valid across rank changes of `Phi(alpha)`.
- Meaning: the linear coefficients are eliminated exactly at every `alpha`, and Gauss-Newton runs on `1/2 ||r(alpha)||^2` in the `p` nonlinear parameters. The two Jacobians coincide when `r = 0`; the Kaufman gradient is inexact for a nonzero residual. A rank-deficient `Phi` is reported (trial rejected, or `ValueError` at the start); no pseudo-inverse is substituted.
- Code: `inverse.nonlinear_extended.variable_projection`, `inverse.nonlinear_extended.variable_projection_reduced_system`.
- Source: [REF-GOLUB-PEREYRA-1973], [REF-KAUFMAN-1975], [REF-OLEARY-RUST-2013].
  - [REF-GOLUB-PEREYRA-1973](../references.md#ref-golub-pereyra-1973): Gene H. Golub and Victor Pereyra. "The Differentiation of Pseudo-Inverses and Nonlinear Least Squares Problems Whose Variables Separate." *SIAM Journal on Numerical Analysis* 10(2), 413-432, 1973. Cited for variable projection. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/0710036](https://doi.org/10.1137/0710036).
  - [REF-KAUFMAN-1975](../references.md#ref-kaufman-1975): Linda Kaufman. "A Variable Projection Method for Solving Separable Nonlinear Least Squares Problems." *BIT Numerical Mathematics* 15(1), 49-57, 1975. Cited for the approximate reduced Jacobian that drops the second term of the Golub-Pereyra derivative. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/BF01932995](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01932995).
  - [REF-OLEARY-RUST-2013](../references.md#ref-oleary-rust-2013): Dianne P. O'Leary and Bert W. Rust. "Variable Projection for Nonlinear Least Squares Problems." *Computational Optimization and Applications* 54(3), 579-593, 2013. Cited for the comparison of full and Kaufman variable projection with joint Gauss-Newton on separable problems. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/s10589-012-9492-9](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10589-012-9492-9).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-NLX-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-nlx-006--variable-projection-reduced-residual-and-jacobian)

## `probstat.inverse.observation`

### EQ-INV-OBS-001 — Additive observation model

`y_i = f(x_i, theta) + delta_i + epsilon_i`, with
`Var(delta_i + epsilon_i) = sigma_delta^2 + sigma_epsilon^2` under independence.

- Domain: paired finite inputs and responses; positive measurement variance;
  non-negative discrepancy variance; independent centered Gaussian terms for
  the implemented likelihood.
- Meaning: measurement noise and model discrepancy combine numerically but
  remain distinct uncertainty sources.
- Code: `inverse.GaussianObservationModel`.
- Source: [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001].
  - [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001](../references.md#ref-kennedy-ohagan-2001): Marc C. Kennedy and Anthony O'Hagan. "Bayesian Calibration of Computer Models." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B* 63(3), 425-464, 2001. DOI: [10.1111/1467-9868.00294](https://doi.org/10.1111/1467-9868.00294).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OBS-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-obs-001--additive-observation-model)

### EQ-INV-RESID-001 — Calibration residual

`r_i(theta) = y_i - f(x_i, theta)`.

- Domain: [EQ-INV-OBS-001].
- Meaning: signed observed-minus-modeled response difference.
- Code: `inverse.GaussianObservationModel.residuals`.
- Source: [REF-SMITH-UQ-2024, Chapter 8].
  - [REF-SMITH-UQ-2024](../references.md#ref-smith-uq-2024): Ralph C. Smith. *Uncertainty Quantification: Theory, Implementation, and Applications*. 2nd edition. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2024. DOI: [10.1137/1.9781611977844](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9781611977844).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-RESID-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-resid-001--calibration-residual)

### EQ-INV-RMSE-001 — Root mean squared calibration residual

`RMSE(theta) = sqrt((1/n) sum_i r_i(theta)^2)`.

- Domain: a non-empty residual vector from [EQ-INV-RESID-001].
- Meaning: response-scale descriptive fit error, not a validation certificate.
- Code: `inverse.GaussianObservationModel.root_mean_squared_residual`.
- Source: [REF-SMITH-UQ-2024, Chapter 8].
  - [REF-SMITH-UQ-2024](../references.md#ref-smith-uq-2024): Ralph C. Smith. *Uncertainty Quantification: Theory, Implementation, and Applications*. 2nd edition. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2024. DOI: [10.1137/1.9781611977844](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9781611977844).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-RMSE-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-rmse-001--root-mean-squared-calibration-residual)

## `probstat.inverse.observation_laws`

### EQ-INV-OBSLAW-001 — Gaussian observation law with a covariance factor

`log p(y | eta) = -1/2 (r' Gamma^-1 r + log det Gamma + m log 2 pi)`, `r = y - eta`, score `Gamma^-1 r`, information `Gamma^-1`.

- Domain: `Gamma` symmetric positive definite (full, diagonal or block covariance factor of `probstat.linalg`), `m` observations.
- Meaning: fully normalized density, so evidences are comparable; correlated errors are allowed. `fisher_information` returns the diagonal of `Gamma^-1` (the full information is the precision matrix). The `log det Gamma` term matters as soon as `Gamma` is estimated.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws.GaussianLaw`.
- Source: [REF-TARANTOLA-2005].
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OBSLAW-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-obslaw-001--gaussian-observation-law-with-a-covariance-factor)

### EQ-INV-OBSLAW-002 — Poisson observation law (identity and log link)

`log p(y | mu) = y log mu - mu - log y!`, `mu = eta` (identity) or `mu = exp(eta)` (log); score `y/eta - 1` or `y - exp(eta)`; information `1/eta` or `exp(eta)`.

- Domain: `y` nonnegative integers; identity link needs `eta > 0`; log link needs `|eta| <= 700`.
- Meaning: the `y log mu` term is dropped for `y = 0`, so the limit `eta -> 0+` is finite (`-eta`) and free of `nan`; a zero rate with a positive count has probability zero and is rejected by the identity link rather than clipped.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws.PoissonLaw`.
- Source: [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989].
  - [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989](../references.md#ref-mccullagh-nelder-1989): P. McCullagh and J. A. Nelder. *Generalized Linear Models*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OBSLAW-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-obslaw-002--poisson-observation-law-identity-and-log-link)

### EQ-INV-OBSLAW-003 — Binomial observation law with logit link

`log p(y | n, eta) = log C(n, y) - y softplus(-eta) - (n - y) softplus(eta)`, `softplus(t) = log(1 + e^t)`, score `y - n p`, information `n p (1 - p)`, `p = expit(eta)`.

- Domain: known integer trials `n_i >= 0`, integer counts `0 <= y_i <= n_i`.
- Meaning: `log p = -softplus(-eta)` and `log(1-p) = -softplus(eta)` stay finite for any finite `eta`, so saturated probabilities do not overflow.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws.BinomialLaw`.
- Source: [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989].
  - [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989](../references.md#ref-mccullagh-nelder-1989): P. McCullagh and J. A. Nelder. *Generalized Linear Models*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OBSLAW-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-obslaw-003--binomial-observation-law-with-logit-link)

### EQ-INV-OBSLAW-004 — Independent Student-t observation law

`log p(y | eta) = sum_i [log t_nu((y_i - eta_i)/s_i) - log s_i]`, score `(nu + 1) r_i / (nu s_i^2 + r_i^2)`, information `(nu + 1)/((nu + 3) s_i^2)`.

- Domain: `nu > 0`, scales `s_i > 0`.
- Meaning: the PRODUCT of univariate t densities. It is not the multivariate t law (shared mixing variable, dependent components) and is not equal to a whitened multivariate t. The scale `s` is not a standard deviation; the variance `s^2 nu/(nu - 2)` exists only for `nu > 2`.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws.StudentTLaw`.
- Source: [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989].
  - [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989](../references.md#ref-lange-little-taylor-1989): Kenneth L. Lange, Roderick J. A. Little, and Jeremy M. G. Taylor. "Robust Statistical Modeling Using the t Distribution." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 84(408), 881-896, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OBSLAW-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-obslaw-004--independent-student-t-observation-law)

### EQ-INV-OBSLAW-005 — Censored Gaussian observation law

`log p(y_i | eta_i) = log N(y_i; eta_i, s_i^2)` for an exact reading, `log Phi((L_i - eta_i)/s_i)` for `y_i = L_i` (left censored), `log(1 - Phi((U_i - eta_i)/s_i))` for `y_i = U_i` (right censored); score `(y - eta)/s^2`, `-phi(a)/(s Phi(a))`, `phi(b)/(s (1 - Phi(b)))`.

- Domain: `L_i < U_i` (infinite when absent), recorded values within `[L_i, U_i]`.
- Meaning: a detection limit is an event probability, not an imputed value. The log-CDF and log-survival come from `probstat.distributions.Normal` and remain finite at 40 sd and beyond; the score ratio is evaluated in log space.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws.CensoredGaussianLaw`.
- Source: [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989].
  - [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989](../references.md#ref-lange-little-taylor-1989): Kenneth L. Lange, Roderick J. A. Little, and Jeremy M. G. Taylor. "Robust Statistical Modeling Using the t Distribution." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 84(408), 881-896, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OBSLAW-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-obslaw-005--censored-gaussian-observation-law)

### EQ-INV-OBSLAW-006 — Expected information of a censored Gaussian observation

`s^2 I = [Phi(b) - Phi(a)] - b phi(b) + a phi(a) + phi(a)^2/Phi(a) + phi(b)^2/(1 - Phi(b))`, `a = (L - eta)/s`, `b = (U - eta)/s`.

- Domain: terms with an infinite limit are zero; without limits `I = 1/s^2`.
- Meaning: `E[score^2]` of the mixed continuous/discrete observation (interior part `int_a^b z^2 phi`, plus the two censoring masses times their squared scores). Verified against quadrature. Cancellation can occur for extreme standardized limits.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws.CensoredGaussianLaw.fisher_information`.
- Source: [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989].
  - [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989](../references.md#ref-mccullagh-nelder-1989): P. McCullagh and J. A. Nelder. *Generalized Linear Models*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OBSLAW-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-obslaw-006--expected-information-of-a-censored-gaussian-observation)

### EQ-INV-OBSLAW-007 — Missing observations and marginal covariance submatrix

`log p(y_O | eta_O) = log N(y_O; eta_O, Gamma_OO)`, `Gamma_OO = Gamma[O, O]`; not `(Gamma^-1)[O, O]`.

- Domain: observed index set `O` non-empty; missingness is assumed ignorable (missing at random).
- Meaning: the marginal law of the observed entries uses the covariance submatrix. A sub-selection of the precision matrix is the conditional covariance given the dropped entries and gives a different (wrong) density. Informative missingness is not modelled; an all-masked data vector is rejected rather than treated as prior-only.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws.MissingMask`.
- Source: [REF-TARANTOLA-2005].
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OBSLAW-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-obslaw-007--missing-observations-and-marginal-covariance-submatrix)

### EQ-INV-OBSLAW-008 — Fisher scoring for the penalized likelihood of a linear predictor

`Phi(x) = -log p(y | A x) + lambda^2/2 ||L (x - x_ref)||^2`; step `x+ = argmin ||W (z - A x)||^2 + lambda^2 ||L (x - x_ref)||^2`, `W'W = I(eta)`, `z = eta + I(eta)^-1 s(y, eta)`; `x <- x + t (x+ - x)`, `t` by Armijo backtracking on `Phi`.

- Domain: `eta = A x`, law-feasible iterates (infeasible trial points count as `Phi = inf`); for a Gaussian law one step is the weighted affine Tikhonov solution.
- Meaning: Gauss-Newton on the deviance with expected information as the Hessian surrogate; the direction is a descent direction for the penalized objective, and convergence is declared on the decrement `-g'd`. Reported status is explicit (`converged`, `stop_reason`).
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws.maximum_likelihood_linear`.
- Source: [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989], [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989](../references.md#ref-mccullagh-nelder-1989): P. McCullagh and J. A. Nelder. *Generalized Linear Models*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall, 1989.
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OBSLAW-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-obslaw-008--fisher-scoring-for-the-penalized-likelihood-of-a-linear-predictor)

### EQ-INV-OBSLAW-009 — Student-t IRLS weights

`w_i = (nu + 1)/(nu s_i^2 + r_i^2)`, `r_i = y_i - eta_i`; step `min_x sum_i w_i (y_i - (A x)_i)^2` (plus penalty).

- Domain: independent fixed scales `s_i`, `nu > 0`.
- Meaning: EM / majorize-minimize weights of the t law; each weighted least-squares step does not increase the negative log-likelihood, and the limit is a local optimum of a non-convex objective (several optima are possible, so the start matters). The weight decreases like `1/r_i^2` for large residuals, which is the downweighting mechanism.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws.StudentTLaw.irls_weights`.
- Source: [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989].
  - [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989](../references.md#ref-lange-little-taylor-1989): Kenneth L. Lange, Roderick J. A. Little, and Jeremy M. G. Taylor. "Robust Statistical Modeling Using the t Distribution." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 84(408), 881-896, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OBSLAW-009](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-obslaw-009--student-t-irls-weights)

## `probstat.inverse.observation_laws_extended`

### EQ-INV-OLX-001 — Negative binomial observation law (mean-dispersion, log link)

`log p(y | mu, theta) = log G(y+theta) - log G(theta) - log y! - theta log(1 + mu/theta) + y log(mu/(theta + mu))`, `mu = exp(eta)`, `E y = mu`, `Var y = mu + mu^2/theta`, `G` the gamma function.

- Domain: `y` nonnegative integer, `theta > 0` real (or `inf`), `|eta| <= 700`.
- Meaning: gamma-Poisson mixture, `y | lambda ~ Poisson(lambda)`, `lambda ~ Gamma(theta, mu/theta)`. With integer `theta` it equals `distributions.NegativeBinomial(r=theta, p=theta/(theta+mu))`; the overdispersion coefficient is `1/theta`. Evaluated with a stable gamma ratio and `softplus` so large `theta` does not cancel.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws_extended.NegativeBinomialLaw`.
- Source: [REF-CAMERON-TRIVEDI-2013], [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989].
  - [REF-CAMERON-TRIVEDI-2013](../references.md#ref-cameron-trivedi-2013): A. Colin Cameron and Pravin K. Trivedi. *Regression Analysis of Count Data*. 2nd edition. Cambridge University Press, 2013. Poisson and negative binomial (NB2) regression with exposures, robust sandwich standard errors, overdispersion, and the zero-inflated and hurdle models (chapters "Basic Count Regression", "Generalized Count Regression", and "Model Evaluation and Testing"). DOI: [10.1017/CBO9781139013567](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139013567).
  - [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989](../references.md#ref-mccullagh-nelder-1989): P. McCullagh and J. A. Nelder. *Generalized Linear Models*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OLX-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-olx-001--negative-binomial-observation-law-mean-dispersion-log-link)

### EQ-INV-OLX-002 — Negative binomial score, information and Poisson limit

`d log p / d eta = (y - mu)/(1 + mu/theta)`, `I(eta) = mu/(1 + mu/theta)`; as `theta -> inf` the law, score and information tend to those of Poisson with rate `mu` (`mu`, `y - mu`, `mu`).

- Domain: as [EQ-INV-OLX-001], dispersion `theta` known and fixed.
- Meaning: the expected information is for the mean parameter only; `theta` is not estimated here (it is orthogonal to `eta` in the expected information). `theta = math.inf` is accepted and returns the exact Poisson law.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws_extended.NegativeBinomialLaw`.
- Source: [REF-CAMERON-TRIVEDI-2013], [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989].
  - [REF-CAMERON-TRIVEDI-2013](../references.md#ref-cameron-trivedi-2013): A. Colin Cameron and Pravin K. Trivedi. *Regression Analysis of Count Data*. 2nd edition. Cambridge University Press, 2013. Poisson and negative binomial (NB2) regression with exposures, robust sandwich standard errors, overdispersion, and the zero-inflated and hurdle models (chapters "Basic Count Regression", "Generalized Count Regression", and "Model Evaluation and Testing"). DOI: [10.1017/CBO9781139013567](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139013567).
  - [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989](../references.md#ref-mccullagh-nelder-1989): P. McCullagh and J. A. Nelder. *Generalized Linear Models*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OLX-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-olx-002--negative-binomial-score-information-and-poisson-limit)

### EQ-INV-OLX-003 — Interval-censored Gaussian likelihood

`log p = log N(l; eta, s^2)` if `l = u`; `log(Phi(b) - Phi(a))`, `a = (l - eta)/s`, `b = (u - eta)/s`, if `l < u` (`a = -inf` or `b = +inf` allowed; `(-inf, inf)` contributes 0).

- Domain: `s > 0`, `l <= u`, the interval is data held by the law; `y` is a representative inside it and is otherwise unused.
- Meaning: the likelihood is `P(l < Y <= u)`; censored entries are never imputed. Tails use `log_cdf`/`log_sf` and a log-difference `log(e^p - e^q)`; narrow intervals (`(b-a)(1+max|.|) < 0.1`) use a five-point Gauss-Legendre rule on the log scale, with the width `(u-l)/s` computed before standardization, so a narrow interval does not cancel.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws_extended.IntervalCensoredGaussianLaw`.
- Source: [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989], [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984].
  - [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989](../references.md#ref-lange-little-taylor-1989): Kenneth L. Lange, Roderick J. A. Little, and Jeremy M. G. Taylor. "Robust Statistical Modeling Using the t Distribution." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 84(408), 881-896, 1989.
  - [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984](../references.md#ref-davis-rabinowitz-1984): Philip J. Davis and Philip Rabinowitz. *Methods of Numerical Integration*. 2nd edition. Academic Press, 1984. ISBN 978-0-12-206360-2.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OLX-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-olx-003--interval-censored-gaussian-likelihood)

### EQ-INV-OLX-004 — Interval-censored score and observed information

`d log p / d eta = (phi(a) - phi(b)) / (s m)`, `-d^2 log p / d eta^2 = score^2 - (a phi(a) - b phi(b)) / (s^2 m)`, `m = Phi(b) - Phi(a)`; exact entries: `(l - eta)/s^2` and `1/s^2`.

- Domain: as [EQ-INV-OLX-003].
- Meaning: the interval probability is log-concave in `eta`, so the information is nonnegative; it is the OBSERVED information (the expected information is undefined for fixed intervals) and serves as the Newton weight of the scoring step. For narrow intervals the quadrature-weighted mean and variance of `z` replace the ratios: score `E_q[z]/s`, information `(1 - Var_q z)/s^2`.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws_extended.IntervalCensoredGaussianLaw`.
- Source: [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989].
  - [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989](../references.md#ref-lange-little-taylor-1989): Kenneth L. Lange, Roderick J. A. Little, and Jeremy M. G. Taylor. "Robust Statistical Modeling Using the t Distribution." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 84(408), 881-896, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OLX-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-olx-004--interval-censored-score-and-observed-information)

### EQ-INV-OLX-005 — Contaminated-normal (Gaussian mixture) outlier law

`log p = log[(1 - eps) N(r; 0, s^2) + eps N(r; 0, (kappa s)^2)]`, `r = y - eta`; responsibility `rho = eps N_out / ((1 - eps) N_in + eps N_out)`.

- Domain: `0 < eps < 1`, `kappa > 1`, `s > 0`.
- Meaning: two-term log-sum-exp; `rho` is the posterior probability that an entry is an outlier. The likelihood is multimodal in `eta`, so a robust start is advisable.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws_extended.GaussianMixtureOutlierLaw`.
- Source: [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989], [REF-MCLACHLAN-PEEL-2000].
  - [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989](../references.md#ref-lange-little-taylor-1989): Kenneth L. Lange, Roderick J. A. Little, and Jeremy M. G. Taylor. "Robust Statistical Modeling Using the t Distribution." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 84(408), 881-896, 1989.
  - [REF-MCLACHLAN-PEEL-2000](../references.md#ref-mclachlan-peel-2000): Geoffrey McLachlan and David Peel. *Finite Mixture Models*. Wiley, 2000. ISBN 978-0-471-00626-8.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OLX-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-olx-005--contaminated-normal-gaussian-mixture-outlier-law)

### EQ-INV-OLX-006 — Mixture score, EM weights and information factor

`d log p / d eta = r w`, `w = (1 - rho + rho/kappa^2)/s^2`; `I = J(eps, kappa)/s^2`, `J = int g'(u)^2 / g(u) du`, `g = (1 - eps) phi(u) + eps phi(u/kappa)/kappa`.

- Domain: as [EQ-INV-OLX-005].
- Meaning: `w` is the Gaussian-scale-mixture EM weight, so the weighted least-squares step never increases the objective. `J` has no closed form and is computed by composite Simpson quadrature (cached); `J -> 1` as `eps -> 0`.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws_extended.GaussianMixtureOutlierLaw`.
- Source: [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989].
  - [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989](../references.md#ref-lange-little-taylor-1989): Kenneth L. Lange, Roderick J. A. Little, and Jeremy M. G. Taylor. "Robust Statistical Modeling Using the t Distribution." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 84(408), 881-896, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OLX-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-olx-006--mixture-score-em-weights-and-information-factor)

### EQ-INV-OLX-007 — Huber density with exact normalizing constant

`rho_k(u) = u^2/2` (`|u| <= k`), `k|u| - k^2/2` otherwise; `log p = -rho_k(r/s) - log(s Z_k)`, `Z_k = sqrt(2 pi) erf(k/sqrt 2) + 2 exp(-k^2/2)/k`.

- Domain: `k > 0`, `s > 0`.
- Meaning: the Huber LOSS is an M-estimation criterion and defines a probability model only after normalization; `Z_k` makes `exp(-rho_k)/Z_k` a proper density (Gaussian centre, exponential tails), so evidence and posterior statements are valid under this declared density. The un-normalized `-sum rho_k` (`pseudo_log_likelihood`) must not be used for scale estimation or model comparison: it increases without bound as `s` grows.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws_extended.HuberLaw`.
- Source: [REF-HUBER-1964], [REF-HUBER-1981].
  - [REF-HUBER-1964](../references.md#ref-huber-1964): Peter J. Huber. "Robust Estimation of a Location Parameter." *The Annals of Mathematical Statistics* 35(1), 73-101, 1964.
