The minimax M-estimator of location and the score function `max(-k, min(k, u))`. DOI: [10.1214/aoms/1177703732](https://doi.org/10.1214/aoms/1177703732).
  - [REF-HUBER-1981](../references.md#ref-huber-1981): Peter J. Huber. *Robust Statistics*. Wiley Series in Probability and Statistics. Wiley, 1981. M-estimators of location, scale, and regression, their asymptotic covariances, and the iterative algorithms that compute them. DOI: [10.1002/0471725250](https://doi.org/10.1002/0471725250).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OLX-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-olx-007--huber-density-with-exact-normalizing-constant)

### EQ-INV-OLX-008 — Huber score, IRLS weights and sandwich covariance

`d log p / d eta = psi_k(r/s)/s`, `psi_k(u) = max(-k, min(k, u))`; IRLS weight `min(1, k s/|r|)/s^2`; information `P(|u| <= k)/s^2`; sandwich `cov = B^-1 M B^-1`, `B = A' diag(1{|r| < k s}/s^2) A`, `M = A' diag(psi^2/s^2) A`.

- Domain: independent observations; `B` of full rank; asymptotic justification for large `m`.
- Meaning: the sandwich treats `rho_k` as an M-estimation criterion and does not assume the Huber density is the true error law (the model-based `Fisher^-1` would). The robust covariance and the robust point estimate are distinct objects.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws_extended.huber_sandwich_covariance`.
- Source: [REF-HUBER-1981], [REF-WHITE-1982].
  - [REF-HUBER-1981](../references.md#ref-huber-1981): Peter J. Huber. *Robust Statistics*. Wiley Series in Probability and Statistics. Wiley, 1981. M-estimators of location, scale, and regression, their asymptotic covariances, and the iterative algorithms that compute them. DOI: [10.1002/0471725250](https://doi.org/10.1002/0471725250).
  - [REF-WHITE-1982](../references.md#ref-white-1982): Halbert White. "Maximum Likelihood Estimation of Misspecified Models." *Econometrica* 50(1), starting on page 1, 1982. The sandwich covariance `A^-1 B A^-1` of a quasi-maximum-likelihood estimator. DOI: [10.2307/1912526](https://doi.org/10.2307/1912526).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OLX-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-olx-008--huber-score-irls-weights-and-sandwich-covariance)

### EQ-INV-OLX-009 — Multivariate Student-t observation law with scatter matrix

`log p = log G((nu+m)/2) - log G(nu/2) - (m/2) log(nu pi) - (1/2) log det Sigma - ((nu+m)/2) log(1 + delta/nu)`, `delta = r' Sigma^-1 r`.

- Domain: `Sigma` symmetric positive definite (scatter, not covariance), `nu > 0`.
- Meaning: `Cov = nu/(nu - 2) Sigma` only for `nu > 2`; for `nu <= 2` no covariance exists and `covariance_matrix` refuses. The components share one mixing variable and are dependent even for diagonal `Sigma`, unlike a product of univariate t laws.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws_extended.MultivariateStudentTLaw`.
- Source: [REF-KOTZ-NADARAJAH-2004], [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013].
  - [REF-KOTZ-NADARAJAH-2004](../references.md#ref-kotz-nadarajah-2004): Samuel Kotz and Saralees Nadarajah. *Multivariate t Distributions and Their Applications*. Cambridge University Press, Cambridge, 2004. ISBN 978-0-521-82654-9.
  - [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-gelman-etal-2013): Andrew Gelman, John B. Carlin, Hal S. Stern, David B. Dunson, Aki Vehtari, and Donald B. Rubin. *Bayesian Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. CRC Press, 2013. ISBN 978-1-4398-4095-5.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OLX-009](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-olx-009--multivariate-student-t-observation-law-with-scatter-matrix)

### EQ-INV-OLX-010 — Multivariate t score, information, EM weight and marginal

`d log p / d eta = (nu+m)/(nu+delta) Sigma^-1 r`, `I = (nu+m)/(nu+m+2) Sigma^-1`, EM weight `(nu+m)/(nu+delta)`; the marginal at indices `S` is `t_nu(eta_S, Sigma_SS)`.

- Domain: as [EQ-INV-OLX-009].
- Meaning: the weighted least-squares step with `W = sqrt((nu+m)/(nu+delta)) C^-1` majorizes the negative log-likelihood. Marginals keep `nu` and take the sub-scatter matrix, which is what makes `MissingMask` valid.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws_extended.MultivariateStudentTLaw`.
- Source: [REF-KOTZ-NADARAJAH-2004], [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989].
  - [REF-KOTZ-NADARAJAH-2004](../references.md#ref-kotz-nadarajah-2004): Samuel Kotz and Saralees Nadarajah. *Multivariate t Distributions and Their Applications*. Cambridge University Press, Cambridge, 2004. ISBN 978-0-521-82654-9.
  - [REF-LANGE-LITTLE-TAYLOR-1989](../references.md#ref-lange-little-taylor-1989): Kenneth L. Lange, Roderick J. A. Little, and Jeremy M. G. Taylor. "Robust Statistical Modeling Using the t Distribution." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 84(408), 881-896, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OLX-010](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-olx-010--multivariate-t-score-information-em-weight-and-marginal)

### EQ-INV-OLX-011 — State-dependent Gaussian law and its exact gradient

`log p = -1/2 (r' Gamma(eta)^-1 r + log det Gamma(eta) + m log 2 pi)`, `r = y - eta`; `d log p / d eta_j = a_j - 1/2 tr(Gamma^-1 Gamma_j) + 1/2 a' Gamma_j a`, `a = Gamma^-1 r`, `Gamma_j = dGamma/d eta_j`. Diagonal case `v(eta)`: `r/v + (v'/2)(r^2/v^2 - 1/v)`.

- Domain: `Gamma(eta)` symmetric positive definite with supplied derivatives.
- Meaning: the log-determinant and covariance-derivative terms are part of the gradient with respect to the prediction; for a nonlinear predictor `eta = G(x)` multiply by the Jacobian. Predicted (state-dependent) scale is distinct from a known fixed scale. Masks are refused because the marginal depends on the dropped entries.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws_extended.StateDependentCovarianceLaw`, `inverse.observation_laws_extended.HeteroscedasticGaussianLaw`.
- Source: [REF-TARANTOLA-2005].
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OLX-011](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-olx-011--state-dependent-gaussian-law-and-its-exact-gradient)

### EQ-INV-OLX-012 — State-dependent expected information

`F_jk = (Gamma^-1)_jk + 1/2 tr(Gamma^-1 Gamma_j Gamma^-1 Gamma_k)`; diagonal case `1/v + v'^2/(2 v^2)`.

- Domain: as [EQ-INV-OLX-011].
- Meaning: Fisher information of `N(eta, Gamma(eta))` with respect to `eta`; `F` is positive definite, and its Cholesky factor gives the exact full-matrix scoring step.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws_extended.StateDependentCovarianceLaw`.
- Source: [REF-TARANTOLA-2005].
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OLX-012](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-olx-012--state-dependent-expected-information)

### EQ-INV-OLX-013 — Profile likelihood in an unknown common scale

`sigma_hat^2 = q/m`, `q = r' Gamma0^-1 r`; `log p_profile = -(m/2)(log 2 pi + 1 + log(q/m)) - 1/2 log det Gamma0`; gradient `(m/q) Gamma0^-1 r`.

- Domain: `y ~ N(eta, sigma^2 Gamma0)`, `Gamma0` known, `q > 0`.
- Meaning: not a probability model for `y` (it is not normalized, ignores the uncertainty in `sigma` and has the maximum-likelihood divisor `m`), so expected information and simulation are not defined. For a linear predictor the maximizer is the generalized least-squares estimate.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws_extended.ProfiledScaleGaussianLaw`.
- Source: [REF-TARANTOLA-2005].
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OLX-013](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-olx-013--profile-likelihood-in-an-unknown-common-scale)

### EQ-INV-OLX-014 — Inverse-gamma marginalisation of the noise scale

`sigma^2 ~ IG(alpha, beta)` gives `log p = log G(alpha + m/2) - log G(alpha) + alpha log beta - (m/2) log 2 pi - 1/2 log det Gamma0 - (alpha + m/2) log(beta + q/2)`, i.e. `t_{2 alpha}(eta, (beta/alpha) Gamma0)`; `sigma^2 | y, eta ~ IG(alpha + m/2, beta + q/2)`.

- Domain: `alpha, beta > 0`, `Gamma0` known SPD.
- Meaning: heavy tails arise from scale uncertainty; the law is a multivariate t with `nu = 2 alpha` and scatter `(beta/alpha) Gamma0`, verified against 1D quadrature over `sigma^2`. Score `(alpha + m/2)/(beta + q/2) Gamma0^-1 r`.
- Code: `inverse.observation_laws_extended.MarginalScaleGaussianLaw`.
- Source: [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013], [REF-KOTZ-NADARAJAH-2004].
  - [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-gelman-etal-2013): Andrew Gelman, John B. Carlin, Hal S. Stern, David B. Dunson, Aki Vehtari, and Donald B. Rubin. *Bayesian Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. CRC Press, 2013. ISBN 978-1-4398-4095-5.
  - [REF-KOTZ-NADARAJAH-2004](../references.md#ref-kotz-nadarajah-2004): Samuel Kotz and Saralees Nadarajah. *Multivariate t Distributions and Their Applications*. Cambridge University Press, Cambridge, 2004. ISBN 978-0-521-82654-9.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-OLX-014](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-olx-014--inverse-gamma-marginalisation-of-the-noise-scale)

## `probstat.inverse.ode_models`

### EQ-INV-ODE-001 — Fixed-step RK4 observation map

`x_(k+1) = x_k + h/6 (K1 + 2 K2 + 2 K3 + K4)`, `K1 = f(t, x_k)`, `K2 = f(t + h/2, x_k + h/2 K1)`, `K3 = f(t + h/2, x_k + h/2 K2)`, `K4 = f(t + h, x_k + h K3)`; `G(z) = (H x(t_1), ..., H x(t_J))` with `z = (x0, theta)`.

- Domain: `f` continuously differentiable in `x` and `theta`; output times `t_1 < ... < t_J`, `t_1 >= t0`, exactly `s` equal steps per output interval so every output time is a step boundary (no interpolation); `H` is `q x d`.
- Meaning: the discrete map `z -> G(z)` is what the solver differentiates. It differs from the exact flow by `O(h^4)`, and that error is part of the inference error. Fixed steps only; adaptive stepping, events and discontinuities inside a step are not supported.
- Code: `inverse.ode_models.ODEForwardModel`.
- Source: [REF-HAIRER-NORSETT-WANNER-1993].
  - [REF-HAIRER-NORSETT-WANNER-1993](../references.md#ref-hairer-norsett-wanner-1993): Ernst Hairer, Syvert P. Nørsett, and Gerhard Wanner. *Solving Ordinary Differential Equations I: Nonstiff Problems*. Second revised edition. Springer Series in Computational Mathematics 8. Springer, Berlin, Heidelberg, 1993. ISBN 978-3-540-56670-0. DOI: [10.1007/978-3-540-78862-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-78862-1).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ODE-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ode-001--fixed-step-rk4-observation-map)

### EQ-INV-ODE-002 — Discrete tangent (forward sensitivity of the RK4 scheme)

`dK1 = f_x(t, x_k) s_k + f_theta v_theta`, `dK_i = f_x(t_i, X_i) dX_i + f_theta v_theta`, `dX_2 = s_k + h/2 dK1`, `dX_3 = s_k + h/2 dK2`, `dX_4 = s_k + h dK3`, `s_(k+1) = s_k + h/6 (dK1 + 2 dK2 + 2 dK3 + dK4)`, `s_0 = v_x0`; `J v = (H s(t_j))_j`.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-ODE-001, with `f_x`, `f_theta` evaluated at the stage states `X_i` of the SAME step.
- Meaning: pushing the variational equation through the same stages gives the exact derivative of the discrete map, so the Taylor remainder of `G` is second order down to rounding. It is not the RK4 solution of the continuous sensitivity equation, which would be a different, only `O(h^4)`-close, quantity.
- Code: `inverse.ode_models.ODEForwardModel.jvp`.
- Source: [REF-HAIRER-NORSETT-WANNER-1993], [REF-GRIEWANK-WALTHER-2008].
  - [REF-HAIRER-NORSETT-WANNER-1993](../references.md#ref-hairer-norsett-wanner-1993): Ernst Hairer, Syvert P. Nørsett, and Gerhard Wanner. *Solving Ordinary Differential Equations I: Nonstiff Problems*. Second revised edition. Springer Series in Computational Mathematics 8. Springer, Berlin, Heidelberg, 1993. ISBN 978-3-540-56670-0. DOI: [10.1007/978-3-540-78862-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-78862-1).
  - [REF-GRIEWANK-WALTHER-2008](../references.md#ref-griewank-walther-2008): Andreas Griewank and Andrea Walther. *Evaluating Derivatives: Principles and Techniques of Algorithmic Differentiation*. 2nd edition. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics (SIAM), Philadelphia, 2008. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717761](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717761).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ODE-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ode-002--discrete-tangent-forward-sensitivity-of-the-rk4-scheme)

### EQ-INV-ODE-003 — Exact discrete adjoint of the RK4 steps

With step adjoint `a = a_(k+1)`: `Kbar_1 = h/6 a`, `Kbar_2 = Kbar_3 = h/3 a`, `Kbar_4 = h/6 a`, `xbar = a`; for `i = 4, 3, 2, 1`: `Xbar_i = f_x(t_i, X_i)' Kbar_i`, `thetabar += f_theta(t_i, X_i)' Kbar_i`, `xbar += Xbar_i`, then `Kbar_(i-1) += c_i h Xbar_i` with `c_4 = 1`, `c_3 = c_2 = 1/2`; `a_k = xbar`. Observations add `H' w_j` at `t_j`; `J' w = (a_0, thetabar)`.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-ODE-002.
- Meaning: the transpose of the tangent recursion, so `w'(J v) = (J'w)'v` holds to rounding (tested to `1e-12` relative). One forward and one reverse sweep give the gradient for any number of parameters.
- Code: `inverse.ode_models.ODEForwardModel.vjp`.
- Source: [REF-GRIEWANK-WALTHER-2008], [REF-HAIRER-NORSETT-WANNER-1993].
  - [REF-GRIEWANK-WALTHER-2008](../references.md#ref-griewank-walther-2008): Andreas Griewank and Andrea Walther. *Evaluating Derivatives: Principles and Techniques of Algorithmic Differentiation*. 2nd edition. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics (SIAM), Philadelphia, 2008. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717761](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717761).
  - [REF-HAIRER-NORSETT-WANNER-1993](../references.md#ref-hairer-norsett-wanner-1993): Ernst Hairer, Syvert P. Nørsett, and Gerhard Wanner. *Solving Ordinary Differential Equations I: Nonstiff Problems*. Second revised edition. Springer Series in Computational Mathematics 8. Springer, Berlin, Heidelberg, 1993. ISBN 978-3-540-56670-0. DOI: [10.1007/978-3-540-78862-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-78862-1).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ODE-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ode-003--exact-discrete-adjoint-of-the-rk4-steps)

### EQ-INV-ODE-004 — Checkpointing and work counts

Store `x_k` for `k` a multiple of `c` (`ceil(N/c) + 1` states); in the reverse sweep recompute the `c - 1` interior states of each block from its checkpoint. Peak stored states `<= ceil(N/c) + 1 + c`; the gradient equals the full-storage gradient up to rounding.

- Domain: `N` total RK4 steps, `c >= 1`; `max_stored_states` selects the smallest `c` meeting the budget.
- Meaning: a memory/time trade-off with a deterministic recomputation, reported in `ODEWorkCounts` (`rk4_steps`, `recomputed_steps`, `rhs_evaluations`, `peak_stored_states`). `c = 1` recomputes nothing.
- Code: `inverse.ode_models.ODEWorkCounts`.
- Source: [REF-GRIEWANK-WALTHER-2008].
  - [REF-GRIEWANK-WALTHER-2008](../references.md#ref-griewank-walther-2008): Andreas Griewank and Andrea Walther. *Evaluating Derivatives: Principles and Techniques of Algorithmic Differentiation*. 2nd edition. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics (SIAM), Philadelphia, 2008. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717761](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717761).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ODE-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ode-004--checkpointing-and-work-counts)

### EQ-INV-ODE-005 — External simulator adapter contract

`G` is a user callable `x -> y`; `J v` is the user `jvp` or the central difference `(G(x + e v) - G(x - e v)) / (2 e)`, `e = step (1 + ||x||_2) / ||v||_2`; `J' w` is the user `vjp` or, only if allowed, `(w' J e_1, ..., w' J e_n)` from `n` central-difference columns.

- Domain: `step` is declared by the caller; the finite-difference `vjp` costs `2 n` calls and must be enabled explicitly.
- Meaning: inputs are validated (finite reals, correct length); every call is guarded and an exception, a non-sequence, wrong-length or non-real output, or a non-finite output is recorded as a `SimulatorFailure` and raised (or, for `value` only, returned as `nan` with `on_failure="record"`). The optional cache is keyed by the exact input, off by default, and never stores failures. The finite-difference truncation error is `O(e^2)` plus rounding `eps/e`; a finite-difference pair is not an exact adjoint pair.
- Code: `inverse.ode_models.SimulatorAdapter`.
- Source: [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ODE-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ode-005--external-simulator-adapter-contract)

### EQ-INV-ODE-006 — Heat equation with log-diffusivity, implicit Euler

`u_t = (kappa u_s)_s`, `u(0) = u(1) = 0`, `kappa_f = exp(sum_k c_k phi_k(s_f))` at the `N + 1` face midpoints, `(A(kappa) u)_i = h^-2 [kappa_(i-1)(u_i - u_(i-1)) + kappa_i (u_i - u_(i+1))]`, `(I + dt A) u^(j+1) = u^j`; data `H u^j` with linear interpolation at the sensors.

- Domain: `h = 1/(N + 1)`, `N >= 2`; `kappa` positive and finite (otherwise the evaluation fails and `value` returns `nan`).
- Meaning: `A` is symmetric positive definite and implicit Euler is unconditionally stable, so no time-step restriction applies; the first-order time error and the second-order space error are part of the discretization error against a finer truth. With constant `kappa` and initial `sin(pi s)` the state decays by `(1 + dt lambda)^-j` with the discrete eigenvalue `lambda = 4 kappa h^-2 sin^2(pi h/2)` (tested).
- Code: `inverse.ode_models.HeatCoefficientModel`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ODE-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ode-006--heat-equation-with-log-diffusivity-implicit-euler)

### EQ-INV-ODE-007 — Tangent and exact discrete adjoint through the time steps

Tangent: `(I + dt A) du^(j+1) = du^j - dt A(dkappa) u^(j+1)`, `du^0 = 0`, `dkappa_f = kappa_f (Phi v)_f`. Adjoint: for `j = N .. 1`, `lambda += H_j' w_j`, `p = (I + dt A)^-1 lambda`, `kappabar_f -= dt (Delta p)_f (Delta u^j)_f / h^2`, `lambda <- p`; `J' w = Phi' (kappa kappabar)`.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-ODE-006; `Delta` is the difference across face `f` with zero boundary values.
- Meaning: `A` is linear in `kappa` and `I + dt A` is symmetric, so one factorization serves forward, tangent and adjoint solves; the adjoint costs `N` tridiagonal solves for any number of coefficients (assembly of `Phi'(.)` is `O(N K)`). The pair is exact for the discrete scheme (dot test to `1e-12`).
- Code: `inverse.ode_models.HeatCoefficientModel.vjp`.
- Source: [REF-GRIEWANK-WALTHER-2008], [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-GRIEWANK-WALTHER-2008](../references.md#ref-griewank-walther-2008): Andreas Griewank and Andrea Walther. *Evaluating Derivatives: Principles and Techniques of Algorithmic Differentiation*. 2nd edition. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics (SIAM), Philadelphia, 2008. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717761](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717761).
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ODE-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ode-007--tangent-and-exact-discrete-adjoint-through-the-time-steps)

### EQ-INV-ODE-008 — Damped oscillator with piecewise-constant source (N03)

`x'' + 2 zeta w x' + w^2 x = s(t)`, `s = s_p` on the `p`-th group of `q` output intervals; state `(x, x')`, `f = (x', -w^2 x - 2 zeta w x' + s_p)`, data `x(t_j) + eps_j`, `eps ~ N(0, sigma^2 I)`, unknown `z = (x(0), x'(0), s_1, ..., s_K)` with prior `N(0, tau^2 I)`.

- Domain: `w > 0`, `zeta >= 0`, `sigma, tau > 0`; the source breakpoints coincide with RK4 step boundaries (the `segment` argument), so the forcing is never discontinuous inside a step.
- Meaning: the map is linear (zero for `z = 0`), `G(z) = J z`; the truth is a prior draw from the stream child `truth`, the noise from the child `noise`, and the noise-free data come from a finer time step (`same_discretization = False`).
- Code: `inverse.ode_models.damped_oscillator_benchmark`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007], [REF-TARANTOLA-2005].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ODE-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ode-008--damped-oscillator-with-piecewise-constant-source-n03)

### EQ-INV-ODE-009 — Whitened singular values and initial-state posterior

`sigma_i(J / sigma)`, `sigma_i(J P / sigma)` with `P = tau I`; posterior `C = (J'J / sigma^2 + tau^-2 I)^-1`, mean `C J' y / sigma^2`; the initial-state marginal is the leading `2 x 2` block of `C` and the leading two entries of the mean.

- Domain: linear Gaussian model of EQ-INV-ODE-008.
- Meaning: singular values above one in the prior-whitened set count the data-informed directions; the ratio of posterior to prior standard deviation of `(x(0), x'(0))` measures what the position readings learn about the initial state. The interval `mean +- 1.96 sd` is calibrated only for truths drawn from the stated prior (measured coverage 0.943 for both coordinates over 1000 seeds, Monte Carlo SE 0.007).
- Code: `inverse.ode_models.OscillatorBenchmark`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007], [REF-TARANTOLA-2005].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-ODE-009](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-ode-009--whitened-singular-values-and-initial-state-posterior)

## `probstat.inverse.parameter_selection`

### EQ-INV-SEL-001 — GCV, UPRE, discrepancy gap and discrete L-curve curvature

With whitened residual `r_lambda` and `df = tr(H_lambda)`:
`GCV = m ||r||^2 / (m - df)^2`, `UPRE = ||r||^2 + 2 df - m`,
gap `||r|| - tau delta`, curvature
`kappa = (x'y'' - x''y') / (x'^2 + y'^2)^(3/2)` of
`(log ||r||, log ||L(x - x_ref)||)` by central differences in `log lambda`;
Hutchinson `df` with common probes across `lambda`.

- Domain: whitened unit noise (true covariance); positive increasing grid.
- Meaning: data-driven choices of `lambda`. UPRE is unbiased for the
  observation-space prediction risk `E||W A (x_lambda - x_true)||^2`, not
  the parameter error. Grid-end optima are reported as boundary choices.
- Code: `inverse.parameter_selection.regularization_path`,
  `inverse.parameter_selection.select_regularization`,
  `inverse.parameter_selection.logarithmic_grid`.
- Source: [REF-GOLUB-HEATH-WAHBA-1979], [REF-STEIN-1981], [REF-MALLOWS-1973],
  [REF-VOGEL-2002], [REF-HANSEN-OLEARY-1993], [REF-HUTCHINSON-1989].
  - [REF-GOLUB-HEATH-WAHBA-1979](../references.md#ref-golub-heath-wahba-1979): Gene H. Golub, Michael Heath, and Grace Wahba. "Generalized Cross-Validation as a Method for Choosing a Good Ridge Parameter." *Technometrics* 21(2), 215-223, 1979. Generalized cross-validation for ridge (Tikhonov) regression. DOI: [10.1080/00401706.1979.10489751](https://doi.org/10.1080/00401706.1979.10489751).
  - [REF-STEIN-1981](../references.md#ref-stein-1981): Charles M. Stein. "Estimation of the Mean of a Multivariate Normal Distribution." *The Annals of Statistics* 9(6), 1135-1151, 1981. Cited for Stein's unbiased risk estimate, `||r||^2 + 2 sigma^2 df - m sigma^2` for a linear smoother. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1214/aos/1176345632](https://doi.org/10.1214/aos/1176345632).
  - [REF-MALLOWS-1973](../references.md#ref-mallows-1973): C. L. Mallows. "Some Comments on C_P." *Technometrics* 15(4), 661-675, 1973. Cited for the prediction-risk criterion that the unbiased predictive risk estimator generalizes. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1080/00401706.1973.10489103](https://doi.org/10.1080/00401706.1973.10489103).
  - [REF-VOGEL-2002](../references.md#ref-vogel-2002): Curtis R. Vogel. *Computational Methods for Inverse Problems*. SIAM, Philadelphia, 2002. Cited for the unbiased predictive risk estimator (UPRE) of the regularization parameter and total-variation regularization. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717570](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717570).
  - [REF-HANSEN-OLEARY-1993](../references.md#ref-hansen-oleary-1993): Per Christian Hansen and Dianne Prost O'Leary. "The Use of the L-Curve in the Regularization of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 14(6), 1487-1503, 1993. The corner of the log-log L-curve as the point of maximum curvature (Section 5.1, eq. (8)) and its comparison with generalized cross-validation. DOI: [10.1137/0914086](https://doi.org/10.1137/0914086).
  - [REF-HUTCHINSON-1989](../references.md#ref-hutchinson-1989): M. F. Hutchinson. "A Stochastic Estimator of the Trace of the Influence Matrix for Laplacian Smoothing Splines." *Communications in Statistics - Simulation and Computation* 18(3), 1059-1076, 1989. Cited for the Rademacher-probe trace estimator `tr(H) ~= (1/s) sum_k z_k'H z_k`. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1080/03610918908812806](https://doi.org/10.1080/03610918908812806).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SEL-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sel-001--gcv-upre-discrepancy-gap-and-discrete-l-curve-curvature)

### EQ-INV-SEL-002 — Log evidence of the Gaussian prior implied by the penalty

For the proper prior `x ~ N(x_ref, (lambda^2 L'L)^-1)` (`L` square
invertible): `log p(y) = -1/2 [2 Phi_min + log det(B'B) - log det(lambda^2
L'L) + m log(2 pi) + log det Gamma]`, `B = [W A; lambda L]`,
`log det(B'B) = 2 sum_i log |R_ii|` from the stacked QR.

- Domain: square invertible `L`; normalized, all constants included.
- Meaning: empirical-Bayes choice of `lambda`; equals the density of
  `y ~ N(A x_ref, Gamma + A C_0 A')`. It is not a fully Bayesian treatment
  of `lambda`.
- Code: `inverse.parameter_selection.regularization_path`.
- Source: [REF-STUART-2010], [REF-TARANTOLA-2005].
  - [REF-STUART-2010](../references.md#ref-stuart-2010): Andrew M. Stuart. "Inverse Problems: A Bayesian Perspective." *Acta Numerica* 19, 451-559, 2010. Cited for the Gaussian linear inverse problem, its posterior mean and covariance, function-space priors, and weighted inner products. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/S0962492910000061](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0962492910000061).
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SEL-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sel-002--log-evidence-of-the-gaussian-prior-implied-by-the-penalty)

### EQ-INV-SEL-003 — Leave-one-group-out cross-validation

`CV(lambda) = (1/G) sum_g ||W_g (A_g x_lambda^(-g) - y_g)||^2 / m_g`, with
`x_lambda^(-g)` refitted without group `g`.

- Domain: noise independent across groups (diagonal covariance).
- Meaning: held-out predictive error of meaningful groups (sensors, time
  blocks); with correlated noise across groups the marginal score leaks and
  is refused.
- Code: `inverse.parameter_selection.grouped_cross_validation`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008, Section 2.7].
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SEL-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sel-003--leave-one-group-out-cross-validation)

### EQ-INV-SEL-004 — Efficiency of a parameter-choice rule against the grid oracle

`e_rule = ||x_(lambda_rule) - x_true|| / min_j ||x_(lambda_j) - x_true||`, the
minimum over the shared grid; `e = inf` when the rule makes no choice. The
discrepancy rule uses the expected whitened noise norm `delta = sqrt(m)`.

- Domain: synthetic families with a withheld truth; predeclared seeds and grid;
  identity penalty and `x_ref = 0` as in the study function.
- Meaning: `e = 1` is the best any rule could do on that grid; the median and
  the worst `e` over seeds, with the counts of boundary and missing choices,
  compare rules per family. The values describe these families and seeds only,
  not a general ranking of the rules.
- Code: `inverse.parameter_selection.selection_rule_study`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008, Section 5.1], [REF-VOGEL-2002].
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
  - [REF-VOGEL-2002](../references.md#ref-vogel-2002): Curtis R. Vogel. *Computational Methods for Inverse Problems*. SIAM, Philadelphia, 2002. Cited for the unbiased predictive risk estimator (UPRE) of the regularization parameter and total-variation regularization. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717570](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717570).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SEL-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sel-004--efficiency-of-a-parameter-choice-rule-against-the-grid-oracle)

## `probstat.inverse.pde_models`

### EQ-INV-PDE-001 — Conservative finite-volume diffusion system

`-(k u')' = q` on `(0, L)`, `u(0) = u_L`, `u(L) = u_R`; `h = L/n`; face
conductances `c_f`, jumps `w_f = u_f - u_(f-1)` with `u_(-1) = u_L`, `u_n = u_R`;
cell equations `c_i w_i - c_(i+1) w_(i+1) = h q_i` (`i = 0..n-1`), i.e.
`K(x) u = f(x)` with `K_ii = c_i + c_(i+1)`, `K_(i,i+1) = -c_(i+1)`,
`f_0 = h q_0 + c_0 u_L`, `f_(n-1) = h q_(n-1) + c_n u_R`.

- Domain: `n >= 2` cells, `q` sampled at cell centres, finite Dirichlet values.
- Meaning: second-order accurate in the cell-centre error on smooth
  solutions (observed order 2 in the tests); `K` is symmetric positive
  definite and diagonally dominant, so the Thomas solve is stable. The
  boundary face uses the adjacent cell and half-cell distance.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models.EllipticDiffusion1D`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde-001--conservative-finite-volume-diffusion-system)

### EQ-INV-PDE-002 — Log-conductivity and harmonic face averaging

`k_i = exp(x_i)`; interior face `k_f = 2 k_a k_b / (k_a + k_b)`,
`c_f = k_f / h`; boundary face `c_f = 2 k / h`. Derivatives:
`d log k_f / d x_a = k_b/(k_a + k_b)`, `d log k_f / d x_b = k_a/(k_a + k_b)`,
`d log c / d x = 1` at a boundary face.

- Domain: `|x_i| <= 345` (`k` in `[1e-150, 1e150]`); outside, `ValueError`.
- Meaning: positivity of `k` by construction; the harmonic mean is the
  flux-continuous average across a conductivity jump and is differentiated
  exactly (the derivative is of the discrete model actually solved).
- Code: `inverse.pde_models.EllipticDiffusion1D`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde-002--log-conductivity-and-harmonic-face-averaging)

### EQ-INV-PDE-003 — Tangent (forward sensitivity) solve

`K du = -(dr/dx)[v]`, `(dr/dx)[v]_i = e_i - e_(i+1)`,
`e_f = c_f (dlog c_f[v]) w_f`; `J v = H du`, where `r(u, x) = K u - f`.

- Domain: any admissible `x` and direction `v`; one tridiagonal solve with
  the stored factorization plus `O(n)` assembly.
- Meaning: exact directional derivative of the discrete sensor map
  `G(x) = H u(x) + b`, including the dependence of the boundary right-hand
  side on `x`.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models.EllipticDiffusion1D.jvp`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde-003--tangent-forward-sensitivity-solve)

### EQ-INV-PDE-004 — Adjoint solve and matrix-free vector-Jacobian product

`K' p = H' w`; `(J' w)_j = -sum_f c_f w_f (p_f - p_(f-1)) d log c_f / d x_j`
with `p_(-1) = p_n = 0`.

- Domain: any admissible `x`; one transpose solve and `O(n)` work per call,
  independent of the number of parameters.
- Meaning: equals `-p' (dK/dx_j) u + p' (df/dx_j)`. The Jacobian is never
  formed; the gradient of a scalar misfit costs one forward and one adjoint
  solve. The dot test `w'(J v) = (J'w)'v` holds to rounding.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models.EllipticDiffusion1D.vjp`,
  `inverse.pde_models.PDESolveCounts`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007], [REF-VOGEL-2002].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
  - [REF-VOGEL-2002](../references.md#ref-vogel-2002): Curtis R. Vogel. *Computational Methods for Inverse Problems*. SIAM, Philadelphia, 2002. Cited for the unbiased predictive risk estimator (UPRE) of the regularization parameter and total-variation regularization. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717570](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717570).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde-004--adjoint-solve-and-matrix-free-vector-jacobian-product)

### EQ-INV-PDE-005 — Thomas algorithm and its transpose

`A = L U` with `m_i = l_i / p_i`, `p_(i+1) = d_(i+1) - m_i u_i`; `A x = b` by
`y_(i+1) = b_(i+1) - m_i y_i`, back substitution with `p` and `u`; `A' x = b`
by `U' z = b`, `L' x = z`.

- Domain: tridiagonal `A` with nonzero pivots; no pivoting, so stability
  needs diagonal dominance or symmetric positive definiteness.
- Meaning: `O(n)` time and memory; zero or non-finite pivots raise
  `ValueError`. The factors are reused for forward, tangent and adjoint solves.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models.solve_tridiagonal`,
  `inverse.pde_models.solve_tridiagonal_transpose`,
  `inverse.pde_models.TridiagonalFactorization`.
- Source: [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013].
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde-005--thomas-algorithm-and-its-transpose)

### EQ-INV-PDE-006 — Discrete flux conservation diagnostic

`F_f = -c_f w_f`; cell balance `F_(i+1) - F_i = h q_i`; global balance
`F_n - F_0 = h sum_i q_i`.

- Domain: any admissible `x`.
- Meaning: the finite-volume scheme conserves the source exactly up to the
  linear-solve rounding; the reported residuals are `O(eps)` relative to the
  flux scale, independent of `h`.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models.EllipticDiffusion1D.face_fluxes`,
  `inverse.pde_models.EllipticDiffusion1D.flux_balance`,
  `inverse.pde_models.FluxBalance`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde-006--discrete-flux-conservation-diagnostic)

### EQ-INV-PDE-007 — Sensor interpolation operator

`G_j = (1 - t_j) U_(m_j) + t_j U_(m_j + 1)`, where `U` is the profile with
nodes `0, (i + 1/2) h, L` carrying `(u_L, u_0, ..., u_(n-1), u_R)` and
`s_j` lies in `[node_m, node_(m+1)]`, `t_j = (s_j - node_m)/(node_(m+1) - node_m)`.

- Domain: sensors strictly inside `(0, L)`.
- Meaning: affine in `u`: `G = H u + b`, with `b` the boundary-value
  contribution; `H` has at most two entries per row and `H'` is applied in
  `O(m)`. `b` does not enter derivatives.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models.EllipticDiffusion1D.value`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde-007--sensor-interpolation-operator)

### EQ-INV-PDE-008 — Weighted least-squares misfit gradient by the adjoint

`Phi(x) = 1/2 ||W (G(x) - y)||^2`, `W = diag(1/sigma_j)`,
`grad Phi = J(x)' W' W (G(x) - y)`.

- Domain: positive finite `sigma`.
- Meaning: one forward and one adjoint solve give the whole gradient;
  adding a prior or regularization term is the caller's responsibility.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models.adjoint_gradient`,
  `inverse.pde_models.AdjointGradient`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007], [REF-VOGEL-2002].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
  - [REF-VOGEL-2002](../references.md#ref-vogel-2002): Curtis R. Vogel. *Computational Methods for Inverse Problems*. SIAM, Philadelphia, 2002. Cited for the unbiased predictive risk estimator (UPRE) of the regularization parameter and total-variation regularization. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717570](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717570).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde-008--weighted-least-squares-misfit-gradient-by-the-adjoint)

## `probstat.inverse.pde_models_2d`

### EQ-INV-PDE2-001 — Two-dimensional cell-centred finite-volume diffusion

`-div(k grad u) = q` on `(0, Lx) x (0, Ly)`, `u = 0` on the boundary; cells
`p = j nx + i`, `hx = Lx/nx`, `hy = Ly/ny`; cell balance
`sum_(f of p) c_f (u_p - u_(nbr f)) = hx hy q_p` (`u_nbr = 0` across the
boundary), i.e. `K(x) u = f`, `f_p = hx hy q_p`.

- Domain: `nx, ny >= 2`, finite source sampled at cell centres, homogeneous
  Dirichlet values.
- Meaning: five-point scheme, second-order accurate in the cell-centre error
  for smooth `k` and `u` (measured rates 1.86, 1.97, 1.99 for `k = 1` and
  1.98, 2.02, 1.99 for `k = 1 + x y` on 4, 8, 16, 32 cells per side);
  `K` is symmetric positive definite.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models_2d.EllipticDiffusion2D`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE2-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde2-001--two-dimensional-cell-centred-finite-volume-diffusion)

### EQ-INV-PDE2-002 — Log-conductivity and harmonic face conductances in 2D

`k_p = exp(x_p)`; interior `x`-face `c_f = 2 k_a k_b / (k_a + k_b) hy/hx`,
interior `y`-face `c_f = 2 k_a k_b / (k_a + k_b) hx/hy`; boundary face
`c_f = 2 k_a hy/hx` (resp. `hx/hy`); face weights
`d c_f / d x_a = c_f k_b / (k_a + k_b)`, `d c_f / d x_b = c_f k_a / (k_a + k_b)`
(boundary face: weight 1 on the single adjacent cell).

- Domain: `|x_p| <= 345` (otherwise `exp` over- or underflows and the call
  raises `ValueError`).
- Meaning: positivity of `k` by construction; the harmonic mean is the
  flux-conserving average of a piecewise-constant conductivity.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models_2d.EllipticDiffusion2D`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE2-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde2-002--log-conductivity-and-harmonic-face-conductances-in-2d)

### EQ-INV-PDE2-003 — Tangent solve in 2D

`K du = -(dK/dx)[v] u`, `((dK/dx)[v] u)_p = sum_(f of p) c_f (w_a v_a + w_b v_b)
(u_p - u_(nbr f))`; `J(x) v = H du`.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-PDE2-001.
- Meaning: exact derivative of the discrete model (up to the solver
  tolerance); one solve per direction, so a full Jacobian costs one solve per
  parameter.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models_2d.EllipticDiffusion2D.jvp`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE2-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde2-003--tangent-solve-in-2d)

### EQ-INV-PDE2-004 — Exact discrete adjoint with the same matrix

`K p = H' w` (`K = K'`), `(J' w)_i = -p' (dK/dx_i) u = -sum_(f of i) c_f
w_(i,f) (p_a - p_b)(u_a - u_b)`.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-PDE2-001.
- Meaning: the gradient of a scalar misfit costs one forward and one adjoint
  solve whatever the number of parameters (verified by the solve counters);
  the dot test `w'(J v) = (J' w)' v` holds to roundoff for the Cholesky solver
  and to the PCG tolerance otherwise.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models_2d.EllipticDiffusion2D.vjp`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE2-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde2-004--exact-discrete-adjoint-with-the-same-matrix)

### EQ-INV-PDE2-005 — Linear solves: Jacobi-PCG and banded Cholesky, with reported residual

Jacobi-preconditioned CG, `z = r / diag(K)`, stop at `||r|| <= tol ||b||`
(default `tol = 1e-12`, cap `max(200, 4 N)`); banded Cholesky `K = L L'` with
bandwidth `nx`. After every solve the TRUE residual `||b - K u|| / ||b||` is
recomputed; converged iff it is `<= 10 tol` (PCG) or `<= 1e-8` (Cholesky) and
all pivots are positive.

- Domain: `K` symmetric positive definite (guaranteed by the boundary faces).
- Meaning: a non-converged solve is never hidden: `value` returns `nan`,
  `state`/`jvp`/`vjp` raise `LinearSolverError`, `linear_failures` counts it.
  The solver tolerance is part of the inference error.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models_2d.LinearSolveRecord`.
- Source: [REF-HESTENES-STIEFEL-1952], [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013].
  - [REF-HESTENES-STIEFEL-1952](../references.md#ref-hestenes-stiefel-1952): Magnus R. Hestenes and Eduard Stiefel. "Methods of Conjugate Gradients for Solving Linear Systems." *Journal of Research of the National Bureau of Standards* 49(6), 409-436, 1952. Cited for the conjugate gradient method and its least-squares variant CGLS. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26; Crossref lists the first page only). DOI: [10.6028/jres.049.044](https://doi.org/10.6028/jres.049.044).
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE2-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde2-005--linear-solves-jacobi-pcg-and-banded-cholesky-with-reported-residual)

### EQ-INV-PDE2-006 — Sensor operators in 2D

Point sensor: tensor-product hat interpolation of the cell-centre values
through zero boundary values; window sensor: `y = mean of u_p` over cells whose
centres satisfy `|c_x - s_x| <= r`, `|c_y - s_y| <= r`.

- Domain: sensors strictly inside the rectangle; a window must contain at
  least one cell centre.
- Meaning: `H` is linear and sparse and is part of the discrete model, so the
  adjoint is exact for it; point readings carry an `O(h^2)` interpolation
  error.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models_2d.EllipticDiffusion2D`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE2-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde2-006--sensor-operators-in-2d)

### EQ-INV-PDE2-007 — Global flux balance in 2D

`sum_(boundary faces) c_f u_a = sum_p hx hy q_p`.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-PDE2-001.
- Meaning: summing the cell balances telescopes the interior face fluxes;
  the difference is a conservation diagnostic (roundoff level for a converged
  solve).
- Code: `inverse.pde_models_2d.EllipticDiffusion2D.flux_balance`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE2-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde2-007--global-flux-balance-in-2d)

### EQ-INV-PDE2-008 — Explicit Euler step of the heat equation

`u^(k+1) = T u^k`, `T = I - dt D`, `D = (kappa / h^2) tridiag(-1, 2, -1)`,
`h = L/(n+1)`, i.e. `u^(k+1)_j = u^k_j + r (u^k_(j-1) - 2 u^k_j + u^k_(j+1))`,
`r = kappa dt / h^2`.

- Domain: `kappa, dt, L > 0`, zero Dirichlet values, `n >= 2`.
- Meaning: first order in time; `T` is symmetric, so one backward step is the
  transposed step.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models_2d.HeatExplicitEuler1D`.
- Source: [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013].
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE2-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde2-008--explicit-euler-step-of-the-heat-equation)

### EQ-INV-PDE2-009 — Stability limit and Fourier amplification factors

`g_j = 1 - 4 r sin^2(j pi / (2 (n+1)))`, `j = 1..n` (2D: `1 - 4 r (sin^2_j +
sin^2_k)`); stability `|g_j| <= 1` for all modes is guaranteed for
`dt <= h^2 / (2 d kappa)`.

- Domain: `d = 1, 2, 3`; the factors are exact eigenvalues of `T`.
- Meaning: above the limit the highest-frequency modes have `g < -1` and grow
  as `|g|^N` (measured and predicted growth agree to 1e-8 in the log); the
  grid-independent limit is conservative for a finite grid.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models_2d.explicit_stability`.
- Source: [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013].
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE2-009](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde2-009--stability-limit-and-fourier-amplification-factors)

### EQ-INV-PDE2-010 — Exact transpose of the explicit scheme

`A u0 = (P_k T^k u0)_k`, `A' y = sum_k (T')^k P_k' y_k`, evaluated by the
backward sweep `mu <- 0`; for `k = N..1`: `mu <- mu + P_k' y_k` (if step `k` is
observed), then `mu <- T' mu`; the result is `mu`.

- Domain: observed steps `k in 1..N`, observed nodes `P_k` (selection).
- Meaning: the adjoint of the discrete map is its exact transpose, not the
  discretized continuous adjoint; verified against the materialized matrix.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models_2d.HeatExplicitEuler1D`.
- Source: [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013].
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE2-010](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde2-010--exact-transpose-of-the-explicit-scheme)

### EQ-INV-PDE2-011 — Refusal policy for an unstable forward step

Construction of `HeatExplicitEuler1D` requires `dt <= h^2 / (2 kappa)` unless
`allow_unstable=True`; then `growth_bound = max_j |g_j|^N` is reported and
`explicit_heat_inverse_problem` refuses again unless `allow_unstable=True`.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-PDE2-008.
- Meaning: an inverse problem on an unstable forward map has a forward
  operator whose norm grows like `growth_bound`, so noise and roundoff are
  amplified without control; two explicit flags are needed to proceed.
  Overflow of an allowed unstable run raises (`ValueError` from the operator
  validation) instead of returning infinities.
- Code: `inverse.pde_models_2d.explicit_heat_inverse_problem`.
- Source: [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013].
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PDE2-011](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-pde2-011--refusal-policy-for-an-unstable-forward-step)

## `probstat.inverse.posterior_sampling`

### EQ-INV-SMP-001 — Tempered SMC evidence on a whitened inverse-problem posterior

`log Z_hat = sum_{t=1}^T log( (1/N) sum_i L(x_i)^(beta_t - beta_{t-1}) )`,
with `L(z) = exp(log_likelihood(z) + c)` and `c = -m/2 log 2 pi - 1/2 log det
Gamma`, particles drawn from the `N(0, I)` prior of the whitened coordinate
`z` and mapped back by `x = mu + P z`.

- Domain: `y = G(x) + eps`, `eps ~ N(0, Gamma)`, `x ~ N(mu, P P')`;
  `L` finite or zero. The library resamples at every stage, so the number
  of resampling steps equals the number of stages `T`.
- Meaning: with the normalizing constant `c` included, `Z_hat` estimates the
  marginal likelihood `p(y)` (usable for Bayes factors on the same data
  measure); for a linear-Gaussian `G` it is compared with the closed-form
  normal log density of `y`. The one-run Lee-Whiteley standard error
  excludes the bias of a poorly mixing mutation kernel and ignores the
  adaptive schedule.
- Code: `inverse.posterior_sampling.tempered_smc_from_target`,
  `inverse.posterior_sampling.tempered_smc_posterior`.
- Source: [REF-DELMORAL-DOUCET-JASRA-2006]; [REF-LEE-WHITELEY-2018].
  - [REF-DELMORAL-DOUCET-JASRA-2006](../references.md#ref-delmoral-doucet-jasra-2006): Pierre Del Moral, Arnaud Doucet, and Ajay Jasra. "Sequential Monte Carlo Samplers." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B (Statistical Methodology)* 68(3), 411-436, 2006. Tempered sequences of targets, backward kernels, and the SMC estimate of normalizing constants. DOI: [10.1111/j.1467-9868.2006.00553.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1467-9868.2006.00553.x).
  - [REF-LEE-WHITELEY-2018](../references.md#ref-lee-whiteley-2018): Anthony Lee and Nick Whiteley. "Variance Estimation in the Particle Filter." *Biometrika* 105(3), 609-625, 2018. Unbiased and consistent variance estimators from the genealogy (Eve indices) of one particle run. DOI: [10.1093/biomet/asy028](https://doi.org/10.1093/biomet/asy028).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SMP-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-smp-001--tempered-smc-evidence-on-a-whitened-inverse-problem-posterior)

### EQ-INV-SMP-002 — Run-to-run evidence and mean uncertainty from repeated SMC

`mean_R = (1/R) sum_r log Z_hat_r`, `s_R^2 = (1/(R-1)) sum_r (log Z_hat_r -
mean_R)^2`, `se_R = s_R / sqrt(R)`; the same for each posterior-mean
coordinate across the `R` runs, under distinct child seeds.

- Domain: `R >= 2` independent runs (distinct `RandomStream.child` seeds),
  at least one converged.
- Meaning: an honest Monte Carlo error of the adaptive algorithm as run
  (schedule included). `se_R` is itself noisy for small `R`; tests use a
  multiple of it plus a floor for the `O(1/N)` evidence bias.
- Code: `inverse.posterior_sampling.repeat_tempered_smc`.
- Source: [REF-DELMORAL-DOUCET-JASRA-2006].
  - [REF-DELMORAL-DOUCET-JASRA-2006](../references.md#ref-delmoral-doucet-jasra-2006): Pierre Del Moral, Arnaud Doucet, and Ajay Jasra. "Sequential Monte Carlo Samplers." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B (Statistical Methodology)* 68(3), 411-436, 2006. Tempered sequences of targets, backward kernels, and the SMC estimate of normalizing constants. DOI: [10.1111/j.1467-9868.2006.00553.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1467-9868.2006.00553.x).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SMP-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-smp-002--run-to-run-evidence-and-mean-uncertainty-from-repeated-smc)

### EQ-INV-SMP-003 — Acceptance of pCN versus random-walk Metropolis at equal scale

pCN: `z' = sqrt(1 - beta^2) z + beta xi`, `alpha_pCN = min(1, exp(l(z') -
l(z)))`, `l = log_likelihood`. Random walk: `z' = z + beta xi`, `alpha_RW =
min(1, exp(l(z') - l(z) - (|z'|^2 - |z|^2)/2))`, `xi ~ N(0, I_n)`.

- Domain: whitened prior `N(0, I_n)`; same fixed `beta`, no adaptation. The
  study problem has `u(s) = sum_k x_k cos(k pi s)`, prior std `k^-decay`,
  `m` point observations at `s_j = j/(m+1)`.
- Meaning: `alpha_pCN` involves the likelihood only, which converges as the
  discretisation is refined; `alpha_RW` also contains the prior ratio, whose
  log has a variance growing like `beta^2 n`. The study measures both; the
  values are reported, not assumed. No dimension-independence is claimed for
  nonlinear targets.
- Code: `inverse.posterior_sampling.pcn_dimension_study`.
- Source: [REF-COTTER-ROBERTS-STUART-WHITE-2013]; [REF-ROBERTS-ROSENTHAL-2001].
  - [REF-COTTER-ROBERTS-STUART-WHITE-2013](../references.md#ref-cotter-roberts-stuart-white-2013): Simon L. Cotter, Gareth O. Roberts, Andrew M. Stuart, and David White. "MCMC Methods for Functions: Modifying Old Algorithms to Make Them Faster." *Statistical Science* 28(3), 424-446, 2013. The preconditioned Crank-Nicolson proposal. DOI: [10.1214/13-STS421](https://doi.org/10.1214/13-STS421).
  - [REF-ROBERTS-ROSENTHAL-2001](../references.md#ref-roberts-rosenthal-2001): Gareth O. Roberts and Jeffrey S. Rosenthal. "Optimal Scaling for Various Metropolis-Hastings Algorithms." *Statistical Science* 16(4), 351-367, 2001. DOI: [10.1214/ss/1015346320](https://doi.org/10.1214/ss/1015346320).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SMP-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-smp-003--acceptance-of-pcn-versus-random-walk-metropolis-at-equal-scale)

### EQ-INV-SMP-004 — Tensor Gauss-Legendre reference integrals

`Z = sum_i w_i f(x_i)`, `E[x] = sum_i w_i f(x_i) x_i / Z`, `Cov = sum_i w_i
f(x_i) (x_i - E[x])(x_i - E[x])' / Z`, on the composite tensor rule with
`p_d` panels of `q` Gauss-Legendre nodes per dimension of a box.

- Domain: dimension 1 or 2, finite box, `f = exp(log_density)` computed with
  a max-shift; at most two million grid points.
- Meaning: for the whitened target the integrand is `p(y | z) N(z; 0, I)`,
  so `log Z` is the evidence `log p(y)`. Accuracy is spectral for smooth
  `f` once panels resolve its features; the doubled-panel change of `log Z`
  and of the mean is reported, and a warning is attached when it exceeds
  the tolerance.
- Code: `inverse.posterior_sampling.quadrature_reference`,
  `inverse.posterior_sampling.reference_for_target`.
- Source: [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984]; [REF-GOLUB-WELSCH-1969].
  - [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984](../references.md#ref-davis-rabinowitz-1984): Philip J. Davis and Philip Rabinowitz. *Methods of Numerical Integration*. 2nd edition. Academic Press, 1984. ISBN 978-0-12-206360-2.
  - [REF-GOLUB-WELSCH-1969](../references.md#ref-golub-welsch-1969): Gene H. Golub and John H. Welsch. "Calculation of Gauss Quadrature Rules." *Mathematics of Computation* 23(106), 221-230, 1969.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SMP-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-smp-004--tensor-gauss-legendre-reference-integrals)

### EQ-INV-SMP-005 — Marginal quantiles and the truncation mass estimate

Marginal density at a node: `m_k / w_k` (marginal mass over weight); within a
panel the degree `q - 1` Lagrange interpolant integrated by Gauss-Legendre;
the quantile solves `F(x) = p` by bisection. Truncation estimate per
marginal end: `T = a r / (1 - r)` with `a` the outer-panel mass and `r = a /
b`, `b` the next-inner panel mass (`T = a` and a warning when `r >= 1`).

- Domain: marginal panel masses `a, b >= 0`.
- Meaning: the truncation figure is a heuristic geometric-tail
  extrapolation, summed over both ends of every marginal; it is an estimate,
  not a bound, and it can understate a heavy tail.
- Code: `inverse.posterior_sampling.quadrature_reference`.
- Source: [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984].
  - [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984](../references.md#ref-davis-rabinowitz-1984): Philip J. Davis and Philip Rabinowitz. *Methods of Numerical Integration*. 2nd edition. Academic Press, 1984. ISBN 978-0-12-206360-2.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SMP-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-smp-005--marginal-quantiles-and-the-truncation-mass-estimate)

### EQ-INV-SMP-006 — Sampler validation against a reference and mode occupation

`e_mean = |mean_s - mean_ref| / sd_ref`, `rho = sd_s / sd_ref`, `e_q =
max_p |q_s(p) - q_ref(p)| / sd_ref`, and for a region `R`: `pi_ref(R) =
sum_{x_i in R} w_i f(x_i) / Z`, `pi_s(R) = (1/N) #{s_j in R}`; status
`missed` when `pi_s < pi_ref / 10` and `pi_ref` exceeds the tolerance.

- Domain: samples in the same coordinates as the reference; tolerances
  chosen from the Monte Carlo error of the run.
- Meaning: a screening comparison, not a convergence proof. A region mass is
  exact only when the region boundary coincides with panel boundaries. The
  median and other quantiles of a multimodal law are ill-conditioned in
  `sd_ref` units. A missed mode is reported as a failure; no sampler is
  asserted to find all modes.
- Code: `inverse.posterior_sampling.compare_to_reference`,
  `inverse.posterior_sampling.chain_region_fractions`.
- Source: [REF-COTTER-ROBERTS-STUART-WHITE-2013]; [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984].
  - [REF-COTTER-ROBERTS-STUART-WHITE-2013](../references.md#ref-cotter-roberts-stuart-white-2013): Simon L. Cotter, Gareth O. Roberts, Andrew M. Stuart, and David White. "MCMC Methods for Functions: Modifying Old Algorithms to Make Them Faster." *Statistical Science* 28(3), 424-446, 2013. The preconditioned Crank-Nicolson proposal. DOI: [10.1214/13-STS421](https://doi.org/10.1214/13-STS421).
  - [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984](../references.md#ref-davis-rabinowitz-1984): Philip J. Davis and Philip Rabinowitz. *Methods of Numerical Integration*. 2nd edition. Academic Press, 1984. ISBN 978-0-12-206360-2.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SMP-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-smp-006--sampler-validation-against-a-reference-and-mode-occupation)

## `probstat.inverse.posterior_targets`

### EQ-INV-TGT-001 — Prior-whitened coordinates and posterior density

`x = mu + P z`, `z ~ N(0, I_r)`, `C0 = P P'`, `Gamma = C C'`, `W = C^-1`:
`log pi(z | y) = -1/2 ||W (G(mu + P z) - y)||^2 - 1/2 ||z||^2 + const`, with
likelihood constant `-m/2 log 2 pi - 1/2 log det Gamma` and prior constant
`-r/2 log 2 pi`.

- Domain: `G: R^n -> R^m` differentiable, `P` an `n x r` factor (`r` columns,
  possibly rectangular so that `C0` is singular), `Gamma` positive definite,
  Gaussian additive noise.
- Meaning: the density is that of `z`. The map `z -> x` is affine with the
  constant Jacobian `P`, so no `z`-dependent Jacobian is omitted; a change of
  variables to `x` only adds the constant `log |det P|` (square `P`). The
  prior is part of `log_density` and must not be added again by a sampler that
  already treats it as reference law ([EQ-INV-TGT-003]). A failed (non-finite)
  forward value is a zero likelihood (`-inf`), never clipped.
- Code: `inverse.posterior_targets.WhitenedGaussianPriorTarget`.
- Source: [REF-STUART-2010], [REF-COTTER-ROBERTS-STUART-WHITE-2013],
  [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-STUART-2010](../references.md#ref-stuart-2010): Andrew M. Stuart. "Inverse Problems: A Bayesian Perspective." *Acta Numerica* 19, 451-559, 2010. Cited for the Gaussian linear inverse problem, its posterior mean and covariance, function-space priors, and weighted inner products. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/S0962492910000061](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0962492910000061).
  - [REF-COTTER-ROBERTS-STUART-WHITE-2013](../references.md#ref-cotter-roberts-stuart-white-2013): Simon L. Cotter, Gareth O. Roberts, Andrew M. Stuart, and David White. "MCMC Methods for Functions: Modifying Old Algorithms to Make Them Faster." *Statistical Science* 28(3), 424-446, 2013. The preconditioned Crank-Nicolson proposal. DOI: [10.1214/13-STS421](https://doi.org/10.1214/13-STS421).
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-TGT-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-tgt-001--prior-whitened-coordinates-and-posterior-density)

### EQ-INV-TGT-002 — Gradient of the whitened log posterior by one adjoint product

`grad_z log pi(z | y) = -P' J(x)' W' W (G(x) - y) - z`, `x = mu + P z`.

- Domain: `G` continuously differentiable at `x`; `J(x)'` available as an
  adjoint action (`vjp`), `W' W = Gamma^-1`.
- Meaning: one forward value (cached from the density call at the same `z`)
  and one `vjp` per gradient; the Jacobian is never formed. Exactness of the
  gradient depends on the `jvp`/`vjp` adjoint pair of the model
  (`check_derivatives`).
- Code: `inverse.posterior_targets.WhitenedGaussianPriorTarget.gradient`.
- Source: [REF-STUART-2010], [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-STUART-2010](../references.md#ref-stuart-2010): Andrew M. Stuart. "Inverse Problems: A Bayesian Perspective." *Acta Numerica* 19, 451-559, 2010. Cited for the Gaussian linear inverse problem, its posterior mean and covariance, function-space priors, and weighted inner products. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/S0962492910000061](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0962492910000061).
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-TGT-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-tgt-002--gradient-of-the-whitened-log-posterior-by-one-adjoint-product)

### EQ-INV-TGT-003 — pCN acceptance uses the likelihood of the whitened target only

`z' = sqrt(1 - beta^2) z + beta xi`, `xi ~ N(0, I)`;
`alpha = min(1, exp(l(z') - l(z)))`, `l(z) = -1/2 ||W (G(mu + P z) - y)||^2`.

- Domain: `0 < beta <= 1`; target `pi(z) proportional to exp(l(z)) N(z; 0, I)`.
- Meaning: the proposal is reversible with respect to `N(0, I)`, so the
  Gaussian factors cancel and only the likelihood enters. Passing the full
  `log_density` to pCN would count the prior twice. No dimension-independent
  mixing is claimed for an arbitrary nonlinear `G`; test chosen functionals
  under refinement.
- Code: `inverse.posterior_targets.sample_pcn`.
- Source: [REF-COTTER-ROBERTS-STUART-WHITE-2013], [REF-STUART-2010].
  - [REF-COTTER-ROBERTS-STUART-WHITE-2013](../references.md#ref-cotter-roberts-stuart-white-2013): Simon L. Cotter, Gareth O. Roberts, Andrew M. Stuart, and David White. "MCMC Methods for Functions: Modifying Old Algorithms to Make Them Faster." *Statistical Science* 28(3), 424-446, 2013. The preconditioned Crank-Nicolson proposal. DOI: [10.1214/13-STS421](https://doi.org/10.1214/13-STS421).
  - [REF-STUART-2010](../references.md#ref-stuart-2010): Andrew M. Stuart. "Inverse Problems: A Bayesian Perspective." *Acta Numerica* 19, 451-559, 2010. Cited for the Gaussian linear inverse problem, its posterior mean and covariance, function-space priors, and weighted inner products. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1017/S0962492910000061](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0962492910000061).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-TGT-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-tgt-003--pcn-acceptance-uses-the-likelihood-of-the-whitened-target-only)

### EQ-INV-TGT-004 — Rank uniformity of simulation-based calibration, linear-Gaussian case

`theta ~ N(mu, C0)`, `y = A theta + eps`, `eps ~ N(0, Gamma)`,
`theta_1..L ~ N(m(y), C_post(y))` exact: `r_k = #{l : theta_{l,k} < theta_k}`
is uniform on `{0, ..., L}` for each coordinate `k`; the chi-square statistic
`sum_b (O_b - E_b)^2 / E_b` over `B` rank bins has about `B - 1` degrees of
freedom, with p-value the chi-square survival function.

- Domain: independent (or thinned) posterior draws; continuous parameters
  (ties broken at random by the helper).
- Meaning: a screening heuristic for the sampler. It holds for the simulated
  model only and does not validate the real-world forward model, prior or
  noise law. Correlated draws make a correct sampler look over-dispersed. A
  per-coordinate threshold such as `p > 1e-3` is not a calibrated family-wise
  test.
- Code: `inverse.posterior_targets.simulation_based_calibration_linear_gaussian`.
- Source: [REF-TALTS-ETAL-2018], [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007].
  - [REF-TALTS-ETAL-2018](../references.md#ref-talts-etal-2018): Sean Talts, Michael Betancourt, Daniel Simpson, Aki Vehtari, and Andrew Gelman. "Validating Bayesian Inference Algorithms with Simulation-Based Calibration." Preprint, 2018. [arXiv:1804.06788](https://arxiv.org/abs/1804.06788).
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-TGT-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-tgt-004--rank-uniformity-of-simulation-based-calibration-linear-gaussian-case)

## `probstat.inverse.prediction`

### EQ-CAL-PRED-001 — Posterior push-forward prediction

Latent: `y*_s = g_q(theta^(s); c*)`; observed:
`y*_s + e_s`, `e_s ~ s_q^(s) N(0,1)` or `s_q^(s) t_nu`, with
`s_q^(s)^2 = sigma_q^(s)^2 + sigma_delta_q^2`; quantiles of the samples give
predictive intervals.

- Domain: posterior draws `theta^(s)` (and `sigma^(s)` when calibrated) and
  declared prediction conditions `c*`.
- Meaning: separates parameter uncertainty (latent) from measurement noise
  (observed), closing the calibration-to-prediction loop; extrapolation in
  `c*` is computable but not validated by the calibration data.
- Code: `inverse.predict`, `inverse.PredictedQuantity`.
- Source: [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013, Section 6.3] and
  [REF-SMITH-UQ-2024, Chapter 8].
  - [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-gelman-etal-2013): Andrew Gelman, John B. Carlin, Hal S. Stern, David B. Dunson, Aki Vehtari, and Donald B. Rubin. *Bayesian Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. CRC Press, 2013. ISBN 978-1-4398-4095-5.
  - [REF-SMITH-UQ-2024](../references.md#ref-smith-uq-2024): Ralph C. Smith. *Uncertainty Quantification: Theory, Implementation, and Applications*. 2nd edition. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2024. DOI: [10.1137/1.9781611977844](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9781611977844).
- Registry: [EQ-CAL-PRED-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-cal-pred-001--posterior-push-forward-prediction)

## `probstat.inverse.profile_extended`

### EQ-INV-PLX-001 — Two-dimensional profile negative log-likelihood

`nll_prof(a, b) = min_{theta_rest} nll(theta with theta_i = a, theta_j = b)`, on a rectangular grid `grid_a x grid_b`, visited along a serpentine path (row 0 left to right, row 1 right to left, ...).

- Domain: `nll` finite at the supplied estimate; both grids strictly increasing and bracketing the estimate; the grids respect the bounds of their parameters.
- Meaning: consecutive path cells are grid neighbours, so each cell is warm-started from the previous good cell's nuisance optimum and also from the nuisance block of the estimate; the lower value wins. Every cell carries `converged`, `start` and the optimizer message. A cell with no finite start is stored as `inf` with status `failed`; a finite cell whose optimizer did not meet its tolerance has status `not_converged`. Neither is interpolated or filled. A value below `nll(estimate)` sets `improved_estimate_found`, and the baseline is the better value (not clipped), as in EQ-INV-PROF-002. For a linear Gaussian model the profile is exactly the quadratic form `(t - t_hat)' F_eff (t - t_hat)` with `F_eff` the Schur complement of EQ-INV-PROF-004.
- Code: `inverse.profile_extended.profile_likelihood_2d`.
- Source: [REF-RAUE-ETAL-2009], [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-RAUE-ETAL-2009](../references.md#ref-raue-etal-2009): A. Raue, C. Kreutz, T. Maiwald, J. Bachmann, M. Schilling, U. Klingmüller, and J. Timmer. "Structural and Practical Identifiability Analysis of Partially Observed Dynamical Models by Exploiting the Profile Likelihood." *Bioinformatics* 25(15), 1923-1929, 2009. Cited for profile-likelihood confidence intervals and the distinction between structural and practical non-identifiability. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1093/bioinformatics/btp358](https://doi.org/10.1093/bioinformatics/btp358).
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PLX-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-plx-001--two-dimensional-profile-negative-log-likelihood)

### EQ-INV-PLX-002 — Joint chi-square(2) likelihood-ratio region

`R_alpha = { (a, b) : 2 (nll_prof(a, b) - nll_min) <= chi2_{2, 1-alpha} }`, intersected with the grid.

- Domain: regular asymptotics (interior estimate, identifiable pair, smooth likelihood, large sample).
- Meaning: a cell is `inside` only if its optimization converged and its likelihood ratio is at most the threshold, `outside` if converged and above, and `unreliable` if it failed or did not converge (no membership is claimed). A region with an inside cell on the grid edge is reported with `touches_grid_edge` (it may extend beyond the grid); `components` counts 4-connected inside components (multimodality); `area_estimate` is the inside-cell count times the cell area on uniform grids and carries a first-order grid-resolution error. Boundaries, weak identifiability, multimodality and small samples violate the calibration; use EQ-INV-PLX-003.
- Code: `inverse.profile_extended.profile_region_2d`.
- Source: [REF-RAUE-ETAL-2009], [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003].
  - [REF-RAUE-ETAL-2009](../references.md#ref-raue-etal-2009): A. Raue, C. Kreutz, T. Maiwald, J. Bachmann, M. Schilling, U. Klingmüller, and J. Timmer. "Structural and Practical Identifiability Analysis of Partially Observed Dynamical Models by Exploiting the Profile Likelihood." *Bioinformatics* 25(15), 1923-1929, 2009. Cited for profile-likelihood confidence intervals and the distinction between structural and practical non-identifiability. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1093/bioinformatics/btp358](https://doi.org/10.1093/bioinformatics/btp358).
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PLX-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-plx-002--joint-chi-square2-likelihood-ratio-region)

### EQ-INV-PLX-003 — Parametric-bootstrap likelihood-ratio statistic
