`LR*_b = 2 (c_b - min(g_b, c_b))`, `c_b = min_{theta: theta_I = theta_hat_I} nll_b(theta)`, `g_b = min_theta nll_b(theta)`, `nll_b` the negative log-likelihood of data `D_b ~ model(theta_hat)`, `b = 1..B`.

- Domain: a simulator of the fitted model and a likelihood for simulated data; bounds, if any, are part of both minimizations.
- Meaning: the simulated truth of the interest coordinates `I` is their fitted value, so `LR*` is the null distribution of the profile likelihood-ratio statistic under the fitted model (including boundary and weak-identifiability effects the chi-square law ignores). Replicate `b` uses the stream `seed/"replicate"/b`. A replicate whose global or constrained fit did not converge is dropped and counted (`failed_replicates`), never imputed; dropping can bias the quantile. When the constrained value beats the global one the baseline is the constrained value (counted in `baseline_refits`), not a clipped difference. The simulation truth is an estimate, so the calibration inherits its error.
- Code: `inverse.profile_extended.bootstrap_lr_calibration`.
- Source: [REF-DAVISON-HINKLEY-1997], [REF-SELF-LIANG-1987], [REF-RAUE-ETAL-2009].
  - [REF-DAVISON-HINKLEY-1997](../references.md#ref-davison-hinkley-1997): A. C. Davison and D. V. Hinkley. *Bootstrap Methods and Their Application*. Cambridge University Press, 1997.
  - [REF-SELF-LIANG-1987](../references.md#ref-self-liang-1987): Steven G. Self and Kung-Yee Liang. "Asymptotic Properties of Maximum Likelihood Estimators and Likelihood Ratio Tests under Nonstandard Conditions." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 82(398), 605-610, 1987. Mixtures of chi-square laws as the limit of the likelihood ratio when the null parameter lies on the boundary. DOI: [10.1080/01621459.1987.10478472](https://doi.org/10.1080/01621459.1987.10478472).
  - [REF-RAUE-ETAL-2009](../references.md#ref-raue-etal-2009): A. Raue, C. Kreutz, T. Maiwald, J. Bachmann, M. Schilling, U. Klingmüller, and J. Timmer. "Structural and Practical Identifiability Analysis of Partially Observed Dynamical Models by Exploiting the Profile Likelihood." *Bioinformatics* 25(15), 1923-1929, 2009. Cited for profile-likelihood confidence intervals and the distinction between structural and practical non-identifiability. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1093/bioinformatics/btp358](https://doi.org/10.1093/bioinformatics/btp358).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PLX-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-plx-003--parametric-bootstrap-likelihood-ratio-statistic)

### EQ-INV-PLX-004 — Bootstrap quantile with Monte Carlo uncertainty

`q_hat = LR*_(k)`, `k = ceil(level (n + 1))`; interval ranks `l = max(1, floor(n p - z sqrt(n p (1-p))))`, `u = ceil(n p + z sqrt(n p (1-p))) + 1`, `p = level`, `z = 1.96`; `se(q_hat) = (LR*_(u) - LR*_(l)) / (2 z)`; realized level `P_hat = #{LR*_b <= chi2_{d, level}} / n` with standard error `sqrt(P_hat (1 - P_hat) / n)`.

- Domain: `n` valid replicates; `d` the number of interest coordinates.
- Meaning: the order-statistic interval is distribution-free and approximate (normal approximation to the binomial rank count); an end is `None` when its rank exceeds `n`, and then no standard error is reported. `discrepancy_in_se = (q_hat - chi2_threshold) / se` and `chi2_within_ci` compare with the asymptotic threshold; `coverage_at_chi2` is the realized level of the chi-square test under the bootstrap null (nominal `level` when regular, larger than nominal at a boundary).
- Code: `inverse.profile_extended.BootstrapCalibrationResult`.
- Source: [REF-DAVISON-HINKLEY-1997].
  - [REF-DAVISON-HINKLEY-1997](../references.md#ref-davison-hinkley-1997): A. C. Davison and D. V. Hinkley. *Bootstrap Methods and Their Application*. Cambridge University Press, 1997.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PLX-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-plx-004--bootstrap-quantile-with-monte-carlo-uncertainty)

### EQ-INV-PLX-005 — Forward sensitivity equations

`y' = f(t, y, theta)`, `S = dy/dtheta`, `S' = (df/dy) S + df/dtheta`, `S(t0) = dy0/dtheta`; observation sensitivity `d(C y)/dtheta = C S`.

- Domain: `f` smooth in `(y, theta)`; `y` of dimension `n`, `theta` of dimension `p`.
- Meaning: the augmented system `(y, S)` of dimension `n (1 + p)` is integrated by the library's fixed-step classical Runge-Kutta method (EQ-NUM-ODE-001, EQ-NUM-ODE-003) on each interval between consecutive observation times. `df/dy` and `df/dtheta` are the supplied analytic Jacobians or central differences of `f` (error near `1e-10`). The result is the solution of the continuous sensitivity equations up to the Runge-Kutta error `O(h^4)`; it is not the derivative of the discrete scheme.
- Code: `inverse.profile_extended.ode_sensitivity`.
- Source: [REF-HAIRER-NORSETT-WANNER-1993].
  - [REF-HAIRER-NORSETT-WANNER-1993](../references.md#ref-hairer-norsett-wanner-1993): Ernst Hairer, Syvert P. Nørsett, and Gerhard Wanner. *Solving Ordinary Differential Equations I: Nonstiff Problems*. Second revised edition. Springer Series in Computational Mathematics 8. Springer, Berlin, Heidelberg, 1993. ISBN 978-3-540-56670-0. DOI: [10.1007/978-3-540-78862-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-78862-1).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PLX-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-plx-005--forward-sensitivity-equations)

### EQ-INV-PLX-006 — Sensitivity-matrix rank and unidentifiable direction

`J = W^-1 (C S)(t_k) D`, `W` = noise standard deviations, `D` = parameter scales; `J = U Sigma V'`; `rank = #{k : sigma_k > rtol sigma_1}`; unidentifiable subspace = span of the columns `v_k`, `k > rank`; error scale along `v_k` = `1 / sigma_k`.

- Domain: `J` stacked time-major over the observation times; with fewer rows than parameters the matrix is padded with zero rows so every parameter direction has a singular value.
- Meaning: the rank rule is explicit and relative (`relative_tolerance`, default `1e-8`, which must exceed the integration and Jacobian error ratio). Rank deficiency shows local non-identifiability at this `theta` along `v_k`: to first order, moving `theta` along `v_k` does not change the observations. It is numerical and local: it does not prove structural non-identifiability (a locally rank-deficient model can be identifiable elsewhere) and full rank does not prove structural identifiability; small `sigma_k` means practical (noise-dependent) weak identifiability with standard error `1 / sigma_k` along `v_k`. The null basis is arbitrary within equal singular values; `rank_gap = sigma_rank / sigma_{rank+1}` close to one marks a fragile rank decision.
- Code: `inverse.profile_extended.ODESensitivityResult`.
- Source: [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003], [REF-RAUE-ETAL-2009].
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
  - [REF-RAUE-ETAL-2009](../references.md#ref-raue-etal-2009): A. Raue, C. Kreutz, T. Maiwald, J. Bachmann, M. Schilling, U. Klingmüller, and J. Timmer. "Structural and Practical Identifiability Analysis of Partially Observed Dynamical Models by Exploiting the Profile Likelihood." *Bioinformatics* 25(15), 1923-1929, 2009. Cited for profile-likelihood confidence intervals and the distinction between structural and practical non-identifiability. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1093/bioinformatics/btp358](https://doi.org/10.1093/bioinformatics/btp358).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PLX-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-plx-006--sensitivity-matrix-rank-and-unidentifiable-direction)

## `probstat.inverse.profile_likelihood`

### EQ-INV-PROF-001 — Profile negative log-likelihood

`nll_prof(t) = min_{theta_-i} nll(theta_1, ..., theta_i = t, ..., theta_n)`, with `nll = -l` the negative log-likelihood, `theta_-i` the nuisance block, optionally under box constraints on the nuisance block.

- Domain: `nll` finite on the feasible set; the grid brackets the supplied estimate.
- Meaning: the profile re-optimizes the nuisance parameters at every fixed target value (continuation from the grid point nearest the estimate in both directions, warm starts, the estimate's nuisance block as a second start, optional extra starts). It is not a marginal over nuisance parameters and not the profile of a penalized/MAP objective. A local optimizer can return a local minimum; the flags `converged` and `start` are recorded per point.
- Code: `inverse.profile_likelihood.profile_likelihood`.
- Source: [REF-RAUE-ETAL-2009], [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-RAUE-ETAL-2009](../references.md#ref-raue-etal-2009): A. Raue, C. Kreutz, T. Maiwald, J. Bachmann, M. Schilling, U. Klingmüller, and J. Timmer. "Structural and Practical Identifiability Analysis of Partially Observed Dynamical Models by Exploiting the Profile Likelihood." *Bioinformatics* 25(15), 1923-1929, 2009. Cited for profile-likelihood confidence intervals and the distinction between structural and practical non-identifiability. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1093/bioinformatics/btp358](https://doi.org/10.1093/bioinformatics/btp358).
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PROF-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-prof-001--profile-negative-log-likelihood)

### EQ-INV-PROF-002 — Profile likelihood ratio and baseline refit

`LR(t) = 2 (nll_prof(t) - nll_min)`, `nll_min = min(nll(theta_hat), min_t nll_prof(t))`.

- Domain: grid values `t`; `theta_hat` the supplied estimate.
- Meaning: LR is non-negative by construction because the baseline is the best value found. When some profile value is below `nll(theta_hat)` by more than a tolerance, `improved_estimate_found` is set and the better point is reported; the difference is never clipped.
- Code: `inverse.profile_likelihood.ProfileLikelihoodResult`.
- Source: [REF-RAUE-ETAL-2009], [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003].
  - [REF-RAUE-ETAL-2009](../references.md#ref-raue-etal-2009): A. Raue, C. Kreutz, T. Maiwald, J. Bachmann, M. Schilling, U. Klingmüller, and J. Timmer. "Structural and Practical Identifiability Analysis of Partially Observed Dynamical Models by Exploiting the Profile Likelihood." *Bioinformatics* 25(15), 1923-1929, 2009. Cited for profile-likelihood confidence intervals and the distinction between structural and practical non-identifiability. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1093/bioinformatics/btp358](https://doi.org/10.1093/bioinformatics/btp358).
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PROF-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-prof-002--profile-likelihood-ratio-and-baseline-refit)

### EQ-INV-PROF-003 — Wilks profile-likelihood interval

`{ t : LR(t) <= chi2_{1,level} }`, endpoints by linear interpolation of LR between the last grid point inside and the first outside.

- Domain: regular asymptotics (interior true parameter, identifiable, unimodal profile, large sample).
- Meaning: boundaries, weak identifiability, multimodality and small samples violate the Wilks assumptions. A side without a crossing inside the grid is reported as `None` with status `unbounded_within_grid` (the grid cannot distinguish an unbounded set from one longer than the grid); a non-finite neighbour gives `undetermined_nonfinite`. Disconnected sets are flagged (`connected=False`). Linear interpolation of a convex LR slightly narrows the interval; refine the grid.
- Code: `inverse.profile_likelihood.profile_interval`.
- Source: [REF-RAUE-ETAL-2009], [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003].
  - [REF-RAUE-ETAL-2009](../references.md#ref-raue-etal-2009): A. Raue, C. Kreutz, T. Maiwald, J. Bachmann, M. Schilling, U. Klingmüller, and J. Timmer. "Structural and Practical Identifiability Analysis of Partially Observed Dynamical Models by Exploiting the Profile Likelihood." *Bioinformatics* 25(15), 1923-1929, 2009. Cited for profile-likelihood confidence intervals and the distinction between structural and practical non-identifiability. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1093/bioinformatics/btp358](https://doi.org/10.1093/bioinformatics/btp358).
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PROF-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-prof-003--wilks-profile-likelihood-interval)

### EQ-INV-PROF-004 — Efficient (nuisance-corrected) information

`F_eff = F_tt - F_tn F_nn^{-1} F_nt = ((F^{-1})_tt)^{-1}`, with `F` partitioned into target `t` and nuisance `n` blocks.

- Domain: symmetric `F` with positive-definite `F_nn`.
- Meaning: information about the target after the nuisance parameters are estimated. Computed with a Cholesky factor of `F_nn` and triangular solves, no explicit inverse. If `F_nn` is singular the nuisance block is not estimable and `ValueError` is raised; no generalized inverse is used. For a linear Gaussian model the profile curvature equals `F_eff`.
- Code: `inverse.profile_likelihood.efficient_information`.
- Source: [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003], [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PROF-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-prof-004--efficient-nuisance-corrected-information)

### EQ-INV-PROF-005 — Delta-method variance of a functional

`Var(q(theta_hat)) ~ c' C c`, `c = grad q(theta_hat)`, `C` the covariance of `theta_hat`.

- Domain: smooth `q`, small estimator covariance, `C` symmetric positive semidefinite.
- Meaning: first-order propagation; poor when `q` is strongly nonlinear over the uncertainty region or `C` is itself unreliable (non-identifiable parameters).
- Code: `inverse.profile_likelihood.delta_method_variance`.
- Source: [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003].
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PROF-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-prof-005--delta-method-variance-of-a-functional)

### EQ-INV-PROF-006 — Local estimability of a linear functional

`c' theta` is estimable iff `c` is orthogonal to null(M), i.e. `||(I - V_r V_r') c|| <= tol ||c||`, where `M = U S V'` is the SVD of the Jacobian (or of the Fisher matrix `J'J`, same null space) and `V_r` holds the right singular vectors with `s > max(m,n) eps s_max`.

- Domain: local, linear, in the stated coordinates and units.
- Meaning: a numerical local rank statement at one point; it does not establish structural identifiability and the rank cut is a tolerance choice.
- Code: `inverse.profile_likelihood.functional_estimability`.
- Source: [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003], [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006].
  - [REF-SEBER-WILD-2003](../references.md#ref-seber-wild-2003): G. A. F. Seber and C. J. Wild. *Nonlinear Regression*. Wiley, 2003.
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-PROF-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-prof-006--local-estimability-of-a-linear-functional)

## `probstat.inverse.regularization`

### EQ-INV-REG-001 — Tikhonov functional and its stacked least-squares form

`x_lambda = argmin_x ||A x - b||^2 + lambda^2 ||L (x - x_bar)||^2`, computed
as the least-squares solution of `[A; lambda L] x = [b; lambda L x_bar]`
(for the linear solver `x_bar = 0`) by Householder QR [EQ-LINALG-009]. For
`L = I` it equals the posterior mean of `x ~ N(0, (s^2 / lambda^2) I)` given
`b = A x + e`, `e ~ N(0, s^2 I)`. The nonlinear version minimizes
`||r(x)||^2 + lambda^2 ||L (x - x_bar)||^2` by Levenberg-Marquardt on the
stacked residual `[r(x); lambda L (x - x_bar)]` [EQ-OPT-LM-001].

- Domain: `lambda >= 0`; the null spaces of `A` and `L` intersect only in
  zero (otherwise the stacked matrix is rank deficient and the solve
  raises); `lambda = 0` needs `A` of full column rank.
- Meaning: the penalty selects, among the solutions that fit the data about
  equally well, one with small (semi)norm; `lambda` sets the trade-off. The
  normal equations `(A'A + lambda^2 L'L) x = A'b` are never formed, so the
  solve sees the condition number of the stacked matrix, not its square.
- Code: `inverse.tikhonov`, `inverse.nonlinear_tikhonov`,
  `inverse.regularization._stacked_qr`.
- Source: [REF-TIKHONOV-1963]; [REF-HANSEN-2008, Sections 2.2 and 2.7.1]
  (functional with `x_bar`, stacked form); [REF-TARANTOLA-2005, Chapter 3]
  (Gaussian interpretation).
  - [REF-TIKHONOV-1963](../references.md#ref-tikhonov-1963): Andrei N. Tikhonov. "On the Solution of Ill-Posed Problems and the Method of Regularization." *Doklady Akademii Nauk SSSR* 151(3), 501-504, 1963 (in Russian); English translation "Solution of Incorrectly Formulated Problems and the Regularization Method," *Soviet Mathematics Doklady* 4, 1035-1038, 1963. The penalized least-squares regularization named after him.
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-REG-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-reg-001--tikhonov-functional-and-its-stacked-least-squares-form)

### EQ-INV-REG-002 — Tikhonov filter factors

For `L = I` and the SVD of `A`,
`x_lambda = sum_i f_i (u_i'b / sigma_i) v_i` with
`f_i = sigma_i^2 / (sigma_i^2 + lambda^2)` (`f_i = 0` when `sigma_i = 0`).

- Domain: `lambda >= 0`; the SVD of [EQ-LINALG-SVD-001].
- Meaning: components with `sigma_i >> lambda` pass almost unchanged
  (`f_i ~ 1`), those with `sigma_i << lambda` are damped (`f_i ~
  sigma_i^2 / lambda^2`); a discarded component still contributes about
  `sigma_i (u_i'b) / lambda^2`.
- Code: `inverse.tikhonov`, `inverse.TikhonovSolution.filter_factors`,
  `inverse.regularization._filter_factors`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008, Section 2.4, eq. (2.18)].
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-REG-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-reg-002--tikhonov-filter-factors)

### EQ-INV-REG-003 — Generalized cross-validation function

`G(lambda) = ||A x_lambda - b||^2 / trace(I_m - A A^#)^2`, where
`x_lambda = A^# b`. For `L = I`, `trace(I_m - A A^#) = m - sum_i f_i`; for a
general `L`, with `[A; lambda L] = Q R` and `Q_1` the first `m` rows of `Q`,
`A A^# = Q_1 Q_1'`, so `trace(I_m - A A^#) = m - ||Q_1||_F^2`.

- Domain: `lambda > 0` on a grid (default geometric from
  `max(sigma_k, 16 eps sigma_1)` to `sigma_1`, divided by the extreme
  nonzero singular values of `L` for a general `L`); the grid minimum is
  refined by golden-section search in `log lambda`.
- Meaning: an estimate of the predictive risk that needs no noise level.
  For a square operator `G` tends to a finite limit as `lambda -> 0`, which
  can undercut the interior minimum and give a far too small `lambda`.
- Code: `inverse.generalized_cross_validation`, `inverse.regularization._gcv`,
  `inverse.regularization._Curve.point`.
- Source: [REF-CRAVEN-WAHBA-1979]; [REF-GOLUB-HEATH-WAHBA-1979];
  [REF-HANSEN-2008, Section 2.9, eqs. (2.62)-(2.63)]. The `Q_1` trace form
  follows from `A = Q_1 R` (derived for this library).
  - [REF-CRAVEN-WAHBA-1979](../references.md#ref-craven-wahba-1979): Peter Craven and Grace Wahba. "Smoothing Noisy Data with Spline Functions." *Numerische Mathematik* 31(4), 377-403 (issue dated December 1978, usually cited as 1979). The generalized cross-validation score `n RSS / (n - tr H)^2`. DOI: [10.1007/BF01404567](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01404567).
  - [REF-GOLUB-HEATH-WAHBA-1979](../references.md#ref-golub-heath-wahba-1979): Gene H. Golub, Michael Heath, and Grace Wahba. "Generalized Cross-Validation as a Method for Choosing a Good Ridge Parameter." *Technometrics* 21(2), 215-223, 1979. Generalized cross-validation for ridge (Tikhonov) regression. DOI: [10.1080/00401706.1979.10489751](https://doi.org/10.1080/00401706.1979.10489751).
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-REG-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-reg-003--generalized-cross-validation-function)

### EQ-INV-REG-004 — L-curve curvature

With `rho = ||A x_lambda - b||^2`, `eta = ||L x_lambda||^2`, and hats for
`log sqrt(.)`, the curvature of `(rho_hat, eta_hat)` is
`kappa = (rho_hat' eta_hat'' - rho_hat'' eta_hat') / ((rho_hat')^2 + (eta_hat')^2)^(3/2)`
(primes: derivatives in `lambda`). Using `rho' = -lambda^2 eta'` and
`eta' = (4 / lambda) (L x)'(L z)`, `z = (A'A + lambda^2 L'L)^-1 A'(A x - b)`
(for `L = I`: `eta' = -(4 / lambda) sum_i f_i^2 (1 - f_i) (u_i'b)^2 / sigma_i^2`),
`kappa = 2 rho eta (lambda^2 eta' rho + 2 lambda rho eta + lambda^4 eta eta')
/ (|eta'| (lambda^4 eta^2 + rho^2)^(3/2))`.

- Domain: `lambda > 0` with `rho, eta, eta' != 0` (otherwise `nan`); same
  grid and refinement as [EQ-INV-REG-003], maximizing `kappa`.
- Meaning: the corner where the curve turns from the steep branch (noise
  dominated) to the flat branch (regularization dominated). The closed form
  is checked against finite differences of the log norms in the tests.
- Code: `inverse.l_curve`, `inverse.regularization._curvature`,
  `inverse.regularization._Curve.point`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-OLEARY-1993, Section 5.1, eq. (8)] (curvature in log
  norms, corner at maximum curvature); [REF-HANSEN-2008, Sections 2.5 and
  2.9]. The reduction to `rho`, `eta`, `eta'` is derived for this library
  from `x' = (2 / lambda) z`.
  - [REF-HANSEN-OLEARY-1993](../references.md#ref-hansen-oleary-1993): Per Christian Hansen and Dianne Prost O'Leary. "The Use of the L-Curve in the Regularization of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems." *SIAM Journal on Scientific Computing* 14(6), 1487-1503, 1993. The corner of the log-log L-curve as the point of maximum curvature (Section 5.1, eq. (8)) and its comparison with generalized cross-validation. DOI: [10.1137/0914086](https://doi.org/10.1137/0914086).
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-REG-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-reg-004--l-curve-curvature)

### EQ-INV-REG-005 — Discrepancy principle

Choose `lambda` with `||A x_lambda - b|| = tau delta`, `delta ~ ||e||`,
`tau >= 1`; the root is found by Brent's method [EQ-OPT-BRENT-001] in
`log lambda` after bracketing on the grid.

- Domain: `||A x_lambda - b||` increases from the least-squares residual
  (`lambda -> 0`) to `||b||` (`lambda -> inf`, for `L = I`); a target
  outside that range raises `ValueError`.
- Meaning: fit the data no better than the noise allows. An underestimated
  `delta` gives an under-regularized solution with a large norm, an
  overestimated one an over-smoothed solution.
- Code: `inverse.discrepancy_principle`.
- Source: [REF-MOROZOV-1966]; [REF-HANSEN-2008, Section 2.9, eq. (2.61)].
  - [REF-MOROZOV-1966](../references.md#ref-morozov-1966): Vladimir A. Morozov. "On the Solution of Functional Equations by the Method of Regularization." *Doklady Akademii Nauk SSSR* 167(3), 510-512, 1966 (in Russian). The choice of the regularization parameter from the noise level, known as the discrepancy principle.
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-REG-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-reg-005--discrepancy-principle)

### EQ-INV-REG-006 — Truncated SVD solution

`x_k = sum_(i <= k) (u_i'b / sigma_i) v_i`,
`||A x_k - b||^2 = sum_(i > k) (u_i'b)^2 + ||b - U U'b||^2`.

- Domain: `1 <= k <= min(m, n)` with `sigma_k > 0`.
- Meaning: filter factors `1` for `i <= k` and `0` otherwise; `x_k` is the
  minimum-norm least-squares solution for the closest rank-`k` matrix
  `A_k`.
- Code: `inverse.truncated_svd`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008, Section 2.7.3, eqs. (2.42)-(2.45)].
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-REG-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-reg-006--truncated-svd-solution)

### EQ-INV-REG-007 — Discrete Picard coefficients

`(sigma_i, |u_i'b|, |u_i'b| / sigma_i)` for `i = 1, ..., k`.

- Domain: the SVD of [EQ-LINALG-SVD-001]; `inf` for `sigma_i = 0 < |u_i'b|`
  and `nan` when both vanish.
- Meaning: the discrete Picard condition holds when `|u_i'b|` decays on
  average faster than `sigma_i`; noise makes `|u_i'b|` level off near its
  standard deviation, and from there on the ratios grow.
- Code: `inverse.picard_coefficients`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008, Section 2.4].
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-REG-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-reg-007--discrete-picard-coefficients)

## `probstat.inverse.regularized_ensemble`

### EQ-INV-TEKI-001 — Kalman-type ensemble update on a generic forward map

`x_j^+ = x_j + C_xg (C_gg + alpha Gamma)^-1 (y + eta_j - g_j)`,
`eta_j ~ N(0, alpha Gamma)`, with `g_j = G(x_j)`, ensemble means `x_bar`,
`g_bar`, `C_xg = sum_j (x_j - x_bar)(g_j - g_bar)' / (J - 1)` and
`C_gg = sum_j (g_j - g_bar)(g_j - g_bar)' / (J - 1)`.

- Domain: `J >= 2`, `alpha > 0` finite, `Gamma` SPD (any covariance factor of
  `probstat.linalg`); finite entries.
- Meaning: the stochastic EKI/ES-MDA update with an inflated noise covariance
  `alpha Gamma`. For a linear `G` the expectation over `eta` of the updated
  ensemble mean is the Kalman mean `x_bar + K (y - g_bar)` of the ensemble's own
  sample statistics. The members stay in the affine span of the prior
  ensemble; the update does not produce posterior samples for a finite ensemble
  or a nonlinear `G`.
- Code: `inverse.regularized_ensemble.ensemble_kalman_update`.
- Source: [REF-IGLESIAS-LAW-STUART-2013], [REF-EMERICK-REYNOLDS-2013].
  - [REF-IGLESIAS-LAW-STUART-2013](../references.md#ref-iglesias-law-stuart-2013): Marco A. Iglesias, Kody J. H. Law, and Andrew M. Stuart. "Ensemble Kalman Methods for Inverse Problems." *Inverse Problems* 29(4), 045001, 2013. The iterative ensemble Kalman method with perturbed data `y + eta_j`, `eta_j ~ N(0, Gamma)`, the invariant-subspace property of the ensemble, and its use as a derivative-free optimizer (Algorithm 1, Section 2). DOI: [10.1088/0266-5611/29/4/045001](https://doi.org/10.1088/0266-5611/29/4/045001).
  - [REF-EMERICK-REYNOLDS-2013](../references.md#ref-emerick-reynolds-2013): Alexandre A. Emerick and Albert C. Reynolds. "Ensemble Smoother with Multiple Data Assimilation." *Computers & Geosciences* 55, 3-15, 2013. Repeated assimilation of the same data with inflated error covariances `alpha_i C_D`, `sum 1/alpha_i = 1`, exact for linear-Gaussian problems in the large-ensemble limit. DOI: [10.1016/j.cageo.2012.03.011](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cageo.2012.03.011).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-TEKI-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-teki-001--kalman-type-ensemble-update-on-a-generic-forward-map)

### EQ-INV-TEKI-002 — Observation-space and ensemble-space (Woodbury) solves

With `X`, `Z` the anomalies of members and predictions divided by `sqrt(J - 1)`,
`Gamma = L L'` and `Zw = L^-1 Z`:
`C_xg (C_gg + alpha Gamma)^-1 = X Zw' (Zw Zw' + alpha I_m)^-1 L^-1
= X (Zw' Zw + alpha I_J)^-1 Zw' L^-1`.

- Domain: `alpha > 0`; both matrices are SPD, so Cholesky applies, and no
  inverse is formed.
- Meaning: the second form is the push-through (Woodbury) identity and solves
  a `J x J` system, cheaper when `m >> J`. The two agree to rounding error.
- Code: `inverse.regularized_ensemble.ensemble_kalman_update`
  (argument `solve`).
- Source: [REF-IGLESIAS-LAW-STUART-2013].
  - [REF-IGLESIAS-LAW-STUART-2013](../references.md#ref-iglesias-law-stuart-2013): Marco A. Iglesias, Kody J. H. Law, and Andrew M. Stuart. "Ensemble Kalman Methods for Inverse Problems." *Inverse Problems* 29(4), 045001, 2013. The iterative ensemble Kalman method with perturbed data `y + eta_j`, `eta_j ~ N(0, Gamma)`, the invariant-subspace property of the ensemble, and its use as a derivative-free optimizer (Algorithm 1, Section 2). DOI: [10.1088/0266-5611/29/4/045001](https://doi.org/10.1088/0266-5611/29/4/045001).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-TEKI-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-teki-002--observation-space-and-ensemble-space-woodbury-solves)

### EQ-INV-TEKI-003 — Deterministic ensemble transform square-root update

`x_bar^+ = x_bar + K (y - g_bar)`, `K = C_xg (C_gg + alpha Gamma)^-1`;
anomalies `A^+ = A T`, `T = (I_J + Zw' Zw / alpha)^-1/2` (symmetric),
members `x_j^+ = x_bar^+ + (A T)_j`, with `A` the unscaled parameter
anomalies.

- Domain: `alpha > 0`; `T` is computed by a symmetric eigen-decomposition of an
  SPD matrix with eigenvalues at least one.
- Meaning: for a linear `G` the updated sample mean and covariance equal
  `x_bar + K (y - g_bar)` and `C_xx - K C_gx` exactly, for the ensemble's own
  sample statistics. `T 1 = 1` because the anomalies sum to zero, so the mean is
  unchanged by the transform. `T Q` with `Q` orthogonal and `Q 1 = 1` gives the
  same first two moments; the symmetric choice is one member of this family.
- Code: `inverse.regularized_ensemble.ensemble_kalman_update`
  (argument `perturbed=False`).
- Source: [REF-BISHOP-ETHERTON-MAJUMDAR-2001].
  - [REF-BISHOP-ETHERTON-MAJUMDAR-2001](../references.md#ref-bishop-etherton-majumdar-2001): Craig H. Bishop, Brian J. Etherton, and Sharanya J. Majumdar. "Adaptive Sampling with the Ensemble Transform Kalman Filter. Part I: Theoretical Aspects." *Monthly Weather Review* 129(3), 420-436, 2001. DOI: [10.1175/1520-0493(2001)129<0420:ASWTET>2.0.CO;2](https://doi.org/10.1175/1520-0493(2001)129%3C0420:ASWTET%3E2.0.CO;2).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-TEKI-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-teki-003--deterministic-ensemble-transform-square-root-update)

### EQ-INV-TEKI-004 — Tikhonov EKI by augmented system

`Ga(x) = [G(x); x]`, `ya = [y; mu]`, `Gamma_a = blockdiag(Gamma, C0)`, initial
ensemble `x_j ~ N(mu, C0)`, and per iteration the update of [EQ-INV-TEKI-001]
with `alpha = 1` applied to `(Ga, ya, Gamma_a)`.

- Domain: `C0` SPD covariance of the prior; `J >= 2`; one iteration costs `J`
  forward evaluations.
- Meaning: regularized ensemble Kalman inversion. It is a derivative-free
  optimizer: every iterate stays in the affine span of the initial ensemble
  and the sample covariance collapses as the iterations proceed. The final spread
  is NOT a posterior uncertainty. The formulation follows the augmented-system
  construction built on the existing EKI sources (no dedicated TEKI
  reference is registered in this library).
- Code: `inverse.regularized_ensemble.tikhonov_ensemble_kalman_inversion`.
- Source: [REF-IGLESIAS-LAW-STUART-2013], [REF-SCHILLINGS-STUART-2017].
  - [REF-IGLESIAS-LAW-STUART-2013](../references.md#ref-iglesias-law-stuart-2013): Marco A. Iglesias, Kody J. H. Law, and Andrew M. Stuart. "Ensemble Kalman Methods for Inverse Problems." *Inverse Problems* 29(4), 045001, 2013. The iterative ensemble Kalman method with perturbed data `y + eta_j`, `eta_j ~ N(0, Gamma)`, the invariant-subspace property of the ensemble, and its use as a derivative-free optimizer (Algorithm 1, Section 2). DOI: [10.1088/0266-5611/29/4/045001](https://doi.org/10.1088/0266-5611/29/4/045001).
  - [REF-SCHILLINGS-STUART-2017](../references.md#ref-schillings-stuart-2017): Claudia Schillings and Andrew M. Stuart. "Analysis of the Ensemble Kalman Filter for Inverse Problems." *SIAM Journal on Numerical Analysis* 55(3), 1264-1290, 2017. Continuous-time limit of ensemble Kalman inversion as preconditioned gradient flows of the data misfit; ensemble collapse and over-fitting of noisy data for linear forward models (Sections 3 and 4). DOI: [10.1137/16M105959X](https://doi.org/10.1137/16M105959X).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-TEKI-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-teki-004--tikhonov-eki-by-augmented-system)

### EQ-INV-TEKI-005 — Tikhonov objective and misfit of the mean prediction

`Phi(x) = |Gamma^-1/2 (y - G(x))|^2 + |C0^-1/2 (x - mu)|^2`;
recorded misfit `|Gamma_a^-1/2 (ya - mean_j Ga(x_j))|`.

- Domain: `Gamma`, `C0` SPD.
- Meaning: for linear `G`, `Phi / 2` is the negative log posterior up to a
  constant, so the minimizer of `Phi` is the MAP/Tikhonov solution towards
  which the TEKI ensemble mean moves (in the span of the ensemble; exactly when
  `J - 1 >= n` and in the limit of many iterations). The recorded misfit uses
  the mean of the member predictions, not `G` at the mean of the members.
- Code: `inverse.regularized_ensemble.TikhonovEnsembleResult`.
- Source: [REF-SCHILLINGS-STUART-2017], [REF-IGLESIAS-LAW-STUART-2013].
  - [REF-SCHILLINGS-STUART-2017](../references.md#ref-schillings-stuart-2017): Claudia Schillings and Andrew M. Stuart. "Analysis of the Ensemble Kalman Filter for Inverse Problems." *SIAM Journal on Numerical Analysis* 55(3), 1264-1290, 2017. Continuous-time limit of ensemble Kalman inversion as preconditioned gradient flows of the data misfit; ensemble collapse and over-fitting of noisy data for linear forward models (Sections 3 and 4). DOI: [10.1137/16M105959X](https://doi.org/10.1137/16M105959X).
  - [REF-IGLESIAS-LAW-STUART-2013](../references.md#ref-iglesias-law-stuart-2013): Marco A. Iglesias, Kody J. H. Law, and Andrew M. Stuart. "Ensemble Kalman Methods for Inverse Problems." *Inverse Problems* 29(4), 045001, 2013. The iterative ensemble Kalman method with perturbed data `y + eta_j`, `eta_j ~ N(0, Gamma)`, the invariant-subspace property of the ensemble, and its use as a derivative-free optimizer (Algorithm 1, Section 2). DOI: [10.1088/0266-5611/29/4/045001](https://doi.org/10.1088/0266-5611/29/4/045001).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-TEKI-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-teki-005--tikhonov-objective-and-misfit-of-the-mean-prediction)

## `probstat.inverse.sbi_workflow`

### EQ-INV-SBI-001 — ABC target, insufficient summaries and the linear-Gaussian oracle

`pi_eps(theta | s_obs) ∝ p(theta) P(rho(s(y_sim), s_obs) <= eps | theta)`.
For `theta ~ N(0, tau^2)`, `y_i = theta + N(0, sigma^2)`, `i = 1..n`, the
posterior given all data and given the sufficient summary `ybar` is
`N(m_n, v_n)` with `v_n = (1/tau^2 + n/sigma^2)^-1`, `m_n = v_n n ybar / sigma^2`;
given only `y_1` it is `N(m_1, v_1)` with `v_1 = (1/tau^2 + 1/sigma^2)^-1`,
`m_1 = v_1 y_1 / sigma^2`.

- Domain: `tau, sigma > 0`, `n >= 2`.
- Meaning: as `eps -> 0` the ABC target tends to the posterior given the
  *summary*; for the sufficient `ybar` that is the full posterior, for the
  insufficient `y_1` it is the wider `N(m_1, v_1)` whatever the tolerance.
  Per-seed errors are noisy; only seed averages show the tolerance trend.
- Code: `inverse.sbi_workflow.abc_tolerance_study`,
  `inverse.sbi_workflow.LinearGaussianCase`,
  `inverse.sbi_workflow.run_abc_rejection`, `inverse.sbi_workflow.run_abc_smc`.
- Source: [REF-PRITCHARD-ETAL-1999], [REF-BEAUMONT-CORNUET-MARIN-ROBERT-2009].
  - [REF-PRITCHARD-ETAL-1999](../references.md#ref-pritchard-etal-1999): J. K. Pritchard, M. T. Seielstad, A. Perez-Lezaun, and M. W. Feldman. "Population Growth of Human Y Chromosomes: A Study of Y Chromosome Microsatellites." *Molecular Biology and Evolution* 16(12), 1791-1798, 1999. Rejection sampling with summary statistics and a tolerance, the origin of approximate Bayesian computation in its modern form. DOI: [10.1093/oxfordjournals.molbev.a026091](https://doi.org/10.1093/oxfordjournals.molbev.a026091).
  - [REF-BEAUMONT-CORNUET-MARIN-ROBERT-2009](../references.md#ref-beaumont-cornuet-marin-robert-2009): M. A. Beaumont, J.-M. Cornuet, J.-M. Marin, and C. P. Robert. "Adaptive Approximate Bayesian Computation." *Biometrika* 96(4), 983-990, 2009. ABC population Monte Carlo with importance weights and a Gaussian kernel of twice the weighted population variance. DOI: [10.1093/biomet/asp052](https://doi.org/10.1093/biomet/asp052).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SBI-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sbi-001--abc-target-insufficient-summaries-and-the-linear-gaussian-oracle)

### EQ-INV-SBI-002 — Gaussian synthetic log-likelihood (approximate)

`l_s(theta) = -(1/2) [(s_obs - m)' S^-1 (s_obs - m) + log det S + d log 2 pi]`,
with `m` and `S` the sample mean and unbiased sample covariance of `n`
summaries simulated at `theta`.

- Domain: `n > d`; `S` numerically positive definite (Cholesky pivot ratio at
  least `1e-7`). Otherwise the result carries `status = "singular_covariance"`
  and no value (no jitter, no pseudo-inverse).
- Meaning: the log-likelihood of a Gaussian *model of the summaries* with
  plug-in moments, not the simulator likelihood; the plug-in estimate is
  biased for finite `n` and random, with Monte Carlo variance estimated from
  independent replicates.
- Code: `inverse.sbi_workflow.synthetic_log_likelihood`,
  `inverse.sbi_workflow.synthetic_likelihood_grid`.
- Source: [REF-WOOD-2010].
  - [REF-WOOD-2010](../references.md#ref-wood-2010): Simon N. Wood. "Statistical inference for noisy nonlinear ecological dynamic systems." *Nature* 466, 1102-1104, 2010. Cited for the Gaussian synthetic likelihood built from the mean and covariance of simulated summaries. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1038/nature09319](https://doi.org/10.1038/nature09319).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SBI-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sbi-002--gaussian-synthetic-log-likelihood-approximate)

### EQ-INV-SBI-003 — Held-out ensemble CRPS, central coverage and ledgered cost

`CRPS(F_m, y) = (1/m) sum_i |x_i - y| - (1/(2 m^2)) sum_ij |x_i - x_j|`,
evaluated by sorting with `sum_ij |x_i - x_j| = 2 sum_i (2 i - m + 1) x_(i)`;
`coverage_q = mean_test 1{y in central q-interval of the draws}`; cost is the
number of simulator calls recorded in a `WorkLedger`.

- Domain: finite draws; test pairs independent of training and validation
  (separate child streams).
- Meaning: CRPS is a proper score usable for every method that yields samples;
  the exact posterior attains the smallest expected value but not zero (the
  posterior is bimodal). Coverage of an exact posterior over the prior
  predictive is the nominal level; SBC and coverage average over the prior and
  cannot detect a prior-only posterior.
- Code: `inverse.sbi_workflow.crps_ensemble_value`,
  `inverse.sbi_workflow.run_bimodal_comparison`,
  `inverse.sbi_workflow.WorkLedger`, `inverse.sbi_workflow.make_splits`.
- Source: [REF-TALTS-ETAL-2018], [REF-PAPAMAKARIOS-MURRAY-2016],
  [REF-HERMANS-BEGY-LOUPPE-2020].
  - [REF-TALTS-ETAL-2018](../references.md#ref-talts-etal-2018): Sean Talts, Michael Betancourt, Daniel Simpson, Aki Vehtari, and Andrew Gelman. "Validating Bayesian Inference Algorithms with Simulation-Based Calibration." Preprint, 2018. [arXiv:1804.06788](https://arxiv.org/abs/1804.06788).
  - [REF-PAPAMAKARIOS-MURRAY-2016](../references.md#ref-papamakarios-murray-2016): George Papamakarios and Iain Murray. "Fast epsilon-free Inference of Simulation Models with Bayesian Conditional Density Estimation." In *Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 29* (NeurIPS 2016), 2016. Preprint arXiv:1605.06376v4. [https://arxiv.org/abs/1605.06376](https://arxiv.org/abs/1605.06376).
  - [REF-HERMANS-BEGY-LOUPPE-2020](../references.md#ref-hermans-begy-louppe-2020): Joeri Hermans, Volodimir Begy, and Gilles Louppe. "Likelihood-free MCMC with Amortized Approximate Ratio Estimators." In *Proceedings of the 37th International Conference on Machine Learning* (ICML 2020), Proceedings of Machine Learning Research 119, 2020. A classifier between joint and marginal parameter/data pairs whose logit estimates the likelihood-to-evidence ratio used inside MCMC. Preprint arXiv:1903.04057. [https://arxiv.org/abs/1903.04057](https://arxiv.org/abs/1903.04057).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SBI-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sbi-003--held-out-ensemble-crps-central-coverage-and-ledgered-cost)

### EQ-INV-SBI-004 — Bimodal quadrature reference posterior

`p(theta | y) ∝ 1{|theta| <= h} exp(-(y - theta^2)^2 / (2 s^2))`, normalized
by the trapezoidal rule on a uniform grid.

- Domain: `s, h > 0`; for `y > 0` two modes near `+-sqrt(y)` of width about
  `s / (2 sqrt(y))`.
- Meaning: reference for ABC, NPE, NRE and the prior baseline; symmetric about
  zero so each mode carries mass one half. Discretization error is the
  trapezoid error of a smooth integrand on 2001 points.
- Code: `inverse.sbi_workflow.bimodal_exact_posterior`,
  `inverse.sbi_workflow.bimodal_contract`, `inverse.sbi_workflow.GridDensity`.
- Source: [REF-TARANTOLA-2005].
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SBI-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sbi-004--bimodal-quadrature-reference-posterior)

### EQ-INV-SBI-005 — Posterior score at diffusion time zero (Gaussian case)

`grad_theta log p(theta | y) = grad log p(theta) + grad log p(y | theta)
= -(theta - m0)/s0^2 + a (y - a theta)/sigma^2 = -(theta - mu)/v`,
with `v = (1/s0^2 + a^2/sigma^2)^-1`, `mu = v (m0/s0^2 + a y/sigma^2)`.

- Domain: `theta ~ N(m0, s0^2)`, `y = a theta + N(0, sigma^2)`.
- Meaning: Bayes' rule at the clean state (`t = 0`): the score of the
  posterior is the prior score plus the likelihood gradient, exactly.
- Code: `inverse.sbi_workflow.posterior_score_t0`,
  `inverse.sbi_workflow.GaussianScoreProblem`.
- Source: [REF-SONG-ETAL-2021].
  - [REF-SONG-ETAL-2021](../references.md#ref-song-etal-2021): Yang Song, Jascha Sohl-Dickstein, Diederik P. Kingma, Abhishek Kumar, Stefano Ermon, and Ben Poole. "Score-Based Generative Modeling through Stochastic Differential Equations." In *International Conference on Learning Representations (ICLR)*, 2021. Equation, section, appendix, and algorithm numbers follow arXiv:2011.13456v2.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SBI-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sbi-005--posterior-score-at-diffusion-time-zero-gaussian-case)

### EQ-INV-SBI-006 — Noisy-time posterior score and the failure of naive addition

For `theta_t = alpha theta_0 + s eps` the marginal of the posterior is
`p_t(theta_t | y) = N(alpha mu, alpha^2 v + s^2)`, so the exact score is
`-(theta_t - alpha mu)/(alpha^2 v + s^2)`. Naive addition uses
`grad log p_t(theta_t) + a (y - a theta_t)/sigma^2`; the exact guided score
uses `p_t(y | theta_t) = N(y; a m_t, a^2 V_t + sigma^2)` with
`m_t = E[theta_0 | theta_t]`, `V_t = Var[theta_0 | theta_t]`:
`grad log p_t(theta_t) + a (y - a m_t) / (a^2 V_t + sigma^2) dm_t/dtheta_t`.

- Domain: `s >= 0`, `alpha != 0`, Gaussian prior and linear-Gaussian
  likelihood (the only case where `p_t(y | theta_t)` is closed form here).
- Meaning: naive addition and the Tweedie plug-in (`V_t` ignored) are exact at
  `s = 0` and generally wrong for `s > 0`; the exact guided score equals the
  exact posterior score to rounding, which verifies the decomposition. No
  statement about neural scores or sampling dynamics is made.
- Code: `inverse.sbi_workflow.noisy_score_comparison`,
  `inverse.sbi_workflow.NoisyScoreComparison`.
- Source: [REF-SONG-ETAL-2021].
  - [REF-SONG-ETAL-2021](../references.md#ref-song-etal-2021): Yang Song, Jascha Sohl-Dickstein, Diederik P. Kingma, Abhishek Kumar, Stefano Ermon, and Ben Poole. "Score-Based Generative Modeling through Stochastic Differential Equations." In *International Conference on Learning Representations (ICLR)*, 2021. Equation, section, appendix, and algorithm numbers follow arXiv:2011.13456v2.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SBI-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sbi-006--noisy-time-posterior-score-and-the-failure-of-naive-addition)

### EQ-INV-SBI-007 — Plug-and-play proximal gradient fixed point

`x_{k+1} = D(x_k - tau A'(A x_k - b); tau)`, residual
`r_k = |x_k - x_{k+1}| / tau`. If `D = prox_{tau g}` with `g` closed convex
and `tau <= 1/|A|_2^2`, the iteration is proximal gradient and its limit
minimizes `(1/2)|A x - b|^2 + g(x)`. For `g = (mu/2)|x|^2`,
`D(v) = v / (1 + tau mu)` and the limit is the Tikhonov solution
`(A'A + mu I)^-1 A'b`.

- Domain: dense `A`, `tau > 0`; convergence needs documented assumptions on `D`
  (non-expansive); the reported `max |D(u') - D(u)| / |u' - u|` over iterates is
  a lower bound of its Lipschitz constant and can only refute them.
- Meaning: a general denoiser defines a fixed point, not a MAP objective;
  residual-as-gradient (RED) is not asserted.
- Code: `inverse.sbi_workflow.plug_and_play_proximal_gradient`,
  `inverse.sbi_workflow.gaussian_proximal_denoiser`,
  `inverse.sbi_workflow.PlugAndPlayResult`.
- Source: [REF-VENKATAKRISHNAN-BOUMAN-WOHLBERG-2013], [REF-BOYD-ETAL-2011].
  - [REF-VENKATAKRISHNAN-BOUMAN-WOHLBERG-2013](../references.md#ref-venkatakrishnan-bouman-wohlberg-2013): Singanallur V. Venkatakrishnan, Charles A. Bouman, and Brendt Wohlberg. "Plug-and-Play Priors for Model Based Reconstruction." In *IEEE Global Conference on Signal and Information Processing (GlobalSIP)*, 945-948, 2013. Cited for replacing the proximal step of ADMM-type splitting by an arbitrary denoiser. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1109/GlobalSIP.2013.6737048](https://doi.org/10.1109/GlobalSIP.2013.6737048).
  - [REF-BOYD-ETAL-2011](../references.md#ref-boyd-etal-2011): Stephen Boyd, Neal Parikh, Eric Chu, Borja Peleato, and Jonathan Eckstein. "Distributed Optimization and Statistical Learning via the Alternating Direction Method of Multipliers." *Foundations and Trends in Machine Learning* 3(1), 1-122, 2011. Cited for scaled-form ADMM, its primal and dual residuals, and the absolute-plus-relative stopping thresholds. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1561/2200000016](https://doi.org/10.1561/2200000016).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SBI-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sbi-007--plug-and-play-proximal-gradient-fixed-point)

### EQ-INV-SBI-008 — Mahalanobis out-of-distribution heuristic

`score(s) = (s - m)' S^-1 (s - m)` with `m`, `S` the mean and covariance of the
training summaries; `s` is flagged when `score(s)` exceeds the empirical
`q`-quantile of the training scores.

- Domain: more than `d + 1` training summaries with non-singular covariance
  (otherwise an error; no regularization).
- Meaning: a heuristic, not a test: by construction about `1 - q` of
  in-distribution summaries are flagged, far-away summaries are flagged, and
  shifts that keep a small Mahalanobis score are missed. No coverage guarantee.
- Code: `inverse.sbi_workflow.fit_summary_ood_detector`,
  `inverse.sbi_workflow.SummaryOODDetector`.
- Source: [REF-PROBSTAT-VALIDATION].
  - [REF-PROBSTAT-VALIDATION](../references.md#ref-probstat-validation): Probability and Statistics Library. "Argument validation contract": parameter constraints and supports ([ADR-0005](adr/ADR-0005-support-log-probability-and-categorical-semantics.md)), tail and parameterization contracts ([ADR-0006](adr/ADR-0006-tail-quantile-and-parameterization-contracts.md)), and the rule that invalid input raises `ValueError` or `TypeError` before any computation, following the Python exception hierarchy [REF-PYTHON-EXCEPTIONS].
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SBI-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sbi-008--mahalanobis-out-of-distribution-heuristic)

### EQ-INV-SBI-009 — Variance-preserving noising and the exact noisy posterior score (Gaussian case)

`x_t = alpha(t) theta + s(t) eps`, `alpha(t) = exp(-(1/2) int_0^t beta)`,
`s(t)^2 = 1 - alpha(t)^2`, `beta(t) = beta_min + (beta_max - beta_min) t / T`;
for `theta | y ~ N(mu, Sigma_p)` the noisy marginal is
`N(alpha mu, alpha^2 Sigma_p + s^2 I)` with score
`-(alpha^2 Sigma_p + s^2 I)^-1 (x - alpha mu)`.

- Domain: `theta ~ N(m0, C0)`, `y = A theta + N(0, Gamma)`, `C0` and `Gamma`
  symmetric positive definite; `Sigma_p = (C0^-1 + A' Gamma^-1 A)^-1`,
  `mu = Sigma_p (C0^-1 m0 + A' Gamma^-1 y)`.
- Meaning: the forward variance-preserving SDE acts on the posterior as a
  Gaussian smoothing, so the exact score of the noisy posterior is closed
  form and no network is involved; at `t = 0` it equals the clean posterior
  score of EQ-INV-SBI-005.
- Code: `inverse.sbi_workflow.VPSchedule`,
  `inverse.sbi_workflow.LinearGaussianDiffusionProblem`.
- Source: [REF-SONG-ETAL-2021].
  - [REF-SONG-ETAL-2021](../references.md#ref-song-etal-2021): Yang Song, Jascha Sohl-Dickstein, Diederik P. Kingma, Abhishek Kumar, Stefano Ermon, and Ben Poole. "Score-Based Generative Modeling through Stochastic Differential Equations." In *International Conference on Learning Representations (ICLR)*, 2021. Equation, section, appendix, and algorithm numbers follow arXiv:2011.13456v2.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SBI-009](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sbi-009--variance-preserving-noising-and-the-exact-noisy-posterior-score-gaussian-case)

### EQ-INV-SBI-010 — Euler-Maruyama of the reverse SDE and Euler of the probability-flow ODE; exact moment recursion

Reverse SDE step `x <- x + h (beta x / 2 + beta S(x, t)) + sqrt(beta h) z`,
probability-flow ODE step `x <- x + h (beta x / 2 + beta S(x, t) / 2)`, on the
uniform grid `t_k = T - k h`, `h = T / N`, with the score evaluated at the start
of each step. For an affine score `S(x, t) = G_t x + c_t` each step is
`x <- F_k x + g_k + q_k^(1/2) z`, so the sampler is Gaussian with
`m <- F_k m + g_k`, `C <- F_k C F_k' + q_k I`; `q_k = beta h` (SDE) or `0` (ODE).

- Domain: the exact score of EQ-INV-SBI-009 (affine in `x`); `N >= 1`; start
  at the exact noisy marginal at `t = T` or at `N(0, I)`.
- Meaning: the moments of the discretized sampler are computed without Monte
  Carlo noise, so the measured error against the exact posterior shows the
  time-discretization rate directly (first order for both samplers in the
  measured study). The error of the `N(0, I)` start is `O(alpha(T))`.
- Code: `inverse.sbi_workflow.diffusion_posterior_sampling`,
  `inverse.sbi_workflow.diffusion_convergence_study`.
- Source: [REF-ANDERSON-1982], [REF-SONG-ETAL-2021], [REF-KLOEDEN-PLATEN-1992].
  - [REF-ANDERSON-1982](../references.md#ref-anderson-1982): Brian D. O. Anderson. "Reverse-Time Diffusion Equation Models." *Stochastic Processes and their Applications* 12(3), 313-326, 1982. DOI: [10.1016/0304-4149(82)90051-5](https://doi.org/10.1016/0304-4149(82)90051-5).
  - [REF-SONG-ETAL-2021](../references.md#ref-song-etal-2021): Yang Song, Jascha Sohl-Dickstein, Diederik P. Kingma, Abhishek Kumar, Stefano Ermon, and Ben Poole. "Score-Based Generative Modeling through Stochastic Differential Equations." In *International Conference on Learning Representations (ICLR)*, 2021. Equation, section, appendix, and algorithm numbers follow arXiv:2011.13456v2.
  - [REF-KLOEDEN-PLATEN-1992](../references.md#ref-kloeden-platen-1992): Peter E. Kloeden and Eckhard Platen. *Numerical Solution of Stochastic Differential Equations*. Applications of Mathematics 23. Springer-Verlag, 1992.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SBI-010](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sbi-010--euler-maruyama-of-the-reverse-sde-and-euler-of-the-probability-flow-ode-exact-moment-recursion)

### EQ-INV-SBI-011 — DPS-style approximate noisy posterior score (approximate)

`S_dps(x, t) = grad log p_t(x) + J_t' A' Gamma^-1 (y - A m_t(x))`, with
`m_t(x) = E[theta_0 | x_t = x] = m0 + J_t (x - alpha m0)`,
`J_t = alpha C0 (alpha^2 C0 + s^2 I)^-1`, and
`grad log p_t(x) = -(alpha^2 C0 + s^2 I)^-1 (x - alpha m0)` (the prior marginal).

- Domain: same Gaussian problem as EQ-INV-SBI-009.
- Meaning: the prior score at the noisy time plus the gradient of
  `log N(y; A m_t(x), Gamma)` through the Tweedie mean, ignoring the
  conditional variance `Var[theta_0 | x_t]`. It equals the exact score at
  `t = 0` and is not exact for `t > 0`; its bias does not vanish as the step
  count grows and is measured, not bounded. The gradient-norm step rule of the
  original DPS algorithm is not implemented. Labelled approximate.
- Code: `inverse.sbi_workflow.diffusion_posterior_sampling`.
- Source: [REF-CHUNG-ETAL-2023], [REF-SONG-ETAL-2021].
  - [REF-CHUNG-ETAL-2023](../references.md#ref-chung-etal-2023): Hyungjin Chung, Jeongsol Kim, Michael T. McCann, Marc L. Klasky, and Jong Chul Ye. "Diffusion Posterior Sampling for General Noisy Inverse Problems." arXiv:2209.14687, 2022. Cited for the diffusion-posterior-sampling approximation that evaluates the likelihood gradient at the Tweedie posterior mean of the clean state; no equation or section number is cited. Metadata checked against DataCite (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.48550/arXiv.2209.14687](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2209.14687).
  - [REF-SONG-ETAL-2021](../references.md#ref-song-etal-2021): Yang Song, Jascha Sohl-Dickstein, Diederik P. Kingma, Abhishek Kumar, Stefano Ermon, and Ben Poole. "Score-Based Generative Modeling through Stochastic Differential Equations." In *International Conference on Learning Representations (ICLR)*, 2021. Equation, section, appendix, and algorithm numbers follow arXiv:2011.13456v2.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SBI-011](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sbi-011--dps-style-approximate-noisy-posterior-score-approximate)

### EQ-INV-SBI-012 — Flow-matching posterior in the bimodal comparison (sample-only)

`d theta / dt = v_phi(t, theta, y)` integrated from `N(0, I)`, with `v_phi`
fitted on the prior-predictive pairs of the train split by the conditional
flow-matching loss.

- Domain: pairs `(theta_n, y_n)` from the prior and the simulator only.
- Meaning: the fitted field generates posterior samples; the comparison
  reports sample-based metrics (Wasserstein-1 to the exact quadrature posterior,
  mode occupation, 90% coverage, SBC) and attaches no density, so no evidence
  or Metropolis-Hastings correction is applied to it. Approximate.
- Code: `inverse.sbi_workflow.train_flow_matching`,
  `inverse.sbi_workflow.run_bimodal_comparison`.
- Source: [REF-LIPMAN-ETAL-2023], [REF-WILDBERGER-ETAL-2023].
  - [REF-LIPMAN-ETAL-2023](../references.md#ref-lipman-etal-2023): Yaron Lipman, Ricky T. Q. Chen, Heli Ben-Hamu, Maximilian Nickel, and Matt Le. "Flow Matching for Generative Modeling." In *International Conference on Learning Representations* (ICLR 2023), 2023. Regression of a velocity field on conditional probability paths, including the straight-line (optimal-transport) Gaussian path. Preprint arXiv:2210.02747. [https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.02747](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.02747).
  - [REF-WILDBERGER-ETAL-2023](../references.md#ref-wildberger-etal-2023): Wildberger et al. (with Dax, Buchholz, Green, Macke, and Schölkopf; the order of the authors differs between versions). "Flow Matching for Scalable Simulation-Based Inference." In *Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 36* (NeurIPS 2023), 2023. Flow matching with the observation as a conditioning input, trained on simulated parameter/data pairs, as an amortized posterior estimator. Preprint arXiv:2305.17161. [https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.17161](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.17161).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SBI-012](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sbi-012--flow-matching-posterior-in-the-bimodal-comparison-sample-only)

### EQ-INV-SBI-013 — Proposal posterior and the prior-only training policy

`q~(theta | y) ∝ p(y | theta) q(theta) ∝ p(theta | y) q(theta) / p(theta)`:
a conditional density estimator trained on pairs `theta ~ q(theta)`,
`y ~ p(y | theta)` targets the proposal posterior, not `p(theta | y)`.

- Domain: a proposal `q` with support in that of the prior.
- Meaning: training on proposal draws silently shifts the target unless a
  method-specific correction is applied. This module accepts only
  `training_policy="prior"` and raises `ProposalTrainingRefused` for
  proposal-based requests; no correction is implemented.
- Code: `inverse.sbi_workflow.check_training_policy`,
  `inverse.sbi_workflow.ProposalTrainingRefused`.
- Source: [REF-PAPAMAKARIOS-MURRAY-2016].
  - [REF-PAPAMAKARIOS-MURRAY-2016](../references.md#ref-papamakarios-murray-2016): George Papamakarios and Iain Murray. "Fast epsilon-free Inference of Simulation Models with Bayesian Conditional Density Estimation." In *Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 29* (NeurIPS 2016), 2016. Preprint arXiv:1605.06376v4. [https://arxiv.org/abs/1605.06376](https://arxiv.org/abs/1605.06376).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SBI-013](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sbi-013--proposal-posterior-and-the-prior-only-training-policy)

## `probstat.inverse.sparse`

### EQ-INV-SPARSE-001 — L1-regularised least squares

`min_x 1/2 ||W (A x - y)||^2 + alpha sum_i w_i |x_i - c_i|` (optionally on a box), `W = C^-1` with `C` a covariance factor.

- Domain: `alpha >= 0`, `w_i >= 0`.
- Meaning: `alpha` multiplies an UNSQUARED penalty, unlike the Tikhonov `lambda^2` that multiplies a squared norm. The smooth gradient is `A' W' W (A x - y)`; the penalty prox is EQ-OPT-PROX-001 on the box. With `alpha = 0` the problem is ordinary least squares, solved slowly by proximal gradient when `A` is ill conditioned.
- Code: `inverse.sparse.l1_regularized_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-BECK-TEBOULLE-2009], [REF-DAUBECHIES-DEFRISE-DEMOL-2004].
  - [REF-BECK-TEBOULLE-2009](../references.md#ref-beck-teboulle-2009): Amir Beck and Marc Teboulle. "A Fast Iterative Shrinkage-Thresholding Algorithm for Linear Inverse Problems." *SIAM Journal on Imaging Sciences* 2(1), 183-202, 2009. Cited for ISTA with backtracking and the accelerated FISTA momentum sequence `t_(k+1) = (1 + sqrt(1 + 4 t_k^2)) / 2`. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/080716542](https://doi.org/10.1137/080716542).
  - [REF-DAUBECHIES-DEFRISE-DEMOL-2004](../references.md#ref-daubechies-defrise-demol-2004): Ingrid Daubechies, Michel Defrise, and Christine De Mol. "An Iterative Thresholding Algorithm for Linear Inverse Problems with a Sparsity Constraint." *Communications on Pure and Applied Mathematics* 57(11), 1413-1457, 2004. Cited for iterative soft thresholding of the `l1`-penalized least-squares problem. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1002/cpa.20042](https://doi.org/10.1002/cpa.20042).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPARSE-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sparse-001--l1-regularised-least-squares)

### EQ-INV-SPARSE-002 — Lasso zero threshold

`alpha_max = max_i |(A' W' W (y - A c))_i| / w_i`; for `alpha >= alpha_max` the minimiser of EQ-INV-SPARSE-001 is `x = c`.

- Domain: unconstrained problem (bounds not considered).
- Meaning: subgradient optimality at `x = c` reads `|g_i| <= alpha w_i`; with `c = 0` and unit weights this is `||A' W' W y||_inf`.
- Code: `inverse.sparse.lasso_zero_threshold`.
- Source: [REF-BECK-TEBOULLE-2009].
  - [REF-BECK-TEBOULLE-2009](../references.md#ref-beck-teboulle-2009): Amir Beck and Marc Teboulle. "A Fast Iterative Shrinkage-Thresholding Algorithm for Linear Inverse Problems." *SIAM Journal on Imaging Sciences* 2(1), 183-202, 2009. Cited for ISTA with backtracking and the accelerated FISTA momentum sequence `t_(k+1) = (1 + sqrt(1 + 4 t_k^2)) / 2`. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/080716542](https://doi.org/10.1137/080716542).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPARSE-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sparse-002--lasso-zero-threshold)

### EQ-INV-SPARSE-003 — Discrete total variation

`TV(x) = sum_i w_i |(D x)_i|` with `|.|` the Euclidean norm (isotropic) or the sum of absolute values (anisotropic) of the `c` gradient components at point `i`.

- Domain: `D` a forward-difference or grid-gradient operator (boundary and spacing as defined by that operator), `w_i >= 0` area weights.
- Meaning: constants have `TV = 0`. For a one-dimensional difference both norms coincide. The penalised objective is `1/2 ||W (A x - y)||^2 + alpha TV(x)`, `alpha` unsquared.
- Code: `inverse.sparse.total_variation_value`, `inverse.sparse.total_variation_regularized_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-RUDIN-OSHER-FATEMI-1992], [REF-CHAMBOLLE-POCK-2011].
  - [REF-RUDIN-OSHER-FATEMI-1992](../references.md#ref-rudin-osher-fatemi-1992): Leonid I. Rudin, Stanley Osher, and Emad Fatemi. "Nonlinear Total Variation Based Noise Removal Algorithms." *Physica D: Nonlinear Phenomena* 60(1-4), 259-268, 1992. Cited for the total-variation penalty. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1016/0167-2789(92)90242-F](https://doi.org/10.1016/0167-2789(92)90242-F).
  - [REF-CHAMBOLLE-POCK-2011](../references.md#ref-chambolle-pock-2011): Antonin Chambolle and Thomas Pock. "A First-Order Primal-Dual Algorithm for Convex Problems with Applications to Imaging." *Journal of Mathematical Imaging and Vision* 40(1), 120-145, 2011. Cited for the primal-dual hybrid gradient iteration with over-relaxation, its step condition `tau sigma ||K||^2 < 1`, and the forward-difference discrete gradient used for total variation. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/s10851-010-0251-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10851-010-0251-1).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPARSE-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sparse-003--discrete-total-variation)

### EQ-INV-SPARSE-004 — TV by primal-dual on the stacked operator

`K = [W A; D]`, `g(u, v) = 1/2 ||u - W y||^2 + alpha TV(v)`, `f = indicator(box)`; `prox_{sigma g*}(p, q) = ((p - sigma W y)/(1 + sigma), proj_{|q_i| <= alpha w_i}(q))`.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-SPARSE-003.
- Meaning: the conjugate of the quadratic gives the shifted shrinkage, the conjugate of the TV norm is the indicator of the dual ball of radius `alpha w_i` (EQ-OPT-PROX-005); `f` is the box projection.
- Code: `inverse.sparse.total_variation_regularized_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-CHAMBOLLE-POCK-2011].
  - [REF-CHAMBOLLE-POCK-2011](../references.md#ref-chambolle-pock-2011): Antonin Chambolle and Thomas Pock. "A First-Order Primal-Dual Algorithm for Convex Problems with Applications to Imaging." *Journal of Mathematical Imaging and Vision* 40(1), 120-145, 2011. Cited for the primal-dual hybrid gradient iteration with over-relaxation, its step condition `tau sigma ||K||^2 < 1`, and the forward-difference discrete gradient used for total variation. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/s10851-010-0251-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10851-010-0251-1).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPARSE-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sparse-004--tv-by-primal-dual-on-the-stacked-operator)

### EQ-INV-SPARSE-005 — TV by ADMM with a CGLS x-update

Split `z = D x`; `x+ = argmin 1/2 ||W (A x - y)||^2 + rho/2 ||D x - z + u||^2` is the least-squares problem `[W A; sqrt(rho) D] x = [W y; sqrt(rho)(z - u)]`; `z+` is the prox of `(alpha/rho) TV` (EQ-OPT-PROX-005).

- Domain: no box constraints; stacked operator of full column rank for a unique x-update.
- Meaning: the x-update runs CGLS from a zero start to a relative normal-residual tolerance; the number of inner iterations and the number of inner solves that missed their tolerance are reported, and such failures prevent `converged`.
- Code: `inverse.sparse.total_variation_regularized_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-BOYD-ETAL-2011].
  - [REF-BOYD-ETAL-2011](../references.md#ref-boyd-etal-2011): Stephen Boyd, Neal Parikh, Eric Chu, Borja Peleato, and Jonathan Eckstein. "Distributed Optimization and Statistical Learning via the Alternating Direction Method of Multipliers." *Foundations and Trends in Machine Learning* 3(1), 1-122, 2011. Cited for scaled-form ADMM, its primal and dual residuals, and the absolute-plus-relative stopping thresholds. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1561/2200000016](https://doi.org/10.1561/2200000016).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPARSE-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sparse-005--tv-by-admm-with-a-cgls-x-update)

### EQ-INV-SPARSE-006 — Debiasing refit on the support

`x_debiased = c + S' argmin_b ||W (A S' b - (y - A c))||` with `S` the selection of `{i : x_i != c_i}`.

- Domain: unconstrained problem.
- Meaning: removes the shrinkage of the L1 penalty on the selected coefficients; it is a separate output and is not guaranteed to reduce the error (a wrong support is refitted too).
- Code: `inverse.sparse.l1_regularized_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-BECK-TEBOULLE-2009].
  - [REF-BECK-TEBOULLE-2009](../references.md#ref-beck-teboulle-2009): Amir Beck and Marc Teboulle. "A Fast Iterative Shrinkage-Thresholding Algorithm for Linear Inverse Problems." *SIAM Journal on Imaging Sciences* 2(1), 183-202, 2009. Cited for ISTA with backtracking and the accelerated FISTA momentum sequence `t_(k+1) = (1 + sqrt(1 + 4 t_k^2)) / 2`. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/080716542](https://doi.org/10.1137/080716542).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPARSE-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sparse-006--debiasing-refit-on-the-support)

### EQ-INV-SPARSE-008 — Operator-norm bound for the stacked operator

`||K||_2^2 <= ||W A||_2^2 + ||D||_2^2`, `||M||_2 <= ||M||_F`, `||D||_2 <= 2/h` (one dimension), `||D||_2^2 <= 4/h_x^2 + 4/h_y^2` (grid gradient).

- Domain: `K = [W A; D]`.
- Meaning: certified upper bounds used to validate `tau sigma ||K||^2 < 1` (EQ-OPT-PROX-008); loose bounds only shorten the steps.
- Code: `inverse.sparse.frobenius_norm_bound`, `inverse.sparse.total_variation_regularized_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013], [REF-CHAMBOLLE-POCK-2011].
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
  - [REF-CHAMBOLLE-POCK-2011](../references.md#ref-chambolle-pock-2011): Antonin Chambolle and Thomas Pock. "A First-Order Primal-Dual Algorithm for Convex Problems with Applications to Imaging." *Journal of Mathematical Imaging and Vision* 40(1), 120-145, 2011. Cited for the primal-dual hybrid gradient iteration with over-relaxation, its step condition `tau sigma ||K||^2 < 1`, and the forward-difference discrete gradient used for total variation. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/s10851-010-0251-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10851-010-0251-1).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPARSE-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-sparse-008--operator-norm-bound-for-the-stacked-operator)

## `probstat.inverse.sparse_extended`

### EQ-INV-SPX-001 — Reweighted least-squares step for a smoothed analysis L1 penalty

`x_(k+1) = argmin_x 1/2 ||A x - y||^2 + (alpha/2) sum_i w_i^(k) (L x)_i^2`, `w_i^(k) = 1 / sqrt((L x_k)_i^2 + eps^2)`, equivalently `min ||[A; sqrt(alpha W_k) L] x - [y; 0]||_2`.

- Domain: `alpha > 0`, `eps > 0`, `A` of size `m x n`, `L` of size `p x n`.
- Meaning: each outer step solves one weighted stacked least-squares problem (here by CGLS warm-started at `x_k`); `L = I` gives the lasso, `L = D` anisotropic total variation. `eps` is explicit and fixed; there is no continuation and no exact-L0 claim.
- Code: `inverse.sparse_extended.irls_regularized_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-RODRIGUEZ-WOHLBERG-2009], [REF-DAUBECHIES-DEVORE-FORNASIER-GUNTURK-2010].
  - [REF-RODRIGUEZ-WOHLBERG-2009](../references.md#ref-rodriguez-wohlberg-2009): Paul Rodriguez and Brendt Wohlberg. "Efficient Minimization Method for a Generalized Total Variation Functional." *IEEE Transactions on Image Processing* 18(2), 322-332, 2009. Cited for the iteratively reweighted norm method with a smoothed absolute value for L1 and total-variation penalties. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1109/TIP.2008.2008420](https://doi.org/10.1109/TIP.2008.2008420).
  - [REF-DAUBECHIES-DEVORE-FORNASIER-GUNTURK-2010](../references.md#ref-daubechies-devore-fornasier-gunturk-2010): Ingrid Daubechies, Ronald DeVore, Massimo Fornasier and C. Sinan Gunturk. "Iteratively Reweighted Least Squares Minimization for Sparse Recovery." *Communications on Pure and Applied Mathematics* 63(1), 1-38, 2010. Cited for the reweighted least-squares scheme with an explicit smoothing parameter and its majorisation argument. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1002/cpa.20303](https://doi.org/10.1002/cpa.20303).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPX-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-spx-001--reweighted-least-squares-step-for-a-smoothed-analysis-l1-penalty)

### EQ-INV-SPX-002 — Majorisation and monotone decrease of the smoothed objective

`F_eps(x) = 1/2 ||A x - y||^2 + alpha sum_i sqrt((L x)_i^2 + eps^2)`; `sqrt(s) <= 1/2 (s / sqrt(s_0) + sqrt(s_0))` for `s, s_0 > 0` (concavity of the square root), so `F_eps(x_(k+1)) <= F_eps(x_k)` when the weighted problem is solved exactly.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-SPX-001, exact inner solves.
- Meaning: the iteration is a majorise-minimise scheme; with the finite CGLS tolerance a small increase is possible and is reported as `max_objective_increase`.
- Code: `inverse.sparse_extended.irls_regularized_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-DAUBECHIES-DEVORE-FORNASIER-GUNTURK-2010].
