### EQ-INV-SPX-003 — Smoothing error bound for the true objective

`F(x) = 1/2 ||A x - y||^2 + alpha ||L x||_1`; `F <= F_eps <= F + alpha p eps` with `p` the number of rows of `L`, hence `F(x_eps) <= min F + alpha p eps` for a minimiser `x_eps` of `F_eps`.

- Domain: `0 <= sqrt(t^2 + eps^2) - |t| <= eps`.
- Meaning: the true objective at the smoothed minimiser is at most `alpha p eps` above the optimum, and never below it; entries of the smoothed minimiser are not exactly zero.
- Code: `inverse.sparse_extended.irls_regularized_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-RODRIGUEZ-WOHLBERG-2009].
  - [REF-RODRIGUEZ-WOHLBERG-2009](../references.md#ref-rodriguez-wohlberg-2009): Paul Rodriguez and Brendt Wohlberg. "Efficient Minimization Method for a Generalized Total Variation Functional." *IEEE Transactions on Image Processing* 18(2), 322-332, 2009. Cited for the iteratively reweighted norm method with a smoothed absolute value for L1 and total-variation penalties. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1109/TIP.2008.2008420](https://doi.org/10.1109/TIP.2008.2008420).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPX-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-spx-003--smoothing-error-bound-for-the-true-objective)

### EQ-INV-SPX-004 — Huber function and Huber total variation

`h_delta(s) = s^2 / (2 delta)` for `0 <= s <= delta`, `h_delta(s) = s - delta/2` for `s > delta`; `HTV_delta(x) = sum_i h_delta(|(D x)_i|)`.

- Domain: `delta > 0`; `|.|` the Euclidean norm of the gradient components at a point (isotropic) or entrywise.
- Meaning: `|s| - delta/2 <= h_delta(s) <= |s|`, so `TV(x) - p delta/2 <= HTV_delta(x) <= TV(x)`; the objective `1/2||A x - y||^2 + alpha HTV_delta(x)` is continuously differentiable with the gradient `A'(A x - y) + alpha D' (D x / max(delta, |D x|))`.
- Code: `inverse.sparse_extended.huber_total_variation_value`, `inverse.sparse_extended.huber_total_variation_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-HUBER-1964].
  - [REF-HUBER-1964](../references.md#ref-huber-1964): Peter J. Huber. "Robust Estimation of a Location Parameter." *The Annals of Mathematical Statistics* 35(1), 73-101, 1964. The minimax M-estimator of location and the score function `max(-k, min(k, u))`. DOI: [10.1214/aoms/1177703732](https://doi.org/10.1214/aoms/1177703732).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPX-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-spx-004--huber-function-and-huber-total-variation)

### EQ-INV-SPX-005 — Dual proximal map of the Huber term

`h_delta(s) = max_(|q| <= 1) q s - delta q^2 / 2`; for `g(v) = alpha sum h_delta(|v_i|)` the conjugate is `g*(q) = delta/(2 alpha) |q|^2 + I(|q_i| <= alpha)` and `prox_(sigma g*)(p) = P_(|q_i| <= alpha)(p / (1 + sigma delta / alpha))`.

- Domain: `alpha, delta, sigma > 0`; `P` the projection onto the dual ball (clipping, or pointwise Euclidean projection for isotropic multi-component gradients).
- Meaning: shrink then project; combined with the data-block map `(p - sigma y)/(1 + sigma)` it gives the Chambolle-Pock iteration on `K = [A; D]` with `tau sigma ||K||^2 < 1`.
- Code: `inverse.sparse_extended.huber_total_variation_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-CHAMBOLLE-POCK-2011], [REF-HUBER-1964].
  - [REF-CHAMBOLLE-POCK-2011](../references.md#ref-chambolle-pock-2011): Antonin Chambolle and Thomas Pock. "A First-Order Primal-Dual Algorithm for Convex Problems with Applications to Imaging." *Journal of Mathematical Imaging and Vision* 40(1), 120-145, 2011. Cited for the primal-dual hybrid gradient iteration with over-relaxation, its step condition `tau sigma ||K||^2 < 1`, and the forward-difference discrete gradient used for total variation. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/s10851-010-0251-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10851-010-0251-1).
  - [REF-HUBER-1964](../references.md#ref-huber-1964): Peter J. Huber. "Robust Estimation of a Location Parameter." *The Annals of Mathematical Statistics* 35(1), 73-101, 1964. The minimax M-estimator of location and the score function `max(-k, min(k, u))`. DOI: [10.1214/aoms/1177703732](https://doi.org/10.1214/aoms/1177703732).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPX-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-spx-005--dual-proximal-map-of-the-huber-term)

### EQ-INV-SPX-006 — Limits of Huber total variation in delta

`|min F_delta - min F_TV| <= alpha p delta / 2` for `delta -> 0`; for `delta` so large that `|(D x)_i| <= delta` at the optimum, `HTV_delta(x) = ||D x||^2 / (2 delta)` and the problem is Tikhonov with `lambda^2 = alpha / delta` (normal equations `(A'A + (alpha/delta) D'D) x = A'y`).

- Domain: `alpha > 0`.
- Meaning: small `delta` recovers total variation (with its staircasing), large `delta` quadratic smoothing; the rate of the first limit is linear in `delta`.
- Code: `inverse.sparse_extended.huber_total_variation_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-HUBER-1964], [REF-RUDIN-OSHER-FATEMI-1992].
  - [REF-HUBER-1964](../references.md#ref-huber-1964): Peter J. Huber. "Robust Estimation of a Location Parameter." *The Annals of Mathematical Statistics* 35(1), 73-101, 1964. The minimax M-estimator of location and the score function `max(-k, min(k, u))`. DOI: [10.1214/aoms/1177703732](https://doi.org/10.1214/aoms/1177703732).
  - [REF-RUDIN-OSHER-FATEMI-1992](../references.md#ref-rudin-osher-fatemi-1992): Leonid I. Rudin, Stanley Osher, and Emad Fatemi. "Nonlinear Total Variation Based Noise Removal Algorithms." *Physica D: Nonlinear Phenomena* 60(1-4), 259-268, 1992. Cited for the total-variation penalty. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1016/0167-2789(92)90242-F](https://doi.org/10.1016/0167-2789(92)90242-F).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPX-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-spx-006--limits-of-huber-total-variation-in-delta)

### EQ-INV-SPX-007 — Second-order total generalized variation in one dimension

`TGV^2_(alpha1, alpha0)(x) = min_v alpha1 ||D x - v||_1 + alpha0 ||E v||_1`, with `D` (`n-1` rows) and `E` (`n-2` rows) forward differences of spacing `h`.

- Domain: `alpha1, alpha0 > 0`, `n >= 3`.
- Meaning: vanishes exactly on affine signals (take `v` the constant slope), so ramps are not penalised and are not converted into plateaus; it is a convex functional of `x`. Only the one-dimensional case is implemented.
- Code: `inverse.sparse_extended.tgv2_least_squares_1d`.
- Source: [REF-BREDIES-KUNISCH-POCK-2010].
  - [REF-BREDIES-KUNISCH-POCK-2010](../references.md#ref-bredies-kunisch-pock-2010): Kristian Bredies, Karl Kunisch and Thomas Pock. "Total Generalized Variation." *SIAM Journal on Imaging Sciences* 3(3), 492-526, 2010. Cited for the second-order total generalized variation functional and its avoidance of staircasing. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/090769521](https://doi.org/10.1137/090769521).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPX-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-spx-007--second-order-total-generalized-variation-in-one-dimension)

### EQ-INV-SPX-008 — Primal-dual form of TGV^2 regularised least squares and its step bound

`min_z g(K z)` with `z = (x, v)`, `K z = (A x, D x - v, E v)`, `g(u, q, r) = 1/2 ||u - y||^2 + alpha1 ||q||_1 + alpha0 ||r||_1`; dual prox `((p - sigma y)/(1 + sigma), clip(q, alpha1), clip(r, alpha0))`; steps satisfy `tau sigma L^2 < 1` with `L^2 = ||A||_F^2 + (||D||^2 + 1) + ||E||^2 >= ||K||_2^2` and `||D||, ||E|| <= 2/h`.

- Domain: `L` is a proven bound (stacking: `||K z||^2 <= sum ||K_j||^2 ||z||^2`; `[D, -I][D, -I]^T = D D^T + I`), not a power-iteration estimate.
- Meaning: Chambolle-Pock converges for any `tau sigma L^2 < 1`; the bound is conservative, so the iteration can be slow (thousands of iterations).
- Code: `inverse.sparse_extended.tgv2_least_squares_1d`.
- Source: [REF-CHAMBOLLE-POCK-2011], [REF-BREDIES-KUNISCH-POCK-2010].

### EQ-INV-SPX-009 — Minimax concave penalty and its exact proximal map

`p(t) = lam |t| - t^2/(2 gamma)` for `|t| <= gamma lam`, `p(t) = gamma lam^2 / 2` otherwise; `prox_(s p)(v) = 0` for `|v| <= s lam`, `sign(v) (|v| - s lam)/(1 - s/gamma)` for `s lam < |v| <= gamma lam`, `v` for `|v| > gamma lam`.

- Domain: `lam >= 0`, `gamma > 1`, `0 < s < gamma` (for `s >= gamma` the minimiser of `1/2 (x - v)^2 + s p(x)` need not be unique and the map is refused).
- Meaning: global minimiser of the scalar problem, continuous in `v`, odd, and identity for `|v| > gamma lam` (no shrinkage bias); the penalty is non-convex (weakly convex with modulus `1/gamma`).
- Code: `inverse.sparse_extended.prox_mcp`, `inverse.sparse_extended.mcp_penalty`.
- Source: [REF-ZHANG-2010].
  - [REF-ZHANG-2010](../references.md#ref-zhang-2010): Cun-Hui Zhang. "Nearly Unbiased Variable Selection under Minimax Concave Penalty." *The Annals of Statistics* 38(2), 894-942, 2010. Cited for the minimax concave penalty. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1214/09-AOS729](https://doi.org/10.1214/09-AOS729).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPX-009](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-spx-009--minimax-concave-penalty-and-its-exact-proximal-map)

### EQ-INV-SPX-010 — SCAD penalty and its exact proximal map

`p(t) = lam |t|` for `|t| <= lam`, `(2 a lam |t| - t^2 - lam^2) / (2 (a - 1))` for `lam < |t| <= a lam`, `(a + 1) lam^2 / 2` otherwise; `prox_(s p)(v) = sign(v) max(|v| - s lam, 0)` for `|v| <= (1 + s) lam`, `((a - 1) v - sign(v) a s lam)/(a - 1 - s)` for `(1 + s) lam < |v| <= a lam`, `v` for `|v| > a lam`.

- Domain: `lam >= 0`, `a > 2`, `0 < s < a - 1`.
- Meaning: global minimiser of the scalar problem, continuous, odd and unbiased for `|v| > a lam`; non-convex.
- Code: `inverse.sparse_extended.prox_scad`, `inverse.sparse_extended.scad_penalty`.
- Source: [REF-FAN-LI-2001].
  - [REF-FAN-LI-2001](../references.md#ref-fan-li-2001): Jianqing Fan and Runze Li. "Variable Selection via Nonconcave Penalized Likelihood and its Oracle Properties." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 96(456), 1348-1360, 2001. Cited for the SCAD penalty. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1198/016214501753382273](https://doi.org/10.1198/016214501753382273).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPX-010](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-spx-010--scad-penalty-and-its-exact-proximal-map)

### EQ-INV-SPX-011 — Proximal gradient for non-convex penalties: stationary points only

`x_(k+1) = prox_(t p)(x_k - t A'(A x_k - y))` minimises `1/2||A x - y||^2 + sum p(x_i)`; the objective is non-increasing for `t <= 1/L_f` and `t < gamma` (MCP) or `t < a - 1` (SCAD), and `||x - prox_(t p)(x - t grad f(x))|| / t = 0` characterises a stationary point.

- Domain: as EQ-INV-SPX-009 and EQ-INV-SPX-010.
- Meaning: a fixed point is only a first-order stationary point; different start points can reach different stationary points with different objective values; nothing certifies global optimality.
- Code: `inverse.sparse_extended.nonconvex_sparse_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-BECK-TEBOULLE-2009], [REF-ZHANG-2010], [REF-FAN-LI-2001].
  - [REF-BECK-TEBOULLE-2009](../references.md#ref-beck-teboulle-2009): Amir Beck and Marc Teboulle. "A Fast Iterative Shrinkage-Thresholding Algorithm for Linear Inverse Problems." *SIAM Journal on Imaging Sciences* 2(1), 183-202, 2009. Cited for the minimax concave penalty. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1214/09-AOS729](https://doi.org/10.1214/09-AOS729).

"Variable Selection via Nonconcave Penalized Likelihood and its Oracle Properties." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 96(456), 1348-1360, 2001. Cited for the SCAD penalty. ### EQ-INV-SPX-012 — Second-order total generalized variation on a 2D grid

`TGV^2(x) = min_v alpha1 sum_i |M (G x - v)_i| + alpha0 sum_i |(E v)_i|`, with `G = (D_x; D_y)` the Neumann forward-difference gradient of an `r x c` row-major image (`D_x` zero on the last column, `D_y` zero on the last row), `v = (v_1; v_2)` a vector field, `E v = (D_x v_1, D_y v_2, (D_y v_1 + D_x v_2)/sqrt 2)` (the Euclidean point norm of this triple is the Frobenius norm of the symmetrised gradient), `M` the 0/1 mask that removes the zero-padded entries of `G`, and `|.|` the pointwise Euclidean norm (2 components for the first term, 3 for the second). The reconstruction problem is `min_(x,v) 1/2||W(A x - y)||^2 + TGV^2` and equals `min g(K z)` with `z = (x, v)`, `K z = (A x, M (G x - v), E v)`.

- Domain: `r, c >= 2`; positive weights `alpha1`, `alpha0` (unsquared multipliers); positive spacings `(h_y, h_x)`; the first-order term uses only the valid forward differences.
- Meaning: second-order TGV favours piecewise-affine images (TV favours piecewise-constant ones and staircases on ramps). Because the mask removes the padded boundary differences, every affine image has TGV^2 exactly zero (take `v` constant); the free boundary entries of `v` are controlled only by `E`. This is a discretisation, not the continuum functional; the discrete minimiser's `v` estimates `G x` only where the first-order term is active.
- Code: `inverse.sparse_extended.tgv2_least_squares_2d`.
- Source: [REF-BREDIES-KUNISCH-POCK-2010], [REF-CHAMBOLLE-POCK-2011].
  - [REF-BREDIES-KUNISCH-POCK-2010](../references.md#ref-bredies-kunisch-pock-2010): Kristian Bredies, Karl Kunisch and Thomas Pock. "Total Generalized Variation." *SIAM Journal on Imaging Sciences* 3(3), 492-526, 2010. Cited for the second-order total generalized variation functional and its avoidance of staircasing. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/090769521](https://doi.org/10.1137/090769521).
  - [REF-CHAMBOLLE-POCK-2011](../references.md#ref-chambolle-pock-2011): Antonin Chambolle and Thomas Pock. "A First-Order Primal-Dual Algorithm for Convex Problems with Applications to Imaging." *Journal of Mathematical Imaging and Vision* 40(1), 120-145, 2011. Cited for the primal-dual hybrid gradient iteration with over-relaxation, its step condition `tau sigma ||K||^2 < 1`, and the forward-difference discrete gradient used for total variation. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/s10851-010-0251-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10851-010-0251-1).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPX-012](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-spx-012--second-order-total-generalized-variation-on-a-2d-grid)

### EQ-INV-SPX-013 — Proven operator-norm bound of the 2D TGV^2 primal-dual operator

`||K||^2 <= ||A||_F^2 + 2 ||G||^2 + 1`, `||G||^2 <= 4/h_x^2 + 4/h_y^2` (`= 8/h^2` for a square grid).

- Domain: `K` of EQ-INV-SPX-012. Proof: `(a - b)^2 <= 2a^2 + 2b^2` gives `||D_x||^2 <= 4/h_x^2` and `||D_y||^2 <= 4/h_y^2` with Neumann padding, hence `||G||^2 <= ||D_x||^2 + ||D_y||^2`; `(a + b)^2/2 <= a^2 + b^2` gives `||E v||^2 <= ||D_x v_1||^2 + ||D_y v_1||^2 + ||D_x v_2||^2 + ||D_y v_2||^2 <= ||G||^2 ||v||^2`; `||M (G x - v)||^2 <= (||G||^2 + 1)(||x||^2 + ||v||^2)` by Cauchy-Schwarz; the three blocks add (`||A x||^2 <= ||A||_F^2 ||x||^2`).
- Meaning: a certified, conservative bound (not a power-iteration estimate), so `tau sigma ||K||^2 < 1` holds for the PDHG steps; it can slow convergence.
- Code: `inverse.sparse_extended.tgv2_least_squares_2d`.
- Source: [REF-CHAMBOLLE-POCK-2011].
  - [REF-CHAMBOLLE-POCK-2011](../references.md#ref-chambolle-pock-2011): Antonin Chambolle and Thomas Pock. "A First-Order Primal-Dual Algorithm for Convex Problems with Applications to Imaging." *Journal of Mathematical Imaging and Vision* 40(1), 120-145, 2011. Cited for the primal-dual hybrid gradient iteration with over-relaxation, its step condition `tau sigma ||K||^2 < 1`, and the forward-difference discrete gradient used for total variation. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1007/s10851-010-0251-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10851-010-0251-1).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPX-013](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-spx-013--proven-operator-norm-bound-of-the-2d-tgv2-primal-dual-operator)

### EQ-INV-SPX-014 — IRLS for isotropic total variation: per-pixel weights and gap bound

`F_eps(x) = 1/2||A x - y||^2 + alpha sum_i sqrt(|g_i|^2 + eps^2)`, `g_i = ((D_x x)_i, (D_y x)_i)`; majorant at `g^0`: `alpha/2 sum_i w_i (|g_i|^2 + eps^2) + alpha/(2 w_i)` with `w_i = 1/sqrt(|g^0_i|^2 + eps^2)`; `F(x) <= F_eps(x) <= F(x) + alpha p eps`, so `F(x_eps) <= min F + alpha p eps` at the exact smoothed minimiser, `p` the number of penalty points (pixels).

- Domain: `alpha, eps > 0`; `F` the true isotropic objective `1/2||A x - y||^2 + alpha sum_i |g_i|`.
- Meaning: `s -> sqrt(s + eps^2)` is concave, so its tangent at `s_0 = |g^0|^2` majorises it; each step is a weighted least-squares problem with ONE weight per pixel shared by both gradient components, and the smoothed objective is non-increasing under exact inner solves. The result is the minimiser of the smoothed problem, not the exact TV solution; the zero-padded last row and column add the constant `alpha eps` per pixel.
- Code: `inverse.sparse_extended.irls_regularized_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-RODRIGUEZ-WOHLBERG-2009], [REF-DAUBECHIES-DEVORE-FORNASIER-GUNTURK-2010].
  - [REF-RODRIGUEZ-WOHLBERG-2009](../references.md#ref-rodriguez-wohlberg-2009): Paul Rodriguez and Brendt Wohlberg. "Efficient Minimization Method for a Generalized Total Variation Functional." *IEEE Transactions on Image Processing* 18(2), 322-332, 2009. DOI: [10.1109/TIP.2008.2008420](https://doi.org/10.1109/TIP.2008.2008420).

"Iteratively Reweighted Least Squares Minimization for Sparse Recovery." *Communications on Pure and Applied Mathematics* 63(1), 1-38, 2010. Cited for the reweighted least-squares scheme with an explicit smoothing parameter and its majorisation argument. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). ### EQ-INV-SPX-015 — Whitened data term of the extended sparse solvers

`1/2||W(A x - y)||^2` with `W = C^-1` the whitening operator of a covariance factor `C` (`Gamma = C C'`), solved as the unwhitened problem for `(W A, W y)`.

- Domain: `noise` is a covariance factor of `probstat.linalg` of dimension `m` or `None` (no whitening; results are bit-identical to the unwhitened solver); `forward_norm_bound`, when given, bounds `||W A||_2`.
- Meaning: `W A` and `W y` replace `A` and `y` everywhere, so reported misfits are in whitened units and equal `1/2 (A x - y)' Gamma^-1 (A x - y)`; the penalty and its weights are unchanged. Solving with `noise=DiagonalCovariance(v)` equals solving the manually whitened problem up to floating-point rounding.
- Code: `inverse.sparse_extended.irls_regularized_least_squares`, `inverse.sparse_extended.huber_total_variation_least_squares`, `inverse.sparse_extended.tgv2_least_squares_1d`, `inverse.sparse_extended.tgv2_least_squares_2d`, `inverse.sparse_extended.nonconvex_sparse_least_squares`.
- Source: [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007], [REF-TARANTOLA-2005].
  - [REF-KAIPIO-SOMERSALO-2007](../references.md#ref-kaipio-somersalo-2007): Jari Kaipio and Erkki Somersalo. "Statistical Inverse Problems: Discretization, Model Reduction and Inverse Crimes." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 198(2), 493-504, 2007. Simulating data with the same discretization that inverts them (the inverse crime) hides the discretization and truncation error and makes reconstructions look better than they are; the approximation error should be modelled or the data generated with a finer model. DOI: [10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cam.2005.09.027).
  - [REF-TARANTOLA-2005](../references.md#ref-tarantola-2005): Albert Tarantola. *Inverse Problem Theory and Methods for Model Parameter Estimation*. Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, 2005. DOI: [10.1137/1.9780898717921](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9780898717921).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPX-015](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-spx-015--whitened-data-term-of-the-extended-sparse-solvers)

## `probstat.inverse.spectral`

### EQ-INV-SPEC-001 — Whitened singular spectrum, nullspace and Picard coefficients

`W A = U diag(sigma) V'`; rank `r = #{sigma_i > max(atol, rtol sigma_1)}`;
`P_null = I - V_r V_r'` with a completed orthonormal basis of the full
nullspace; Picard coefficients `|u_i'W y|` and `|u_i'W y| / sigma_i`;
out-of-range data `||(I - U U')W y||`; noise amplification `1/sigma_i`.

- Domain: dense operator (oracle scale); `sigma_i = 0` handled without
  division.
- Meaning: which parameter combinations the data can resolve. The numerical
  cutoff is not a detection threshold: a coefficient `c` along `v_i` changes
  the whitened data by `c sigma_i`, detectable against unit noise only when
  `|c| sigma_i >~ 1` (heuristic). Repeated singular values define subspaces,
  not unique vectors.
- Code: `inverse.spectral.spectral_diagnostics`,
  `inverse.spectral.SpectralDiagnostics.null_projection`,
  `inverse.spectral.SpectralDiagnostics.detectable`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008, Sections 2.1 and 2.4],
  [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013, Section 8.6].
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPEC-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-spec-001--whitened-singular-spectrum-nullspace-and-picard-coefficients)

### EQ-INV-SPEC-002 — Generalized SVD of a pair by QR of the stack

`[A; L] = [Q_A; Q_L] R`, `Q_A = U C V'`, `X = R^-1 V`: `A X = U C`,
`L X = Z S`, `C^2 + S^2 = I`, `gamma_i = c_i / s_i`; `s_i` computed as the
column norms of `Q_L V`.

- Domain: full column rank of `[A; L]`, any `m` (a wide `A` is handled by
  padding `Q_A` with zero rows, which leaves at least `n - m` cosines equal to
  zero); not a general LAPACK GSVD. Rank-deficient stacks: [EQ-INV-SPEC-004].
- Meaning: simultaneous diagonalization of the data and penalty terms; the
  generalized singular values play the role of `sigma_i` for general-form
  Tikhonov.
- Code: `inverse.spectral.generalized_svd`.
- Source: [REF-VAN-LOAN-1976], [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013, Section 8.7].
  - [REF-VAN-LOAN-1976](../references.md#ref-van-loan-1976): Charles F. Van Loan. "Generalizing the Singular Value Decomposition." *SIAM Journal on Numerical Analysis* 13(1), 76-83, 1976. Cited for the generalized singular value decomposition of a matrix pair. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/0713009](https://doi.org/10.1137/0713009).
  - [REF-GOLUB-VANLOAN-2013](../references.md#ref-golub-vanloan-2013): Gene H. Golub and Charles F. Van Loan. *Matrix Computations*. 4th edition. Johns Hopkins University Press, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPEC-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-spec-002--generalized-svd-of-a-pair-by-qr-of-the-stack)

### EQ-INV-SPEC-003 — General-form Tikhonov in GSVD coordinates

`x_lambda = X y`, `y_i = c_i u_i'b / (c_i^2 + lambda^2 s_i^2)`; filter factor
`gamma_i^2 / (gamma_i^2 + lambda^2)`.

- Domain: [EQ-INV-SPEC-002], `lambda > 0` (or every `c_i > 0`).
- Meaning: all Tikhonov solutions for any `lambda` from one decomposition.
- Code: `inverse.spectral.gsvd_tikhonov`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008, Section 2.1].
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPEC-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-spec-003--general-form-tikhonov-in-gsvd-coordinates)

### EQ-INV-SPEC-004 — GSVD restricted to the complement of a common nullspace

`[A; L] = U_s Sigma_s V_s'`, `r = #{sigma_i > tol sigma_1}`, `P = V_s[:, :r]`;
GSVD of `(A P, L P)` by [EQ-INV-SPEC-002], `X = P X_r`; `N = V_s[:, r:]` spans
`null(A) ∩ null(L)`.

- Domain: `null(A) ∩ null(L) != {0}`, explicit relative tolerance `tol`.
- Meaning: every `x + N t` has the same misfit and penalty, so general-form
  Tikhonov has a family of minimisers; [EQ-INV-SPEC-003] on the restricted
  pair returns the one of minimum Euclidean norm, and `N` names the directions
  that neither the data nor the penalty constrain.
- Code: `inverse.spectral.generalized_svd`, `inverse.spectral.gsvd_tikhonov`.
- Source: [REF-VAN-LOAN-1976], [REF-HANSEN-2008, Section 2.1].
  - [REF-VAN-LOAN-1976](../references.md#ref-van-loan-1976): Charles F. Van Loan. "Generalizing the Singular Value Decomposition." *SIAM Journal on Numerical Analysis* 13(1), 76-83, 1976. Cited for the generalized singular value decomposition of a matrix pair. Metadata checked against Crossref (2026-11-26). DOI: [10.1137/0713009](https://doi.org/10.1137/0713009).
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-SPEC-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-spec-004--gsvd-restricted-to-the-complement-of-a-common-nullspace)

## `probstat.inverse.test_problems`

### EQ-INV-REG-PROB-001 — Discrete backward heat operator

`A = V diag(exp(-T mu_k)) V'`, `V_jk = sqrt(2 h) sin(j k pi h)`,
`mu_k = (4 / h^2) sin^2(k pi h / 2)`, `h = 1 / (n + 1)`, `s_j = j h`;
exact solution `x(s) = (27 / 4) s (1 - s)^2` and `b = A x`.

- Domain: `n >= 2` interior points, `T > 0`.
- Meaning: `V diag(mu) V'` is the second-difference matrix with zero
  boundary values, so `A = exp(-T D)` advances the semi-discrete heat
  equation by `T`; its singular values `exp(-T mu_k)` are known exactly and
  decay like `exp(-T pi^2 k^2)`.
- Code: `inverse.backward_heat_problem`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008, Sections 2.1 and 2.3] (discrete ill-posed
  problems); the eigenpairs are verified in `tests/test_regularization.py`.
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-REG-PROB-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-reg-prob-001--discrete-backward-heat-operator)

### EQ-INV-REG-PROB-002 — Midpoint discretization of deriv2

`A_ij = h K(s_i, t_j)`, `s_i = t_i = (i - 1/2) h`, `h = 1 / n`,
`K(s, t) = s (t - 1)` for `s < t` and `t (s - 1)` for `s >= t`; exact
solution `x_j = t_j`, `b = A x`.

- Domain: `n >= 2`.
- Meaning: the Green's function of the second derivative on `[0, 1]`; the
  continuous data for `f(t) = t` are `g(s) = (s^3 - s) / 6`, which `A x`
  matches up to the `O(h^2)` quadrature error. Regularization Tools uses a
  Galerkin discretization, so its matrix differs.
- Code: `inverse.second_derivative_problem`.
- Source: [REF-HANSEN-2008, Chapter 4, deriv2] (kernel and the case
  `f(t) = t`).
  - [REF-HANSEN-2008](../references.md#ref-hansen-2008): Per Christian Hansen. *Regularization Tools: A Matlab Package for Analysis and Solution of Discrete Ill-Posed Problems*, Version 4.1 for Matlab 7.3. Manual, Informatics and Mathematical Modelling, Technical University of Denmark, March 2008; the package is described in *Numerical Algorithms* 46(2), 189-194, 2007. Chapter 2 covers ill-posedness, Tikhonov regularization and its stacked least-squares form, filter factors, the discrete Picard condition, truncated SVD, the L-curve, generalized cross-validation, and the discrepancy principle; Chapter 4 documents the test problem `deriv2`.
- Registry: [EQ-INV-REG-PROB-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-reg-prob-002--midpoint-discretization-of-deriv2)

## `probstat.inverse.unscented_inversion`

### EQ-INV-UKI-001 — Unscented Kalman inversion iteration

Prediction `m^ = m_n`, `C^ = 2 C_n`. Analysis with the augmented map
`G_a(u) = (G(u), u)`, data `y_a = (y, 0)` and `Gamma_a = diag(Gamma, I)`:
`m_{n+1} = m^ + C^uy (C^yy + 2 Gamma_a)^-1 (y_a - y^)`,
`C_{n+1} = C^ - C^uy (C^yy + 2 Gamma_a)^-1 C^uy'`, where `y^`, `C^uy`, `C^yy`
are the sigma-point moments of `G_a` under `N(m^, C^)`
([EQ-INV-UKI-002]). Start: `m_0 = 0`, `C_0 = I` (the prior).

- Domain: prior `N(0, I)` in `u`; `Gamma` positive definite.
- Meaning: the general scheme `m^ = alpha m + (1 - alpha) r_0`,
  `C^ = alpha^2 C + Sigma_w`, analysis with `Sigma_nu`, specialized to
  `alpha = 1`, `Sigma_w = C_n`, `Sigma_nu = 2 Gamma_a`, the choice for which
  the fixed point is the posterior in the affine case
  ([EQ-INV-UKI-003]). Each iteration costs `2 d + 1` forward evaluations.
- Code: `inverse.unscented_kalman_inversion`.
- Source: [REF-HUANG-SCHNEIDER-STUART-2022]; the hyperparameters are
  rederived in ADR-0130.
  - [REF-HUANG-SCHNEIDER-STUART-2022](../references.md#ref-huang-schneider-stuart-2022): Daniel Zhengyu Huang, Tapio Schneider, and Andrew M. Stuart. "Iterated Kalman Methodology for Inverse Problems." *Journal of Computational Physics* 463, 111262, 2022. Unscented Kalman inversion: a Gaussian approximation iterated by a prediction step and a sigma-point Kalman analysis step. DOI: [10.1016/j.jcp.2022.111262](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jcp.2022.111262).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-UKI-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-uki-001--unscented-kalman-inversion-iteration)

### EQ-INV-UKI-002 — Sigma points and weights of the unscented transform

For `N(m, C)` with `C = L L'`: points `m` and `m +/- sqrt(d + kappa) L e_i`,
`i = 1..d`; weights `kappa / (d + kappa)` for the centre and
`1 / (2 (d + kappa))` for the others, used for means and covariances alike.

- Domain: `kappa >= 0` (non-negative weights); `C` positive definite.
- Meaning: the weighted mean and covariance of the points are `m` and `C`
  exactly, so the moments of an affine map are exact; for a nonlinear map
  the transform matches the Taylor expansion of the mean to second order.
- Code: `inverse.unscented_kalman_inversion`.
- Source: [REF-JULIER-UHLMANN-2004].
  - [REF-JULIER-UHLMANN-2004](../references.md#ref-julier-uhlmann-2004): Simon J. Julier and Jeffrey K. Uhlmann. "Unscented Filtering and Nonlinear Estimation." *Proceedings of the IEEE* 92(3), 401-422, 2004. DOI: [10.1109/JPROC.2003.823141](https://doi.org/10.1109/JPROC.2003.823141).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-UKI-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-uki-002--sigma-points-and-weights-of-the-unscented-transform)

### EQ-INV-UKI-003 — Linear fixed point of unscented Kalman inversion

For `G(u) = A u + b`, `P = A' Gamma^-1 A + I`:
`C_{n+1}^-1 = (C_n^-1 + P) / 2` and
`C_{n+1}^-1 m_{n+1} = (C_n^-1 m_n + A' Gamma^-1 (y - b)) / 2`.

- Domain: affine forward map.
- Meaning: the Kalman analysis in information form is
  `C_{n+1}^-1 = (2 C_n)^-1 + A_a' (2 Gamma_a)^-1 A_a` with
  `A_a' Gamma_a^-1 A_a = P`. The unique fixed point is `C = P^-1`,
  `m = P^-1 A' Gamma^-1 (y - b)`, the posterior covariance and mean, and the
  errors of the precision and of the precision-weighted mean halve at every
  iteration. With `alpha < 1` and `r_0 = 0` the same computation gives the
  fixed-point mean `P^-1 A' Gamma^-1 (y - b) / (2 - alpha)`, which is why
  `alpha = 1` is fixed.
- Code: `inverse.unscented_kalman_inversion`.
- Source: derived in ADR-0130 for the scheme of
  [REF-HUANG-SCHNEIDER-STUART-2022].
  - [REF-HUANG-SCHNEIDER-STUART-2022](../references.md#ref-huang-schneider-stuart-2022): Daniel Zhengyu Huang, Tapio Schneider, and Andrew M. Stuart. "Iterated Kalman Methodology for Inverse Problems." *Journal of Computational Physics* 463, 111262, 2022. Unscented Kalman inversion: a Gaussian approximation iterated by a prediction step and a sigma-point Kalman analysis step. DOI: [10.1016/j.jcp.2022.111262](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jcp.2022.111262).
- Registry: [EQ-INV-UKI-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inv-uki-003--linear-fixed-point-of-unscented-kalman-inversion)
